title: Design Decisions

> **Numbering collides across parallel branches — expect it.** This file is append-only with a
> monotonic `D{N}`, and several worktrees append at once, so two branches routinely reach for the
> same number and git merges both without a conflict marker on the *number*. On a collision:
> **the side already on `develop` keeps its numbers**, the side merging in renumbers to the next
> free block, and — the part that gets missed — every cross-reference to the renumbered entries
> moves with them: the anchor links inside this file, `docs/ui/figma-cache/**`, context docs, and
> any `see D{N}` in a Dart doc comment. Grep the old numbers before committing the merge.
> (2026-08-28: D281/D282 → D286/D287, five references chased. 2026-09-02: D312/D313 → D317/D318,
> both numbers claimed twice over, two references chased in `docs/features/discovery.md`.)
>
> The `<!-- Most recent -->` markers at the tail are advisory and several stale copies are stranded
> mid-file from older merges. Trust the highest `### D{N}` heading, not a marker. **That stranding
> is what a careless merge of this file produces**: both sides' marker pairs land inside the same
> conflict hunk, and unioning them — the right call for the entries either side of it — leaves two
> contradictory pairs behind. Strip both and write one authoritative pair after renumbering.

### D345: The feed's no-location CTAs are solid pink + pink-outline, and `SokoCtaButton` gains a `pinkOutline` variant (PROD-4301, Zé on device, 2026-09-09)

- **Context:** the no-location ask-state (`FeedNoLocationState`) stacks two CTAs — "Ativar localização" over "Escolher zona". The first build used `SokoCtaVariant.green` for the GPS button (borrowing profiling onboarding's "Use my exact location" treatment) and `ghost` for the picker.
- **Choice:** GPS is **solid Soko/Pink**; the area picker is **pink-outline** — Soko/Paper fill + 1 px Soko/Pink border at rest, filling Soko/Pink on hover/press. Green read as a foreign accent on `sokoPaper` beside pink chrome, and a borderless ghost under a filled button did not read as a button at all. When permission is already granted the GPS button is hidden and the picker takes the **solid pink**, since it is then the only action on the page.
- **Where the treatment came from:** [D253](#d253-onboarding-profile-ctas-use-pink-outline-pink-only-on-hoverpress-not-a-pink-fill-no-ticket-user-directed) already established pink-outline as the design system's "pink as an accent, not a default fill", as `BtSqIcoVariant.pinkOutline` for the onboarding profile CTAs and location pills. This ports the same colours and the same hover/press fill to `SokoCtaButton` rather than inventing a second treatment.
- **Why port it instead of using `BtSqIco`:** `BtSqIco` is 40 px and `SokoCtaButton` is 44, so mixing the two to obtain an outline would misalign a stacked pair by 4 px. The notice family is already `SokoCtaButton` (`FeedEmptyState` uses it), and the two widgets deliberately share their interaction alphas, so the variant slots in without new design.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a green "Ativar localização", or a borderless "Escolher zona", is the pre-change state.
- **Where it lives:** `soko_cta_button.dart` (`SokoCtaVariant.pinkOutline` + its border and hover/press fill), `feed_notice_states.dart` (`FeedNoLocationState`). Guarded in `feed_no_location_state_test.dart` — a variant nobody asserts is a rule with no guard.

### D342: The venue's type and location are TWO meta lines, type first — on the bundle row and the grid tile alike (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-09-08)

- **Choice:** a venue in the discovery feed renders its two facts on **two icon meta lines**, `→ type` above `📍 neighbourhood`. This replaces the bundle row's single `neighbourhood · type` line (D307) and **flips** the grid tile, which shipped location-first.
- **Why:** Zé, 2026-09-08 — *"on the bundles that return venues as rows… we want to break out the type and location into two lines, like other bundles have meta line 1 and meta line 2. the first should contain type, the other the location."* A venue row now reads the same way as the event row beside it (a kind line, then a place line), and the venue's 66 px content column matches the event row's inside the same fixed 80 px box, so venue rows stop being visibly shorter than their neighbours.
- **Both surfaces move together.** The row and the tile carry the same two facts about the same entity; showing them in opposite orders is the drift, not the consistency. `FeedBundleRow._venueContent` and `FeedVenueGridTile._meta` are the two places, and they must stay in step.
- **Location means the NEIGHBOURHOOD alone, never the city.** Every bundle is city-scoped by the backend — a radius around the caller's own city — so the city is a constant across every row on the page and would say nothing. When the neighbourhood is null the line is **dropped**, not backfilled from `city`, exactly as the event row drops its own location line. `FeedVenueItem.city` stays parsed and unrendered; wiring it in means revisiting that scoping first. (BE noted separately that the `venues_with_events` bundle ships `city: null` anyway.)
- **The separator retires with the shared line.** `FeedMetaSeparator` and its dangling-dot guard existed only because the two facts shared a row. On separate lines each is independently present-or-absent, so a missing half is a missing line.
- **The gap under the title stays 12** when a venue has a location but no type — a type-less row must not inherit the tighter 8 px inter-meta gap.
- **Supersedes the last bullet of [D307](#d307-the-venue-grid-tile-is-a-new-card--reuse-the-near-you-meant-the-data-path-not-nearyoucard-prod-4108-user-directed-2026-09-01)**, which recorded the row as `neighbourhood · type` and provisional. It is still true that there is **no Figma frame** for the venue bundle row and none was wanted — **for `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: still do not file the venue bundle row against a design.** What changed is the prose it is built from.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a venue showing `neighbourhood · type` on one line, or showing the location above the type, is the pre-change state.

### D295: Card + detail save/feedback toggles use a solid Soko Ink active state, not a pink fill (no ticket, user-directed)

- **Choice:** The like/dislike thumbs and the save bookmark signal their active state (selected / saved / hovered) with a **solid Soko/Ink filled glyph** — never a Soko/Pink fill. Rest state is the same glyph as a thin Soko/Ink outline. Implemented once for the card overlays as `SokoToggleGlyph` (`lib/shared/widgets/soko_toggle_glyph.dart`): inactive = outline glyph tinted Soko/Ink; active = fill glyph tinted Soko/Ink. (An intermediate "reversed glyph" — ink fill under a paper outline — was tried and reverted; the active glyph is a plain solid ink fill.)
- **Where it lives:**
  - **Card overlays** — the like/dislike thumb control (`soko_entity_card.dart`, `SokoThumbCluster`, used by main-chat + onboarding vibe carousels) per Figma `7285:23799`, and the zine-cover quick-save tab (`onboarding_zines_carousel.dart`, `_QuickSaveButton`) per Figma `7285:23952`. Both are Soko/Paper tabs flush to the image top with a hairline top border and rounded outer bottom corners (10px thumbs, 20px bookmark).
  - **Detail save chips** — `SokoSaveButton` (venue) and `EventSaveButton` (event), circle **and** bare variants: the circle chip keeps a neutral `Soko/Ink @ 6%` background in **every** state (no pink), and the bookmark fills solid Soko/Ink when saved/hovered.
  - **Detail like/dislike thumbs** — `signal_chips_section.dart` `_thumb`: the fill that cross-fades in on select is tinted solid Soko/Ink (was the baked-pink `thumb-up-fill.svg`). The 200ms cross-fade is kept.
  - **Zine item-page bookmark** — `list_zine_item_page.dart` `_ZineBookmarkButton` (the 40px circle on a zine cover/item page): saved now shows the **solid Soko/Ink filled bookmark** SVG, replacing Lucide `bookmark_check` (a check-mark bookmark was the wrong glyph); unsaved is the ink outline.
- **Deliberate divergence from the design system:** `SokoSaveButton`'s design-system component (Figma `4032:4009`) still specifies a **pink** Hover/Selected background (see D65). We intentionally override it so every save/feedback toggle speaks one dark active-language, matching the newer card-overlay frames the user is designing against. If the design-system component is later updated to dark, this entry becomes the record of why the code moved first.
- **Explicitly out of scope (still pink / unchanged):** the list-page **follow** affordance in `list_page_header.dart` — the "Seguir" action button (pink via the shared `BtSqIco` `selected` variant, which is reused across sheets app-wide) and the inline `_StatsBookmark` (Material `Icons.bookmark`, follow semantics). Those are a *follow* toggle, not *save*, and changing them means either a new shared-component variant or a separate decision. Flagged for a follow-up.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a pink fill behind a saved bookmark or a selected 👍/👎 is the pre-change state; the intended active state is a solid Soko/Ink filled glyph on a neutral/paper surface.

### D253: Onboarding profile CTAs use pink-outline (pink only on hover/press), not a pink fill (no ticket, user-directed)

- **Choice:** The onboarding profile-step buttons "Editar perfil" (`_ProfileEditGate`) and "Encontra os teus contactos" (`_FindContactsSection`) render with a new `BtSqIcoVariant.pinkOutline` — Soko/Paper fill + Soko/Pink border at rest, filling Soko/Pink **only on hover/press** — instead of the always-pink `selected` fill the Figma frame shows. Mirrors the existing onboarding location pills (`_LocationPill`).
- **Why:** User-directed — pink should read as the interaction accent, not a default background fill; a page full of solid-pink CTAs is heavier than intended. Deliberate divergence from Figma `7285:24113`/`7285:24221`.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a solid-pink "Editar perfil" / "Encontra os teus contactos" button at rest is the pre-change state; the intended rest state is a pink **outline** on paper, going solid pink on hover/press.
- **Where it lives:** `bt_sq_ico.dart` (`pinkOutline` variant + hover/press fill), `onboarding_identity_name_screen.dart` (`_ProfileEditGate`, `_FindContactsSection`).

### D252: Venue business claim is a low-salience action-grid button (no ticket, user-directed)

- **Choice:** On venue detail, remove the **Save** action from `VenueActionGrid`. Eligible users see **Claim this business** beside **Report** in Row 1; **Visit website** and **Open in Maps** share Row 2. The existing 40 px Save button next to the venue title remains unchanged. Claim uses the same muted `sokoInk @ 6 %` action-button chrome as Report rather than its former full-width ink CTA; it is shown only when the `business-ownership` PostHog flag is on, the user is authenticated, and the server reports an unclaimed venue. Pending status remains in its existing inline notice position.
- **Why:** Claiming a business is useful but should not dominate a venue page. Grouping it with the lower-frequency Report action makes it available to the right person without competing with the venue's factual details, while Website and Maps stay paired as outbound destination actions.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a full-width dark Claim this business button above the details is a regression. The intended eligible state is a 2-up Row 1 (**Claim this business · Report**) and, when both destinations exist, a 2-up Row 2 (**Visit website · Open in Maps**). The title-row Save control is intentionally still present.
- **Where it lives:** `venue_action_grid.dart` (claim-state gating + two-row layout), `venue_claim_cta.dart` (pending notice only when mounted from venue detail), `venue_detail_body.dart` (suppresses the old standalone claim action).

### D250: Descobre a cidade + shelf see-more redesign — flat Soko-palette filter chips, all-pink tab selection, page-style overlay header, outlined input/pills (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** Four coupled visual decisions from the 2026-07 Figma mock (`Soko_Home_SearchResults`), applied to the Discovery search overlay AND the new `/shelves/<slug>` see-more pages:
  1. **Filter chips** (`EventFiltersBar` / `PlaceFiltersBar`) rebuilt on the shared `SokoFilterChip` chrome: flat Soko-palette fills — sokoRed / sokoLilac / sokoYellow cycling by the facet's **stable catalog index** (so selection-reorder never recolors a chip), sokoBlue reserved for the time-range trigger — radius 9, height 32, ink text/icons; **selection = 1.5 px ink border + medium weight**, the fill never changes. Supersedes **D205** (sokoLight3 fill + border-color-only selection). Selecting a chip animates the strip's scroll back to the front so a chip tapped deep in the scrolled tail doesn't vanish when it floats forward.
  2. **Category tabs** select in **Soko/Pink for every category**, superseding the PROD-2221 per-category tints (yellow zines / green events / blue places) — one consistent selection signal.
  3. While the overlay is open, the **SOKO wordmark gives way to a page-style header**: back arrow (closes the overlay + restores the feed scroll, same path as the input X) + centered "Descobre a cidade" (Season Mix 32/300).
  4. The **search input and the location pill go outlined**: transparent/paper fill + hairline `sokoShade45` border (input), `BtSqIco` idle variant (pill). The BtSqIco idle border color moved from ink @ 6 % to `sokoShade45` app-wide so all idle outline pills read against sokoPaper.
- **Why:** Figma mock supplied by João (zips 22/24/25); the sampled fills map 1:1 to existing Soko tokens (sokoRed/sokoBlue/sokoLilac/sokoYellow/sokoShade45), so no new colors entered the system.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** colored flat chips with ink borders when selected are intentional; the old pink-fill chips or per-category tab tints are the pre-change state. The see-more pages' chips are the SAME widgets (padding param only).
- **Where it lives:** `soko_filter_chip.dart` (chrome), `event_filters_bar.dart` / `place_filters_bar.dart`, `search_category_tabs.dart` (`_selectedColors`), `discovery_header.dart` (header swap), `search_overlay.dart` (input outline), `action_bar_location_pill.dart`, `bt_sq_ico.dart` (idle border).

### D251: Leitores (people) tab added to Discovery search — supersedes D54's dropped Membros tab (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** The Discovery overlay's category row gains a 4th tab, **Leitores** (EN "Locals", `DiscoverySearchCategory.leitores`), Discovery-only via the overlay's `categoryOrder` passthrough — `/yours` and Saved keep their 3-tab rows. `ReadersSearchSection` renders people rows (TexturedAvatar 50×63 + name/@handle + `CompactFollowButton`, `analyticsSource: 'discovery_readers'`): a typed query ≥ 2 chars (leading `@` not counted) searches `GET /users/search` via `peopleSearchProvider`; the browse state lists "Locais sugeridos" via `suggestedUsersProvider` minus session-dismissed suggestions. Guests get the sign-in CTA (both endpoints are auth-gated). Every exhaustive `DiscoverySearchCategory` switch gained a benign `leitores` branch (grid search provider returns empty; `/yours` falls back to zines).
- **Amended (2026-08-26, [D277](#d277-one-rounded-square-for-every-avatar-the-locals-shelf-becomes-the-follow-card-no-ticket-user-directed-2026-08-26)):** the row's avatar is no longer `50×63` as written above — it is a **rounded square of 50**, via `TexturedAvatar`'s own `kRadiusRatio`. Everything else in this entry holds.
- **Why:** D54 dropped the Figma Membros tab in PROD-1520 because people-search didn't exist. It now does (PROD-2777/2821 find-people infrastructure), and the 2026-07 mock reinstates the tab under the Leitores name. Product-driven (João).
- **Known gap:** `/users/search` and `/users/suggested` are absent from the OpenAPI spec (pre-existing — see `docs/openapi-gaps.md`).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a 4-tab row (Eventos / Sítios / Zines / Leitores) on Discovery is intentional; a 4th tab on `/yours` or the profile Saved tab is a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `search_category_provider.dart` (enum), `search_category_tabs.dart`, `discovery_search_overlay.dart` (`categoryOrder`), `readers_search_section.dart`, `discovery_screen.dart` (`_DiscoveryBody` routing).

# Design Decisions

> **Source of truth** for intentional design choices. Consulted by `/compare-ui`, `/visual-audit`, and `/figma-review` to distinguish intentional divergences from bugs.
>
> Decisions are made against **Figma** as the baseline. Entries D1–D74 (homepage hero + nav) also reference the legacy Lovable prototype as a secondary baseline — that was relevant when Lovable was an active source of UI ideas. Lovable is no longer used as a baseline; cite it only if you're documenting a historical comparison.

## How this doc is maintained

Entries are added when an intentional divergence from Figma is made. The doc gets updated through these triggers:

| Trigger | Mechanism | Who/what writes the entry |
|---|---|---|
| End of feature work via `/ship` | `pre-pr-gates.md` invokes `/w-extract-conversation-to-docs`, which scans for "UX decision that diverges from Figma" | Automated during `/ship` |
| Visual audit | `/visual-audit` consults this doc to skip known divergences and flag new ones; new ones get appended | Automated during `/visual-audit` |
| UI comparison | `/compare-ui` reads this doc as source-of-truth for "is this a bug or intentional" | Read-only |
| Figma review | `/figma-review` proposes D# entries for intentional divergences (vs Implementation Gap / Stale Figma / Cache Stale) | Manual append unless `--apply` is used |
| Manual review against a Figma file | Append by hand at the end of a session | Author |

**Numbering**: sequential D1, D2, …. Always pick the next free number (check the highest existing entry). Don't renumber existing entries.

**What belongs here**: divergences we've **chosen** to live with, including deferrals from Figma. **What does NOT belong here**: TODOs to match Figma later — those are Linear tickets, not design decisions.

---

## Homepage Hero

### D1: Input Card — Light Mode
- **Choice**: Figma two-tone split — Soko/Paper `#F9F0F0` top + Soko/Shade4.5 `#E7DBDE` bottom, dotted divider (was `#FEEFEF` / `#EFDFE4` pre-PROD-1738; Figma re-tokened in 2026-05).
- **Lovable**: Uniform frosted glass
- **Why**: Clearer visual separation between input and action row

### D2: Input Card — Dark Mode
- **Choice**: Figma — Soko/Paper at 20% alpha `rgba(249,240,240,0.2)` + `backdrop-blur(25.5px)`, uniform (no split). (Was `rgba(254,239,239,0.2)` pre-PROD-1738; Figma re-tokened Soko/Paper to `#F9F0F0` in 2026-05.)
- **Lovable**: Warm brown glass
- **Why**: Paper-tinted glass is more on-brand

### D3: Placeholder Text
- **Choice**: Lovable animated typing (16 cycling phrases)
- **Figma**: Static "Ask me something"
- **Why**: Animation adds personality; Figma can't represent animation

### D4: Primary Action Pill ("Your Daily Drop")
- **Choice**: Hybrid — Lovable label + Figma color `#F0F288`
- **Lovable label**: "Your Daily Drop" (more branded than Figma's "Suggest something")
- **Behavior**: Navigates to `/drop` overlay (production Daily Drop feature)

### D9: "Download Now" Button
- **Choice**: Not implementing
- **Why**: Future feature, no target link yet

### D10: Desktop Suggestion Prompts
- **Choice**: Hybrid — Figma visual style + Lovable animation cycling
- **Detail**: 3 fading prompts below card (60%/40%/20% opacity), separated by dotted lines, cycling with the typing placeholder

### D11: Desktop Footer
- **Choice**: Lovable — Terms/Privacy + LinkedIn/Instagram
- **Figma**: No footer (likely oversight)

### D12: Background Image (PT)
- **Choice**: Lovable — Ponte 25 de Abril bridge for both mobile and desktop
- **Figma**: Different images per viewport
- **Why**: Consistency

### D13: Gradient Overlay
- **Choice**: Figma PT values — `rgba(0,0,0,0.8)` top → transparent at ~49% (mobile), 0.6 (desktop)
- **Image blur**: 1px (Figma spec)

### D14: Input Card Dotted Divider (Light Mode)
- **Choice**: Yes — separates input from action row (Figma spec)
- **Dark mode**: No visible divider (single-tone glass)

---

## Headline ("What are you in the mood for?")

### D17: Font Size — Responsive
- **Desktop**: 64px (Figma specifies 101px for Season Mix TRIAL — too large with current interim font UnJamoBatang)
- **Mobile**: Scales with screen height — 40px (667px screen) to 52px (844px+) via smooth interpolation
- **Gap to card**: Desktop 32px (SizedBox). Mobile 16-32px (scales with screen height)
- **Why**: Figma sizes designed for Season Mix TRIAL font metrics; our interim fonts need different sizes. Will retune when final font is licensed.

### D24: Mobile Headline + Card Layout
- **Choice**: Single Column with explicit gap (not separate Positioned widgets)
- **Why**: Gap between headline and card is exact (not dependent on guessing card height). Font and gap scale smoothly for small screens.

---

## Placeholder & Focus Behavior

### D25: Hide Placeholder on Focus
- **Choice**: When input is focused (even without typing), hide the animated placeholder and fade out fading suggestion prompts
- **Why**: Blinking cursor + changing placeholder text is distracting. Clean empty field on focus.

---

## Desktop Navigation

### D8: Nav Label
- **Choice**: Figma — "Start" (not Lovable's "Home")
- **Active pill**: Solid foreground color. **Inactive pills**: semi-transparent + blur

### D26: Hamburger Icon
- **Choice**: Paragraph-style 3-line icon (18/18/12px widths) — visible on all pages
- **Figma/Lovable**: Standard hamburger, only on some pages
- **Why**: Different widths feel more refined. Always visible avoids layout shift between pages.

### D27: Desktop Nav Structure
- **Choice**: Unified structure on all pages — dotted lines, pills, logo, transparent background
- **Lovable**: Different nav on hero vs other pages
- **Why**: Zero layout shift when navigating between pages

---

## Mobile Bottom Navigation

### D5: Items
- **Choice**: 3 items — Memories (brain), Soko center, Lists
- **Excluded from Figma**: Skip back, Plus, Message circle (features not yet built)

### D6: Styling
- **Choice**: Figma — no border
- **Light**: Solid Soko/Paper `#F9F0F0`. **Dark**: `rgba(249,240,240,0.2)` + `backdrop-blur(25.5px)` (pre-PROD-1738 values were `#FEEFEF` / `rgba(254,239,239,0.2)`).

### D16: No Border
- **Choice**: Figma (no border). Lovable adds subtle border — intentionally skipped.

### D18: Compact Pill Dimensions
- **Choice**: 220px wide, 56px tall (48px landscape)
- **Previous**: 264px wide, 62px tall
- **Why**: Tighter grouping, items feel connected rather than spread out

### D19: 3-Column Grid Layout
- **Choice**: Soko icon centered via equal `Expanded` columns, side items center in remaining space
- **Previous**: `spaceEvenly` distribution
- **Why**: Guarantees Soko is dead center

### D20: Selected State — Icon Only + Underline
- **Choice**: Selected item shows larger icon (+4px), no text label, 28px underline accent at pill edge
- **Unselected**: Normal icon + label (w200 weight)
- **Why**: Label is redundant when on the page. Bigger icon + underline gives clearer feedback.

---

## Profile Avatar

### D21: Unified Colors
- **Light mode**: `sokoPaper` background, `sokoInk` text, no border
- **Dark mode**: `sokoPaper` at 20% opacity, `sokoPaper` text, no border
- **Previous**: Different colors across desktop (sokoPink), mobile hero (primaryColor + pink border), mobile conversation (primaryColor + shadow + border), memories (primaryColor + shadow + border)
- **Why**: Consistent identity across all screens

---

## Mobile Header

### D7: No Hamburger (Mobile)
- **Choice**: Lovable — no hamburger on mobile
- **Elements**: Language pill, Theme toggle pill, Profile avatar
- **Why**: Nothing useful behind it on mobile (only Home/Lists/Memories in bottom nav)

---

## Hover & Interaction Effects

### D22: Hover Scale on Action Items (Desktop)
- **Choice**: MouseRegion + AnimatedScale — 1.08x hover, 0.95x press (150ms)
- **Applies to**: Daily Drop pill, Prompt pills, History button, Send button (when enabled)
- **Why**: Pointer + scale feedback for non-touch screens. No-op on touch devices.

### D23: Send Button Scale — Disabled State
- **Choice**: No scale effect when `canSend` is false
- **Why**: Disabled button should not feel interactive

---

## Orientation

### D28: Portrait Lock (Mobile)
- **Choice**: Lock to portrait on iOS and Android (not web)
- **iOS**: `UISupportedInterfaceOrientations` portrait only (iPad unchanged)
- **Android**: `android:screenOrientation="portrait"`
- **Flutter**: `SystemChrome.setPreferredOrientations` (portrait up + down)
- **Why**: App not designed for landscape; avoids broken layouts

---

## Fading Suggestion Prompts

### D15: Desktop + Mobile
- **Choice**: Desktop shows 3 prompts, mobile shows 2 prompts
- **Previous decision**: Desktop only. Updated to include mobile (experimental, easy to revert).
- **Spacing**: 8px top padding on both desktop and mobile

### D29: Left-Aligned Text
- **Choice**: Left-aligned (Lovable style)
- **Figma**: Also left-aligned (`items-start`)

---

## Item Detail Sheet

### D31: Phone + Opening Hours — No "Open now / Closed" badge (PROD-1304)
- **Choice**: Display opening hours as a static expandable list (label + today's hours + chevron). Do NOT compute or show an "Open now" / "Closed" badge.
- **Why**: The `opening_hours` JSON from the backend contains only day-name → hours-string mappings. There is no `open_now` field (verified: 0 out of 14,800 venues have it). Computing open/closed status client-side would require parsing multiple inconsistent formats — `"11:00 AM – 9:00 PM"` (12h with Unicode narrow spaces), `[{"open": "00:00", "close": "24:00"}]` (24h array), `"Closed"`, `"Open 24 hours"` — plus handling timezone ambiguity (hours are in the venue's local timezone, not the user's). The risk of showing incorrect open/closed state outweighs the UX benefit.
- **Future**: If the backend adds a computed `open_now` boolean to the venue detail API response, the webapp can display it. The l10n keys `detailOpenNow` / `detailClosedNow` are pre-added for this purpose.

### D30: Website Button — Venues vs Events (PROD-770)
- **Choice**: Only show the "Website" button when there is an actual website URL
- **Venues**: Button visible only when the backend `website` field is non-null (37% of venues). Never falls back to `url` (which is a Google Maps URL for venues).
- **Events**: Button visible when `website` or `url` is set (`url` is the event source/ticketing link, not Google Maps).
- **Event detail screen**: Website button hidden entirely (not just disabled) when no URL exists.
- **Why**: Previously, the button fell back to the `url` field which contained a Google Maps URL for venues, causing the "Website" button to open Google Maps with a raw `place_id` search string.

### D35: Hero Photo — Fixed-Height Container + `BoxFit.contain` on Neutral Backdrop (PROD-1329)
> **Note**: this entry describes the **legacy `ItemDetailSheet`** which still ships for non-Discovery surfaces pending PROD-1672 cutover. The new full-screen venue page diverges with `BoxFit.cover` on a 16:9 aspect (no letterbox) — see [D66](#d66-hero-photo--169-cover-crop-no-letterbox).

- **Choice**: The hero photo renders inside a **fixed 200px tall** full-width container with `BoxFit.contain` on a `surfaceVariant` background. The image is letterboxed against the neutral backdrop regardless of source aspect ratio; the container height is identical on desktop and mobile so the sheet layout is consistent.
- **Previous behavior**: `BoxFit.cover` inside `height: isLandscape ? 120 : 200`. On desktop the sheet is wider (~560px), so the hero became ~2.8:1 and `cover` chopped away most of the photo (ceiling, people, context). On mobile the ratio was closer to 1.7:1 so the photo survived — the bug was desktop-only.
- **Alternatives explored**:
  - `AspectRatio(16/9)` + `BoxFit.cover` — still crops, portrait sources are chopped even harder.
  - Blurred backdrop + `contain` — no crop and looks premium, but adds `ImageFilter.blur` cost and a `RepaintBoundary` for marginal gain over a solid backdrop.
  - Variable-height `contain` (`minHeight: 180, maxHeight: 280`) — no crop but layout shifts depending on image aspect ratio.
  - Cropped hero + "tap to view full photo" affordance — doesn't fix the default state, adds a new viewer surface to maintain.
  - Reduced height + gradient fade into content — cosmetic only, still cropped.
- **Why**: The image is always fully visible (no crop ever), the container height is predictable, and the solid backdrop matches the app palette without competing with the photo. Simpler than blur for no visible downside.
- **Loading/error**: `Image.network` default loading behavior over the neutral backdrop (acts as a shimmer-like placeholder). `errorBuilder` returns `SizedBox.shrink()` — fail-silent rather than showing a broken-image icon.

### D36: Hero Collapses When There is No Photo (PROD-1329)
- **Choice**: When `imageUrl == null` the hero returns `SizedBox.shrink()` and the following spacer is skipped too — the title becomes the first content below the sheet divider. If the detail API later populates `imageUrl`, the `ListView` reflows to make room.
- **Previous behavior**: A 200px tall tinted box with a large 64px pin/event icon (`_buildImagePlaceholder`) rendered whether or not a photo existed. Method removed.
- **Why**: The placeholder added vertical weight without information value; for address-only results (e.g., "R. da Palma 268") it pushed the title, address, and map below the fold for no reason. The sheet earns vertical space only when it has real content.

### D37: Bookmark Chip Sits Next to the Title (PROD-1329)
> **Note**: this entry describes the **legacy `ItemDetailSheet`**. The new full-screen venue page also keeps the Save bookmark next to the title row — but as a 40 px circular `SokoSaveButton` (Default / Hover / Selected states from design-system Figma `4032:4009`) instead of a save-count chip. See [D65](#d65-save-button-moves-to-right-of-venue-name-figma-divergence).

- **Choice**: The save-count bookmark pill lives inline with the content, right-aligned on the title row (`Row` with `Expanded(SelectableText(title))` + 8px gap + `_buildBookmarkChip`). Chip uses `surfaceVariant` background (legible in light + dark) with `Icons.bookmark_border` and the `_socialProof.saveCount` when > 0. Tap still opens `AddToListSheet`.
- **Previous location**: `Positioned(top: 8, right: 8)` inside a `Stack` overlaying the hero image. Disappeared visually whenever there was no image (D36) and used a white-alpha background that only made sense on top of a photo.
- **Considered**: Moving the chip into the sheet header next to the close `X`. Rejected in review — the close X is chrome, not content; the bookmark feels more natural as content-adjacent UI and scrolls with the title it applies to.
- **Why**: Always visible regardless of photo presence, visually tied to the item (title) rather than to a chrome slot, and reads correctly against the app's light/dark palette.

## Adding Items to Lists

### D32: Paste Google Maps URL — detect inside existing search input, no new surface (PROD-1325)
- **Choice**: In `SearchAddToListSheet` (the drawer opened from a list's "+"), detect a pasted Google Maps URL inside the existing search text field. Switch into URL mode: force-select the `Lugares` tab, dim `Eventos` (tap is a no-op — URL resolution always returns a place), and render the resolved venue through the **existing** `SearchResultCard` widget as a single result row. No bespoke preview card, no new entry point, no visual cue on the row itself.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - A separate "Add from URL" sheet/dialog — extra surface, less discoverable, duplicates state.
  - A "URL detected" chip or banner above the search field that the user has to tap to confirm — extra click, contradicts the "paste and it works" user intent stated by Zé at spec time.
  - A visual marker on the row saying "from link" — unnecessary once the disabled `Eventos` tab already signals we're in URL mode.
- **Why**: The user's intent is "paste Google Maps URL = add that venue". Any extra surface or tap dilutes that. Reusing `SearchResultCard` means the row looks like any other search hit, keeps code surface small, and lets the existing `_addToList` save flow handle the tap unchanged.
- **Backend contract**: `POST /api/v1/app/places/resolve-url` ([PROD-1382](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-1382)). Returns a `ResolveVenueOut`. 200 = resolved from DB; 201 = created via Google Places fallback. Error codes map to typed `ResolveUrlFailure` variants consumed by URL-specific error copy.

---

## List Detail Empty State

### D33: Empty List — Owner CTA + No Icon (PROD-1322)
- **Choice**: Owner sees title + subtitle + prominent `FilledButton.icon` ("Adicionar primeiro item"). Non-owner sees only the title. No icon in either case.
- **Wiring**: The CTA calls `_showSearchAddSheet(context, state)` — the same flow as the small header "+ Adicionar" button. No new backend calls or sheets.
- **Subtitle copy**: "Adiciona eventos e locais à tua lista" (EN "Add events and places to your list", PT-BR "Adicione eventos e lugares à sua lista"). Previously "… das sugestões da conversa", but items can be added many ways (search, Google Maps URL, chat) — the old copy was misleading.
- **Why (owner-only)**: The header "+ Adicionar" button is easy to miss. A primary CTA inside the empty state makes the next step obvious.
- **Why (non-owner has no CTA / subtitle)**: Non-owners can't add items to someone else's list, so both the CTA and the subtitle (which describes what owners can do) would be dead UI.
- **Why (no icon)**: The circle icon added vertical noise without information gain — the list name in the header already makes context clear.

---

## Lists Hub Header

### D34: "Perto de mim / Mundo" as a filter chip on the header row (PROD-1377)
- **Choice**: Render the location-scope toggle as a pill chip on the Lists hub filter row, sibling to the `Todas ▾` section dropdown. Tapping it flips `listsGlobalSearchProvider` directly (no popup menu — only two states). Active state tints the icon and label in the primary color when showing `Mundo`.
- **Previous location**: The toggle lived as the `suffixIcon` of the search `TextField`, so it read as a search-only control even though it filters every section (Tuas / Da Soko / A seguir / Recomendadas / Discover) whether or not the user is searching.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Segmented control on a dedicated row (`[ Perto de mim | Mundo ]`) — takes more vertical space and breaks the visual language of the existing `Todas ▾` chip.
  - Icon-only toggle — saves space but loses the "always-visible active filter" cue that the ticket explicitly asked for.
  - Popup menu with caret (matching `Todas ▾` exactly) — unnecessary for a 2-state toggle; direct tap is simpler.
- **Why**: The scope is an always-on filter, not a search qualifier. Placing it next to `Todas ▾` communicates "this is a filter like the section filter" and keeps it visible whether the user is searching or scrolling lists.
- **Styling**: Matches `_buildFilterDropdown` — 36 h, radius 18, `mutedBg` background, 14/500 label, `Icons.travel_explore` leading glyph. No caret.

## Create List Dialog (Chat)

### D38: Chat success-state buttons — "Ver lista" + "Continuar a conversa" (PROD-1405)
- **Choice**: After a list is created from the in-chat composer pill (`CreateListPill` in `chat_bar.dart`; via `ChatItemsPickerSheet` pre-flight since PROD-1894), the shared `CreateListDialog` (non-admin) or `CreateZineScreen` (admin, gated by `showSuccessState: true`) shows its chat-only success state with two buttons: primary **"Ver lista"** (navigates to `/lists/{slug}`) and secondary **"Continuar a conversa"** (closes back to the same chat session). Secondary uses `Icons.chat_bubble_outline`.
- **Previous copy**: "Começar a adicionar elementos" / "Fechar" — described what the button did mechanically but not what the user was choosing between.
- **Why**: The two actions represent the user's post-creation intent (go see the new list vs. keep chatting). Framing the buttons as destinations instead of verbs makes the choice obvious. The secondary icon shifts from "close" to "chat bubble" to reinforce that it's a return-to-context action, not a dismissal.
- **Scope**: Chat flow only. Lists hub and add-to-list sheet do not render this success state — they navigate externally via their own `onCreateList` callback, which handles the pop + push. The success state is gated on `onStartAdding != null` in the shared dialog.
- **Smart prompt toggle**: The chat flow was previously hiding the Soko smart-list-suggestions toggle because it didn't forward the `showSuggestionsToggle` param. It now passes `ref.read(experimentServiceProvider).enableSmartListSuggestions` like the other call sites, so the prompt field appears consistently across entry points when the flag is on.
- **l10n keys**: `listsViewList` + `listsContinueConversation` (renamed from `listsStartAddingElements` + `listsClose`).
