## Discovery Page (PROD-1517)

### D39: Soko wordmark — extra top padding on desktop only (PROD-1517)
- **Choice**: `DiscoveryHeader` adds **48px** top padding when viewport width ≥ 1024px. On mobile the padding stays at 0 — the wordmark sits close to the top per the Figma spec (header frame at y=69, just under the iOS status-bar inset that `SafeArea` already provides).
- **Figma**: Header frame at y=69 on a 430-wide mobile viewport. No desktop variant exists for the Discovery page.
- **Why (mobile)**: Vertical real estate is tight; the chat bar, daily drop, and shelves all need to fit above the fold. Pinning the wordmark close to the safe-area top is the right answer.
- **Why (desktop)**: With ~900–1200px of vertical room, having the wordmark 16px from the top of the viewport reads as if it's clipped to the chrome — the page feels unfinished. 48px gives the wordmark visual breathing room without blowing up the proportions.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_header.dart` — the padding is computed inside the widget so the screen-level layout doesn't need to know about the breakpoint. Marked as a desktop interim (`// TODO(desktop-redesign):` family) — revisit when designer ships a true desktop layout.
- **Amended 2026-08-28 (PROD-3998) — the mobile figure is `SafeArea` + a margin, and the feed v2 page had neither.** The mobile `0` above was never "flush with the viewport"; it is measured from *below* the status bar, which the legacy page supplies with `SafeArea(bottom: false)` in `discovery_screen.dart`. `FeedTopHeader` (the server-driven feed's header) copied the `0` without the `SafeArea`, so on web the wordmark touched the top edge and on a notched device it would have rendered under the status bar. It now wraps in `SafeArea(top: true, bottom/left/right: false)` and takes **`kFeedPageMargin` (15)** on mobile — the page's own margin unit, so the gap above the wordmark is the gap the content keeps to the sides and matches the inset the pinned header opens with. Desktop is unchanged at 48. Left/right insets are deliberately not taken: no other sliver on that page takes them, and a landscape notch would otherwise inset the header alone. Pinned in `test/features/discovery/feed_v2/feed_spacing_test.dart` § "above the wordmark" — one test per half, because either is invisible in the other's absence.
- **Still open**: `_pinnedChrome` in `discovery_feed_v2_screen.dart` sits at `Positioned(top: 0)` with a 15 px inset and no `SafeArea`, so the pinned bar has the same status-bar overlap on a notched device. Out of scope here (it also needs its `ColoredBox` to extend under the status bar rather than start below it) — verify on device before the feed goes to all users.
- **Trigger to revisit**: When the Discovery desktop design lands, this `48` becomes either spec-driven or unnecessary (e.g., if the wordmark moves into a desktop top-nav).

---

## Discovery Page (PROD-1522)

### D40: Highlighted (Em destaque) shelf — forked 195 × 242 card (PROD-1522, updated 2026-05-19)
- **Choice**: The Highlighted shelf forks the canonical card into `HighlightedShelfCard` at the larger 195 × 242 image size from Figma `d4BCnyUHe2705J7ecQtaIH` node `6144:5096`. Same 5 px radius, same 3-line text block (Zalando Sans Medium 18 / Light 14 / Light 14 at 30 % Ink), still no horizontal scroll and no Ver mais — a fixed `Row` with a 10 px gap. Every card uses the standard "by owner" attribution.
- **Supersedes**: 2026-05-05 spec also placed a "SOKO" badge as a `Positioned` overlay on Card 1. Removed 2026-05-19 (PROD-1986/1987) — marketing now owns curation via the backoffice, so the Soko/community distinction is no longer surfaced on the shelf. The original D40 (2026-04-29) shipped canonical 160.418 × 213 chrome as an interim while the designer's new spec was being finalised.
- **Why**: Em destaque is the page-opening editorial moment; the larger card lets cards read like full editorial covers without breaking the canonical typography rhythm. Marketing-curated ordering (PROD-1986) replaces the prior algorithmic Soko/community split.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/highlighted_shelf_card.dart` (forked) + `highlighted_shelf.dart` (composer) + `providers/highlighted_shelf_provider.dart` (slices BE response to first 2).

### D41: Verificados shelf dropped per product (PROD-1522)
- **Choice**: The Verificados sub-shelf was removed from the Discovery launch. The `verified` boolean on `UserListOut` (added in PROD-1513) stays in place but no shelf consumes it.
- **Alternatives rejected**: Ship as an additional shelf — would have required confirmed product copy and BE seed data. Neither available at the cutoff.
- **Why**: Product chose to defer pending a clearer answer to "what does *verified* mean to the user" — operator-curated quality vs. partner verification vs. Soko-vetted. The DB column already exists, so flipping it on later costs nothing.
- **Re-enabling later**: hit `GET /api/v1/app/lists/public?verified=true&limit=10`; the FE pattern matches Editor Picks. New ARB keys for the title and (likely) badge.

### D42: Image cropping via `Stack(fit: StackFit.expand)` + `BoxFit.cover` (PROD-1522)
- **Choice**: `_ArtworkTile` wraps the image in a `Stack(fit: StackFit.expand)` with a placeholder `Container` underneath, then `CachedImage(fit: BoxFit.cover)` *without* explicit width/height. The Stack forces the image to fill the box; `BoxFit.cover` then center-crops the overflow.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Passing `width` and `height` directly to `CachedImage` (the original implementation) — landscape source images sometimes rendered at their natural aspect ratio inside the portrait card on the Near you shelf, especially on cold cache. The Stack pattern is the one that works deterministically.
  - `BoxFit.contain` — leaves blank space; visually inconsistent.
  - Custom alignment (e.g. `Alignment.topCenter`) for landscape sources — viable but `BoxFit.cover` with center alignment is fine for most photos and avoids per-shelf tuning.
- **Why**: Discovery cards are uniformly portrait (105×140 compact, 160×213 canonical). Source photos come from Google Places via `cached_images` and are mixed orientations. Center-cropping with `BoxFit.cover` inside an expanded Stack is the bulletproof Flutter pattern for "always crop to fill".
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/_artwork_tile.dart`.

### D43: Forked card widgets per shelf, shared `_ArtworkTile` primitive (PROD-1522)
- **Choice**: Each Discovery shelf has its own card widget file (e.g. `near_you_card.dart`); shelves that share the canonical layout (Yours, Editor Picks, Recommended, Highlighted, Spaces) compose `CanonicalShelfCard` directly with their own attribution and subtitle rules. The artwork tile (image + clip + grunge-overlay placeholder) is the only shared primitive (`_artwork_tile.dart`). The shelf scaffold (`discovery_shelf.dart`) is also shared because it is plumbing, not visual chrome.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - One `ShelfCard` parameterised over text-block slots — a single god-card pulled in `if (variant == X)` branches the moment Editor Picks, Verificados, Mais seguidas, etc. diverged. The fork-on-doubt rule from PROD-1526 specifically targets this case.
  - Forking the shelf scaffold per shelf — would have meant 7 near-identical copies of snap, padding, skeleton, error, empty, and pagination plumbing. That plumbing is genuinely shared infrastructure, not visual chrome.
- **Why**: The card text block, attribution rule, tap intent, and likely future badges genuinely differ per shelf. Forking the cards keeps each shelf's quirks isolated to one file. Reusing the shelf scaffold and the artwork primitive avoids 7 copies of plumbing nobody wants to maintain.
- **Implication**: Adding a new shelf is "fork the closest existing card + write a 30-line shelf widget + wire a provider", not "extend a god-card". See `features/discovery.md` § Adding a new shelf.
- **PROD-2323 note**: `near_you_see_more_tile.dart` (a per-shelf trailing tap-to-load tile) was deleted when every paginating shelf migrated to auto-fire infinite scroll via `DiscoveryShelf`'s `onLoadMore` prop. The fork-per-shelf rule still applies to *card* widgets; tap-to-load tiles aren't part of the shelf vocabulary anymore.

### D44: Discovery scroll lives at the shell, not the screen (PROD-1522)
- **Choice**: The vertical `SingleChildScrollView` is owned by `DiscoveryShell`, not by individual screen widgets. `DiscoveryScreen` and any future Discovery surface is a non-scrolling `Column` that stacks inside the shell's outer scroll.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - `DiscoveryScreen` as a `ListView` (the original implementation) — limited the scroll surface to the centred 600 px container on desktop (cursor outside that band wouldn't trigger wheel) and left a native scrollbar visible.
  - Per-section inner scrolls — wheel events compete with the page-level scroll and produce a "jump-to-bottom on scroll-up" glitch.
- **Why**: A single outer scrollable means (a) the entire viewport width captures wheel events on desktop, (b) a custom `ScrollBehavior` hiding the scrollbar applies once and inherits everywhere, (c) horizontal `ListView`s in shelves coexist cleanly because they're cross-axis to the outer scroll.
- **Implementation**: `discovery_shell.dart` builds the `SingleChildScrollView` + `ScrollConfiguration(_NoScrollbarScrollBehavior)` + `ClampingScrollPhysics`. `_NoScrollbarScrollBehavior` returns the child unwrapped from `buildScrollbar` and overrides `dragDevices` so mouse / trackpad / touch / stylus all drag-scroll.
- **Heads-up for PROD-1518 / PROD-1521**: do NOT introduce a `ListView` or `SingleChildScrollView` inside Daily Drop, History grid, or Weekly Bundle widgets. See `learnings/discovery-shell-scroll-architecture.md`.

### D45: "Para ti" section grouping deferred from Discovery launch (PROD-1522 follow-up)
- **Choice**: The Discovery page renders Tuas, Perto de ti, etc. as flat sibling shelves. The latest Figma design (file `nJJ4z2UO58wWMxZstpFVle`, Discovery node `3932:1928`) introduces a **"Para ti"** section header that groups Tuas + Perto de ti as personalised shelves under one banner.
- **Why**: The grouping is visual hierarchy only — the underlying shelves are already shipped. Adding the section header without a clear container interaction (collapse? scroll? tab?) risks a half-formed pattern. Deferred until product/design clarifies what "Para ti" means as a UX primitive (vs the deferred [PROD-1519](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-1519) full "Para ti" personalised-lists feature).
- **Trigger to revisit**: When PROD-1519 gets picked up, OR when the designer ships a Para ti section pattern that other Discovery surfaces will use too.
- **Where it would live**: A new section primitive in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/`, wrapping the shelves.

### D46: Hub / Unicorn Factory shelf deferred from Discovery launch (PROD-1522 follow-up)
- **Choice**: The Discovery page does not render the Hub shelf (shown speculatively as "Unicorn Factory" in the Figma mock at node `3932:2073`).
- **Why**: Hub is a separate product surface (groups of curated lists by partner / community / theme). It was scoped as a future feature, not part of the PROD-1522 shelves cut. The Figma mock includes it speculatively; product confirmed it's not for this cycle.
- **Trigger to revisit**: When the Hub feature ticket is opened and prioritised. At that point the BE endpoint contract, card variant, and pagination behaviour need confirming separately.
- **Where it would live**: New shelf widget under `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/`, new provider, new BE endpoint.

### D47: Discovery History grid hides silently on empty (PROD-1521)
> **Status (2026-05-14):** History grid is currently gated off for every user via the compile-time const `EnvironmentConfig.discoveryHistoryGridEnabled` (default `false`) — see [`features/discovery.md` § History grid](../features/discovery.md#history-grid) and [`features/feature-flags.md`](../features/feature-flags.md#compile-time-flags). The decision below stays accurate: when the grid is re-enabled it still hides silently on empty.
- **Choice**: When `GET /users/me/activity` returns `{items: []}` (HTTP 200), the History grid renders `SizedBox.shrink()` — no heading, no empty-state card, no editorial fallback.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Empty-state CTA card ("Aqui ficam as tuas últimas paragens — começa a explorar") — at a 60-px-tall chip footprint the copy is cramped, and a CTA on a *personal* feed reads as nagging.
  - Editorial fallback shelf (e.g. inject a Soko-starter shelf into the History slot) — there's no curated source for "things you might have looked at", and reusing a shelf chrome would muddy the History/shelves boundary.
- **Why**: The History grid is a personal feed; if the user has no recent activity (or all of it has been server-filtered as deleted/inactive), showing nothing is more honest than performing engagement. Discovery already has plenty of editorial shelves above and below.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/history_grid.dart` — `if (items.isEmpty) return const SizedBox.shrink();`. Same code path covers loading errors (silent hide).
- **Convention**: this is the empty-state default for *personal* Discovery feeds. Editorial shelves keep their own empty-state semantics (e.g. Yours' "create your first list" CTA earns its place because it nudges first-list creation).
- **Trigger to revisit**: post-launch engagement data on the redesign — if users with no activity feel like the page is broken, swap in a CTA card.

### D48: Discovery History grid renders odd item counts as-is, not padded or truncated (PROD-1521)
> **Status (2026-05-14):** grid currently gated off (see D47 banner).

- **Choice**: When the BE returns N < 6 items, render exactly N cards in 2-column rows. A row with 1 card sits left-aligned; the right slot is an empty `SizedBox.shrink()` half. Don't truncate to even, don't centre the orphan, don't pad with placeholders.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Round down to even (e.g. drop the 3rd card when 3 items return) — throws away a server-hydrated activity item, which is the opposite of what the user expects from a "recent activity" surface.
  - Centre the orphan — breaks the 2-column rhythm shared with the rest of the grid; visually inconsistent.
  - Pad with neutral placeholders to fill the row — was explicitly called out as "don't pad client-side" in the BE retrospective on PROD-1514, and creates fake density.
- **Why**: The half-empty row reads fine in this design language (confirmed by Zé). Dropping the orphan would burn a real piece of recent activity for no UX gain.
- **Where it lives**: `_HistoryGridLayout` in `history_grid.dart` — iterates pairs and emits `Row(Expanded(card), gap, Expanded(right ?? SizedBox.shrink()))`.
- **Generalises to**: other 2-column compact grids driven by a server-paged feed where the BE may filter items server-side.

### D49: Mixed-entity image fallback strategy on the History grid (PROD-1521) — *superseded by PROD-1578 (2026-04-29)*
- **Status**: Superseded. The cache was generalised to all three `EntityKind` cases in PROD-1578 — venues, events, and lists now share the cross-surface URL fallback via `entityImageCacheProvider`. See [`learnings/discovery-image-cache-propagation.md`](../learnings/discovery-image-cache-propagation.md) for the current pattern.
- **Original choice (now superseded)**: For History items where `EntityRef.image_url` is null, look up `venueImageCacheProvider[id]` only when `EntityKind == venue`. Events and lists fell back directly to a neutral `Soko/Shade5` chip — no client-side cache for those entity types.
- **Alternatives originally rejected**:
  - Generalise the cache to all entity types in PROD-1521 — too much surface area (touches detail screens, not Discovery code) and explicitly out of PROD-1521's scope. Filed as PROD-1578 instead and shipped 2026-04-29.
  - Render a lettered avatar fallback (first letter of title) — mixes a chat-suggestion pattern (`place_card_with_letter.dart`) into the printed-zine aesthetic. Reads as a "glitch" against the rest of Discovery's chrome.
  - Hide cards with null `image_url` — drops real activity items because the BE is cold-cache. Worse than a neutral chip.
- **Why deferred at the time**: Ship the venue case (the existing pattern, zero new surface area) in PROD-1521; defer the events + lists generalisation to a focused follow-up. The neutral chip matches the card background, so a missing thumbnail reads as design intent rather than a broken-image placeholder — that fallback rendering still applies when neither the feed nor the cache has a URL.

## Design System — Bt_Sq_Ico

### D50: Bt_Sq_Ico paints a 1 px transparent border on Normal and Selected variants
- **Figma spec**: `4108:2975` `Bt_Sq_Ico` defines three variants — Normal (filled `rgba(68,19,29,0.06)`, no border), Idle (1 px `rgba(68,19,29,0.06)` border, transparent fill), Selected (filled `#FFB8CB` Soko/Pink, no border).
- **Choice**: Implementation paints a **1 px border on every variant**. Idle uses the spec colour; Normal and Selected paint the border at `Colors.transparent`. Padding / typography / radius stay 1:1 with Figma.
- **Why**: Flutter's `BoxDecoration` reserves border space inside the content box, so a Container that toggles between "no border" and "1 px border" with the same `padding` ends up 2 px narrower / 2 px shorter in the bordered state. Toggling Idle ↔ Selected (the category-tab interaction in the Discovery action bar) would visibly shift the icon + label by 1 px on each axis, including any siblings to the right. Painting an always-present transparent border keeps the box's outer rect identical across variants.
- **Where it lives**: `_BtSqIco` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_action_bar.dart` — `Border.all(color: borderColor, width: 1)` with `borderColor` resolved per variant (transparent for Normal/Selected, `inkAt6` for Idle).
- **Convention — applies anywhere we instantiate Bt_Sq_Ico**: future re-uses of this component (whether extracted into a shared widget or copy-pasted) **must** carry the same transparent-border trick. If we lift `_BtSqIco` into `lib/shared/widgets/` later, this behaviour belongs in the shared widget, not at each call site.
- **Trigger to revisit**: if a future Flutter release lets `BoxDecoration` paint borders outside the content box (e.g. an `outsetBorder` flag) we can drop the workaround. Until then, treat the 1 px transparent border as part of the component's contract.

## Discovery — Pixel-Perfect Pass (PROD-1522 follow-up)

### D51: Season Mix TRIAL Light is the Discovery H1 family (PROD-1522)
- **Choice**: Discovery shelf titles ("Tuas", "Em destaque", "Recomendado", …) and the page footer render their 42 px H1 in **Season Mix TRIAL Light (300)** via `AppTheme.displayPrimary(...)`. Other display surfaces in the app keep using `AppTheme.display(...)` which stays bound to Zalando Sans.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Re-pointing `AppTheme.display(...)` to Season Mix globally — would have rolled the editorial face into every page title across the app at once. Too invasive without per-surface design review.
  - Inline `TextStyle(fontFamily: 'SeasonMix')` at each call site — would scatter the family string and lose a single point of control for letter-spacing / line-height defaults.
- **Why**: The Figma specs Season Mix specifically as the *editorial* family for shelf titles; body lines on cards keep the Zalando Medium / Light geometry. Splitting the helper keeps the scope explicit.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/core/theme/app_theme.dart` adds `_displayPrimaryFontFamily = 'SeasonMix'` + the `displayPrimary({...})` helper. Fonts registered in `pubspec.yaml` (`SeasonMixTRIAL-{Light,Regular,Medium,SemiBold}.ttf`).
- **Trigger to revisit**: if more screens adopt the editorial face we can either reuse `displayPrimary` from those sites or — once the rollout is wide enough — flip `_displayFontFamily` to Season Mix and retire the split.

### D52: Dotted shelf divider as a leading divider, owned by each shelf (PROD-1522)
- **Choice**: Inter-shelf rhythm lives in a shared `ShelfDivider` widget that renders `Padding(symmetric vertical 30) > DottedLine(color: AppColors.sokoShade4)`. **Each shelf prepends the divider as its own leading widget** (inside its return Column) rather than the screen sandwiching dividers between siblings. Shelf scaffolds (`DiscoveryShelf`, `HighlightedShelf`) no longer add bottom padding of their own; title-to-row gap tightens from 54 px to 16 px to match Figma `6144:5092`.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - **Standalone dividers between shelves on the screen** — when an upstream shelf collapsed to `SizedBox.shrink()` (empty/error), the divider above it stayed visible, producing two consecutive dividers stacked over a hidden shelf. Embedding the divider as a leading widget makes it disappear together with the shelf.
  - Keeping the 32 px bottom padding *and* the divider sandwich — over-spaced, didn't match Figma rhythm.
  - Painting the dotted line via `BoxDecoration` border-bottom on each shelf — couples the divider to one shelf's lifecycle and doesn't let us add a divider above the first shelf without a special case.
- **Why**: One reusable widget owns the entire "between two shelves" rhythm, so adding/removing/reordering shelves doesn't desynchronise the spacing. Per-shelf ownership means visibility tracks the shelf's own collapse logic for free. Reuses existing `DottedLine` (`lib/shared/widgets/dotted_line.dart`) and `AppColors.sokoShade4` (`#C1B2B5`) — no new tokens.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/shelf_divider.dart`; rendered as the first child of every `DiscoveryShelf` and `HighlightedShelf` build.

### D53: Discovery footer ships an end-of-page sign-off (PROD-1522)
- **Choice**: After the last shelf the page renders a centred 115 × 115 illustration tile followed by a 42 px Season Mix Light "That's all folks" line (PT-PT: "É tudo, malta", PT-BR: "É isso, galera"). Implemented as `DiscoveryFooter` in `widgets/sections/`.
- **Spacing**: **120 px of empty gap between the last visible shelf and the illustration tile, with no dotted divider in between.** This intentionally breaks the inter-shelf dotted rhythm — the gap is the visual full-stop signalling end-of-page. Figma `6144:5235`.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Adding a dotted divider above the footer (the original implementation) — visually treated the footer like another shelf and lost the "we're done" feeling.
  - Skipping the footer (bottom of the page would feel abrupt).
- **Asset blending — depends on the variant**:
  - Default footer (`DiscoveryFooter()`) and `.comingSoon()` use `soko-seating-and-reading.webp`, which ships with a transparent background. Rendered directly with no blend.
  - `.doneLooking()` uses `soko-walking-and-reading.webp`, which is still RGB/opaque. The widget detects this via `assetHasTransparentBg` and applies `BlendMode.multiply` against the active shell scaffold colour — `AppColors.sokoPaper` (`#F9F0F0`) in light mode, `AppColors.backgroundDark` in dark — so the white asset surround collapses cleanly into the page. See [`learnings/discovery-blend-color.md`](../learnings/discovery-blend-color.md). (Pre-PROD-1885 the shell painted `AppColors.background` `#F5F1EC` and the blend used that value; PROD-1885 aligned the shell to Soko/Paper per Figma `6353:26730`, and the footer now matches.)
- **Why**: The Figma cut (`6144:5235`) treats the footer as a structural part of the page, not a decoration — it's the visual full-stop for the Discovery scroll. The transparent-asset path drops the blend trick entirely; the opaque-asset path keeps it as a backwards-compatibility shim until `soko-walking-and-reading.webp` is also re-exported transparent.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/discovery_footer.dart`; assets at `heyl_app/assets/images/illustrations/soko-seating-and-reading.webp` (transparent) and `…/soko-walking-and-reading.webp` (opaque); l10n key `discoveryFooterEnd` in `intl_{en,pt,pt_BR}.arb`. The `doneLooking` variant uses the dedicated `discoveryFooterDoneLooking` key — see D61.

## Discovery — Action Bar Search (PROD-1520)

### D54: Membros tab dropped from Discovery search (PROD-1520) — SUPERSEDED by D251 (Leitores tab, 2026-07-24)
- **Choice**: The Discovery action bar's category row has 3 tabs — Zines / Eventos / Sítios. The Membros tab from Figma node `6144:5555` is intentionally not implemented.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Ship the tab and stub it with a "Em breve" / "Coming soon" empty state — adds dead UI, encourages users to tap something that can't help them, and creates a gravity well for design polish on a feature we haven't committed to.
  - Hide the tab via a feature flag — same dead-UI risk once flipped on, plus an extra moving part to maintain.
- **Why**: The backend has no member-search endpoint today and no plan to add one in the near term (confirmed with backend team 2026-05-05). The three remaining tabs each map onto an already-shipped fast-mode endpoint (`GET /lists/public?q=`, `POST /events/search`, `POST /places/search`).
- **Trigger to revisit**: when product prioritises a follow / discover-people surface and the backend lands a `GET /users/search` (or equivalent) endpoint. At that point, restoring Membros means: re-add the icon + label + a 4th `BtSqIco`, add a `members` enum value to `DiscoverySearchCategory`, add a `searchUsers()` client, and map the response onto the canonical card chrome.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_action_bar.dart` — the category list has 3 entries, not 4. ARB key `discoveryCategoryMembers` removed from `intl_{en,pt,pt_BR}.arb` alongside.

### D55: Search results reuse the Em destaque card chrome and end with the doneLooking footer (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: When the action-bar search panel is open and the debounced query is ≥ 2 chars, the shelf stack is replaced by `SearchResultsSection`: a 2-column grid of `HighlightedShelfCard` (195 × 304, the same chrome Em destaque uses) followed by `DiscoveryFooter.doneLooking()` (caption diverges from the regular footer — see [D61](#d61-search-results-footer-caption-diverges-from-the-main-shelves-footer-prod-1520)). Loading shows a 3-row skeleton with the same dimensions; empty results show a centred "Sem resultados para ‘…’" line; errors show a centred retry message. Below the minimum query length, the section renders nothing so the existing shelves remain visible.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - A bespoke compact card per entity type — would have meant three new card variants and three loading skeletons. Reusing the Em destaque card keeps the search results visually anchored to the editorial language Discovery already uses.
  - Replacing `DiscoveryFooter()` with `doneLooking` only when results are non-empty AND below a minimum count — adds a state that's hard to test against and provides no visual benefit (the footer reads as "this is the bottom" regardless of result count).
  - Hiding the shelves the moment the search panel opens (before the user types) — leaves a tall blank area below the action bar that reads as a broken state. Keeping the shelves visible until a query is entered preserves continuity.
- **Why**: The user's design (`6144:5521`) shows results in the same card geometry as Em destaque, and `done-looking.webp` was already authored on the pixel-perfect commit explicitly for this state. Mapping every result type onto a single card via the `SearchResultRow` adapter keeps the widget free of per-entity branches.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/search_results_section.dart`; provider `discoverySearchResultsProvider` in `providers/search_results_provider.dart`; mounted by `_DiscoveryBody` in `screens/discovery_screen.dart`.

### D56: Image-to-title gap is 10 px on canonical / Em destaque cards, 8 px on the compact Perto de ti card (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: `imageToTitleGap = 10` on `HighlightedShelfCard` and `CanonicalShelfCard`; `imageToTitleGap = 8` on `NearYouCard`. All three are exposed as static `const` so callers don't drift from the spec.
- **Previous behaviour**: 2 px on canonical / Em destaque, 6 px on Perto de ti (original cuts of `6144:5092` and the figma-cache spec). Felt visually cramped — the 18 px name baseline sat too close to the artwork edge.
- **Why per-card values**: the 14 px name on the compact card needs less breathing room than the 18 px name on canonical / Em destaque. Two-px difference comes straight from Figma `6144:3690` (Perto de ti) vs `6144:5149` (canonical / Em destaque).
- **Trigger to revisit**: if the designer ships a new card layout (e.g. social-proof shelf), audit the gap separately rather than reusing one of the existing values by default — the gap is per-card-type, not global.
- **Where it lives**: `imageToTitleGap` constants on each card class in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/`. Total card heights bumped to absorb the change: Em destaque 304→326, canonical 275→297, Perto de ti 215→240 (the 2-line title bump from D57 also folded into these numbers).

### D57: Card titles wrap to 2 lines with ellipsis (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: All three card classes (`HighlightedShelfCard`, `CanonicalShelfCard`, `NearYouCard`) render their name with `maxLines: 2, overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis`. Total card heights reserve room for the worst-case 2-line render.
- **Previous behaviour**: `maxLines: 1, overflow: TextOverflow.clip, softWrap: false`. List names like "Curadoria Vintage por Pedro Pires" or "A Escola - Nova temporada" got truncated mid-word with no ellipsis affordance, leaving the user guessing what the full title was.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Wrap to 3+ lines — pushes the cards too tall on the canonical 2.5-card layout; the second card on a row would dominate the shelf vertically.
  - Variable card height (1 line on cards with short names, 2 lines on cards with long names) — the row's `ListView` needs a uniform height, so the row has to reserve worst-case space anyway. Variable height also produces a jagged baseline across the shelf.
  - Auto-shrink font size to fit — clashes with the editorial typography hierarchy (Zalando Sans Medium 18 / 14) that the design specifies elsewhere.
- **Why**: Reserving the second line guarantees a consistent baseline across cards and gives long titles a way to land readably. The cost is whitespace below short single-line titles, which reads as breathing room rather than a layout bug given the Soko aesthetic.
- **Re-tested and upheld (PROD-3090, 2026-07-14)**: challenged on the Map results drawer, where the "whitespace reads as breathing room" argument does **not** hold — the drawer's `half` snap is one card row tall, so the reserved line is paid for in *map*, not in harmless padding. Kept anyway: ellipsising real names was the worse trade, and the "variable card height" alternative rejected above is exactly what a `SliverGrid` forbids (uniform `mainAxisExtent` → the row reserves for the tallest name in the **list**). See [D236](#d236-a-card-reserves-a-subtitle-slot-only-when-its-given-a-subtitle--and-d57s-2-line-name-stays-prod-3090), which *did* reclaim the adjacent ~36 px subtitle slot the map never uses.
- **Where it lives**: each card's `Text(name, maxLines: 2, overflow: TextOverflow.ellipsis, …)` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/`. Total height constants reserve room for the second line.

### D58: Card attribution prefix is "By {name}" / "Por {name}" — with a `Pela` exception for Soko in PT (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: The default localized prefix `discoveryShelfTrendingAttributionPrefix` is `"By "` (EN) / `"Por "` (PT-PT, PT-BR), and the per-shelf `discoveryShelfYoursAttribution` is `"By you"` / `"Por ti"` / `"Por você"`. All four hardcoded `'Edt. $ownerName'` call sites in `recommended`, `editor_picks`, `highlighted`, and `city_guides` shelves read the prefix from l10n via the helper `attributionPrefixFor(l10n, ownerHandle)`.
- **Soko exception**: when `ownerHandle == EnvironmentConfig.sokoHandle` (default `'soko'`), the helper returns `discoveryShelfFeminineAttributionPrefix` instead — `"By "` (EN, unchanged) / `"Pela "` (PT-PT, PT-BR). Soko reads as feminine in Portuguese, and "por + a" contracts to "pela", so `"Pela Soko"` is more idiomatic than `"Por Soko"`. The Daily Drop and Weekly Bundle bylines use the same logic — EN `"By Soko"`, PT `"Pela Soko"`.
- **Why a single-handle exception, not a general gendered-attribution feature**: there is no `gender` / `pronoun` column on `user_profile` (verified against `heyl/apps/user/models/user_profile.py` and the alembic migrations on 2026-05-06). Without that data we can't generalise; and inferring gender from `full_name` is fragile (cross-cultural names, gender-neutral names, non-binary users). The Soko handle is the only owner whose grammatical gender we know with certainty, so it gets the exception.
- **Previous copy**: `"Edt. {name}"` (a literal abbreviation of "Editor"). Read as a job-title to native PT speakers and as a typo to native EN speakers; neither audience interpreted it as the intended "by this curator".
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Spell out "Editor" in full — too formal for the editorial-zine voice, and the curator field is already labelled visually by its position in the card.
  - Drop the prefix entirely (just show the name) — loses the implicit "this list is curated by …" framing and confuses self-curated lists ("by you" reads naturally; a bare "you" doesn't).
  - Keep the existing key name `discoveryShelfTrendingAttributionPrefix` semantically misleading even though it now applies to every shelf — accepted as minor debt; renaming would cascade into 4 call sites and a regenerated l10n file for no functional gain.
- **Where it lives**: ARB keys `discoveryShelfTrendingAttributionPrefix`, `discoveryShelfYoursAttribution`, `discoveryDailyDropByline`, `discoveryWeeklyBundleByline` in `lib/l10n/intl_{en,pt,pt_BR}.arb`. Shelf widgets read the prefix via `l10n.discoveryShelfTrendingAttributionPrefix`.

### D59: Shelf cards size to viewport width with a fractional "visible card count" hint, no per-card design-width cap (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: `DiscoveryShelf` accepts `visibleCardsHint` (e.g. `2.5` for canonical shelves, `3.5` for Perto de ti) plus a `cardHeightForWidth: double Function(double)` callback. The shelf wraps each cell in a `SizedBox(width: cellWidth)` where `cellWidth = (maxWidth − floor(hint) × cardGap) / hint`. Two-card layouts (Em destaque, search results) use `cellWidth = (maxWidth − gap) / 2`. Cards always fill the row; their internal `LayoutBuilder` scales the artwork proportionally and keeps the text block at fixed font sizes.
- **Previous behaviour**: cards had hardcoded widths (160.418 / 105 / 195) regardless of viewport. On phones < 400 px content area the Em destaque row overflowed by ~42 px; on viewports between mobile (~430 px) and the desktop breakpoint (1024 px), the canonical shelf showed 2.79 cards visible (no half-peek) and Perto de ti showed 5–7 full cards.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Cap card growth at the design width and accept that the row has free space on wider viewports — breaks the "always show ½ card on the right" affordance the design relies on for scrollability cues.
  - Cap card growth at e.g. 1.3× design width (a soft maximum) — produces an arbitrary hand-tuned threshold and still has the half-peek-or-not split behaviour around the cap; not worth the complexity vs simply letting cards grow.
  - Skip the half-peek and snap to integer card counts (e.g. always 2 / 3 / 4 cards depending on viewport) — loses the editorial cue that more cards exist; the half-card peek is what tells the user the row is scrollable.
- **Why**: With the text block at fixed font sizes, scaling the image proportionally keeps the printed-zine aesthetic intact at any width while honouring the per-shelf "show N.5 cards" rule. The shell-level cap (D60) provides the upper bound; redundant per-card caps just produced inconsistent layouts in the 600–1023 px range.
- **Where it lives**: `_effectiveCardWidth` / `_effectiveCardHeight` on `DiscoveryShelf` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/discovery_shelf.dart`. Each shelf opts in by passing `visibleCardsHint` + `cardHeightForWidth: SomeCard.heightForWidth`. The 2-card layouts (`HighlightedShelf._HighlightedRow`, `SearchResultsSection._SearchRow`) compute the cell width inline.

### D60: Discovery centred-column breakpoint at 600 px (was 1024 px) (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: `DiscoveryShell._desktopBreakpoint = 600`. Any viewport ≥ 600 px wide gets a centred 480 px content column; below 600 the page renders full-width.
- **Previous breakpoint**: 1024 px. Anything between 600 and 1023 (iPad mini portrait, foldables open, narrow desktop windows, Surface tablets) sat in a "shelves grow unbounded" zone — canonical cards reached 286 px on iPad mini portrait (1.78× the 160 design) and the editorial card chrome looked stretched.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Keep the 1024 breakpoint and add a per-card max-width cap — defeats the purpose of D59's "always fill the row to honour the half-peek". Brings back the inconsistent layouts the unbounded-card change was meant to fix.
  - Use a graduated cap (e.g. 600–900 → cap 600, ≥ 900 → cap 480) — three values to keep in sync as the design evolves and the visual jump as the user resizes a desktop window across 900 px feels arbitrary.
  - Lower the breakpoint to 768 (iPad mini width) — still leaves the 600–767 range (foldables, large landscape phones) with grow-unbounded cards.
- **Why**: 600 px is Material Design's compact→medium boundary. The text block on shelf cards is fixed font sizes (18 px name, 14 px subtitle), so once cards grow past ~480 px content area the artwork-to-text ratio degrades. Centring at 480 above 600 keeps the editorial chrome at the dimensions it was tuned for; on viewports < 600 the responsive cards (D59) handle scaling cleanly.
- **Trade-off accepted**: tablets in portrait get visible side margins around the centred column. Acceptable on Discovery specifically because the page already uses a paper-tinted background — the margins read as intentional editorial layout, not as wasted space.
- **Where it lives**: `_desktopBreakpoint` and `_desktopContentMaxWidth` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart`. Variable name kept as `_desktopBreakpoint` even though it now governs tablets too.

### D61: Search-results footer caption diverges from the main shelves footer (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: `DiscoveryFooter.doneLooking()` renders the dedicated caption "Done looking?" (EN) / "Já viste tudo?" (PT-PT) / "Já viu tudo?" (PT-BR) via a separate l10n key (`discoveryFooterDoneLooking`). The main shelves footer keeps `discoveryFooterEnd` ("That's all folks" / "É tudo, malta" / "É isso, galera").
- **Previously**: both variants shared `discoveryFooterEnd` (a TODO note on the constructor flagged "revisit if design wants different copy"). The shared key produced the same celebratory "That's all folks" line on the search-results state, which read as a victory lap when the user might just be browsing — wrong tonal register for a search-results footer.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Reuse `discoveryFooterEnd` and just swap the illustration — already the previous behaviour and read incorrectly per the above.
  - One key with a `{variant}` placeholder — would require the variant string to leak into `intl_*.arb` and forces translators to think about plurals/cases for what is actually two distinct sentences.
- **Why**: Different surfaces deserve different bylines. The search-results footer is conversational ("done looking?") and invites the user to either start a new search or close the panel; the shelves footer is a sign-off ("that's all folks").
- **Where it lives**: l10n keys `discoveryFooterEnd` + `discoveryFooterDoneLooking` in `intl_{en,pt,pt_BR}.arb`; resolution via `_isDoneLooking` flag on `DiscoveryFooter` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/discovery_footer.dart`.

### D62: Discovery footer reserves 200 px of caption-to-screen-bottom spacing (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: From the bottom of the footer caption to the bottom of the screen there are exactly 200 px of empty space (ignoring the bottom nav, which overlays the content via `Stack` rather than taking layout space). Implemented as `bottomNavReserve = 96` on `DiscoveryScreen`'s outer `Padding` plus a footer-internal `trailingGap = 104`. 96 + 104 = 200.
- **Why split the spacing**: keeping the 96 px reserve at the screen level means non-footer Discovery content (e.g. when shelves all collapse silently to `SizedBox.shrink`) still clears the bottom nav by 96 px. The extra 104 px is footer-specific breathing room — it pushes the editorial sign-off well clear of the nav so the page doesn't read as truncated. Bumping the screen-level reserve to 200 instead would have inflated every Discovery render by 104 px regardless of content presence.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Bump `bottomNavReserve` to 200 px globally — over-reserves on screens that don't render the footer.
  - Drop the trailing gap and rely on the nav-reserve alone — falls 104 px short of the 200 px spec.
- **Where it lives**: `bottomNavReserve` in `lib/features/discovery/screens/discovery_screen.dart`; `trailingGap` (104 px `SizedBox` after the caption) in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/discovery_footer.dart`.

### D63: DiscoveryFooter claims full available width so its centred column reads correctly (PROD-1520)
- **Choice**: The footer wraps its centred `Column` in `SizedBox(width: double.infinity)`. Without this, the centred column shrinks to its widest child (typically the caption text) and ends up hugging the start of the parent — visually indistinguishable from start-aligned content.
- **Trigger**: `SearchResultsSection._SearchResultsList` uses `crossAxisAlignment: CrossAxisAlignment.start` for the 2-column results grid above the footer. That alignment cascades to the footer's parent and made the footer's own `crossAxisAlignment: CrossAxisAlignment.center` a no-op.
- **Alternatives rejected**:
  - Change the parent's `crossAxisAlignment` to `stretch` — would force every other child of `_SearchResultsList` to fill the row, breaking the 2-card grid layout above.
  - Wrap the footer at every call site in `SizedBox(width: double.infinity)` or `Center` — pushes layout responsibility outside the widget; easy to forget at a future call site.
- **Why**: Centring is part of the footer's intrinsic visual identity. Having the widget claim full width itself makes it composable — callers don't have to know about the centring requirement.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/discovery_footer.dart` — outer `SizedBox(width: double.infinity)` around the `Column`.

## Venue Detail Page (PROD-1670)

### D65: Save button moves to right of venue name (Figma divergence)
- **Choice**: The 40 px circular Save bookmark sits at the right edge of the venue-name row, vertically centred against the name (works for single-line and wrapped names alike).
- **Figma `6144:4133`**: Save lives next to "Deixar nota" in the row beneath the tags.
- **Why**: "Deixar nota" only renders when the user is viewing the venue from a list **they own** (D67 / design doc § 4.3). On every other entry point (standalone, in-list non-owner, in-list private fallback) the inline-actions row collapses entirely. Save needs a permanent home so it's reachable on every entry point — the name row is the right place because the name is always rendered.
- **Where it lives**: `_NameAndTagBlock` in `lib/features/venue_detail/screens/venue_detail_screen.dart`. The `Row` uses `CrossAxisAlignment.center` + `Expanded(name) + 10px gap + SokoSaveButton(size: 40)`.

### D66: Hero photo — 16:9 cover crop, no letterbox
<!-- STALE(2026-06-03): "No background fill" no longer holds — the hero now renders through `SokoCardImage` (`venue_hero_photo.dart`), which paints a type-aware brand-colour floor + texture beneath the photo and shows it when `image_url` 404s. Superseded by D200. The 16:9 cover-crop + radius 6 still hold. -->
<!-- STALE(2026-08-28): the 16:9 cover no longer holds for the DETAIL pages — superseded by D289 (PROD-4074): the detail hero is now SokoPhotoCollage, and a lone photo renders as the left-aligned big tile at 4:5, not 16:9 (alignment flipped right→left 2026-09-01). As of 2026-08-30 the daily-drop detail page adopted SokoPhotoCollage too (D289 parity), so `VenueHeroPhoto` is now UNUSED — it instantiates nowhere in `lib/`; only doc-comment pattern references remain. This entry describes that legacy widget. -->
- **Choice**: `AspectRatio(16/9)` + `CachedImage(fit: BoxFit.cover)` + `radius: 6`. No background fill (the image always fills the box).
- **Figma `6144:4119`**: 264 × 330 portrait photo at the head of a 3-photo grid (the secondary photos are deferred to the multi-photo gallery follow-up — PROD-1676/1677).
- **Original design-doc rule (`docs/designs/venue-event-details-redesign.md` § 5.2)**: port the legacy sheet's pattern — fixed-height container + `BoxFit.contain` against a `surfaceVariant` letterbox fill. This was authored before runtime testing.
- **Updated runtime decision (Zé, 2026-05-06)**: live testing showed letterbox bars on portrait sources felt "broken" against the editorial Soko/Blue page. Cropping was preferred over letterboxing.
- **Why 16:9**: lands the hero at ~240 px tall on a 430 px Figma frame and ~270 px on the 480 px desktop content cap — comfortable proportions that don't dominate the long scroll, and well-known card aspect that source photos look natural inside.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_hero_photo.dart`.

### D67: Venue routes register flat under DiscoveryShell, with a smart back-button
- **Choice**: Both `/venues/:venueId` and `/lists/:listId/venues/:venueId` register as **flat siblings** of `/discovery` inside the existing `ShellRoute(builder: DiscoveryShell)`. They are NOT nested under the existing MainShell-owned `/lists/:listId` route. The synthetic back-stack on cold deep-load is handled by `VenueBackButton`, not by GoRouter parent-child config.
- **Design-doc rule it overrides** (`docs/designs/venue-event-details-redesign.md` § 7.2): use GoRouter parent-child route nesting so cold-load `/lists/:listId/venues/:venueId` automatically pushes `/lists/:listId` underneath, giving "free" synthetic back-stacks.
- **Why we diverged**: the existing `/lists/:listId` lives inside MainShell (legacy bottom nav). Nesting the venue route under it would inherit the wrong shell — the legacy 3-icon Memórias / Soko / Listas nav instead of the new 5-icon Discovery nav the redesign requires (`docs/ui/figma-cache/screens/detail/venue-page.md` row 11). Moving `/lists/:listId` into DiscoveryShell is out of scope for PROD-1670 (lists hub redesign hasn't started).
- **Smart back-button**: `lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_back_button.dart` — `Navigator.canPop()` → `context.pop()`; otherwise routes to `/lists/<listId>` for the in-list URL or `/` for standalone. Implements the design-doc § 11.0 row 11 contract.
- **Trade-off**: the synthetic stack is "appears to work" rather than "is real". A user who deep-links `/lists/abc/venues/xyz` and taps back lands on `/lists/abc` via `context.go`, not via a genuine pop. Acceptable because the visual outcome is identical and the alternative would have meant either a half-finished cutover or a partial lists-hub redesign.
- **Path-matching note**: GoRouter is longest-prefix-first, so `/lists/:listId/venues/:venueId` does not collide with the MainShell-owned `/lists/:listId`. The flat siblings are unambiguous.

### D68: Venue page background is always Soko/Blue (no per-state switching)
- **Choice**: `ColoredBox(color: AppColors.sokoBlue)` wraps the entire page; venues with and without upcoming events render on the same Soko/Blue. Event detail (PROD-1671) will use Soko/Lilac on its own screen.
- **Briefly considered (then reverted)**: switching between Soko/Blue (with events) and Soko/Lilac (without events) based on `upcomingEvents.isNotEmpty`. Implementation-and-test cycle revealed the dynamic switch was based on a stale designer message; the current Figma `6144:4113` ships venues on Soko/Blue regardless of event presence.
- **Why fixed**: the page bg is part of the venue's brand identity, not a state indicator. Switching by event presence felt like a feature flag, not a colour decision.
- **Where it lives**: `_VenueDetailScreenState.build` in `venue_detail_screen.dart`. Wrapped in `ConstrainedBox(minHeight: viewportHeight)` so the colour fills the viewport on short / loading / error states.

### D69: Venue page vertical rhythm — 14 px default, 30 px section breaks
> **Superseded (2026-05-07)** — the venue page now matches Figma [`6181:5531`](https://www.figma.com/design/d4BCnyUHe2705J7ecQtaIH/Soko--shared-?node-id=6181-5531&m=dev) exactly, so the rhythm is no longer a divergence and doesn't belong here. See [`docs/features/venue-detail.md` § Vertical rhythm](../features/venue-detail.md#vertical-rhythm) for the current rule (30 px sections / 10 px intra / 6 px around action buttons / 5 px between details-grid rows / 71 px top inset).
>
> The original D69 below described the spacings the FE shipped *initially* — those were taken from a stale design (`6181:5421`) and were wrong; the corrected implementation lives in commit `718d823`. Kept (rather than removed) so that earlier code, comments, and PRs that reference "D69" still resolve.

- **Choice (original, no longer current)**: Default inter-section gap **14 px**, with **30 px** exceptions at hero → name and map → "Aparece em".
- **Why removed**: Figma `6181:5531` shows a uniform 30 px between every top-level section under a `flex-col gap-[30px]` container. There's no 14 px default in the design — that was an artifact of reading the older `6181:5421` mockup. Bringing the FE in line removed the divergence; nothing intentional remains to record here.
- **71 px top inset rule still applies** — the back-arrow row sits 71 px from the absolute top of the screen, computed as `(71 - MediaQuery.padding.top).clamp(0, 71)`. That's now documented in the venue-detail feature doc, not here, because it's a layout rule rather than a Figma divergence.

### D70: "Aparece em" rendered as manual horizontal scroll, not via DiscoveryShelf
- **Choice**: The 4.5-card "Aparece em" shelf is hand-composed in `venue_appears_in_shelf.dart` — header label + a `LayoutBuilder` + `ListView.separated(scrollDirection: Axis.horizontal)` of `CanonicalShelfCard`s. The card-sizing math `(maxWidth − floor(hint) × cardGap) / hint` mirrors `DiscoveryShelf._effectiveCardWidth` inline.
- **Why not reuse `DiscoveryShelf`**: the shelf scaffold renders its own title in **Mobile/H1 (Season Mix Light 42 px)** — the family + size used by all main Discovery shelves. The Figma cache for the venue detail page (`6144:4229`) calls for the "Aparece em" header in **Mobile/B1 Bold (Zalando Sans Medium 18 px)** — the same Discovery shelves use for card-internal text. The scaffold also forces a leading dotted `ShelfDivider` (D52) which has no place on a detail page.
- **Why not extend `DiscoveryShelf` to take an optional title style**: the title is part of the scaffold's design intent (D52); making it pluggable to fit a one-off detail-page surface would weaken the abstraction for marginal reuse.
- **What we did reuse**: `CanonicalShelfCard(subtitle: null)` (the Trending 2-line variant), `attributionPrefixFor` for "By "/"Por "/Soko-feminine handling, the visible-cards-hint math.
- **Owner attribution**: empty in v1 — `ListSummary` from `getVenueDetail` doesn't carry an owner block until PROD-1669 ships `ListSummaryOut.owner`. When it lands, the existing `CanonicalShelfCard` picks up the attribution automatically; no FE change beyond reading the new field.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_appears_in_shelf.dart`.

### D71: Detail-page routes use `state.pageKey`, not path-derived ValueKey or `UniqueKey()`
- **Choice**: All four detail routes (`/venues/:venueId`, `/lists/:listId/venues/:venueId`, `/events/:eventId`, `/lists/:listId/events/:eventId`) use `key: state.pageKey` on their `NoTransitionPage`. `state.pageKey` is GoRouter's per-stack-entry `ValueKey<String>` — minted once when the route is pushed and reused on every subsequent router rebuild.
- **Why not path-derived ValueKey**: the navigation chain `event-A → venue-B → event-A again` (event A lives at venue B and shows up in venue B's "Eventos aqui") used to crash with "GlobalKey was used multiple times — `_ScrollSemantics`". GoRouter's Navigator treats matching `Page.key`s as the same widget instance and reuses Element state across the two stack entries; the inner `Scrollable._scrollSemanticsKey` (a Flutter-internal `GlobalKey`) then ends up assigned to two different rendered Scrollables, tripping the duplicate-GlobalKey assertion. See [`learnings/gorouter-duplicate-page-key-crash.md`](../learnings/gorouter-duplicate-page-key-crash.md) for the full repro and Flutter-side mechanics.
- **Why not `UniqueKey()`**: `key: UniqueKey()` inside a `pageBuilder` callback regenerates a brand-new key on **every router rebuild**, not just on push. The framework can't reconcile the existing page widget with the new one (mismatched keys), so it tears down and remounts the entire screen subtree mid-frame on routine rebuilds — throwing `markNeedsLayout outside layout phase` (`rendering/object.dart:2524`) and `Element.update` lifecycle assertions (`framework.dart:4379`) every few hundred ms. This was the original approach for D71 and it was wrong.
- **Why `state.pageKey`**: GoRouter creates `state.pageKey` once when a route enters the Navigator stack (`_getUniqueValueKey()` for imperative pushes; `ValueKey('${matchedPath}')` for declarative navigations) and **caches it** on the route match. On subsequent rebuilds the same key is handed back. That gives both properties we need: (a) stable across rebuilds → no element-tree churn; (b) distinct between different pushes of the same path → no `_ScrollSemantics` GlobalKey collision.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` — the four detail-page `pageBuilder` callbacks.

---

## Location Scope Picker

### D64: Discovery requires a concrete city; Add-to-list does not
- **Choice**: `showLocationScopePicker(...)` and `LocationScopePickerSheet` take a `requireCity` flag (default `false`). Discovery's action-bar pill passes `requireCity: true`; the add-to-list sheet leaves it default. **(Superseded 2026-07-23, PROD-3204: `LocationScopePickerSheet` and the `requireCity` param were removed when the map picker went to 100% — the map picker is now the only picker. Kept for historical context.)**
  - With `requireCity: true`: the **Apply** button is disabled until the working scope is a `SearchScopeCountryCity`; the inline "Remover cidade" pill is hidden so users can't drop the city back out; the empty city-row reads `searchScopeSelectCityRequired` ("Selecionar cidade") instead of the optional copy.
  - With `requireCity: false` (default): country-only scopes are valid Apply targets, the "Remover" pill is visible, and the empty row reads `searchScopeAddCityOptional`. This is the existing add-to-list behaviour, preserved verbatim.
- **Why split the contract**: Discovery's shelves (`Em destaque`, `Espaços`) are city-keyed — a country-only scope produces empty shelves and a meaningless pill label. Add-to-list's broader place-search backend handles country-only happily, so forcing a city there would just push extra friction onto a flow that doesn't need it.
- **Paired with the auto-scope cascade**: `discoveryAutoScopeProvider` always resolves to a `SearchScopeCountryCity` (via the user-profile → IP-city → flagship → global-default cascade — see [`docs/features/discovery.md` § City resolver](../features/discovery.md#city-resolver)). Together, the cascade + the `requireCity` flag make "country-only on Discovery" unreachable in normal operation; the picker's defensive country-only fallback only fires if every cascade step has failed for network reasons.
- **Apply-gate logic** lives in the public `canApplyScopeChange(working, current, requireCity)` predicate — extracted as `@visibleForTesting` so the contract is exercised by direct unit tests rather than synthetic widget interactions.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/location_scope_picker_sheet.dart` (picker + flag + predicate), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_action_bar.dart` (passes `true`), `lib/features/lists/widgets/search_add_to_list_sheet.dart` (passes default).
- **Trigger to revisit**: if add-to-list ever needs a city-mandatory mode (e.g. proximity-restricted searches) we can flip `requireCity` per-call there too — no API change needed. If a third caller wants a non-binary policy (e.g. "city or postcode"), reach for an enum instead of stretching the bool.

## List Page Redesign (PROD-1700 / PROD-1702)

The decisions below pin the foundations ticket (PROD-1702) of the list-page redesign umbrella. The full living spec lives at [`docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md`](../designs/list-page-redesign.md).

### D72: Bell button hidden on both owner + non-owner action rows in v1
- **Choice**: The list-page header's action row does NOT render the Bell (notifications) button on either ownership variant. Owner action row collapses to `Toggle · Share · Edit`; non-owner to `Toggle · Seguir · Share · Flag`.
- **Figma `6181:5916` (owner) / `6181:5861` (non-owner)**: shows a 40 px circular Bell icon in both variants.
- **Why hidden**: there is no BE flow for "subscribe to list updates" yet. Rendering the icon would imply a feature that doesn't work; v1 ships the rest of the redesign and we revisit when the BE surface lands. Tracked as a follow-up under the umbrella (PROD-1700 follow-ups #10 + #11).
- **When this changes**: once the BE notification subscription endpoint ships, re-add the Bell to `_ActionRow` in `list_page_header.dart` and wire to the new endpoint.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` — the comment in `_ActionRow.build` references this decision.

### D73: Bookmark icon hidden in title row on owner's own list
- **Choice**: The title-row stats group renders `[avatar] · Por X · N págs. · save_count [bookmark]` for non-owners. On the owner's own list the trailing Bookmark icon is HIDDEN — the line ends with `save_count`.
- **Figma `6181:5849` (cover, owner variant)**: shows the Bookmark icon trailing the stats line for owners too.
- **Why hidden**: a user cannot follow their own list — the surface is meaningless on the owner variant. Tapping the bookmark on a non-owner list toggles list-follow via the existing `toggleFollow()` plumbing; on owner's own list there's nothing to toggle.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` — `_StatsLine.build` gates the trailing `_StatsBookmark` on `!state.isOwner`.

### D74: List-page route mounts in DiscoveryShell with a builder-time admin/non-admin dispatcher
- **Choice**: `/lists/:listId` is registered as a flat sibling under `DiscoveryShell`'s `ShellRoute` (next to `/venues/:id`, `/events/:id`, etc.). Inside the route's `builder`, a `_ListDetailDispatcher` widget watches `authStateProvider` and returns the redesigned `ListPageScreen` for admins (`role == admin`) or the legacy `ListViewScreen` for everyone else.
- **Design-doc rule it overrides** ([`docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md`](../designs/list-page-redesign.md) § 13.2 #3): "register inside `DiscoveryShell` behind admin `discovery-page` PostHog flag; existing route in `MainShell` stays live until cutover".
- **Why we diverged**:
  - GoRouter forbids two registrations at the same path. The strict reading of the design-doc rule was contradictory.
  - The `discovery-page` flag referenced in the doc isn't in `experiment_service.dart`; the detail-page predecessors (D67) gate purely on `role == admin` via `_isAdminOnlyRoute`. Admin role is server-issued and can't be misconfigured remotely. Mirroring that gate avoids inventing a PostHog dial that adds rollout-failure surface for no benefit during the build window.
  - The `_isAdminOnlyRoute` gate redirects non-admins to `/`, which would 403 every non-admin viewer of `/lists/:listId`. Dispatching inside the route preserves non-admin access to the legacy body while routing admins to the new shell.
- **Trade-off**: non-admin users now see the DiscoveryShell wrapper (5-icon Discovery bottom nav) while their underlying screen content is still the legacy `ListViewScreen`. Acceptable because the cutover (PROD-1700 #6) drops the dispatcher and routes all users to `ListPageScreen` anyway.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` — `_ListDetailDispatcher` widget + the `GoRoute` registration inside the DiscoveryShell `ShellRoute`. The MainShell registration was deleted; only a comment-stub remains where it used to live.

### D75: List-page bg = Soko/Paper, applied via `_pageBgForRoute` (not via `pageBackgroundColorProvider`)
- **Choice**: The list-page Scaffold bg is Soko/Paper (`#F9F0F0`, was `#FEEFEF` pre-PROD-1738), painted by `DiscoveryShell` via `_pageBgForRoute(location)`. The mapping for `^/lists/[^/]+$` lands on `AppColors.sokoPaper`. The screen itself wraps content in an inner `ColoredBox(AppColors.sokoPaper)` so the body stays self-sufficient on desktop's 480 px column. (Post-PROD-1885 the shell's default light-mode fallback is also `sokoPaper`, so `_pageBgForRoute` returning `sokoPaper` is now a no-op vs the fallback — kept explicit because future bg changes might diverge.)
- **Design-doc rule it overrides** ([`docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md`](../designs/list-page-redesign.md) § "`pageBackgroundColorProvider` integration"): "Push `AppColors.sokoPaper` into `pageBackgroundColorProvider` on `initState` post-frame; reset to `null` on `dispose`."
- **Why we diverged**: the `pageBackgroundColorProvider` `StateProvider` was deleted in PR #308 (commit `a3957ae`). The lifecycle-coupled approach (push on `initState`, reset on `dispose`) left stale colours after back-nav under web's `NoTransitionPage` — `addPostFrameCallback` and `dispose` interleaved unreliably, leaving the shell holding the previous page's colour. Route-derived paint is a pure function of `matchedLocation` so the bg can never lag the visible page.
- **Anchored regex**: `^/lists/[^/]+$` (end-anchored) so the `^/lists/[^/]+/venues/...` and `^/lists/[^/]+/events/...` matches above keep their precedence. Without the `$` the list-page mapping would shadow the in-list venue/event detail bg.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart` — `_pageBgForRoute()`. The two-layer paint pattern ([`learnings/detail-page-shell-bg-paint.md`](../learnings/detail-page-shell-bg-paint.md)) extends naturally to the list-page case.

### D76: Suggestion-row trailing chips — `[plus-circle, bookmark]`, dismiss removed (PROD-1704)
- **Choice**: Owner-side suggestion rows in the new List view (mode 2) trail with two 30×30 chips — plus-circle ("add to *this* list") + bookmark ("save to one of *my* lists"). The legacy dismiss button is **gone** from the visible chrome.
- **Figma `6197:5954` § Sugerido para ti** (own list-view): the suggestion row contains exactly `Frame9235` (plus-circle) + `Frame9236` (bookmark). No dismiss affordance.
- **What changed in code**: `suggestion_item_row.dart` previously rendered `[Add (32×32 rose), Dismiss (32×32 X)]`. Now it renders `[PlusCircleChip (30×30), BookmarkChip (30×30)]`. The `onDismiss` callback was renamed `onSaveToOtherList`; the bookmark tap opens `showAddToListSheet`. The `_handleDismiss` path + `slideRight` exit animation in `list_suggestions_section.dart` were removed accordingly. The provider's `dismissSuggestion` method stays orphaned for a future swipe affordance.
- **Trade-off**: users lose the explicit "hide this suggestion" button. Acceptable per Zé's call ("match spec literally") — dismiss can be re-introduced as a swipe gesture in a follow-up if friction surfaces. Analytics tracking for `trackSuggestionDismiss` is no longer wired (event still fireable from the provider should we restore it).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/suggestion_item_row.dart`, `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_suggestions_section.dart`, `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_view_mode/list_view_item_row.dart` (the chip widgets `PlusCircleChip` / `BookmarkChip` live here and are reused).

### D77: Suggestion `PlusCircleChip` uses neutral Soko/Ink @ 6 % chrome with Soko/Pink hover, not rose CTA (PROD-1704)
- **Choice**: The "add to this list" chip on the new List view's suggestion rows is a 30×30 circle with `AppColors.sokoInk` @ 6 % bg + a 14 px Soko/Ink plus icon. Hover swaps the bg to `AppColors.sokoPink` (`#FFB8CB`). `isSaved` dims the icon to Soko/Ink @ 30 % alpha (bg stays at 6 %) and disables the tap.
- **Figma design-system master `4032:3999`** (`Frame9235`): `Property 1=Default` is `bg-[rgba(68,19,29,0.06)]`; `Property 1=Hover` is `bg-[#ffb8cb]`. The page-level instance on `6197:4858` shows 8 % — that's an instance override, not the canonical chrome. The design-system master is authoritative and matches every other Soko/Ink chip in the codebase (header action buttons, stats line, etc.).
- **Lovable / legacy precedent**: the existing `suggestion_item_row.dart` rendered Add as a 32×32 rose-filled circle (CTA primary) and Dismiss as an X — a clear visual hierarchy between the two affordances. The new design intentionally flattens them: both chips read as siblings.
- **Why neutral chrome**: the design pairs the two chips so the user reads them as parallel actions ("add here" vs "save elsewhere"), not "primary CTA + dismiss". Rose CTA implied the Add was the dominant affordance, which the new design rejects.
- **`isSaved` divergence note**: the design-system master defines only `Default` and `Hover` for plus-circle (no "Saved" or "Disabled" variant — `Frame9236` Bookmark has a `Selected` Soko/Pink-fill state but plus-circle does not). When a suggestion is already in the list, plus-circle's tap is meaningless; we dim the icon (Soko/Ink @ 30 %) while keeping the bg uniform with the bookmark sibling. This is a divergence from the design system but maintains visual parity between the two chips on the row.
- **Where it lives**: `BookmarkChip` + `PlusCircleChip` widgets in `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_view_mode/list_view_item_row.dart`. Reused from `suggestion_item_row.dart`.

### D78: Forked `list_view_item_row.dart` instead of restyling `list_item_row.dart` (PROD-1704)
- **Choice**: The new List view (mode 2) uses a fresh `list_view_item_row.dart` rather than restyling the legacy `list_item_row.dart`. The two now coexist; the legacy row stays as-is for `list_view_screen.dart`.
- **Why fork**: the legacy row carries five affordances that don't apply to the new design — expandable content (with an animated chevron), inline tip-edit (textarea + save/cancel), drag handle for reorder, cover-badge overlay, and a delete IconButton. Stripping all of them from a shared widget would have left a tangle of `if (isLegacy) ... else ...` branches and shrunk the legacy widget's surface area in ways that risk regressing today's `list_view_screen.dart` flow.
- **Figma `6197:5954`** (own list-view): rows are static — 50×63 thumbnail + B1 Reg name + B2 Reg sub-line + 30×30 bookmark chip. No expand, edit, drag, cover-badge, or delete.
- **Trade-off**: two row widgets must stay visually consistent on the items they share (thumbnail style, name/sub-line typography). Today they diverge — the new row uses 18 px / w300 (Mobile/B1 Reg) and `AppColors.sokoShade4` for the sub-line; the legacy row uses 17 px / w400 (`AppTheme.listItemTitle`) and 12 px with 70 % alpha. This is acceptable while the new list page is admin-gated; at cutover (PROD-1700 #6) the legacy row goes away.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_view_mode/list_view_item_row.dart` (new) vs `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_item_row.dart` (legacy).

---

## List Page Redesign — Zine view (PROD-1703)

The decisions below pin the Zine view (cover + N item pages + in-list detail). Source design: [`docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md`](../designs/list-page-redesign.md) § 6 + § 7 + § 11 + § 9.2.

### D79: Curator avatar hidden when `owner.avatar_url` is null
- **Choice**: The list-page header's title-row stats group renders the 16×16 curator avatar **only** when `UserList.ownerAvatarUrl` is non-null. When the field is missing the avatar slot is suppressed entirely — the stats line starts directly at the attribution text.
- **Design-doc rule it overrides** ([`docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md`](../designs/list-page-redesign.md) § 7.1): "Falls back to a default Soko mark when the curator has no avatar."
- **Why hidden**: the BE doesn't surface `owner.avatar_url` on `UserListOut.owner` yet, so the placeholder would render on every list — a permanent visual state masquerading as transient. Suppressing the slot keeps the header clean until the BE field lands; the wiring stays so the avatar lights up automatically when populated. OpenAPI gap recorded at [`openapi-gaps.md`](../openapi-gaps.md) § PROD-1703 owner avatar (BE follow-up tracked as [PROD-1726](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-1726)).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` — `_StatsLine.build` gates `CachedThumbnail` + the trailing `SizedBox(width: 6)` on `list.ownerAvatarUrl != null && isNotEmpty`.

### D80: Polygon corner glyph is two-tone Soko/Shade5 + Soko/Red, not lilac
- **Choice**: The bottom-right polygon glyph on every zine cover hero (97.5×84) is composed of two layers — a Soko/Shade5 (`#F1E5E6`) outer triangle and a Soko/Red (`#F68686`) inner pentagon with a 10 px rounded top-left corner — combined into `assets/images/icons/list/polygon-corner.svg`.
- **Design-doc rule it overrides** ([`docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md`](../designs/list-page-redesign.md) § 9.2 #36): "Two-tone **lilac** polygon — the implementer pulls the SVG via Figma MCP `get_design_context` against either node and saves under `assets/images/icons/list/polygon-corner.svg`."
- **Why we diverged**: the doc cited node IDs `6181:5837 / 6181:5838` from a pre-handover Figma snapshot. Those nodes don't exist in the current canonical file (`d4BCnyUHe2705J7ecQtaIH`); the live cover at `3989:7149` uses Polygon 3 (`3989:7151`, fill `#F1E5E6` Soko/Shade5) + Polygon 4 (`3989:7152`, fill `#F68686` Soko/Red). The polygon SVG matches what the live Figma renders, not what the doc described. Cached extracts at [`docs/ui/figma-cache/icons/polygon-3.svg`](figma-cache/icons/polygon-3.svg) + [`docs/ui/figma-cache/icons/polygon-4.svg`](figma-cache/icons/polygon-4.svg). Same gotcha pattern as PROD-1704's `6181:6172` → `6197:5954` rename — recorded as an anti-pattern in [`learnings/figma-external-designer-workflow.md`](../learnings/figma-external-designer-workflow.md).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_corner_glyph.dart` (overlays the polygon + "Pág. N" indicator on a hero photo).

### D81: Section dividers across the new list page (Zine + List view) use the shared dotted-line variant, not the scallop divider
- **Choice**: All between-section dividers on `/lists/:listId` — Zine cover (image ↔ map ↔ calendar ↔ suggestions), Zine item pages (item-card ↔ map ↔ calendar ↔ suggestions), AND List view (Sítios ↔ Eventos ↔ map ↔ calendar ↔ suggestions per § 8 of the design doc) — use the shared `DottedSectionDivider` (`lib/shared/widgets/dotted_section_divider.dart`). Soko/Shade4 dots at ~3.5 px pitch with 30 px vertical breathing, matching Discovery's `ShelfDivider` and the detail-page section-break pattern.
- **Design-doc rule it overrides** ([`docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md`](../designs/list-page-redesign.md) § 9.2 #28): "Reuse the existing `ScallopDivider` at `lib/shared/widgets/scallop_divider.dart`…" — the doc named the wrong widget; the rest of the app uses dotted dividers, not scallops, between major editorial sections.
- **Why we diverged**: dotted-line dividers are the existing visual language across Discovery, venue detail, and event detail. Mixing in the scalloped variant on the list page would have been a one-off that drifts from the rest of the app. The new `DottedSectionDivider` extracts the same `DottedLine`-wrapping pattern as Discovery's `ShelfDivider` so the rule is shared, not forked. PROD-1704's List view body (which originally shipped with `ScallopDivider` per the design doc text) was migrated as part of PROD-1703's rebase.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/dotted_section_divider.dart` (new in PROD-1703), consumed by `list_zine_cover_page.dart`, `list_zine_item_page.dart`, the per-page event/venue calendars, AND `list_view_mode_body.dart` (PROD-1704's body; the previous private `_SectionDivider` wrapper was deleted as part of the unification).

### D82: Zine hero "Adicionar foto" placeholder is shown to all viewers, not just owners
- **Choice**: When a zine item has no photo (cover or item page), the `ListZineHeroPlaceholder` (image-plus icon + "Adicionar foto" label, dimmed) renders for **everyone** — owners, non-owner authenticated viewers, and unauthenticated viewers. There is no owner-gating on the placeholder itself.
- **Why**: User photo upload is a contribute-anywhere feature in the product roadmap — every viewer should be able to add a photo to a venue or event, not just the list curator. The placeholder is a permanent advertisement of "you can fill this in here". The actual upload flow ships later in [PROD-1729](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-1729) (per-user-private uploads first) + [PROD-1730](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-1730) (moderation pipeline before user-uploaded photos go public). Until those land, the placeholder is purely visual; tapping it shows a "coming soon" SnackBar (a follow-up tweak in this same ticket — see PROD-1703 conversation 2026-05-08).
- **Trade-off**: non-owners see a "Add photo" affordance that isn't yet wired. Acceptable because the alternative (owner-only) would mean re-plumbing the surface when uploads ship — and it would silently teach users that "photo contribution = curator-only", which is the opposite of where we're heading.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_hero_placeholder.dart` (the widget) + `_CoverImageCard` / `_ItemCard` in `list_zine_cover_page.dart` / `list_zine_item_page.dart` (the call sites).

### D83: Cover hero falls back to first item photo before the placeholder
- **Choice**: When `UserList.coverImageUrl` is null/empty on the zine cover page, the cover hero falls back to the **first item with a non-empty `imageUrl`** before showing `ListZineHeroPlaceholder`. The full chain is: `coverImageUrl` → first `state.items[i].imageUrl` → placeholder. Item pages are unaffected — they keep the per-item placeholder when the specific item has no photo.
- **Source pattern**: matches the legacy `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_card.dart::_buildImageSection` chain. Lifted to the zine cover so the redesign behaves identically in the "user never picked a cover" common case. Lists whose items all have photos always render a real cover, even when the user hasn't picked one explicitly.
- **Why this matters**: shipping the v1 placeholder without the fallback chain made the placeholder appear on every list whose owner hadn't explicitly chosen a cover — even if every item had a photo. That violated the legacy expectation ("cover = curator pick OR first item photo") and made the placeholder feel like broken data instead of an invitation to contribute.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_cover_page.dart::ListZineCoverPage.build` — small inline fallback chain (3 lines) in the widget's `build` method, kept inline rather than extracted to a helper since it's a single-call-site rule.

### D84: First-view zine nav-hint is lifetime-once via SharedPreferences, not session-once
> **Superseded by D85 (2026-05-08).** Field testing showed the lifetime-once approach was too aggressive — users who dismissed the hint once stopped seeing it forever, including in lists they'd never opened. The replacement is per-mount with a directional + hover-aware twist; see D85.

- **Original choice**: The first-time onboarding chevrons (`ListZineNavHint`, faded chevron-left + chevron-right over the cover hero) appeared **once per device, lifetime-once**, then never again. Persistence flag was `zine_nav_hint_dismissed_v1` in SharedPreferences via `zineNavHintDismissedProvider`. Auto-fade after 4 s OR on first navigation gesture (whichever first).
- **Why it was lifetime initially**: once a user understands the tap-to-navigate gesture, they shouldn't need a refresher on every visit. Session-once would re-show the hint after every app restart, which feels like a bug.
- **What changed**: see D85 — counter-based, per-mount, directional, hover-aware. The lifetime-flag (`zine_nav_hint_dismissed_v1`) is no longer read.

### D85: Zine nav-hint is per-mount + directional + hover-aware (replaces D84)
- **Choice**: The nav-hint shows on every list-open for the first **two navigations within that mount**, then disappears for the rest of the open. No persistence — opening another list re-shows the hint. The provider is `zineNavHintCounterProvider` (`StateNotifierProvider.autoDispose<int>`); `ListZineView` `ref.watch`es it so the counter survives across cover↔item navigation within one mount but tears down when the user leaves the list. `ListZineView._goTo` calls `recordNavigation()` on every prev/next so cover and item navigation both count.
- **Directional**: each side renders only when there's a destination there:
  - cover with 0 items → no arrows
  - cover with 1+ items → right arrow only
  - middle item → both arrows
  - last item → left arrow only

  `ListZineNavHint` takes `showLeft` / `showRight` bools; cover and item pages compute them from `state.items.length` + `pageNumber`. Hint is mounted on item pages too — D84 was cover-only.
- **Hover-aware**: hovering either half of the hero highlights the corresponding arrow (subtle 1.12× scale + brighter bg + deeper shadow, 140 ms ease-out via `AnimatedScale` + `AnimatedContainer`). Click still navigates. Touch platforms (where `MouseRegion.onEnter` never fires) see the static badge unchanged. `ListZineHeroSlot` owns the hint mounting so it can plumb hover state from `MouseRegion`-wrapped tap-zones into `ListZineNavHint.leftHovered` / `rightHovered`; cover/item parents pass directional bools via the slot.
- **SVG asset**: `assets/images/icons/detail/back-arrow.svg` (the same asset used by detail-page back navigation), 180°-mirrored for the right chevron via `Transform.rotate`. `assets/images/icons/discovery/arrow-right.svg` was tried first but rejected — that one carries an 80×140 Soko/Shade5 rounded-rect background tile baked into the SVG (it's a list-card decoration, not a bare icon), which leaked through the circular badge.
- **Why per-mount, not lifetime**: see D84's superseded note. Per-mount is also debug-friendly — the team can dogfood the hint across multiple list-opens during testing.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_nav_hint.dart` (widget), `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_hero_slot.dart` (mounts the hint, owns hover state), `lib/features/lists/providers/zine_nav_hint_provider.dart` (counter).

### D86: Cover-selection sheet has two entry points; "Adicionar foto" tap is owner-only routing
