- **Choice**: Owners can open the cover-selection sheet from two places:
  1. **Cover hero "Adicionar foto" button tap** — when the cover slot shows the bordered placeholder (no displayable cover), tapping it opens `showChangeCoverSheet(...)` for owners; non-owners get the same coming-soon SnackBar item-page placeholders use.
  2. **Edit-list dialog "Escolher capa" row** — `EditListDialog` accepts an optional `onChooseCover` callback; when non-null, it renders a Soko-styled `OutlinedButton.icon` row under the form fields. `ListPageScreen._handleEdit` passes `() => showChangeCoverSheet(...)` so the button pops the dialog and opens the sheet.
- **Sheet is always available** — empty/single-photo states are handled inside the sheet via copy ("Ainda sem fotos…") rather than gating the entry point. This was a deliberate UX call: gating on `items.length >= 2` would hide the affordance from new lists where the user wants to upload their own photo first (the "Carregar a tua foto" tile inside the sheet fires the same coming-soon SnackBar today; PROD-1729 wires the real upload).
- **Item-page placeholder is unchanged** — items still show the SnackBar on tap. There's no item-level cover selection yet; future per-item upload lands under PROD-1729.
- **Implementation**: extracted the legacy `_ChangeCoverSheet` from `lib/features/lists/screens/list_view_screen.dart` (lines 1668–1929 as of pre-extraction) into the public, shared `lib/features/lists/widgets/change_cover_sheet.dart`. The legacy main-shell list view now delegates to the same shared helper, so PROD-1703 doesn't fork the cover-selection UI.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/change_cover_sheet.dart` (sheet + `showChangeCoverSheet` helper), `lib/features/lists/widgets/edit_list_dialog.dart` (edit dialog with optional `onChooseCover`), `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_cover_page.dart` (owner-vs-non-owner routing for the cover hero tap).

### D87: Zine has a per-page progress line above the card
- **Choice**: Above each zine card (cover and items) we render a thin segmented line — `items.length + 1` segments (cover + N items), separated by 4 px gaps. The first `activeUpTo` segments highlight in `sokoInk @ 70 %` while the remaining sit at `sokoInk @ 12 %`. Cover = 1 segment lit; item index `K` = `K + 2` segments lit (cumulative).
- **Why**: The original zine had no orientation indicator beyond the bottom-right "Pág. N" text inside each card — easy to miss. A subtle top-of-card progress line is a familiar pattern (Stories, slide decks, onboarding) and makes "where am I in this list?" answerable without scrolling.
- **PROD-1955 superseded the sizing and interactivity** — bars are now 2 px inactive / 8 px on active or hover with a 16 px tap target and 2 px corner radius; the line is tap-to-seek with a page-turn cascade and a hover tooltip. See [D124](#d124-zine-progress-line-is-tap-to-seek-with-a-forwardbackward-page-turn-cascade)–[D128](#d128-sokotooltip-is-the-design-system-tooltip--sokoshade2-background-paper-text). The visual concept (progress line above each card) is unchanged; the dimensions and behaviour above are the current state.
- **PROD-1738 originally mirrored a static copy onto the in-list detail** (`list_in_context_detail_screen.dart`). That mirror has since been removed — `ZineProgressLine`'s only call site today is `list_zine_view.dart`. Removal date wasn't traced; if you need the history, `git log -S 'ZineProgressLine' lib/features/lists/screens/list_in_context_detail_screen.dart`.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/zine_progress_line.dart` (the widget — `totalSections` + `activeUpTo`); composed by `list_zine_view.dart` above each card via `AnimatedBuilder(animation: _turnController, …)` so the bar tracks the page-flip animation at 60 FPS.

### D88: Tapping anywhere on a zine item card opens the in-list detail; "Vê mais" stays as the labeled affordance
- **Choice**: The whole `_ItemCard` (Soko/Shade5 container — title + description + hero + buttons + details + page indicator) wraps in a `GestureDetector(onTap: → in-list detail)`. The "Vê mais" `BtSqIco` is kept beside Guardar — the tap-anywhere card behavior is additive, not a replacement. Both routes go to the same destination (`/lists/$listId/(venues|events)/$id` → `ListInContextDetailScreen`). Guardar's own GestureDetector wins the gesture arena for taps inside that button, so tapping Guardar still saves and never accidentally opens detail.
- **Why both**: The button is the discoverable, labeled, and keyboard-tab-reachable affordance. The card-body tap is the affordance everybody actually uses — most users tap somewhere in the photo or near the title, not on a small button. Removing "Vê mais" entirely (one earlier iteration of this PR) made the only navigation feel hidden; keeping it both matches the legacy mental model and serves accessibility tools that walk button-to-button.
- **Trade-off**: small risk of accidental detail-opens when a user means to swipe or scroll. Mitigated by Flutter's gesture arena: `onHorizontalDragEnd` on the same card claims horizontal drags before `onTap` fires, and vertical scrolls are owned by the outer `SingleChildScrollView`.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_item_page.dart::_ItemCard.build` — outer `GestureDetector(behavior: HitTestBehavior.opaque, onTap: _openInListDetail, onHorizontalDragEnd: …)` wrapping the existing card `Container`. Cover doesn't get this — covers don't represent items.

### D89: Card-only horizontal swipe (mobile + desktop) replaces page-wide native + web-only card swipe
- **Choice**: Page navigation via horizontal swipe is wired on the card itself (cover and item) on **both** native and web. The previous split — page-wide `GestureDetector(onHorizontalDragEnd:)` on `ListZineView` for native + `kIsWeb`-gated card-scoped swipe — is gone. Map / calendar / suggestions sections **below** the card sit outside the swipe surface, so dragging horizontally on a map pin or calendar day no longer flips the page. Hero image is also no longer a navigation surface on item pages (its left/right tap zones — D85 / `ListZineHeroSlot` layer 2 — are skipped when both `onPrev` / `onNext` are null).
- **Why**: Two bugs the legacy layout produced — (1) horizontal-scrolling the venue's events calendar on mobile would unexpectedly flip to the next zine page, and (2) the hero on item pages was the navigation surface, which collided with the new "tap card body → open detail" rule (D88). Confining swipe to the card resolves both — the card is the page, and what's below the card is editorial content that gets scrolled, not paged.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart` (no longer wraps the body in a swipe `GestureDetector`); `list_zine_cover_page.dart::_CardSwipe` (renamed from `_MaybeWebSwipe`, now both-platforms); `list_zine_item_page.dart::_ItemCard` (`onHorizontalDragEnd` directly on the wrapping GestureDetector); `list_zine_hero_slot.dart` (layer 2 tap-zone Row gated on `onPrev != null || onNext != null`).

### D90: Desktop side arrows live in the page gutter via `OverlayPortal` + `LayerLink`, not inside the constrained content column
- **Choice**: On viewports `>= PageLayout.desktopBreakpoint`, hovering a zine card reveals two 32 px circular arrows (Soko/Shade5 bg) positioned 28 px outside the card's left/right edges — in the empty space between the 480 px content column and the screen edge. Implemented with `OverlayPortal(overlayLocation: OverlayChildLocation.rootOverlay)` so arrows paint and receive pointer events outside `PageContent`'s 480 px constraint. `CompositedTransformFollower` with `showWhenUnlinked: false` anchors each arrow to the card's `centerLeft` / `centerRight`. Visibility is hover-driven on the card; a 220 ms hide-debounce keeps arrows visible while the cursor transits the gutter card → arrow. `IgnorePointer(ignoring: !visible)` ensures invisible arrows don't latch their own hover state once shown. Touch viewports (`MouseRegion` never fires) see no arrows — they navigate via swipe (D89). On the cover, only the right arrow appears (no previous page); on the last item only the left.
- **Why outside the content column**: Earlier iterations placed the arrows inside the 15 px parent padding via `Stack(clipBehavior: Clip.none)` with negative `Positioned` offsets. They were visibly cropped at the screen edge because the parent's hit-test bounds clip pointer events outside `PageContent`'s 480 px maxWidth — arrows drew but couldn't be clicked. The desktop gutter is hundreds of pixels wide and otherwise unused; the only way to escape `PageContent`'s constraint without restructuring the shell is the root Overlay.
- **Why `rootOverlay` specifically**: With the default `nearestOverlay`, items mounted mid-`AnimatedSwitcher` transition (cover → item) failed to attach to whichever Overlay `OverlayPortal` resolved to from inside the FadeTransition + SlideTransition wrappers — arrows showed for the cover (no transition) but never for items. Routing to the root `MaterialApp` overlay sidesteps the lookup. See [`learnings/overlayportal-inside-animatedswitcher.md`](../learnings/overlayportal-inside-animatedswitcher.md).
- **Why `showWhenUnlinked: false`**: Without it an unlinked follower paints at its raw offset on the first frame after mount — the LEFT arrow would land at viewport `x = -arrowGap` (off-screen). With it, arrows hide until the leader's layer links, then snap into place.
- **D85 amendment — on-image nav-hint is mobile-only now**: On desktop the hover-revealed side arrows already provide discoverability; rendering both stacked a second right-arrow on top of the cover photo. The hint stays as a touch-only affordance for the first 2 navigations of every list-open.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/zine_side_arrows.dart` (the `ZineSideArrows` wrapper + overlay); composed by `list_zine_cover_page.dart` (right only, hidden when no items) and `list_zine_item_page.dart` (left + right, gated by `currentIndex` / `isLast`). Cover-page mobile-only nav-hint gating in `list_zine_cover_page.dart::build` reads `MediaQuery.of(context).size.width >= PageLayout.desktopBreakpoint`.

## Theme System (PROD-1738)

### D91: Force light mode app-wide; theme switcher hidden behind a kill switch
- **Choice**: `MaterialApp.themeMode` is hardcoded to `ThemeMode.light` and every user-facing theme toggle is hidden, gated by `EnvironmentConfig.themeSwitchingEnabled` (default `false`). Both `AppTheme.light` and `AppTheme.dark` `ThemeData` definitions stay in the codebase, and the `themeModeProvider` / `ThemeModeNotifier` keep working — a previously-persisted user preference in `SharedPreferences['theme_mode']` is honored automatically when the kill switch flips back on.
- **Figma divergence**: Figma has full dark-mode designs (cached under `docs/ui/figma-cache/screens/*-dark.md` for mobile-pt/br + desktop-pt/br). `/compare-ui` and `/visual-audit` should treat those screens as **intentionally not rendered** in the running app for now — not as missing implementation. When dark mode is ready and the kill switch is flipped, those screens become live targets again.
- **Why**: Dark mode currently looks broken; rather than rip it out (and lose ground we'd have to re-implement) we lock the app to light while we keep both themes alive. Hardcoded const, not a remote PostHog flag — flipping it requires a build either way (the actual fixes ship in code), so a remote flag would buy nothing. See [`features/feature-flags.md` § Compile-time flags](../features/feature-flags.md#compile-time-flags).
- **What's hidden**: 4 toggle surfaces — chat hero header pill, chat conversation header `IconButton`, `desktop_top_nav._ControlPill`, and `auth_header._ThemeToggle` (which covers login, register, OTP, forgot-password, activation-pending, hello, onboarding screens).
- **Where it lives**: flag at `lib/core/config/environment.dart::EnvironmentConfig.themeSwitchingEnabled`; `MaterialApp` binding at `lib/app.dart` (`themeMode: EnvironmentConfig.themeSwitchingEnabled ? ref.watch(themeModeProvider) : ThemeMode.light`); UI gates in `lib/features/chat/screens/chat_screen.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/desktop_top_nav.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/auth_header.dart`.

## List description + inline note editor (PROD-1782)

### D92: List description renders below the cover (zine) and at the top of the body (list view)
- **Choice**: The list's free-text `description` is brought back onto the redesigned list page. **Zine view**: rendered in the per-page slot below the progress line, **only on the cover page** (`currentIndex == 0`); item pages use the same slot for the item's `tip` (see D93). **List view**: rendered at the very top of the body, above the first section ("Sítios" / "Eventos" / etc.), with no scallop divider before the first section. Both surfaces use the shared `ListDescription` widget — Soko/Ink Mobile/B2 Reg, 3-line truncate with chevron-tap-to-expand.
- **Design-doc rule it overrides**: `docs/designs/list-page-redesign.md` doesn't mention the description at all — the redesign omitted it. We brought it back because users actively use the description to add context to lists they share, and stripping it on the new screen meant existing curated lists silently lost a chunk of their copy.
- **Why cover-only on zine**: every other Zine page is dedicated to a single item, and that page's slot belongs to the item's `tip`. The cover IS the list — it's the natural home for list-level metadata. Cross-fading the slot's content (description on cover, tip on item pages) is driven by the same `AnimatedSwitcher` keyed on `currentIndex` that handles the aux content swap below.
- **Editing**: the description stays read-only in this slot. Owners edit it via the existing list edit dialog (header pencil → `EditListDialog`). No inline edit on the description itself — that affordance is reserved for item notes (see D94).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_description.dart` (the widget); `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart` (cover branch of the per-page slot); `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_view_mode/list_view_mode_body.dart` (top of body, threaded into the children list before the section blocks).

### D93: Item notes are plain Soko/Ink prose with inline pencil + chevron-expand, not a tinted callout
- **Choice**: `ListItemNote` (the affordance for `UserListItem.tip`) renders as plain Soko/Ink Mobile/B2 Reg text — no tinted background, no left-rail border, no italic. Visually identical to `ListDescription`. For owners, an edit-pencil sits at the right of the row (top-aligned to the first text line); when the text overflows 3 lines, a chevron-down sits **left of the pencil** and toggles full-text expansion on tap. Empty state for owners is the existing "Deixar nota" pill.
- **Original direction**: an earlier pass rendered the populated note as a tinted callout — sokoInk @ 6 % bg, 2-px sokoInk @ 30 % left rail, italic Zalando Sans. That treatment was kept verbatim from the previous `_UserTipBlock` (modal-sheet era) — it read as "another card on the page", competing with the actual item cards (`_ItemCard`) and the "Sugerido para ti" section, and broke the new screen's "everything is prose on Soko/Paper" rhythm.
- **Why pencil right-most, chevron left of pencil**: the pencil is the discrete edit action; the chevron is a text-relationship affordance (expands the text). Putting the pencil at the right edge keeps "tap the right-most icon to edit" stable across short and long notes — the chevron only appears when needed, and inserts between text and pencil rather than displacing the pencil. Chevron next to text also visually links the affordance to its target.
- **Why the alignment trick**: a 28×28 `IconButton` in a `Row` with `CrossAxisAlignment.start` makes the row 28 px tall — taller than the ~17 px text line, leaving 11 px of dead space below the text. That 11 px shows up next to the following `DottedSectionDivider` as asymmetric whitespace (31 px above the dotted line vs 20 px below). Fix: outer `SizedBox(height: textLineHeight)` + `OverflowBox(maxHeight: 28)` + `Alignment.topCenter` on both the wrapper and the `IconButton`. Tap target stays 28×28, layout matches the text line. See [`learnings/iconbutton-vs-text-line-layout.md`](../learnings/iconbutton-vs-text-line-layout.md).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_item_note.dart::_TipCallout` (the populated state) and `_FirstLineAlignedAffordance` (the icon-alignment helper, used by both pencil and chevron). Mounted on three surfaces today: the zine view's per-page slot (item pages), `VenueInlineActions`, and `EventInlineActions`.

<!-- STALE(2026-05-12): The Cancelar / Guardar buttons described below were dropped in PROD-1783 — `ListItemNote._Editor` now auto-commits on focus-loss (no buttons), with the character counter rendered inside the panel at bottom-right via Stack overlay. See D96 for the auto-save pattern. -->
<!-- STALE(2026-09-01): The `_Editor` visual chrome below ("sokoInk @ 6 % fill, 1-px sokoInk border, 6-px radius") is superseded by D315 — the note editor is now the Soko/Paper chat-composer capsule (22-px radius, ink @ 12 % hairline) with an explicit rectangular Soko-Pink send button. Autosave-on-blur (D96) is retained. -->
### D94: Inline note editing replaces the modal `showLeaveNoteSheet` bottom sheet
- **Choice**: Tapping the pencil on a populated note (or the "Deixar nota" pill on an empty one) flips the same widget into an inline editor in place — `TextField` (sokoInk @ 6 % fill, 1-px sokoInk @ 30 % border idle / solid sokoInk on focus, max 280 chars) + Cancelar / Guardar row underneath. Save is `await ref.read(unifiedListProvider(listId).notifier).updateItemTip(...)`; on success the editor collapses back to the populated callout, on failure a `SnackBar` fires and the editor stays open. The previous `showLeaveNoteSheet` modal bottom sheet (`lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/leave_note_sheet.dart`) is **deleted**; both former call sites (`VenueInlineActions` and `EventInlineActions`) now mount `ListItemNote` directly.
- **Why inline**: the modal sheet was the only modal on the redesigned list/detail surfaces — every other affordance now lives inline (edit list dialog excepted, since it edits the list as a whole). The modal also took two extra taps for the common case ("change the wording of my own note") and obscured the rest of the page during edit. Inline editing keeps the page state visible and the action local.
- **Why the focused-border value matters**: the `TextField`'s default focus border picks up the theme's primary colour (Soko salmon). On a Soko/Paper page next to plain prose, a salmon outline reads as an error/highlight state. Explicit `enabledBorder`/`focusedBorder`/`disabledBorder`/`errorBorder`/`focusedErrorBorder` overrides ship sokoInk @ 30 % idle and solid sokoInk on focus.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_item_note.dart::_Editor` (the inline editor). `_ListItemNoteState` toggles between `_TipCallout` (view) and `_Editor` (edit) via a single `_editing` flag.

### D95: Page-level edit mode replaces the modal `EditListDialog` on the redesigned list page
- **Choice**: Tapping the Edit pencil on the new list page (admin-flag scope) flips a single `editMode: bool` flag on `UnifiedListState` instead of opening `EditListDialog`. The page itself becomes editable: title, description, cover, and per-item notes all switch to inline editing affordances; items render with drag handles + delete buttons; the action row + stats line + IG-share badge hide; the chrome back arrow hides and Done appears in its trailing slot. `EditListDialog.show(...)` is gone from `list_page_screen.dart` (the legacy `ListViewScreen` still uses it for non-admins until cutover).
- **Why state, not modal**: the modal forced users to leave the page to make changes — they couldn't see the items they were sorting, the cover they were changing, or the notes they were rewriting. Edit mode keeps the entire list visible while it's being edited. The cost is a more complex page-level state machine, but the editable surfaces all share lifecycle (enter → mutate → exit) so the complexity is bounded.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/providers/unified_list_provider.dart` (`editMode`, `enterEditMode()`, `exitEditMode()`, `commitListUpdate(...)`), `lib/features/lists/screens/list_page_screen.dart::_handleEdit / dispose`, `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` (action-row collapse), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/pinned_page_chrome.dart` (back-arrow hide + Done button). PROD-1783.

### D96: Every editable surface in edit mode auto-commits on focus-loss — no Save / Cancel buttons
- **Choice**: Title (in `PinnedPageChrome`), description (`ListDescription` editable variant), and item notes (`ListItemNote` autoExpand variant) all save when the user moves focus away from the field — no explicit Save / Cancel pair. The title editor commits via `notifier.commitListUpdate(name: text)`, description via the same with `description:`, notes via `notifier.updateItemTip(itemId, text)`. Each compares against a `_lastCommittedText` snapshot so a focus-loss with no change is a no-op. Drafts that are mid-edit when the user taps Done get flushed because Done's `onTap` calls `FocusManager.instance.primaryFocus?.unfocus()` before `exitEditMode()`.
- **Why no Cancel**: legacy parity ("Editar elementos" in `ListViewScreen` had no Cancel either — every mutation committed immediately), AND the auto-save pattern was already in use on PROD-1782's description display (focus-loss commit). Adding a Cancel button would require buffering edits across multiple surfaces (title, description, ~N notes) and either rolling them back atomically on Cancel or asking the user to confirm — both are heavy for what the user is actually doing (small inline tweaks). The cost: in-flight typing that hasn't blurred yet is silently discarded when edit mode exits if the user doesn't tap Done (e.g., navigating away). Mitigation: Done's unfocus-then-exit chain flushes all drafts; `ListPageScreen.dispose()` exits edit mode but doesn't try to flush drafts (the screen is unmounting and the per-field state is gone anyway).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_description.dart::_initEditable / _onFocusChanged`, `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_item_note.dart::_onFocusChanged / _onBlur`, `lib/features/discovery/widgets/pinned_page_chrome.dart::_onTitleFocusChanged`.

### D97: Done button lives in the pinned chrome, gated on `pendingMutations` so users can't navigate away mid-save
- **Choice**: The edit-mode exit affordance ("Done", check icon, 40×40 Soko/Ink @ 6 % circular — matching the other action buttons) lives in the chrome's trailing slot (`PinnedPageChrome._DoneButton`), NOT in the scrolling header. Stays visible through scroll, mirrors the back-arrow's leading position when not in edit mode. While `state.pendingMutations > 0`, the check icon is replaced by a 14-px `CircularProgressIndicator`, the cursor becomes `wait`, and `onTap` is `null` — the user cannot exit edit mode until every in-flight save settles.
- **Why pendingMutations is a counter, not a boolean**: title + description + N item notes can all save concurrently when Done's `unfocus` chain blurs the active field (only one field is focused at a time, but a previous field's save may still be in flight from earlier blur). A boolean would re-enable Done after the first save settles, letting the user exit while others race. The counter increments on every API mutation start and decrements on settle; Done only re-enables when the counter hits zero. Wrapped via `_withPendingMutation<T>(Future<T> Function())` so the increment/decrement pair is bulletproof against thrown exceptions.
- **Why pin in chrome instead of action row**: the header (and its action row) scrolls with the body. In a long list, Done would scroll off-screen — users would lose the only exit affordance. Pinning in the chrome (already a `Stack` overlay above the scrollable) keeps it always reachable.
- **Where it lives**: `pinned_page_chrome.dart::_DoneButton`. The counter is `UnifiedListState.pendingMutations` (incremented by `_withPendingMutation` wrapping `commitListUpdate`, `updateItemTip`, `setCoverFromUrl`, `removeItem`, `reorderItems`).

### D98: Edit mode forces List view and hides every read-only chrome element
- **Choice**: Entering edit mode (`notifier.enterEditMode()`) atomically flips `viewMode = ListViewMode.list` AND `editMode = true`. The cover row + reorderable items + inline editors only make sense in List view (the cover row needs a horizontal layout; Zine view's paginated cards don't support reorder). While edit mode is active: chrome back arrow → 40-px transparent spacer; stats line ("Por ti · N págs. · save_count") + IG-share badge → hidden; action row (Toggle / Share / Add / Edit) → hidden entirely (Done lives in the chrome instead). The header collapses to just the wrapper's bottom padding.
- **Why hide everything**: the page's read-only metadata is irrelevant while editing. Hiding it (a) removes visual noise so the editable affordances read clearly, (b) prevents accidental Share / Follow / nav-back interactions that would discard in-flight drafts (the back arrow's hide is especially load-bearing — the only exit is Done, which flushes drafts). View-mode toggle stays hidden so the user can't accidentally jump to Zine view, which would dismount the editable surfaces.
- **On exit**: `exitEditMode()` flips `editMode = false` but leaves `viewMode = list` — Done doesn't restore the user's previous Zine/List choice. Re-entering the list page from elsewhere lands on the default Zine view (`ListPageScreen.initState` forces it on every mount).
- **Where it lives**: `unified_list_provider.dart::enterEditMode`, `list_page_header.dart` (action-row + stats-line guards), `pinned_page_chrome.dart` (back-arrow + Done trailing slot).

### D99: `ReorderableListView` drag proxy uses a Soko/Ink @ 6 % panel matching the other edit-mode chrome
- **Choice**: A custom `proxyDecorator` on the edit-mode `ReorderableListView.builder` replaces Flutter's default white-elevated Material card with a Soko/Ink @ 6 % filled rectangle + 1-px solid Soko/Ink border + 6-px radius + 8-px internal padding. The padding sits INSIDE the border (between the panel edge and the row) so the dragged proxy grows around the row rather than squeezing the content tighter.
- **Why custom**: the default white-card-on-Soko-Paper looks like a foreign Material widget on a non-Material page. The new chrome matches the cover row, description panel, and note editor exactly — drag reads as "this is one of the same surfaces, slightly emphasised". Elevation is intentionally zero so the proxy stays flat with the rest of the page.
- **Why padding inside the border**: ReorderableListView gives the proxy loose constraints from the overlay layer, so the `Container` grows by the inner padding rather than crushing the child. The visible result: dragged item is wider/taller than its slot, with breathing room between the border and the row content.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_view_mode/list_view_mode_body.dart::_buildEditMode` → `proxyDecorator`. See [`learnings/flutter-reorderable-listview-proxy-decorator.md`](../learnings/flutter-reorderable-listview-proxy-decorator.md) for the gotcha (stripping the default Material wrapper requires re-wrapping in a transparent Material).

### D100: Guest mode is web-only — native builds gate the entire app behind sign-in
**Reversed by [D190](#d190) on 2026-05-30** — kept here as historical context for the rationale, but the rule no longer holds. Native builds now mirror the web guest experience after Apple flagged the login wall under Guideline 5.1.1(v).

- **Choice**: The "Continue as guest" CTA on `/login` is hidden on iOS / Android (`if (kIsWeb) { ... }`). The router redirect bounces any unauthenticated native visit — including `/chat`, `/`, `/lists`, `/lists/:id` deep links, and `/discovery/lists/new` — to `/login`, preserving the matched URL via `returnUrlProvider` so the post-OAuth flow restores the destination. Web keeps the full guest experience: splash → guest lands on `/chat`, and the three auth-gated nav targets (`/`, `/lists`, `/discovery/lists/new`) render `GuestGateScreen` in place of their real content so the home grid's auth-only 401-storming endpoints never fire for unauthenticated viewers.
- **Why web-only**: the mobile app is the conversion surface — every native session that doesn't convert to a signed-in account is dead weight (no notifications, no saved sessions, no recommendations). On web, anonymous discoverability still matters (shared list URLs, search-engine traffic, casual browsing) so guest mode pays for itself. Splitting on `kIsWeb` (rather than a feature flag) keeps the rule strict and load-bearing: native builds simply can't see guest UI.
- **Why `/chat` for web guests, not `/`**: the home grid's data fetches are auth-only (`/feed/near-you`, `/feed/highlighted`, `/recommendations/{daily,weekly}`, `/users/me/*`). Landing a guest on `/` produced a console full of 401s and an empty gray page. `/chat` is the only route fully usable without auth — it's where the conversion ask actually happens.
- **Why a gate page instead of redirecting `/`, `/lists`, `/discovery/lists/new`**: the bottom nav stays visible on the gate (rendered under `DiscoveryShell`) so Conversar and the Menu drawer remain reachable. Redirecting would have either dumped the user on `/chat` (no signal about *why* the tap didn't work) or `/login` (too aggressive — they didn't ask to sign in, they tapped Zines). The gate explains the situation and offers two CTAs ("Falar comigo" → `/chat`, "Iniciar sessão" → login with returnUrl preserved).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` (redirect logic + `_GuestGated` wrapper), `lib/features/auth/screens/guest_gate_screen.dart` (the gate widget), `lib/features/auth/screens/login_screen.dart` (`kIsWeb`-gated guest button). Cross-cuts the entire entry flow — see [`features/guest-mode.md`](../features/guest-mode.md) for the full map.

### D101: Discovery action-bar create-zine button matches the magnifier (Soko/Shade5), not Soko/Yellow
- **Choice**: The `BtSqIco` for the "Cria nova zine" pill in the discovery action bar uses `BtSqIcoVariant.shade5` (Soko/Shade5 `#F1E5E6` fill), matching the magnifier `_CircleIconButton` to its left. Reverts the PROD-1849 cutover's switch to `BtSqIcoVariant.yellow`.
- **Why revert**: visually the yellow pill broke the rhythm with the magnifier button — they read as two distinct chips rather than a matched pair, even though they sit side by side in the same row. The action bar's location pill (right) keeps the Soko/Pink `selected` variant for emphasis on the current scope, but the create-zine button has no per-state emphasis — it's just a navigation CTA. Matching the magnifier (also a navigation CTA without per-state emphasis) makes the bar read as "two grouped neutral actions + one emphasised state pill" instead of "three competing colours".
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_action_bar.dart`. The `BtSqIcoVariant.yellow` variant still exists in `lib/shared/widgets/bt_sq_ico.dart` for any future use, just no longer wired here.

### D102: Auth-gated bottom-nav items render at 60 % opacity for web guests — full opacity stays on tappable items
- **Choice**: When the viewer is unauthenticated on the web build (`!ref.watch(isAuthenticatedProvider)`), the three auth-gated bottom-nav items (Zines / Início / Criar) wrap in `Opacity(opacity: 0.6, ...)`. Conversar and Menu stay at 100 % opacity. Tap targets are unchanged — tapping any of the three still navigates to its route, where `GuestGateScreen` renders.
- **Why dim, not hide**: hiding the three items would make the nav read as a two-item bar (Conversar + Menu) — the user wouldn't know more sections exist. Dimming preserves the affordance ("there's more here") while signalling "you'll need to sign in." It matches the iOS / Android system convention for "feature exists but requires X" (greyed icons in tab bars, dimmed cells in lists).
- **Why 60 %**: brighter than `disabled` Material defaults (38 %) but visibly dimmed against the 100 % active items. Empirical tap-affordance check: at 60 % the icons + labels are still high-contrast on Soko/Paper, and the dim reads as "secondary" not "broken."
- **Native is moot**: native guests never see the bottom nav — the router bounces them to `/login` first. The check is intentionally gated on auth state alone (not `kIsWeb` AND auth state) because that simpler form is correct on every platform: native authenticated users see full opacity, and the dim path is unreachable on native.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_bottom_nav.dart` (`dimAuthGated` field threaded into `_NavBar`).

---

## DS Bottom-Sheet Polish (PROD-1866)

### D103: Bottom-sheet scrim is Soko/Ink @ 30 %, not the Flutter default black54
- **Choice**: `showBottomSheetWithHiddenNav` (and `showDraggableSheetWithHiddenNav` by inheritance) default `barrierColor` to `AppColors.sokoInkSecondary` — the precomputed Soko/Ink @ 30 % token (`Color(0x4D44131D)`). Per-call callers can still override via the existing parameter.
- **Why**: The Flutter default `black54` is a flat neutral grey that reads as "system Material" against the warm sokoPaper sheet fill — the two surfaces clash in tint. Soko/Ink @ 30 % is the same warm dark ink that's used everywhere else in the DS (secondary text, dividers, button outlines), so the scrim feels like part of the Soko visual language instead of a generic chrome layer.
- **Why not a new token**: `sokoInkSecondary` already exists (PROD-1804 introduced it as "Soko/Ink 30 %, named `Secondary` not `Ink30`"). Reusing it keeps the palette flat — no need for a parallel `sokoScrim` alias.
- **Coverage**: Applies to every sheet that flows through the wrapper — location-scope picker, country picker, city picker, add-to-list, list action menu, login prompt, import flows, profile sheet, browser instructions, etc. Raw `showModalBottomSheet` callers (e.g. the Maps-app launchers) are unaffected; if they need to converge later, that's a separate cleanup.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/utils/bottom_sheet_utils.dart` (the wrapper default).

### D104: Selectable rows in DS sheets get a soft-pink (`sokoLight3`) hover — web / desktop only
- **Choice**: The country picker (`_CountryRow`), city picker (`_CityRow`), and city picker's "Remove city" shortcut (`_RemoveCityRow`) wire `hoverColor: AppColors.sokoLight3` (the same warm pink the add-to-list sheet uses for its selected-row tint) onto their `InkWell`. Tap behaviour is unchanged. Touch devices receive no hover (`InkWell.hoverColor` only renders on pointer-equipped devices — by Flutter's design).
- **Why pink, not grey**: Soko's DS uses `sokoLight3` as the "interactive emphasis" tint across every redesigned sheet (selected row in add-to-list, header banner). Hover sharing that palette reinforces "tappable surfaces look pink-tinted in Soko" instead of introducing a parallel neutral hover treatment.
- **Why same colour as selected (not lighter)**: in the pickers, selection is shown via a `check_rounded` icon + bold weight, not via a background tint — there's no visual conflict between hover and selected. In add-to-list, selected IS the pink background, so its rows don't expose `hoverColor` at all. The two patterns coexist cleanly.
- **Mechanics gotcha**: each `InkWell` must be wrapped in `Material(color: Colors.transparent, …)` because `DSSheetShell` paints its sokoPaper fill via a plain `Container` decoration — the hover ink renders onto the nearest Material ancestor, and without an explicit wrapper the wash gets painted under the Container fill and stays invisible. See [`learnings/inkwell-hover-invisible-under-container-fill.md`](../learnings/inkwell-hover-invisible-under-container-fill.md) for the paint-order trace.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/country_picker_sheet.dart`, `lib/features/lists/widgets/city_picker_sheet.dart` (both `_CityRow` and `_RemoveCityRow`).

### D105: The city picker's "Remove city" shortcut names the current selection — "Remove Lisbon", not "Remove city"
- **Choice**: When the city picker opens with a `preselectedCityId`, the inline shortcut row reads `searchScopeRemoveCityNamed` ("Remove {cityName}" / "Remover {cityName}") instead of the generic `searchScopeRemoveCity`. The name is threaded from the location-scope picker (`_openCityPicker` passes `city.name` alongside `city.id`) into `showCityPickerSheet(... preselectedCityName: ...)` and read by `_RemoveCityRow`.
- **Why**: the row used to read "Remove city" / "Remover cidade" — generic to the point of feeling disconnected from the actual selection. When the user knows they have Lisbon picked, "Remove Lisbon" reads as a concrete action; "Remove city" reads as a generic option. Same pattern as gmail-style "Remove inbox" → "Remove [label name]".
- **Defensive fallback**: if `preselectedCityName` is null (shouldn't happen in our flow — `preselectedCityId` and `preselectedCityName` are populated together from the same `SearchScopeCountryCity.city`), the row falls back to the legacy `searchScopeRemoveCity` copy. Semantics / screen-reader label is unchanged (still the generic "Remove city") because that reads better outside the visual context.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/city_picker_sheet.dart` (`_RemoveCityRow`), `lib/features/lists/widgets/location_scope_picker_sheet.dart` (`_openCityPicker` passes the name through), `lib/l10n/intl_en.arb` / `intl_pt.arb` / `intl_pt_BR.arb` (new `searchScopeRemoveCityNamed` key with `{cityName}` placeholder).

## List Page — Owner Edit Dropdown (PROD-1883)

### D106: New shared `DSDropdownMenu` design-system component
- **Choice**: extracted the list-page owner edit menu (Editar zine / Tornar zine pública/privada / Apagar lista) into a reusable widget at `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/dropdown/ds_dropdown_menu.dart`. Public API: `DSDropdownMenu<T>({child, items, onSelected, offset, semanticLabel})` + `DSDropdownMenuItem<T>({value, icon, label, destructive})`. Internally wraps `GlassmorphicPopupMenu` with locked Soko styling — every call site gets the same surface, radius, border, hover, and row chrome.
- **Why**: the dropdown pattern is reused-shaped (other owner action menus, sort/filter menus, etc.). Locking the visual identity in one widget means future tweaks (radius, hover color, typography) land in one place instead of being re-implemented per page.
- **Locked styling** (matches Figma `6353:31759` "Overlay/Edit"): `AppColors.sokoShade5` surface (#F1E5E6 solid), 1 px `sokoInk @ 8%` border, 6 px radius, no backdrop blur, `sokoPaper @ 0.8` per-row hover wash, 14 px Lucide icon + 8 px gap + Mobile/B2 Reg label, `AppColors.sokoRed` for destructive rows.
- **Position behaviour**: inherited from `GlassmorphicPopupMenu` — left-side triggers auto-align left, right-side triggers auto-align right. Default `offset: Offset(0, 8)` sits the panel 8 px below the trigger's bottom edge.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/dropdown/ds_dropdown_menu.dart` (top-level DS widget). `GlassmorphicPopupMenu` (`heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/glassmorphic_popup_menu.dart`) stays as the low-level primitive — still used by `locale_picker.dart` for its frosted-glass variant.

### D107: Menu icons use Lucide instead of the Figma menu's bespoke SVGs
- **Choice**: `LucideIcons.pencil_line` / `LucideIcons.eye` / `LucideIcons.trash_2` for the three menu rows. The trigger button continues to use `LucideIcons.pencil`. No SVG assets are vendored for these icons.
- **Why**: the Figma menu's `Icon/See` (eye) has a natural viewBox of 13.83 × 10.33 — wider than tall. Rendering at `width: 14, height: 14` with `BoxFit.contain` letterboxes it to ~14 × 10.45, which reads visibly compressed next to the (taller) pencil and trash icons in the same menu. Lucide glyphs are designed for uniform visual weight at the same `size:`, so all three menu rows render at the same perceived height with no per-icon adjustment.
- **Why not normalise the Figma SVGs**: tried first; the eye's natural aspect ratio means any centred 14 × 14 viewBox leaves visible vertical padding around the eye relative to the others. Scaling the eye up to fill the height overflows horizontally; cropping distorts the shape. Lucide gives the desired uniformity for free.
- **Trade-off accepted**: minor design fidelity loss vs Figma's literal asset choice in exchange for visual consistency across menu rows. Trigger button + chrome buttons across the app already use Lucide, so this is also a single-source-of-icons win.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` (`_OwnerEditMenu` build).

### D108: "Editar zine" label (not the Figma's "Editar elementos")
- **Choice**: the first dropdown item reads `Editar zine` / `Edit zine` — ARB key `listActionEditZine`. The Figma source uses `Edit elements`.
- **Why**: the page-level edit mode the action triggers lets the owner change **both** items (elements) **and** list-level details (cover, title, description). "Edit elements" reads as too narrow — a user who taps it expecting only to edit items but discovers they can also rename the list / change the cover reads the label as a misdirection. "Editar zine" is the umbrella that matches what edit mode actually does.
- **Defensive non-rename**: the older ARB key `listActionEditElements` is left in the file (also unused by current code) so the new key doesn't accidentally collide with anything that landed in flight on develop. Cleanup of the unused key is deferred.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/l10n/intl_en.arb`, `intl_pt.arb`, `intl_pt_BR.arb` (the `listActionEditZine` keys), `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` (the menu item).

### D109: Discovery chat-input typeahead accent strip uses detail-page bg colors, not tag colors
- **Choice**: The 3 px vertical accent strip on each typeahead result row uses the row's eventual destination's **page background** color — `AppColors.sokoEvent` (green) for event rows, `AppColors.sokoVenue` (blue) for venue rows, `AppColors.sokoListAccent` (yellow alias) for zine rows. First implementation used the existing `sokoEventAccent` / `sokoVenueAccent` tokens — the same blue/purple chips on chat venue/event cards — but a quick user review pushed back: the strip should signal "tap here → land on a page in this color," not just "this is the category accent we use elsewhere."
- **Lovable / Figma**: no direct mockup of this overlay; pattern is derived from the Linear ticket screenshot (a typeahead row over a paper bg).
- **Why**: prepares the eye for the imminent navigation. The user sees a blue strip → expects a blue venue detail page → gets one. Using tag colors here would have been correct visually-against-the-chat-card-system but wrong visually-against-the-overlay-the-user-is-actually-in.
- **Zines special case**: list detail pages use `sokoPaper` (the same as the typeahead bg), so there's no detail-page bg to mirror. `sokoListAccent` is aliased to `sokoYellow` as an explicit accent for this row type — visually distinct from event-green and venue-blue. Comment in `typeahead_result_row.dart` documents why this row doesn't follow the "match detail bg" rule.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/typeahead_result_row.dart` (`typeaheadAccentColorFor`), `lib/core/theme/app_colors.dart` (new `sokoListAccent` token). PROD-1909.

## Zine Cover Aspect Ratios

### D110: Zine covers are designed for two aspect ratios — 3:4 (canonical shelves) and 4:5 (featured + list page cover + all list-feature surfaces)
- **Choice**: The zine cover artwork (the recipe-driven `ListZineCover` widget — colour/texture + title + logo + optional photo) is designed to render in **exactly two portrait aspect ratios across the app**:
  - **3:4 (0.75)** — canonical discovery shelves (Recommended, Trending, City Guides, Yours, Editor Picks). `CanonicalShelfCard.imageWidth/imageHeight = 160.418 × 213`.
  - **4:5 (0.80)** — featured/"Em destaque" discovery shelf, the big list-page cover, the lists-hub `ListCard`, the edit-cover-row thumbnail, and the change-cover-sheet items grid. `HighlightedShelfCard.imageWidth/imageHeight = 195 × 242`.
- **Why two ratios (not one)**: the featured shelf intentionally renders chunkier, more poster-like cards than the canonical shelves to draw the eye — the 6 % ratio difference (0.75 → 0.80) is subtle but visible at the cards' actual rendered widths and carries the "this row is special" signal without resorting to colour or chrome treatment. Collapsing to one ratio would either flatten the featured shelf's emphasis (if everything became 3:4) or make canonical-shelf rows feel taller without justification (if everything became 4:5).
- **Marketing implication (load-bearing)**: any uploaded cover photo, designer-supplied artwork, partner-provided imagery, or marketing asset destined for a zine cover MUST be composed so it reads well at **both 3:4 and 4:5 crops** of the same source. The same recipe renders at both ratios across the app — a venue/event/list cover photo cropped for 4:5 will be re-cropped (centred) for the 3:4 shelf surfaces and vice versa. Practical guidance for marketing: shoot/compose with the subject centred and key content within the inner ~80 % vertical band so neither crop loses it. Avoid bottom-anchored compositions (the 4:5 frame has more vertical room than 3:4, so the bottom of a 4:5 asset is the first thing to disappear in the 3:4 crop).
- **List-page hero — the SE-class content floor (PROD-1920)**: the list-page pager (`list_zine_view.dart:230-233`) sizes the cover/item card as `max(width × 5/4, kListZinePageMinHeight)` with `kListZinePageMinHeight = 400`. On every viewport ≥ ~320 px wide the formula resolves to true 4:5; on SE-class phones (≤ ~320 wide → card ~290 wide) the 400-px floor kicks in to preserve the item card's `Expanded` hero photo `minHeight: 150` against the worst-case content stack (title + 2-line description + 40 px buttons + address + hours + paddings ≈ 375 px). At the floor the cover renders at ~0.73 ratio, visually indistinguishable from 4:5 = 0.80. The pager hosts both the cover (page 0) and item cards (pages 1..N) at the same dimensions — `TurnPageView` requires uniform page sizing for the flip animation, so the cover ratio dictates the item-card ratio. The floor is the trade-off.
- **Hub `ListCard` (PROD-1920)**: wraps `ListZineCover` in `AspectRatio(4/5)`. Matches the list-page hero so the hub-card → cover-hero tap reads as a continuous visual transition. Replaces the legacy 5:4 landscape (which stretched the portrait zine-cover composition into a wider-than-tall frame). The mirror surfaces — `social_proof_row.dart` (the hub-style horizontal scroll for non-logged-in users), `import_progress_card.dart` (in-hub placeholder during list import), `lists_hub_screen.dart` horizontal-scroll `scrollHeight` formula — track the same 4:5 ratio.
- **Edit-cover thumb + change-cover items grid (PROD-1920)**: edit-cover-row thumb (`list_edit_cover_row.dart:28-29`) is 88 × 110 (4:5); the change-cover-sheet items grid (`change_cover_sheet.dart:472`) uses `childAspectRatio: 4/5`. The items grid renders item *photos* (`CachedImage`, not `ListZineCover`) — 4:5 aligns the picker preview with the crop that photo will actually paint at when chosen as a cover. PROD-1918 batch 3 then dropped the edit-row thumb's `showTitle: false, showLogo: false` overrides so the preview now composes the full title-block + logo (the user is editing the cover; they want to see what it looks like).
- **Single recipe resolver (PROD-1918 batch 3)**: every cover surface — zine view, edit row, change-cover sheet preview, lists hub `ListCard`, all 7 discovery shelves, search results — now derives its `ZineCoverRecipe` through one factory: **`ZineCoverRecipe.fromUserList(list, {itemLookup?})`** (`zine_cover_recipe.dart`). Surfaces with the full item collection (zine view, edit row, change-cover sheet) pass an `itemLookup`; surfaces without items (shelves, hub cards, search) omit it. The factory honours `cover_type` and `cover_item_id` from the BE for both groups — replacing the legacy `forListSummary` + the inline `fromFields` duplication in `list_card.dart` that previously forced `cover_type=background_color` and sneaked `previewImages[0]` in via the legacy-photo channel (the shelf-vs-zine divergence reported as the PROD-1918 batch 3 trigger).
  - **"Every cover surface uses `fromUserList`" is a snapshot, not a standing property (verified 2026-09-14, PROD-4391)**: `fromUserList` is a convenience wrapper; the single resolver underneath is `ZineCoverRecipe.fromFields`. Every endpoint that returns a slimmer, purpose-built zine payload (not a `UserList`) writes its own adapter and calls `fromFields` directly — **9 such adapters on `develop` today**: library, feed v2, the event + venue "appears in" shelves, onboarding zines, profiling smart lists, discover-people, provenance panel, and the loading sentinel in `list_zine_cover_page.dart`. Each re-lists the arguments by hand, and `fromFields`' `showTitle`/`showTexture`/`showLogo` default to `true` — so an adapter that omits one compiles and renders decorated with no warning. Two instances so far: PROD-3888 (onboarding dropped the item-image source) and PROD-4391 (library dropped the three flags). See [`learnings/cover-adapter-that-omits-a-default-true-flag-paints-chrome-silently.md`](../learnings/cover-adapter-that-omits-a-default-true-flag-paints-chrome-silently.md).
  - **Closed by PROD-1932**: `ListIdentityOut.cover_item_image_url` now ships from the BE — a server-resolved, already-proxied image URL for the `cover_item_id`. `fromUserList` consumes it directly (prefers it over `itemLookup`, with `itemLookup` retained as a defensive fallback for the optimistic-update window where the BE-derived field is stale). Every surface — shelves, hub cards, search results — now renders the same item-image cover as the zine view for `cover_type=item_image` lists. PROD-1918 batch 6.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/canonical_shelf_card.dart` (3:4 constants), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/highlighted_shelf_card.dart` (4:5 constants), `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_cover.dart` (the shared recipe-driven cover widget), `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart` (list-page pager — `max(width × 5/4, 400)`), `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_item_page.dart` (`kListZinePageMinHeight = 400`), `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_card.dart` (hub card — 4:5), `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_edit_cover_row.dart` (88 × 110), `lib/features/lists/widgets/change_cover_sheet.dart` (items grid 4:5; live 80 × 100 preview), `lib/features/lists/widgets/import_progress_card.dart` (4:5 progress / failed states), `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (horizontal-scroll `scrollHeight` formula), `lib/shared/widgets/social_proof_row.dart` (4:5 mirror), `lib/features/lists/utils/zine_cover_recipe.dart` (single `fromUserList` resolver — PROD-1918 batch 3).

## Add-Items Flow (PROD-1852)

### D111: Add-items flow is a full-page route, not a bottom sheet
- **⚠️ Superseded by [D134](#d134-add-items-flow-is-a-bottom-sheet-after-all--reverts-d111) (2026-05-18).** The full-page route forced the list page underneath (`ListZineView` + cover `MapboxMapPlatform` + the PROD-1955 zine pager) to dispose + remount on each round trip. That mount churn drove a render-time exception storm (`Future.delayed → _container!` null-deref, then a per-frame `markNeedsLayout` `_debugCanPerformMutations` assertion cascade) that froze the UI on the first add to an empty zine. The flow reverted to a `DSSheetShell` modal bottom sheet that overlays the list page; the visual treatment from PROD-1852 was preserved verbatim — only the outer chrome changed. See [bugfixes/pr-432-zine-add-item-freeze.md](../bugfixes/pr-432-zine-add-item-freeze.md) and the PROD-1978 follow-up for the deeper map-side work.
- **Choice**: tapping "+" on an owner-mode zine pushes `/lists/:listId/add-items` (full-page route under `DiscoveryShell`). Replaces `showSearchAddToListSheet` (the legacy `DraggableScrollableSheet` bottom drawer). The page reuses every behavior of the sheet — Sítios/Eventos scope toggle, search debounce, URL-paste mode (PROD-1325), optimistic add-to-list mutation — but renders them in a full-bleed scrollable column under the standard `PinnedPageChrome` instead of a partial-height sheet over the underlying list page.
- **Why**: the redesign brief (Figma frame `9300`) treats add-items as a top-level navigation destination, not a transient overlay. A full page (a) gives the search-results list the full viewport height (more items visible at once on mobile), (b) lets the URL bar reflect the user's location (`/lists/abc/add-items`) which matters for browser-back and deep-linking on web, (c) lines up with the "everything is a page" pattern the rest of the redesigned list flow uses (zine cover → item detail → add-items are three sibling pages, not a page + two sheets), and (d) removes the awkward double-scroll (sheet over an already-scrollable page) that the legacy `DraggableScrollableSheet` introduced.
- **Trade-off accepted**: the user loses the at-a-glance "this is happening on top of my list" cue the sheet gave them. The PROD-1852 brief argues that the back-arrow + the persistent route segment carry that context well enough — the user knows where they came from because the route is explicit. We are NOT going to support a "swipe-down-to-dismiss" gesture on the page because that's a sheet idiom; back-arrow + native browser-back are the dismissal paths.
- **Cutover**: `_HeaderActionButton` for "+" in `list_page_header.dart` now calls `context.push('/lists/${state.list?.id ?? widget.listId}/add-items')` (still the resolved UUID per the slug/UUID rule). `showSearchAddToListSheet` and `SearchAddToListSheet` widget remain in the codebase for the non-admin legacy list view (`list_view_screen.dart`) but are unreferenced from the redesigned owner flow. They will be deleted in the PROD-1700 admin-gate cutover.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/screens/add_items_to_list_page.dart` (the page, ~1232 LOC verbatim port of the sheet logic), route declared in `heyl_app/lib/core/router/app_router.dart`, push wiring in `heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart`, `pinned_page_chrome.dart`, `list_page_screen.dart`, and `list_page_header.dart`.

### D112: Added-state card paints with the tab accent — Soko/Blue for Sítios, Soko/Green for Eventos
- **Choice**: when an item is successfully added to the zine, the entire `SearchResultCard` row paints with the active tab's accent color (`AppColors.sokoBlue` `#8BDFFF` for places, `AppColors.sokoGreen` `#B0EF8B` for events) — not just the trailing icon. The 1 px Soko/Ink @ 6 % bottom divider drops out on added rows so two adjacent fills don't stripe. The `Icon/Add` glyph swaps from `circle_plus` to `circle_check` over Soko/Ink (no color shift on the glyph itself — the fill carries the success signal).
- **Why**: a check-only success state was indistinguishable at a glance from "not yet tapped" on mobile (14 px glyph against a 56 px row of text). Painting the whole row pulls the success state out of the icon micro-detail and into the row silhouette — you can see at a glance which items in the scroll list are already in your zine. Matching the tab accent (Soko/Blue tab → blue added-state for Sítios; Soko/Green tab → green added-state for Eventos) ties the row colour to the active filter pill so the user reads "Sítio that's already in my zine" without parsing the icon.
- **Why not the global `Soko/Green` for success**: green-for-success would clash with the tab accent on the Sítios scope (the active filter pill is blue but added rows would be green — two unrelated success signals fighting for the same surface). Letting the row colour follow the tab keeps the colour grammar consistent inside the page.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/search_result_card.dart` — `_accentColor` getter switches on `item.type`, applied to the outer `Container.decoration.color` when `isAdded`; the conditional `Border` drops the bottom side in that same branch.

### D113: `Icon/Add` is a bare 14 px Lucide glyph — no 30 px wrapping circle, instant icon swap
- **Choice**: the add control is a bare `LucideIcons.circle_plus` (untapped) / `LucideIcons.circle_check` (added), rendered at `size: 14` inside a 32 × 32 invisible `SizedBox` tap target. There is no surrounding 30 px outlined circle (the legacy variant had a `BoxDecoration` border around a Material `add` icon). Icon swap on tap is instant — no `AnimatedSwitcher`, no fade, no rotate.
- **Why**: the Lucide `circle_plus` / `circle_check` glyphs already carry the circular shape — wrapping them in another 30 px circle was visual redundancy. The bare glyph reads as part of the row's typographic rhythm (sits at the optical x-height of the 15 px title) instead of as a button competing with the row's tap target. The 32 × 32 `SizedBox` keeps the tap target at the iOS / Material accessibility floor without adding visible chrome.
- **Why instant (no animation)**: Figma spec for this control reads `Animate: Instantâneo; animation-duration: 0ms`. The add-to-list mutation already shows a 14 px `CircularProgressIndicator` in the same slot during the optimistic-add round-trip — that's the only motion in the lifecycle. An additional fade on the resolved-state swap would compete with the spinner's appearance/disappearance and make the in-flight state harder to read. The same instant-swap rule applies on every glyph swap on this surface (`circle_plus` → spinner → `circle_check`).
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/search_result_card.dart` `_AddButton` — fixed 32 × 32 `SizedBox`, `Icon(glyph, size: 14, color: AppColors.sokoInk)`. The same `_AddButton` is used by the empty-state "Add suggested item" rows in `list_suggestions_section.dart`.

## Lists Hub (PROD-1911)

### D114: `CityActionBar` is a single shared widget across `/discovery` and `/lists`
- **Choice**: the action bar (magnifier circle button + "Cria nova zine" pill + location pin pill) is one widget — `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/city_action_bar.dart` — used as-is on both pages. The location pill writes to a single shared `cityScopeProvider`, so changing the city on one page rescopes the other. PROD-1911 renamed the original `DiscoveryActionBar` and moved it out of `features/discovery/`.
- **Parameterized for host needs**: `onSearchTap`, `showSearchOverlay`, `searchOverlay`, optional `onCreateZineTap`. The host owns search-open state (forked per-surface, see D115) and provides its own `searchOverlay` widget; the location pill stays internal.
- **Why not duplicate**: the visual identity (3-button row, Soko/Shade5 + Soko/Pink chrome) must stay synchronised. One widget = one place to land tweaks. The same applies to the underlying providers — `cityScopeProvider` and friends moved to `lib/providers/` so future surfaces share without re-renaming.
- **YAGNI on extensibility**: the 3-button layout is fixed. No `trailing: Widget?` slot, no `actions: List<...>` builder. When a real need arises to differ per host (4-button variant on some future page), the refactor is cheap because all callsites go through one widget. Decision logged here so the next dev doesn't re-debate.
- **PROD-2145 amendment**: the location pill is now conditional via `showLocationPill: bool = true`. `/yours` passes `false` (the page no longer filters by city). `/discovery` still renders all three buttons. See [D169](#d169-cityactionbar-location-pill-is-conditional-per-host-surface-prod-2145).
- **Where it lives**: widget at `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/city_action_bar.dart`, providers at `heyl_app/lib/providers/city_{scope,auto_scope,auto_location_label}_provider.dart`. SharedPreferences key kept as `discovery_scope_<userId>` for backward compat with users who saved a scope pre-PROD-1911.

### D115: Lists hub forks its search-overlay state from `/discovery`
- **Choice**: `/lists` has its own `listsSearchOpenProvider` and `listsSearchQueryProvider` (in `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/providers/lists_search_providers.dart`), separate from `/discovery`'s `searchOpenProvider` / `searchQueryProvider`. The `CityActionBar` (D114) accepts host-provided open/overlay props and doesn't read either provider directly.
- **Why**: typing on `/lists` must not bleed into `/discovery`'s search state, and vice versa. Mounting one global `searchOpenProvider` would mean: open search on `/lists`, navigate to `/discovery` via bottom nav, and the search panel is still open with the previous query.
- **Different shapes too**: `/discovery`'s search has Zines/Eventos/Sítios category tabs (`searchCategoryProvider`); `/lists` search is single-category (owner's lists + items inside), no tabs. Forking lets each surface build its own overlay widget without leaking the other's UI.
- **Trade-off**: two near-identical provider files. Acceptable cost — they're 3 lines each and the boundary is explicit. The `DiscoverySearchOverlay` and `ListsSearchOverlay` widgets are also separate, but the **input-pill chrome and category-tabs row are now extracted into shared widgets** as of PROD-1928 — see [D122](#d122-searchresultsview--searchoverlay-are-shared-widgets-each-surface-owns-data-routing-only). The two overlay classes survive as 30-line wrappers that just plug their forked providers into `SearchOverlay`.
- **/lists tab parity (PROD-1934 follow-up)**: as of PROD-1928, `/lists` also has all three Zines/Eventos/Sítios tabs (shared `SearchCategoryTabs`); the category state is forked into `listsSearchCategoryProvider`. The earlier "/lists search is single-category" framing is gone.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/providers/lists_search_providers.dart`, `lib/features/lists/widgets/lists_search_overlay.dart`.

### D116: Lists hub always renders the map and the calendar (no empty-state placeholders)
- **Choice**: when the user has zero items for the selected city + month, the `/lists` hub still paints the map (centered on the city, no pins) and the calendar grid (current month, no dots). Earlier version showed "Ainda não há nada no mapa" / "Sem eventos este mês" cards instead.
- **Why**: the geographic + temporal context **is** the page's identity. Replacing it with empty-state copy hides the "where am I / what month is this" affordance and makes empty users feel like the page is broken. The map's city framing immediately answers "where am I looking?" even with no pins, and the calendar's month chevron is the only way to discover that other months may have events.
- **What `ListMapView` does on empty**: drops the fit-bounds button + bottom drawer (nothing to act on); fills the 500 px container with the Mapbox tile; falls back to the city centroid when no markers + no bbox. Other callers of `ListMapView` (zine cover, list-detail view-mode) gate the whole map block on `isNotEmpty` upstream — their behaviour is unchanged.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (no empty branch), `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_map_view.dart` (no early return on empty).

<!-- STALE(2026-05-26): superseded by D173 — the chat composer now ALSO renders a bottom row, hosting a History button (right) plus the existing Create-list pill (left, when there are saveable cards). The discovery composer's three-shortcut row is unchanged; only the chat composer regained a bottom strip. -->
### D117: Discovery composer keeps the "Adiciona à Soko" / history / WhatsApp bottom row; chat composer doesn't
- **Choice**: the chat-bar bottom row (pink strip with `AddToSokoPill` + history button + `WhatsAppBrandIcon`) renders on the **Discovery** surface only. The chat conversation composer (`MessageInput`) and the chat empty state (`DiscoveryChatBar` mounted on `/chat`) keep the PROD-1894 simplified layout — top row only, plus a Create-list pill when there are saveable cards.
- **Wiring**: `DiscoveryChatBar` gained a `showDiscoveryActions: bool` flag; `discovery_screen.dart` passes `true`, everywhere else defaults to `false`. The bottom row is a new `ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow` widget in `shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart`, distinct from the existing `ChatBarBottomRow` (chat-side Create-list pill).
- **Why**: PROD-1894 removed the bottom row from the chat composer to declutter the conversation surface. The Discovery composer was caught in that cleanup unintentionally — the "Adiciona à Soko" shortcut + chat-history affordance are useful entry points on the home page where there's no top nav, but redundant inside a conversation where the user is already in the chat history. The bottom row is hidden during voice recording on Discovery for the same reason as before — the recorder owns the full bar silhouette.
- **Trade-off**: two visually distinct composer variants exist now (was one). The shared `ChatBarTopRow` primitive is unchanged; only the bottom row composes conditionally.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart` (`ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow`, `AddToSokoPill`, `WhatsAppBrandIcon`), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_chat_bar.dart` (`showDiscoveryActions` flag + handlers), `lib/features/discovery/screens/discovery_screen.dart` (opts in). PROD-1863.

### D118: SnackBar bg color removed when migrating errors to Soko notifications — Paper bg, error-colored icon only
- **Choice**: When the ~100 `ScaffoldMessenger.showSnackBar(...)` call sites moved to `showSoko(...)` (PROD-1863, see [ui/soko-notifications.md](soko-notifications.md)), error notifications **lost the full-red bg** (`AppColors.error`) used by ~10 sites. The Soko notification card is always `sokoPaper`; errors signal via `LucideIcons.triangle_alert` tinted `AppColors.error`. Similarly, the two `Colors.green` success bgs got coalesced into the Paper variant (icon: `LucideIcons.circle_check`).
- **Why**: brand consistency. The user request was "follows our design tokens, not really the system snackbar". Letting individual variants override the bg would re-introduce the snackbar feel the system was meant to retire.
- **Escape hatch**: if a future flow needs the heavy-error emphasis, add a new variant (e.g. `errorEmphasized`) rather than parameterising the bg. Don't let call sites pass arbitrary colors.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/notifications/heyl_notification.dart` (`SokoCard._iconTint`), `lib/shared/notifications/notification_state.dart` (`SokoVariant`). PROD-1863.

## Lists hub search refresh (PROD-1928 / PROD-1934)

<!-- STALE(2026-05-20): superseded by PROD-2026. On `/yours` the scallop now sits BELOW the action bar (page title → input → scallop → body). See D138 + lists.md changelog 2026-05-20 (PROD-2026). -->
<!-- STALE(2026-05-27): further superseded by D180 — the page-level DiscoverySectionDivider was removed entirely once the toggle gained a per-title scallop underline. Header rhythm on `/yours` is now title → 36 → action bar → body, no page-level scallop. -->
### D119: `/lists` header rhythm — scallop divider sits ABOVE the action bar, not below
- **Choice**: on `/lists` the top of the content area renders, top to bottom: 45 px gap → `DiscoverySectionDivider` (Soko-Ink scallop) → 35 px gap → `CityActionBar` → body. On `/discovery` the order is reversed — action bar first, scallop below — to match the original Figma. The two pages intentionally diverge.
- **Why**: the `/lists` hub doesn't have the "ask anything" Discovery chat card under the action bar that the scallop visually rests on. With the action bar at the top, the scallop becomes a closing punctuation rather than a between-sections rhythm element — which on a non-chat surface reads as a dropped accent instead of a header.
- **What sits below the action bar instead**: `YoursShelf` mounts directly. Its built-in `ShelfDivider` (dotted, 30 px above + below) becomes the natural visual break between header and body, so no extra divider needs adding.
- **Trade-off**: the page-header rhythm now differs across the bottom-nav surfaces. Acceptable — `/discovery` and `/lists` are visually distinct destinations and the user benefits from that distinction. An earlier iteration of this PR added a second `DottedSectionDivider` below the action bar; that was a duplicate of YoursShelf's built-in ShelfDivider — removed.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` — top-level `Padding(EdgeInsets.fromLTRB(16, 45, 16, _kBottomNavReserve))` + the explicit ordering of `DiscoverySectionDivider` → `SizedBox(35)` → `CityActionBar` in the host Column.

### D120: `/lists` search is unscoped — no location filter, no date filter — and the user sees a notice
- **Choice**: when the user opens the `/lists` search overlay, the results **don't** honour the action bar's selected city pill, and (for events) **don't** honour the calendar's visible month. The default-state hub map + calendar stay city- and month-scoped; only the search results lift those filters.
- **Why**: searching for "Lisboa" should return things saved in Lisboa even if the user is currently scoped to Barcelona. Searching for an event in July should surface it even if the calendar is showing May. The hub's city / month framing makes sense for browsing ("what's around me this month") but breaks for recall ("find that thing I saved").
- **User communication**: a small Soko-Ink-tinted info row sits above the results grid: "Os resultados não estão filtrados por localização ou data." (EN: "Results aren't filtered by location or date.") The `listsHubSearchUnscopedNotice` l10n key drives this; surfaces in `_ListsSearchUnscopedNotice` in `lists_hub_screen.dart` and rendered via the shared `SearchResultsView`'s `topBanner` slot.
- **Trade-off**: temporarily inconsistent with the rest of the page — the city pill above the search shows "Lisboa" but the results aren't limited to Lisboa. The notice carries the explanation. Acceptable until filters + sort controls land (see D121's BE follow-up).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (`_ListsSearchUnscopedNotice`), `lib/features/lists/providers/lists_search_results_provider.dart` (no city/date params in any branch).

### D121: Lists search field set is unified server-side via `/saved-items` (PROD-1934)
- **Choice**: Zines / Eventos / Sítios all resolve `q` against the same field set, server-side. Zines hits `listMyLists(q=…)`; Eventos and Sítios hit the new `/api/v1/app/users/me/lists/saved-items?q=&item_type=` endpoint (PROD-1934, BE PR #1229). The SQL primitive `item_text_match_predicates` is shared between both BE paths so adding a searchable field touches one place.
- **Why**: matching by venue address on Zines but only by name on Sítios was a real bug — a "Lisboa" query would surface a list (because some venue's address contained "Lisboa") but tapping Sítios returned empty. The FE can't paper over this; the BE owns which fields are searchable and the FE should ask the BE for results, not re-implement matching.
- **Fields matched** (per row, item-scoped): event title, venue name (trigram fuzzy + accent-insensitive); event categories / vibes / tags, venue city / address / type / types[], user's `tip` on the saved item (ILIKE + accent-insensitive).
- **Pre-PROD-1934 fallback (deleted)**: the FE called `listMyLists(q=…)` to find matching lists then fanned out to per-list `listItems(id, itemType=…)` and filtered client-side by `item.title.contains(q)`. Title-only, exact substring, fan-out capped at 20 lists. That code is gone; `lists_search_results_provider.dart` is now ~30 lines of switch-on-category dispatch.
- **Server-driven subtitle**: each `SavedSearchItemOut` carries a pre-computed `subtitle` (events: first future occurrence's `venue.name` → fallback city; places: `venue.type` → `types[0]` → city). The FE renders it verbatim, so future subtitle tweaks don't need a FE deploy.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/data/models/saved_search_item.dart` (FE model — class name disambiguated from the legacy `SavedItem`; matches the BE's renamed `SavedSearchItemOut` after the duplicate-schema rename), `lib/features/lists/providers/lists_search_results_provider.dart` (provider switch), `lib/data/datasources/api/lists_api.dart#searchSavedItems` (typed client method).

### D122: `SearchResultsView` + `SearchOverlay` are shared widgets — each surface owns data + routing only
- **Choice**: two shared widgets at `lib/shared/widgets/` own every piece of the lists/discovery search presentation: input pill + close X + debounce + centered category tabs (`SearchOverlay`), and loading skeleton + 2-up paired grid + cell chrome + footer + states (`SearchResultsView`). Each surface (`/discovery`, `/lists`) becomes a thin wrapper that plugs in its own forked state providers + hint text, and supplies an adapter from its domain rows to a presentation-only `SearchResultCard` shape.
- **Why**: roughly ~600 LOC of identical UI was duplicated between Discovery's `SearchResultsSection` and Lists' `_ListsSearchResultsBody` before the refactor. Worse, the duplication produced exactly the kind of regression duplication does: tap-to-detail on event/venue cards was wired on Discovery but missed on `/lists`. Sharing one widget closes the gap and makes future visual changes (typography, grid gap, error copy, footer art) land in one file.
- **Routing stays per-surface**: each page wires its own `onTap` lambda into the `SearchResultCard` it produces — Discovery pushes `/lists/<slug>` with `referrer='/'`, `/lists` uses `referrer='/lists'`, both share `/events/<id>` and `/venues/<id>` for items. The shared view never knows about routes.
- **State stays forked**: `searchOpenProvider` vs `listsSearchOpenProvider`, same for `query` / `category` — the shared widgets take a `StateProvider<T>` parameter and consume whichever is passed. The fork rationale from [D115](#d115-lists-hub-forks-its-search-overlay-state-from-discovery) holds.
- **`SearchResultCard` is presentation-only**: it carries `name`, `subtitle`, `attribution`, `imageUrl` or `coverRecipe`, `rowKey`, `onTap`. The discovery side adapts its sealed `SearchResultRow` union; the lists side adapts the sealed `ListsSearchResults` union (which now also carries server-shaped `SavedSearchItem`s per D121).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/search_overlay.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/search_results_view.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/search_category_tabs.dart` (the tabs row was extracted earlier in PROD-1928).

### D123: Lists search results dedupe by entity, with conditional attribution (PROD-1936)
- **Choice**: when the same event or venue is saved in N of the caller's lists, the `/lists` search results collapse those N memberships into a single card. The card's attribution line is rendered conditionally — the list's name when the entity is in exactly one list, the localised `listsHubSearchSavedInNZines` plural ("Saved in N zines") when it's in more than one. Tap always opens the entity detail (`/events/<id>` or `/venues/<id>`); the per-list breakdown isn't a tappable surface today.
- **Why**: pre-PROD-1936, an event saved in 3 lists rendered as 3 identical cards with 3 different list names — noise, not signal. Users came to recall an entity, not browse list rows.
- **Where the dedupe lives — server-side**: `/api/v1/app/users/me/lists/saved-items` returns one row per unique `(item_type, entity_id)`; the contributing memberships are in `list_refs[]` (always non-empty, BE-guaranteed `minItems: 1`). `total` and pagination operate on unique entities. Ranking takes the best per-row word-similarity score across collapsed memberships (degenerate today because the score is entity-scoped, but the SQL preserves the clause for future field-dependent scoring). See PROD-1936 BE PR #1230.
- **FE shape**: `SavedSearchItem` carries a `listRefs: List<SavedSearchItemListRef>` (always non-empty). The FE never special-cases "deduped vs not" — every row has `listRefs`. Attribution is one ternary: `listRefs.length == 1 ? listRefs.first.listName : l10n.listsHubSearchSavedInNZines(listRefs.length)`. `rowKey` switched from the membership id (`user_list_items.id`) to the entity id (`event_id` / `venue_id`) so swap animations stay stable when re-fetches change which memberships back the row.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/data/models/saved_search_item.dart` (`SavedSearchItem`, `SavedSearchItemListRef`), `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (`_attributionFor` helper used by `_eventCard` / `_placeCard`), `lib/l10n/intl_{en,pt,pt_BR}.arb` (`listsHubSearchSavedInNZines`).

## Interactive Zine Progress Line (PROD-1955)

PROD-1955 turned the previously-static progress line above each zine card into a tappable scrubber with a page-turn cascade animation. The visual rhythm changed (taller bars, bigger tap target, less-rounded corners) and the indicator now adapts to long lists with a separate continuous-bar render mode. Cover semantics in numbering also changed.

### D124: Zine progress line is tap-to-seek with a forward/backward page-turn cascade
- **Choice**: Tapping any inactive segment (or any point in continuous mode — see [D126](#d126-progress-line-degrades-to-a-continuous-scrubber-bar-above-30-segments-instant-jump-for--20-step-cascades)) navigates the zine pager to that page via an awaited per-flip cascade — flip N+1 begins the moment flip N finishes, no fixed inter-flip gap. Tap on the currently-active segment is a noop. While a cascade is in flight: the progress line ignores further taps, the pager drag is disabled (`useOnSwipe: false`), pointer events are dropped (`IgnorePointer`), and the desktop side arrows + cover-hero advance ignore. The aux + slot `AnimatedSwitcher`s gate their key on a `_cascadeSourceIndex` so they cross-fade exactly once at the end of the cascade instead of fanning through every intermediate page.
- **Why**: The Lovable prototype always had the line as a passive indicator. Users frequently tried to tap it as a scrubber — there's no other shortcut for "skip 3 pages forward" besides 3 drag-flips or the cover map pin (which only jumps from the cover). The cascade animation keeps the tactile zine feel; an instant jump would feel like a different surface entirely.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart` — `_animateToPage(int targetPageIndex)` builds the schedule and drives the cascade via the vendored `TurnPageController.animateToPageWithSchedule`; `_isAnimating` flag + `_cascadeSourceIndex` gate the lockout and the aux/slot keys. `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/zine_progress_line.dart` emits the segment index via `onSegmentTap`; "tap on active = noop" + lockout enforcement live in the host, not the widget.

### D125: Variable-speed cascade schedule — 50 ms floor, 300 ms settle
- **Choice**: Per-flip durations for an N-step cascade:

  | N | Schedule (ms) |
  | - | ------------- |
  | 1 | `300` |
  | 2 | `100, 300` |
  | 3 | `50, 100, 300` |
  | 4+ | `50 × (N - 3)`, then `50, 100, 300` |

  50 ms floor (≥ ~1.5 frames so the flip doesn't visibly snap on lower-end devices). 300 ms final flip matches the natural drag-flip duration so the cascade lands on the destination at the same cadence as if the user had dragged there themselves. Trailing 50 → 100 → 300 gives the destination a recognisable page-turn feel. The natural drag-to-flip gesture keeps the package default of 300 ms per flip — only the tap-driven cascade uses the variable schedule.
