- **Why**: A fixed 50 ms cascade (the upstream `animateToPage` does this) fans the aux cross-fade through every intermediate page and feels chaotic. A fixed 300 ms cascade (i.e. each flip at the natural duration) takes too long for any jump > 2 pages. Variable speed splits the difference — fast through intermediates, deliberate on the destination.
- **Trade-off after on-device testing (PROD-1955 review pass)**: The first iteration ended on 250 ms. The user reported it felt clunky vs the natural drag-flip cadence; bumped to 300 ms to match `defaultTransitionDuration` on the upstream package.
- **Where it lives**: `_ListZineViewState._buildCascadeSchedule(int n)` in `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart`.

### D126: Progress line degrades to a continuous scrubber bar above 30 segments; instant jump for > 20-step cascades
- **Choice**: Two independent thresholds:
  - **Visual mode** switches at `totalSections > 30`. Below: discrete tappable dots (the design from [D87](#d87-zine-has-a-per-page-progress-line-above-the-card-mirrored-on-the-in-list-detail)). Above: a single 2 px scrubber bar that fills from left to right in proportion to `activeUpTo / totalSections`, with an 8 px round marker pip at the fill edge. Tap anywhere on the bar emits the segment whose fractional position is closest to the tap-x ratio.
  - **Animation mode** switches at `|targetIndex - currentIndex| > 20`. Below: cascade animation (see [D125](#d125-variable-speed-cascade-schedule--50-ms-floor-300-ms-settle)). Above: instant `jumpToPage` — same path the cover map pin uses.

  The two compose: a 300-item list with a 5-page jump still cascades; the same list with a 200-page jump uses the instant-jump path. A short list with any jump still cascades.

- **Why**: With `Expanded` segments + a mandatory 4 px gap, the dot row overflows around N ≈ 42 on a 360 px phone — at N = 300 each `Expanded` would need negative width. Past that, even if it rendered, individual dots aren't legible as separate pages so the user perceives a bar anyway. And: a 300-step cascade at the 50 ms floor is 15 s of frenetic flipping — not "fluid", just exhausting. The instant-jump fallback above 20 steps keeps the page-turn animation reserved for cases where it genuinely reads as a page turn.
- **Tooltip on the continuous bar**: Flutter's `Tooltip` widget anchors to a child widget; it can't follow a cursor along an arbitrary x. We render the design-system tooltip body directly via `SokoTooltipChip` (see [D128](#d128-sokotooltip-is-the-design-system-tooltip--sokoshade2-background-paper-text)) inside a `Stack(clipBehavior: Clip.none)` positioned at the hover x, centred via `FractionalTranslation(Offset(-0.5, 0))`.
- **Where it lives**: `_kContinuousBarThreshold = 30` (top of `zine_progress_line.dart`) drives the visual switch; `_kMaxCascadeSteps = 20` (in `list_zine_view.dart`) drives the animation switch. `_ContinuousBar` is the render path for long lists; `_animateToPage` short-circuits to `jumpToPage` when `steps > _kMaxCascadeSteps`.

### D127: Cover is not counted as a "page" in user-facing numbering
- **Choice**: The list-page header stats line shows `state.items.length` (was `+ 1`) — 0 items = "0 pages", 3 items = "3 pages". Progress-line tooltips: segment 0 (the cover) shows the `zineProgressLineCoverTooltip` ("Cover" / "Capa"); segment K ≥ 1 shows `zineProgressLinePageTooltip(K)` ("Page K" / "Página K"). Segment 1 (the first item) is "Page 1", segment N is "Page N". The progress line itself still draws `items.length + 1` segments (cover stays a visible-but-unnumbered indicator).
- **Why**: A zine cover isn't a numbered page in any zine, magazine, or printed-book convention — it's a cover. Numbering the cover as "Page 1" made the per-item card labels ("1", "2", "3", …) misalign with the header count ("4 pages" for 3 items + cover). Splitting "cover" out of the numbering aligns the three surfaces — card label, header count, tooltip — on the same mental model.
- **Where it lives**: `_StatsLine` in `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` (`pageCount = state.items.length`); `tooltipFor(int segmentIndex)` in `zine_progress_line.dart` (branches on `segmentIndex == 0`); `zineProgressLineCoverTooltip` + `zineProgressLinePageTooltip` in `intl_{en,pt,pt_BR}.arb`.

### D128: SokoTooltip is the design-system tooltip — Soko/Shade2 background, paper text
- **Choice**: New widget pair at `lib/shared/widgets/soko_tooltip.dart`. Both surfaces share the same private design tokens at the top of the file (`_kBackground = sokoShade2`, `_kTextColor = sokoPaper`, `_kRadius = 4`, `_kPadding = 8h × 4v`, `_kTextStyle = 12 px / w500 / 1.2 line-height`). Two forms:
  - **`SokoTooltip(message, child)`** — anchored form. Wraps Flutter's `Tooltip` so we inherit hover-on-desktop / long-press-on-mobile trigger logic and overlay positioning. Default `waitDuration: 200 ms`.
  - **`SokoTooltipChip(label)`** — standalone bubble for manual positioning inside a `Stack`/`Positioned`. Used by the continuous-bar progress line to render a cursor-following tooltip; usable anywhere a `Tooltip`-anchored child doesn't fit (custom painters, overlay rendering alongside a hover-tracking widget).
- **Why a single component**: future visual changes to tooltips (background colour, radius, typography) need to land in one file. The progress line's discrete-mode segments use `SokoTooltip` and the continuous-mode hover preview uses `SokoTooltipChip`; both pick up token changes automatically.
- **Soko/Shade2 = `#534B4D`** — paper text on it is well-contrasted; the bubble reads as a dark companion to the surrounding Soko/Paper page surface without going pure-black (which would feel like a debug overlay against the brand palette).
- **Out of scope (housekeeping pass)**: existing call sites using Flutter's raw `Tooltip` (`feedback_toolbar`, `places_map_modal`, `list_item_note`, `system_list_badge`, `profile_sheet`) were intentionally left alone in PROD-1955 — migrating them is a separate pass.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/soko_tooltip.dart`; tokens are private constants in that file (single source of truth, intentionally not in `app_colors.dart` since they're tooltip-specific composites).

### D129: Bottom nav — 64px content height, 5 equal `Expanded` cells, full-cell tap targets
- **Choice**: `DiscoveryBottomNav._NavBar` renders a `SizedBox(height: 64)` (down from ~88px content) inside the existing `SafeArea(top: false)` wrapper. Children are `Row(children: [Expanded × 5])`, with each cell containing a centred icon + label. The previous `Row(MainAxisAlignment.center)` + fixed `SizedBox(width: 40)` gaps are gone. Touch target = full cell via `GestureDetector(behavior: opaque)` on each `_NavItem`.
- **Why**: PROD-1952. The old layout (a) ate too much screen on small phones, (b) pushed first/last items toward the bar edges visually (uneven margins), and (c) had ~40px dead zones between icons where taps did nothing. Equal cells fix both the spacing balance (each cell is 1/5 of the bar width, content centred — visually `spaceEvenly`) and the touch area (no dead zones).
- **Where it lives**: `_NavBar.build` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_bottom_nav.dart`. The 600px desktop `ConstrainedBox` max-width is preserved (the 5 `Expanded` cells divide that width).

### D130: Bottom nav selection — label collapses (not just fades), icon scales, SVG swap is instant
- **Choice**: Selected `_NavItem` shows a 28px icon (22→28 for Home), label hidden; unselected shows the 20px icon (22 for Home) + label. Transition is 400 ms / `Curves.easeInOutCubic`, symmetric forward/reverse, concurrent on the old + new items. Three sub-animations off a single controller via `Interval`s:

  | Tween | Forward interval | Effect |
  |---|---|---|
  | Label opacity + height-factor (both collapse together via `ClipRect + Align(heightFactor)`) | 0.00 – 0.30 | 1.0 → 0.0 — label disappears AND its layout box shrinks to 0 |
  | Icon scale | 0.25 – 0.85 | base → 28px |
  | SVG variant swap | discrete, driven by `widget.isSelected` (not the controller value) | inactive → active before the size animation runs |

- **Why**:
  - **Label height collapse (not just opacity)**: with opacity-only fade, the invisible label still occupied ~14.4 px → the icon stayed anchored above its old position. Collapsing the height too lets the icon migrate to the cell's true vertical center (y = 32 in a 64 px bar) when selected. Centering on the cell is the right "selected" affordance.
  - **SVG swap driven by `widget.isSelected`, not by a controller threshold**: a controller threshold (e.g. swap at value ≥ 0.80) makes the SVG flip AFTER the size animation finishes; reading off `widget.isSelected` directly makes the SVG flip BEFORE the size animation runs. User perception: "icon changes, then expands" (forward) / "icon changes, then contracts" (reverse). Symmetric on both directions because the SVG swap is intent-driven, not progress-driven.
  - **400 ms / cubic vs the originally-spec'd 200 ms / fastOutSlowIn**: 200 ms felt snappy/abrupt; the redesigned animation needed to read as a state-transition, not a flicker. Symmetric S-curve (`easeInOutCubic`) for that flowing feel.
- **Where it lives**: `_NavItem` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_bottom_nav.dart`. The label height collapse is `ClipRect(child: Align(alignment: Alignment.topCenter, heightFactor: _labelOpacity.value, child: Padding(top: 6, child: Opacity(...))))` — the 6 px gap between icon and label is inside the collapsing region, so it disappears with the label.

### D131: Criar icon — `plus.svg` rotates 0 → 45° on select instead of swapping to `plus-active.svg`
- **Choice**: When Criar is selected (the Create sheet is mounted), the existing `plus.svg` rotates 0 → π/4 radians (45°) in lockstep with the scale animation (same `_iconProgress` interval). The result reads as a `+` morphing into an `×`. The `_NavItem.rotateOnSelect` flag toggles this behavior; `activeAsset` is passed as `null` for Criar, so the asset-swap path is short-circuited. `assets/images/icons/nav/plus-active.svg` is in the repo but no longer referenced.
- **Why**: An `×` icon doubles as a visual close cue — it tells the user "tap to dismiss" the way a sticky-bar `×` would, reinforcing the new toggle behavior (see [D132](#d132-create-menu-as-a-persistent-bottom-sheet-tap-outside-and-tap-criar-again-to-dismiss)). Rotation animates smoothly with the size change in one tween; an asset swap would have been a discrete flip mid-expand which felt unrelated to the size animation.
- **Where it lives**: `_NavItem.rotateOnSelect` + `_selectedRotationRadians = π/4` constant in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_bottom_nav.dart`. The Criar item passes `activeAsset: null, rotateOnSelect: true`.

### D132: Create menu as a persistent bottom sheet; tap-outside and tap-Criar-again to dismiss
- **Choice**: `CreateMenuSheet` opens via `Scaffold.of(ctx).showBottomSheet(...)` (persistent), NOT `showModalBottomSheet` (modal). Persistent because it must satisfy three constraints at once: viewport-anchored on long pages, bottom nav visible underneath, bottom nav still tappable. No `showModalBottomSheet` configuration satisfies all three — see [learnings/persistent-vs-modal-bottom-sheet-discoveryshell.md](../learnings/persistent-vs-modal-bottom-sheet-discoveryshell.md) for the detailed comparison.

  Dismissal paths (all close the same sheet):
  - Tap Criar again — `CreateMenuSheet.toggle()` closes the live `PersistentBottomSheetController` instead of re-opening.
  - Tap any other bottom nav button — that button's `onTap` calls `_closeCreateSheetIfOpen()` as a side effect before performing its own action (navigate / open drawer).
  - Tap anywhere on the body or pinned chrome — `_CreateMenuDismissOverlay` in `DiscoveryShell`'s body Stack (positioned above the chrome, below the drawers) catches the tap with `HitTestBehavior.opaque` and closes. Inert via `IgnorePointer` when the sheet is closed.
  - Swipe down on the sheet's drag handle — `enableDrag: true`.
  - From inside a menu option ("Cria uma nova zine", etc.) — option calls `onDismiss()` (passed by the caller, wired to the controller) before doing its own follow-up.
- **Why option B (overlay above chrome, not below)**: the user picked "any tap outside closes" including over the chrome — back-arrow / list-title taps absorb to the sheet dismissal instead of doing their own action. Pragmatic for this surface: while the sheet is open the user's primary intent is "what do I want to create"; navigation back is a secondary intent better served by re-tapping the chrome after dismiss.
- **Why no scrim**: the constraint "bottom nav stays tappable" rules out a modal barrier. The trade-off — no visual focus dim — is acceptable because the menu options are visually prominent enough on their own (paper bg, drag handle, divided rows).
- **Single trigger / shared state**: the chat bar's "Add" pill (`DiscoveryChatBar._handleAddTap`) also calls `CreateMenuSheet.toggle()`, so both entry points share the same open/close state via `createMenuControllerProvider`. Tapping the pill while the sheet is open closes it (matches "any click outside the modal closes it").
- **Reusability**: the persistent-sheet glue (dismiss-overlay, nav-button side-effect, toggle plumbing) is hardcoded to Create today. Extracting a reusable `PersistentDismissSheet` helper is deferred until a second consumer appears (rule of three).
- **Where it lives**:
  - `CreateMenuSheet.toggle()` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/create_menu_sheet.dart`
  - `createMenuControllerProvider` in `lib/providers/bottom_nav_provider.dart`
  - `_CreateMenuDismissOverlay` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart` (private widget at the bottom of the file)
  - `_closeCreateSheetIfOpen()` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_bottom_nav.dart`

### D133: Continuous progress line has tap-and-drag scrub preview on touch (PROD-1968)
- **Choice**: On the continuous-bar progress line (`totalSections > 30`, see [D126](#d126-progress-line-degrades-to-a-continuous-scrubber-bar-above-30-segments-instant-jump-for--20-step-cascades)), touch interactions get a live floating `Page N` tooltip that follows the finger during press-and-drag. The seek commits on release with whatever segment the final finger position maps to. Mirrors the existing **desktop hover-preview** behaviour so mobile users no longer have to guess where a tap will land.
- **Discrete mode is unchanged**: at `totalSections ≤ 30`, each per-segment dot already presents itself as a discrete tap target — drag-preview adds no clarity and would require lifting gesture handling out of `_Segment` into a row-level handler.
- **Mechanism**: `_ContinuousBarState._dragX` mirrors `_hoverX`. Set in `onTapDown` (after `kPressTimeout` ≈100 ms for a press) and `onHorizontalDragStart` (after ~18 px of horizontal motion). Updated in `onHorizontalDragUpdate`. Cleared and seek-committed in `onTapUp` / `onHorizontalDragEnd`. `onHorizontalDrag*` (not `onPan*`) so vertical drags on the bar fall through to the parent scrollable — no accidental scrub-vs-scroll conflict.
- **Why `onTapDown` not `Listener.onPointerDown`**: a raw `Listener` would give pixel-zero feedback but doesn't claim the gesture arena, so a parent scrollable could compete for vertical motion. `GestureDetector` + `onHorizontalDrag*` is the simplest path that owns horizontal motion while letting vertical pass through. The 100 ms tap delay matches every other tap interaction in the app. If this latency feels wrong in practice, the upgrade path is `RawGestureDetector` with a custom immediate-claim recognizer.
- **Tooltip content**: same `tooltipFor(segmentIndex)` resolver used by hover and discrete-mode tooltips — segment 0 reads "Cover" / "Capa", segment K ≥ 1 reads "Page K" / "Página K" (per [D127](#d127-cover-is-not-counted-as-a-page-in-user-facing-numbering)). Renders as a floating `SokoTooltipChip` ([D128](#d128-sokotooltip-is-the-design-system-tooltip--sokoshade2-background-paper-text)) inside the bar's Stack with `Clip.none`, positioned at `previewX = _dragX ?? _hoverX`.
- **Lock-in**: `heyl_app/test/features/lists/widgets/zine/zine_progress_line_test.dart` asserts quick-tap shows tooltip + seeks on release; press-and-drag updates the tooltip and only commits on release with the final x; cover-segment tooltip; non-interactive bar shows nothing; discrete mode untouched.
- **Where it lives**: `_ContinuousBar` and `_ContinuousBarState` in `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/zine_progress_line.dart`.

### D134: Add-items flow is a bottom sheet after all — reverts D111
- **Choice**: tapping "+" on an owner-mode zine opens `AddItemsToListSheet` as a modal bottom sheet via `showAddItemsToListSheet(context, ref:, listId:, listName:)`. The legacy full-page route `/lists/:listId/add-items` (D111, PROD-1852) is deleted, along with `AppRoutes.listAddItems`, the `addItemsToListPageName` chrome bindings in `discovery_shell.dart` + `pinned_page_chrome.dart`, and the `AddItemsToListPage` widget itself. The same Figma frame `9300` visual treatment from PROD-1852 is preserved verbatim — headline, search row, Lugares/Eventos pills, results list, Instagram-share footer — inside a `DSSheetShell` (header / body / footer slots) hosted by `showBottomSheetWithHiddenNav<bool>`. The Instagram footer button still calls `Navigator.of(context).pop(true)` so the caller (`list_page_screen._handleAdd`) keeps its existing handoff to `showInstagramShareSheet`.
- **Why we reverted**: pushing a route to add items disposed the underlying list view on every entry and rebuilt it on every dismiss. That mount/dismount cycle drove three problems simultaneously: (1) the cover `MapboxMapPlatform` got a `dispose()` call inside its own 100 ms `Future.delayed(...) → _initializeMap(_container!)` window, which threw `NullCheckOperatorException` every frame and froze the UI; (2) the PROD-1955 zine pager's `ValueKey('zine-pager-$pageCount')` re-keyed when `state.items.length` flipped 0 → 1, tearing the whole subtree; (3) the cross-provider listener in `unified_list_provider.dart` fired the optimistic/API-success/`loadLists` triple-update against a tree that was mid-remount, producing layout-time `markNeedsLayout` / `_debugCanPerformMutations` and `RenderBox.size accessed outside of permitted layout` assertions per-frame. PR #432 verification: dev-log Mapbox lifecycle events during an add went from "full dispose + WebGL release + cache clear + re-init cycle every click" to zero; framework assertions went from hundreds-per-frame (freezing) to ~20 single-shot. See [bugfixes/pr-432-zine-add-item-freeze.md](../bugfixes/pr-432-zine-add-item-freeze.md).
- **Why a sheet specifically**: the underlying list page stays mounted the whole time the sheet is open. No route push, no chrome re-resolution, no `unifiedListProvider` cascade fighting a half-mounted widget tree. Adding items optimistically settles cleanly because the listener fires against the same widget tree the user is looking at. Swipe-down / scrim-tap dismiss the sheet without ever re-mounting the page.
- **Why D111's stated rationale doesn't actually hold up in retrospect**:
  - D111 (a) "more items visible at once on mobile" — `DSSheetShell.maxHeightFraction` defaults to 0.92, so the sheet body is effectively the full viewport minus a 20 px drag-handle bar and ~60 px footer. The per-screen rows-visible count differs by ~1 vs. the page; not a deal-breaker.
  - D111 (b) "URL reflects user's location" — true but unused. Nothing deep-links to `/lists/:id/add-items` (no QR codes, no share links, no analytics referrers). The `unifiedListProvider` listener also already fires a duplicate `list_open` deep-link event on the round trip back, which was actively harmful.
  - D111 (c) "everything is a page" — a useful pattern when the page is a navigation destination. Adding an item is a transient action over the list, not a navigation destination. The Lovable reference (`SaveToListSheet`) was always a sheet.
  - D111 (d) "removes awkward double-scroll" — the new sheet has `DSSheetShell` outer scroll only; results list lives inside `Flexible(child: …)`. No double-scroll.
- **What we did NOT revert**: the PROD-1852 visual redesign (Frame 9300) stays. Headline ("Adicionar a [list name]" with the list name underlined), Soko/Ink @ 8 % search-field fill, Lugares/Eventos tab pills with Soko/Blue / Soko/Green accents on active, [D112](#d112-added-state-card-paints-with-the-tab-accent--sokoblue-for-sítios-sokogreen-for-eventos) full-row tint on added rows, [D113](#d113-iconadd-is-a-bare-14-px-lucide-glyph--no-30-px-wrapping-circle-instant-icon-swap) bare 14 px Lucide `circle_plus`/`circle_check` add control, and [`flutter-textfield-isdense-height-collapse`](../learnings/flutter-textfield-isdense-height-collapse.md) workaround all carry over unchanged. Only the outer chrome (Scaffold → `DSSheetShell`) and the dismissal mechanic (`context.pop` → `Navigator.of(context).pop`) differ.
- **What's still owed (PROD-1978)**: the deeper render-time mutation the sheet sidesteps is still there. After the migration, an add still produces ~20 single-fire `markNeedsLayout` / `_debugCanPerformMutations` / `RenderBox.size`-outside-layout assertions. The map widget identity churn (`MapMarker.letter(...)` constructs new instances every build, `_areMarkersEqual` uses identity-`==`, `_fitToMarkers` over-fires) and the degenerate-bbox `fitBounds` failure for 1-pin lists are flagged on PROD-1978 (app-wide map UX overhaul) so the next pass cleans them up properly across hub + list-page + detail.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/add_items_to_list_sheet.dart` (the sheet + helper, ~1208 LOC ported from the page), `lib/features/lists/screens/list_page_screen.dart` `_handleAdd` (now `await showAddItemsToListSheet(...)`), `lib/features/lists/widgets/empty_zine_state.dart` (now `ConsumerWidget`, calls the same helper). The defensive `if (!mounted || _container == null) return;` guard at `mapbox_map_web.dart:207` is independent of this migration and stays — it's correct on its own merits.

### D135: "Adiciona à Soko" pill is Soko/Pink on every surface — chat-bar pill and end-of-scroll pill share the fill (reverts PROD-1959 yellow)

- **Choice**: the "Adiciona à Soko" CTA uses `AppColors.sokoPink` (Soko/Pink, `#FFB8CB`) with `AppColors.sokoInk` icon + label everywhere it renders — `AddToSokoPill` in `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart` (chat bar bottom row) and the left half of `DiscoveryEndActions` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_end_actions.dart` (under the "That's all folks" footer, PROD-1980). One CTA → one fill.
- **What changed**: PROD-1959 / PR #429 recoloured `AddToSokoPill` from Soko/Pink to Soko/Yellow with the rationale that the pairing was "already proven in `bt_sq_ico` and `_weekly_bundle_cover`". PROD-1980 reverts that to Soko/Pink so the chat-bar pill matches the new end-of-scroll pill and the CTA reads as one consistent action on every surface.
- **Why pink**: it's the original token. The reverted yellow undermines the consistency rule below — PROD-1980 introduces a second "Adiciona à Soko" surface, and shipping two different fills for the same CTA on the same screen would make the action feel like two separate things.
- **Consistency rule (new, enforced going forward)**: every CTA that maps to a single user intent + single backend handler (here: tap → `CreateMenuSheet.toggle`) MUST use the same fill on every surface. If a future surface needs visual differentiation (e.g. to imply hierarchy), differentiate via *size* / *position* / *iconography* — not fill colour. Different fills mean different actions.
- **Why "Fala com a Soko" is allowed to be different**: it's a different action class (start a new chat session, not "add"). It uses `sokoInk` bg + `sokoPaper` glyph deliberately, mirroring the chat-bar's active **send** button — same visual signal as "send a message to Soko".
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart` `AddToSokoPill` (Container `decoration.color`); `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_end_actions.dart` `DiscoveryEndActions` (left `_Pill.background`).
- **Tickets**: introduced in PROD-1980. Reverts the PROD-1959 / PR #429 recolour.

### D136: Pin tap on multi-pin maps shows a single-item sheet — no card drawer (PROD-1978)
- **⚠️ Superseded by [D138](#d138-pin-tap-shows-an-anchored-tooltip-replaces-d136-bottom-sheet-prod-2016) (2026-05-20)** — the bottom-sheet flow was replaced by an anchored tooltip rendered next to the pin. The sheet's premise (open full card on tap) was right in spirit but wrong in form: a sheet covers half the map, hides the spatial context the user just gained by zooming in, and forces a sheet-dismiss before the next pin tap. The tooltip stays anchored to the pin, keeps the surrounding map visible, and lets the user move between pins without dismiss-then-tap rituals. `ListItemCardWithLetter`, `_showItemDetailSheet`, and the `DSSheetShell` mounting in `list_map_view.dart` are gone.
- **Original choice (historical)**: tapping a pin on a multi-pin map (`ListMapView` — list cover + `/lists` hub) opened a `DSSheetShell` modal containing the matching item's `ListItemCardWithLetter`. The legacy horizontal card drawer at the bottom of the map was gone. Cluster taps zoomed in instead — they did not open a sheet, because they don't correspond to a single item.
- **Why we removed the drawer**: with clustering active, the drawer's premise ("show me every item that has a pin") stops scaling — a 355-item list rendered ~200 cards in a horizontal `ListView.builder` underneath ~200 pins, and the user couldn't tell which card matched which pin without a pin tap → `_scrollToCard` animation. The pin tap was always the actual interaction. The drawer was just expensive scenery for that interaction.
- **Why a sheet specifically**: matches [D134](#d134-add-items-flow-is-a-bottom-sheet-after-all--reverts-d111) — keeps the underlying map mounted, no route push, no layout churn. Same `DSSheetShell` + `showBottomSheetWithHiddenNav` infrastructure.
- **What's still owed**: empty-state behaviour when the user pans the map to an area with no pins is unhandled — the previous drawer hid itself when there were no items, the new model has nothing to show because no pin was tapped. Refinement deferred per user direction; if it becomes an issue we add a peek banner "Pan to find places nearby" or a re-anchor button.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_map_view.dart` — `_ListMapViewState._onMarkerTapped` calls `_showItemDetailSheet` which builds the `DSSheetShell`. `_ItemsDrawer`, `_cardListController`, `_selectedIndex`, `_scrollToCard`, `_onCardTapped` and all their `_focusLat`/`_focusLng` plumbing for keeping drawer-scroll in sync with selected pin are deleted. The "fit all" floating button (`Icons.fit_screen`) is retained as the way to recover the wide view after panning.

### D137: Cluster bubble colour reflects per-type contents — pure → matches pin colour, mixed → Soko Pink
- **Choice**: on multi-pin maps with clustering enabled, a cluster bubble takes the pin colour when its contents are homogeneous — pure-place cluster → Soko Blue (`mapPinVenue`), pure-event cluster → Soko Green (`mapPinEvent`). When the cluster contains both types, OR when features carry no `item_type` (legacy / fallback), the bubble renders the neutral Soko Pink (`mapClusterFill`).
- **Why**: the cluster bubble is a preview of "what's under here". A pure-place cluster that's already coloured like a place pin lets the user predict the zoom-in result without inspecting. The same goes for pure-event. A neutral colour for mixed clusters is the honest choice — there is no single colour that represents "venues AND events". Picking majority would be misleading at the boundary, splitting the bubble in two would over-design a transient affordance.
- **Implementation**: Mapbox source-layer `clusterProperties` aggregations on the `heyl-pins` source — `event_count` and `place_count` each sum a `['case', ['==', ['get','item_type'], 'X'], 1, 0]` evaluator per feature. The cluster `circle-color` is a `case` expression: both counts > 0 → pink; only `event_count` > 0 → green; only `place_count` > 0 → blue; fallback → pink. Same expression replicated on web (`mapbox_map_web.dart`) and native (`mapbox_map_native.dart`).
- **Depends on**: PROD-1993 — the typed `item_type` field on `SlimMapPin` / `MapPinOut`. Without it, every feature would fall into the untyped fallback and clusters would all render pink.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_web.dart` (source spec + cluster layer paint), `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_native.dart` (same on the Maps Flutter API), `heyl_app/lib/core/theme/app_colors.dart` (tokens). Doc: [features/maps.md § 4](../features/maps.md#4-pin-colour-system-driven-by-item_type).

### D138: Pin tap shows an anchored tooltip — replaces D136 bottom sheet (PROD-2016)

<!-- STALE(2026-05-21): D147 (PROD-2046) replaced the anchor model. The tooltip no longer renders "next to the pin" via edge-clamp + above/below flip — the platform layer eases the camera to bring the tapped pin to a fixed screen target, and the tooltip renders at that anchor every time. Hover-to-show was removed in the same change (panning on every cursor sweep was jarring). See D147 for the current behaviour. The "click promotes hover to sticky" rule below is dead code; nothing sets `_selectionFromHover = true` anymore. The "Edge clamping" rule is replaced by pan-to-target. The button choice + selected-pin enlargement + analytics + A11y bullets below remain accurate. -->

- **Choice**: tapping a pin on any map (cover map, `/lists` hub, zine item aux) opens a small anchored tooltip rendered next to the pin, containing: item name, item type label ("Lugar" / "Evento"), and a **"Ver detalhes"** button that routes to the in-list detail page (or standalone item page outside a list — host-supplied callback). Web also gets hover-to-show: hovering an unclustered pin opens the tooltip immediately with a 150 ms grace timer on pin-leave. Cluster taps still zoom in (no tooltip). Native is click-only — accepted divergence (no hover on touch).
- **Why we superseded the sheet**: the sheet covered the bottom half of the map, hiding exactly the spatial context the user gained by zooming in. Moving between pins required dismiss-then-tap. The anchored tooltip stays in the user's eye-line, lets them sweep across pins (especially on desktop hover), and reads more like an "info point" than a full-screen interrupt. The "View details" button is the one explicit jump into a full detail page — the only thing the sheet was actually for.
- **Why anchored vs fixed-bottom**: anchored to the pin preserves the spatial link — the user knows which pin the info belongs to without reading the title. Fixed-bottom would lose that linkage on a map with overlapping pins, and would re-introduce the half-screen cover problem the sheet had.
- **Click on a hover-opened tooltip promotes it to sticky (it does NOT close)**: hovering opens the tooltip; clicking the same pin while hovering flips the mode flag (`_selectionFromHover = false`) so the tooltip survives the next pin-leave. A *second* click on the same pin closes it (true toggle), as does any click outside. **Why**: the natural intent of clicking a pin you're already hovering is "keep this one open" — without the promotion, the click on a pre-shown tooltip felt like a bug ("why did my click dismiss what I wanted to read?"). Click without prior hover opens directly in sticky mode (same outcome, fewer steps).
- **Selected-pin enlargement**: the tapped pin renders ≈ 1.5× normal radius (12 px vs 8 px) so the user can spot which pin is selected even when the tooltip is large enough to overlap nearby pins. Wired via Mapbox `case` expression on `circle-radius` keyed on a per-feature `selected` boolean property that the shell stamps onto the matching GeoJSON feature.
- **Edge clamping**: tooltip auto-positions — prefer right of pin; flip left if overflow right; shift up if overflow bottom; fall back to fixed bottom-centre when the viewport is narrower than the tooltip itself. Minimum 8 px margin on all sides.
- **Locked-map pin tap stays locked**: tapping a pin in static (`staticByDefault: true`) mode opens the tooltip but **does NOT activate gestures**. The `!widget.interactive` gate on the cluster + leaf click handlers (introduced in PROD-1978 phase 4) was removed.
- **Analytics**: `map_pin_tooltip_open` (PostHog-only, 300 ms stability debounce to filter hover-sweep spam) + `map_pin_view_details_click` (synchronous on button tap, before navigation). `analyticsContext` prop on `MapboxMapWidget` lets each consumer label its surface (`'list_cover'` / `'lists_hub'` / `'zine_item_aux'`).
- **A11y**: Esc closes the tooltip and returns focus to the surrounding map; `Semantics(container: true, liveRegion: true)` announces the title + subtitle to screen readers when the tooltip appears; the "View details" button has explicit `Semantics(button: true)` to override `InkWell`'s implicit role.
- **Tradeoffs we accepted**: a tooltip near densely clustered pins can hide an adjacent pin — by design, tap outside dismisses. We considered showing the tooltip in a fixed corner instead but the spatial-anchor benefit outweighs the occlusion risk.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_widget.dart` — the `MapboxMapWidget` is now a `ConsumerStatefulWidget` (it reads `unifiedAnalyticsProvider` directly). The tooltip's clamping math lives in `_PinTooltipOverlay._resolvePosition`. Hover wiring lives in `mapbox_map_web.dart`'s mouseenter/leave handlers (split into cluster-only-cursor vs unclustered-cursor-plus-tooltip variants). The DOM-overlay path on web and the `PointAnnotationManager` path on native were retired in the same PR — every consumer now uses the single cluster source-layer pipeline.

<!-- Most recent: D138 -->

<!-- STALE(2026-05-27): amended by D180 — typography-only swap was insufficient as a tab affordance per user feedback. Active 42 → 52 px, inactive 22 → 24 px, and a sliding scallop underline now flags the active label. The "animated H1 toggle, not a tab bar" decision still stands; only the visual affordance was strengthened. -->
### D139: Yours/Following is an animated H1 page-title toggle, not a tab bar (PROD-2026)
- **Choice**: on `/yours`, the page H1 is two text labels — `Yours` and `Following` — rendered side-by-side. The active label is 42 px sokoInk; the inactive is 22 px sokoInk @ 35 %. Tapping the inactive one swaps which is large via a 400 ms `Curves.easeInOutCubic` interpolation that tweens font size + opacity together (`heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/yours_following_toggle_header.dart`). The active label has no tap handler; only the inactive one fires the swap.
- **Why not a TabBar / pill row**: TabBars / pill rows separate the *control* from the *content title*. On a page where the H1 IS the active filter ("Yours" or "Following"), that's a redundant control. Folding the toggle into the title preserves the existing canonical 42 px H1 and the existing shelf-title rhythm — surfaces that render the shelf on its own (Discovery home) keep the static `Text(title)` behaviour because the new toggle is wired in via the additive `DiscoveryShelf.titleOverride: Widget?` slot.
- **Why 400 ms `easeInOutCubic`**: same timing the bottom-nav selection swap uses (`discovery_bottom_nav.dart`), so the app's motion grammar stays consistent. Two labels animating in opposite directions read as one fluid swap rather than two independent state changes.
- **Cursor affordance**: the inactive label is wrapped in `MouseRegion(cursor: SystemMouseCursors.click)` so web users get a pointer hover; the GestureDetector alone wouldn't change the cursor.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/yours_following_toggle_header.dart` (the widget); `lib/features/lists/providers/yours_following_mode_provider.dart` (screen-local `StateProvider.autoDispose<YoursFollowingMode>`); `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/discovery_shelf.dart` (the `titleOverride` slot). The page-level header wrapper `_YoursPageHeader` in `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` also mirrors `DiscoveryHeader`'s [D39](#d39-desktop-only-vertical-padding-above-the-soko-wordmark) 48 px desktop-only top pad.

### D140: Following mode's map + calendar are FE-aggregated from `listPublicItemsSlim` (PROD-2026)
- **Choice**: on `/yours` in Following mode, the map + calendar chrome populates from a new `followingHubItemsProvider` that aggregates per-followed-list items client-side rather than calling a single owned-flow endpoint. For every followed list on the shelf (capped at 10), the provider fires `listPublicItemsSlim(listId, limit: 500)` in parallel via `Future.wait`, flattens the responses, FE-filters to the resolved city bbox + visible-month window, runs each `UserListItemSlim` through the existing `slimItemToHeavy` adapter, and emits the same `ListsHubData` shape so the existing `ListMapView` / `ListCalendarView` widgets render unchanged.
- **Why FE aggregation**: the existing `/api/v1/app/users/me/lists/map-pins` + `/api/v1/app/users/me/lists/calendar-events` endpoints BE-scope to "lists the caller owns" (+ optional collaborative). There is no `?include_followed=true` flag and no `/following/map-pins` twin in the OpenAPI spec. We considered (a) showing the owns-only data in Following mode (misleading), (b) leaving Following's map + calendar empty (page-height jump + confusing UX), and (c) FE-aggregating from per-list slim payloads. Option (c) wins because the slim endpoint already has every field the hub widgets read (`latitude`, `longitude`, `title`, `image_url`, `next_occurrence_at`, `occurrence_dates`) and 10 parallel HTTPs is well within budget for an interactive view.
- **Critical: use the PUBLIC slim endpoint, not the owner-scoped one**. `listItemsSlim` (`/lists/{id}/items/slim`) requires ownership and 403s for followers. Followed lists are by definition not owned by the caller. The right endpoint is `listPublicItemsSlim` (`/lists/{id}/public/items/slim`) — same response shape, no auth scope check beyond the list being public. See learning [`learnings/list-items-slim-owner-vs-public.md`](../learnings/list-items-slim-owner-vs-public.md).
- **Per-occurrence venue compromise**: the slim payload doesn't carry per-occurrence venue coordinates (multi-venue events get one pin at the event's listed lat/lng). Same compromise the owned `_calendarEventToSyntheticItem` makes for occurrences without venues — acceptable for v1.
- **Page-height stability**: both modes render the same map + calendar chrome (always visible). The mode-change `ref.listen` invalidates both `listsHubItemsProvider` AND `followingHubItemsProvider` so flipping back and forth refetches cleanly (covers cases like the user followed / unfollowed a list while on Yours).
- **Future-proofing**: when the BE adds a real `/following/map-pins` (+ calendar twin), drop the FE aggregator and switch the Following branch to it. The widget contract (`ListsHubData`) is identical so only the provider needs replacing.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/providers/following_hub_items_provider.dart`; consumed by `_ListsHubDefaultBody` in `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` via a mode-aware ternary on `listsHubItemsProvider` vs `followingHubItemsProvider`.

### D141: "All" pill is a shared opt-in across Discovery and Lists search (PROD-2026)
- **Choice**: the shared `SearchCategoryTabs` widget (used by both Discovery and Lists search) accepts `includeAll: bool = false`. When `true`, an "All" pill prepends the existing Zines / Eventos / Sítios row. **Both** `DiscoverySearchOverlay` and `ListsSearchOverlay` pass `true`, so both surfaces render a 4-pill row. The flag exists so a future surface can opt out — not because either of today's two consumers does.
- **Why surface "All" on both**: the affordance is identical on both surfaces (search across all three entity types) and the discovery-vs-yours visual chrome is already aligned via the shared `SearchOverlay` widget. Splitting on the pill row would make the two surfaces feel like separate products. (Earlier in PROD-2026 only Lists exposed "All"; PR-time review flipped Discovery to opt in too once we confirmed Discovery's flat-list results shape made the fan-out trivial.)
- **Enum + exhaustive switches**: `DiscoverySearchCategory` is the shared enum. PROD-2026 added `all` as a fourth variant. Both `discoverySearchResultsProvider` and `listsSearchResultsProvider` switch on this enum; Dart requires the switches to be exhaustive.
  - Discovery's switch handles `all` by fanning out `_searchLists` + `_searchEvents` + `_searchPlaces` in parallel via `Future.wait` (all three legs share the same `CancelToken` so a query / category change tears them down together) and concatenating the rows in Zines → Events → Places order. Each leg returns `List<SearchResultRow>` already; merging is a `[...lists, ...events, ...places]` spread.
  - Lists' switch handles `all` by fanning out `listMyLists` + `searchSavedItems(event)` + `searchSavedItems(place)` in parallel and bundling them into a new `ListsSearchResultsAll` sealed variant; the body flattens that variant into the same `SearchResultCard` order.
- **Why two different shapes**: Discovery's results provider already returns a flat `List<SearchResultRow>` (the section renders them as a single grid), so concatenation is the natural shape. Lists' results provider returns a sealed union per category to drive per-card routing differences (zines push to `/lists/:id`, events to `/events/:id`, places to `/venues/:id`); the "All" variant preserves the per-type routing by keeping the three lists separate inside one wrapper.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/search_category_tabs.dart` (the `includeAll` flag); `lib/shared/widgets/search_overlay.dart` (`includeAllCategory` passthrough); `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_search_overlay.dart` and `lib/features/lists/widgets/lists_search_overlay.dart` (both pass `true`); `lib/features/discovery/providers/search_results_provider.dart` (concatenation fan-out for `case .all`); `lib/features/lists/providers/lists_search_results_provider.dart` (sealed-variant fan-out for `case .all`).

### D142: Auto-list filter is a screen-local toggle, not persisted (PROD-2026)
- **Choice**: the "show / hide auto-generated lists" affordance on `/yours` (Yours mode only) is backed by a screen-local `StateProvider.autoDispose<bool>` that defaults to `true` (auto lists visible). It resets to `true` every time the user leaves and re-enters the page. No SharedPreferences write.
- **Why no persistence**: the hub already follows a "city scope persists, ephemeral filters don't" rule — the city / location pill writes to SharedPreferences via `cityScopeProvider`, but the search query, search-category pill, search-open state, etc. are all autoDispose providers that reset between visits. The auto-list filter is in the second class — a quick screen-local toggle, not a profile-level preference. Persisting it would (a) need a new SharedPreferences key + migration, (b) bias new users into a hidden state if they ever toggled it once, and (c) diverge from the rest of the hub's filter rhythm. Worth revisiting if user feedback shows people want it sticky.
- **What gets filtered**: when the toggle is off, `yoursShelfProvider.build()` drops any `UserList` where `isSystemManaged` is true (i.e. `systemKind != null` — Saved Items, weekly bundle mirror, Instagram-share imports, onboarding seed lists). Provider-level filtering keeps the shelf's `itemCount` accurate so the empty-state card renders correctly when the user owns only auto lists.
- **UI placement**: subtle `TextButton.icon` with the eye / eye-off Lucide glyph + "Show / Hide auto-generated lists" label, sokoInk @ 60 %, centered under the shelf row. Visible only in Yours mode (Following lists aren't system-managed, and the guest empty card has nothing to filter). The label flips to communicate the action: when auto lists are showing, the button says "Hide …" (tap to hide); when hidden, "Show …".
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/providers/auto_lists_filter_provider.dart` (the StateProvider); `lib/features/discovery/providers/yours_shelf_provider.dart` (the filter application); `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (`_AutoListFilterButton` widget).

### D143: Map pin tooltip uses DS BtSqIco + shared SokoTag pill, and the cover map "Ver detalhes" jumps direct to in-list detail (PROD-2033)

- **Choice**: the anchored pin tooltip ([D138](#d138-pin-tap-shows-an-anchored-tooltip-replaces-d136-bottom-sheet-prod-2016)) now uses the same design-system primitives the detail pages use:
  - The "Ver detalhes" CTA is `BtSqIco(variant: selected)` (Soko/Pink, 40 px tall, 6 px radius) — was a bespoke pink container with raw padding.
  - The item-type label ("Sítio" / "Evento") is rendered as a `SokoTag` pill (radius 2, padding 6/2, Zalando Sans Light 14 px Soko/Ink) — was a plain `Text` with `.withValues(alpha: 0.72)` subtitle styling. Tag background is keyed off `marker.category`: `MapMarkerCategory.venue` → `AppColors.sokoVenueAccent` (Soko/Purple), `MapMarkerCategory.event` → `AppColors.sokoEventAccent` (Soko/Blue) — same primitive the user sees on the page they navigate to.
  - The item-name title bumps from 14 px to 18 px bold (`height: 1.2`) so the name reads as the primary content.
  - Tooltip width 220 → 240 px so longer two-line titles still ellipsize cleanly at the larger size. Fixed-height positioning math: 144 px with the button, 80 px without (when `MapPinTooltipContent.showViewDetails: false` per PROD-2017).
- **Why use the DS primitives**: the tooltip is a "preview card for the detail page" — same item-type pill the detail page renders below the name, same primary-action button the rest of the app uses. The bespoke chrome wasn't a deliberate choice in PROD-2016, just unaddressed scope. Re-using `BtSqIco` + `SokoTag` removes a fork the user can perceive (subtle but real — the old button's font weight, radius, and padding all diverged slightly from every other CTA in the app).
- **Where the SokoTag lives**: new shared widget `lib/shared/widgets/soko_tag.dart`. Replaces three identical private `_Tag` classes that had been duplicated across `card_tag_row.dart` (chat cards), `venue_tag_row.dart` (venue detail), and `event_tag_row.dart` (event detail). All three callers and the new tooltip use the shared primitive now.
- **Cover map "Ver detalhes" routing change**: tapping a pin on the **list cover map** (zine view, page 0) and clicking "Ver detalhes" now opens the **in-list detail page directly** (`/lists/<listId>/venues/<id>` or `/lists/<listId>/events/<id>`), the same destination the zine item card's "Ver mais" button leads to. Previously it jumped the zine `TurnPageController` to the matching item page, requiring a second tap on "Ver mais" to reach the detail. One step instead of two.
- **Why route directly**: the user's intent when clicking a tooltip's "Ver detalhes" is to see the detail page — the zine pager middle step was an artifact of the old `onItemTap` plumbing (which existed only to jump the pager), not a deliberate UX choice. The zine item card's tap-anywhere body (and the "Vê mais" button on it) are unchanged — the in-pager flow is still available for users who got there a different way.
- **Slim-pin → heavy-item lookup**: the cover map gets `SlimMapPin` payloads which carry `venueId` but not `eventId`. The cover's `onItemTap` resolves the slim pin to its full heavy `UserListItem` in `state.items` (the zine's loaded list) before pushing — that's where `eventId` comes from for event pins. If the slim pin's `item_id` hasn't been appended yet by the background tail-fetch (large lists, slow networks), the helper silently no-ops; user can re-tap once items finish loading.
- **Shared helper `openInListItemDetail`** (`lib/features/lists/utils/open_in_list_item_detail.dart`): single source of truth for the push + `DetailSiblings` payload. Called by both the cover map's `onItemTap` and the zine item card's `_openInListDetail` ("Vê mais") so the two surfaces can't drift.
- **Critical web-only bug from the routing change**: `context.push` synchronously after `onDismiss()` (the `setState` that closes the tooltip overlay) corrupted the widget tree on web. Both the cover card and the cover map rendered as empty space after pop-back. Fixed by deferring the push to `WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback`. See [`learnings/context-push-after-setstate-build-scope-cascade.md`](../learnings/context-push-after-setstate-build-scope-cascade.md) — strong grep signature so future devs hitting "Tried to build dirty widget in the wrong build scope" cascades find the fix.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_widget.dart` (the `_PinTooltipOverlay` rewrite + deferred push), `lib/shared/widgets/soko_tag.dart` (the shared primitive), `lib/features/lists/utils/open_in_list_item_detail.dart` (the route helper), `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_cover_page.dart` (the cover map `onItemTap`). Removed: `_jumpToItem` from `_ListZineViewState` and the `onJumpToItem` plumbing from `ListZineCoverAux` (the cover map was the only caller).

### D144: Add-to-list sheet batches chip toggles into a single Save-time commit
- **Choice**: in `AddToListSheet`, tapping a per-row chip only stages selection locally (writes to `ListMembershipStaging.selected`). No API call, no per-row snackbar, no provider write. The diff against `.initial` (snapshot taken when the sheet opens) is committed when the user presses the commit button (labelled **"Confirm"** / **"Confirmar"** since PROD-2138 — was "Guardar"/"Salvar"; see [D206](#d206-add-to-zine-sheet--destructive-remover-de-todas-link-trashcan--neutral-confirmar-commit-button-prod-2138)) — `_persistNoteAndClose` fires `addToListsOptimistic` and `removeFromLists` in parallel and surfaces **one** consolidated snackbar (`addToListSuccess` / `addToListRemoveSuccess` / `addToListUpdateSuccess`). No changes → silent close.
- **Why**: the previous per-row optimistic-commit flow was technically correct but felt chatty — every tap fired a snackbar and a backend write, so toggling a few rows produced a stack of overlapping toasts and a quick succession of detail-screen refreshes. Batching aligns the sheet's commit model with how users mentally use it ("I'm staging which lists this goes into, then I save once"), and converts an interaction many users were treating as draft anyway into the actual implementation.
- **Failure behaviour**: if any leg of the batch fails (`addToListsOptimistic.onError` or `removeFromLists` returns `false`), a single `addToListError` snackbar fires and the sheet still closes; the user reopens to retry. We keep this simple because the underlying optimistic providers already give the UI immediate feedback — the snackbar is the only thing the user sees stutter.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/add_to_list_sheet.dart` — `_togglePerRow` (local-only), `_persistNoteAndClose` (the batched commit), `_summaryMessage` (snackbar copy selection). Staging lives in `lib/features/lists/utils/list_membership_staging.dart` so a late `contains_*` fill cannot re-tick a row the user already tapped (PROD-4185).

### D145: Add-to-list sheet shows system-managed lists only when the item is already saved (removal-only)
- **Choice**: `UserList.isSystemManaged` lists (`saved_items`, `from_instagram_share`, `from_onboarding`, `weekly_bundle`) are **filtered out** of the browseable "Save to" section in `AddToListSheet`. They appear in the pre-selected "Already saved in" section only when the current item is already a member, so the user can untick to remove. Tapping a system row in non-member state is a no-op.
- **Why**: these lists are backend-managed — they auto-populate from external signals (Instagram shares, onboarding choices, weekly bundle, the per-user Saved Items). Letting the user manually add to them from this sheet would muddy the "system-managed" contract and confuse the user about why the same item appears with multiple sources. Removal is allowed because users do legitimately want to take items out of auto-populated lists.
- **Default-list guard**: `_initializeListSelection`'s auto-preselect (when no list is yet selected) skips system-managed defaults **and** skips the path entirely if the user already staged a tap/Clear-all (`ListMembershipStaging.userToggled`) — an empty selection after deselecting Saved Events is not a "new item" (PROD-4185).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/add_to_list_sheet.dart` — `_togglePerRow` (`if (list.isSystemManaged && !isSelected) return;`), `_buildListContent` (filters `!l.isSystemManaged` from `otherLists` and from search results), `_initializeListSelection` (default-list guard).

### D146: Add-to-list "Create-list" CTA uses the dark Soko button, not the pink primary
- **Choice**: the Create-new-list affordance at the top of `AddToListSheet` (above the search field) is a dark Soko `ElevatedButton.icon` (`AppColors.sokoDark` background, `Colors.white` foreground, 44 px tall, 6 px radius, hugs text + centered) — same token pairing as the canonical confirm button in `user_profiling_flow_screen.dart`. The sheet's bottom Save button stays pink (`AppColors.sokoPink` + `sokoInk`).
- **Why**: the sheet has two primary actions — "create a list to add this to" (top) and "save" (bottom). Two pink CTAs on the same surface compete for visual primacy and confuse the eye. The Save action is what the user *will most often do*, so it gets the pink. Create-list is a secondary primary — frequent enough to live above the fold and styled with a confident solid fill, but visually subordinate to Save. The dark variant is the existing design-system answer to that contrast need.
- **Layout**: the button sits at the very top of the sheet body, **above** the search field. Earlier iterations placed it below the search with a dashed-border secondary style (the legacy `_CreateNewListRow`); the new ordering surfaces the "I don't have a list for this yet, make one" path as the first thing the user sees after the item header.
- **Related**: the underlined "Clear all" text link on the "Already saved in" section header uses the same `AppColors.sokoDark` (with `decorationColor: AppColors.sokoDark`) so the two dark accents inside the sheet visually rhyme.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/add_to_list_sheet.dart` — `_CreateNewListButton` widget; body assembly in `AddToListSheet.build` (Column order: header → SizedBox(12) → CreateButton → SizedBox(10) → SearchField → SizedBox(10) → list); `_ClearAllLink` widget for the matching text link.

### D147: Daily/Weekly Drop sections hide entirely when the BE returns `unsupported_city` — no CTA card (PROD-2045)
- **Choice**: when either `dailyDropProvider` or `weeklyBundleProvider` flips `isUnsupportedCity = true` (BE returned `status: "unsupported_city"`), the corresponding `DailyDropSection._buildBody` / `WeeklyBundleSection._buildBody` returns `SizedBox.shrink()`. The surrounding `AnimatedSize` collapses the slot. **No explanatory CTA is rendered.**
- **Why**: PROD-2045's original ticket spec called for an explanatory CTA ("We're not live in this city yet"). After iterating in-session, the choice was to hide both sections instead — and rely on the existing Discovery New-City hero (`DiscoveryNewCitySection`, PROD-1849) to carry the "this city isn't fully covered" message. Two CTAs in the same page area produced redundant signal; collapsing the drops slots keeps the page concise and lets the New-City hero own the message.
- **The signal isn't lost — analytics still fires.** Each notifier carries a `_unsupportedCityImpressionLogged` bool that gates a once-per-session `trackUnsupportedCityImpression(surface: 'daily' | 'weekly', city_id, lat, lng)` call. The event is named "impression" even though no UI is shown — it sizes the unsupported-drops volume for market-expansion prioritisation, which is what the original AC asked for.
- **Branch order in `_buildBody`**: `isUnsupportedCity` checked BEFORE `isCtaProfiling` and any skeleton/ready branch. A new user (< 3 days) in an unsupported city sees neither the profiling CTA nor a drop — both surfaces collapse. The Discovery New-City hero carries the message.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/daily_drop_section.dart` (`_buildBody` first branch), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/weekly_bundle_section.dart` (`_buildBody` first branch). Analytics: `lib/core/services/unified_analytics_service.dart::trackUnsupportedCityImpression`. Notifier wiring: `lib/features/daily_drop/providers/daily_drop_provider.dart` and `lib/features/weekly_bundle/providers/weekly_bundle_provider.dart`.
- **Earlier iterations (rejected mid-session)**: (a) explanatory CTA per surface (two separate cards); (b) single shared `UnsupportedDropsCtaSection` between Daily and Weekly that linked to `CreateMenuSheet` to invite contributions. Both removed in favour of the hide-only approach.

### D148: Soko-Ink scallop motif rendered above the chat composer surfaces
- **Choice**: the `ScallopDivider` (Soko-Ink scalloped tile, Figma `3932:1986`, 18.19 × 9 px tiled across the available width) is rendered immediately above each chat-composer surface in the app:
  - `/chat` conversation state — above `MessageInput` in `chat_screen.dart`'s bottom section (16 px horizontal inset to match the input's padding, 8 px bottom gap before the input).
  - `/chat` welcome/empty state — above the `inputWidget` slot inside `WelcomeView`, with an 8 px gap.
  - Discovery full-screen typeahead overlay (`DiscoveryTypeaheadOverlay`) — above the bottom-pinned `ChatBarTopRow` (16 px horizontal inset, 8 px bottom gap).
- **Why**: anchors every "talk to Soko" surface to the same Soko-Ink scallop motif the Discovery section divider already uses — gives the composer a consistent decorative top edge across the three chat-flavoured entry points (welcome, conversation, typeahead) without introducing a new design primitive.
- **Subsequent change**: the original list also included the Discovery inline search overlay (`DiscoverySearchOverlay`, the `CityActionBar` swap). That was removed because `DiscoveryScreen` already renders a page-level `DiscoverySectionDivider` 30 px above the action bar, so the inline scallop visibly duplicated it when the search panel opened. See [D168](#d168-discovery-inline-search-overlay-does-not-render-its-own-scallop--the-page-level-divider-already-provides-one).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/chat/screens/chat_screen.dart` (conversation-state composer), `lib/features/chat/widgets/welcome_view.dart` (empty state), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_typeahead_overlay.dart` (full-screen). Tile widget: `lib/shared/widgets/scallop_divider.dart`.

### D149: Calendar layout is identical on mobile and desktop (PROD-2018)
- **Choice**: `ListCalendarView` renders the same rounded-square day cells with the same density-pink fills on every viewport. The previous responsive split (mobile = dot-only cells, desktop = in-cell preview chips) was dropped.
- **Why**: the mobile variant was the weaker UX — a dot can't disambiguate "1 event" vs "5 events". An identical grid reads as more intentional and lets the density signal (D151) carry the same information across screens.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_calendar_view.dart`. Five mount sites inherit the layout: Yours hub, zine cover, zine item per-event, zine venue events-here, list view body.

### D150: Past days are always greyed; pink density signals only current and future
- **Choice**: past-day cells (`cellDay < today`, strict) render with `sokoShade3` text and no pink fill — regardless of how many events they had. Past-with-events get a fill so they're distinguishable from past-empty days; pink is reserved for current and future days.
- **Amended 2026-09-15** — the past-with-events fill is **one more step of `sokoInk8` over the card**, not the solid `sokoShade4` (`#C1B2B5`) this entry originally specified. `sokoShade4` against the rebuilt `sokoInk8` card is roughly 3x the contrast of the pink steps beside it, so a finished day read *heavier* than an upcoming one — the exact inversion this decision exists to prevent. The decision itself is unchanged; only the token is. See [D356](#d356-the-library-calendar-is-rebuilt-from-measured-figma-values-and-its-agenda-rows-are-the-shared-bundle-row-2026-09-15-no-ticket).
- **Why**: a fully-pink calendar (past + future highlighted equally) hides which days the user can still act on. Greying the past creates an unambiguous hierarchy — the eye lands on cells that matter. First-pass rendered past-events with a faded pink fill; user feedback ("past should be grey") drove the reorder of the cell-state branches: `isSelected → isPast → hasEvents → isToday`.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_calendar_view.dart::_buildDayCell`. Token: `AppColors.sokoInk8` (was `AppColors.sokoShade4`).

### D151: Calendar has three pink density shades for 1 / 2 / 3+ events on a day (PROD-2018)
- **Choice**: cells with events render with one of three pink fills, scaled by event count: 1 event → `Color.lerp(sokoPink, sokoPaper, 0.65)` (lightest), 2 events → `sokoPink` (`#FFB8CB`), 3+ events → `sokoPinkMiddle` (`#EF8EA8`). Same hue family; only saturation/brightness changes.
- **Why**: a single-pink calendar can't communicate "Saturday is packed" at a glance. Three discrete shades give a fast density read without adding numerical chips or count badges. The lerp-derived "deep pink" (`Color.lerp(sokoPink, sokoInk, 0.3)` ≈ `#C58294`) read muddy in early prototypes — `sokoPinkMiddle` was added as a first-class design token to match the Figma palette.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_calendar_view.dart::_pinForEventCount`. Token: `AppColors.sokoPinkMiddle`. See [`ui/design-tokens.md`](design-tokens.md#soko-palette).

### D152: Shared `openItemDetail` helper decides list-scoped vs standalone routes (PROD-2018)
- **Choice**: a single helper at `lib/core/router/list_item_routes.dart` decides whether a tapped `UserListItem` opens its detail page list-scoped (`/lists/<listId>/events/<id>`) or standalone (`/events/<id>`). The rule: if `item.listId` is non-empty, route list-scoped; otherwise standalone. Used by the calendar agenda card, the hub calendar tap, the zine per-event calendar tap, the zine venue events-here calendar tap, and the hub map pin tooltip's "View details" button.
- **Why**: previously, three near-duplicate inline routers each rebuilt the same `if (listId.isEmpty) … else …` logic at the call site (calendar block, hub screen, zine item page). Adding a fourth caller (the agenda card whole-row tap) would have grown the drift. The hub map pin tooltip's "View details" was silently inert because the hub didn't supply an `onItemTap` — wiring it through the helper closed that gap as a side effect.
- **Subtlety**: the cover map pin tooltip uses a *different* helper, [`openInListItemDetail`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/utils/open_in_list_item_detail.dart) (PROD-2033), which always routes list-scoped AND attaches a `DetailSiblings` payload for prev/next swipe. Don't merge the two — they have different semantics. The new helper is for "decide between list-scoped and standalone"; the cover helper is for "list-scoped with siblings".
- **Where it lives**: `lib/core/router/list_item_routes.dart::openItemDetail`. Callers wired in `list_page_calendar_block.dart`, `lists_hub_screen.dart`, `zine/list_zine_item_page.dart`, plus the hub `ListMapView`'s `onItemTap`.

### D153: A controlled-prop without a callback is an anti-pattern — widget owns state, syncs via `didUpdateWidget` (PROD-2018)
- **Choice**: stateful widgets that expose both a "current value" prop AND an optional `onChanged` callback should own the value locally. The prop seeds initial state in `initState`; later prop changes are picked up in `didUpdateWidget` by comparing against the *old* prop (not local state). The callback, when present, is fired but is not load-bearing.
- **Why**: the previous "controlled component" shape — `effectiveValue => widget.value ?? _value` — silently breaks on any mount site that passes the value without a callback. Local taps mutate `_value` but `effectiveValue` keeps returning the parent's frozen prop, so the UI appears dead. This shipped in `ListCalendarView` for ~5 months before being surfaced by the PROD-2018 redesign's visual QA. Two of the four mount sites had dead chevrons.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_calendar_view.dart` — reference implementation. The pattern doc with the trap explained is [`learnings/flutter-controlled-prop-needs-callback-or-local-state.md`](../learnings/flutter-controlled-prop-needs-callback-or-local-state.md).

### D154: Map pin tooltip — pan-to-fixed-target placement (PROD-2046)

- **Choice**: the anchored pin tooltip ([D138](#d138-pin-tap-shows-an-anchored-tooltip-replaces-d136-bottom-sheet-prod-2016), [D143](#d143-map-pin-tooltip-uses-ds-btsqico--shared-sokotag-pill-and-the-cover-map-ver-detalhes-jumps-direct-to-in-list-detail-prod-2033)) no longer computes its position by edge-clamping against the pin's current screen pixel. Instead, on every pin tap the **platform layer EASES the camera** so the tapped pin lands at a fixed screen target — `(viewport.width / 2, viewport.height * 0.55)`, slightly below centre to leave room for the tooltip above. The tooltip then renders at that constant anchor every time, with the notch always centred on the pin by construction.
- **Why we threw away the edge-clamp + above/below flip**: QA on the earlier algorithm showed three independent sources of perceived inconsistency: (a) above-vs-below flip when the pin was near a vertical edge of the viewport, (b) horizontal edge-clamp shifts when the pin was near a left/right edge — and the `arrowOffsetX` slid to the card's safe-arrow inset, so the notch no longer pointed at the pin, (c) the Mapbox click event's `pixel` is the user's CLICK point (not the pin's geometric centre), so even projecting pin coordinates from the click introduced per-click offsets. Three small symptoms, one user-perceivable conclusion: "the tooltip moves around depending on where I click". Option A collapses all three into a single guaranteed placement.
- **What changed in the geometry math**: `_PinTooltipOverlay._resolvePosition` is now a 3-line function — `cardLeft = anchor.dx - _width/2`, `boxBottom = viewport.height - anchor.dy + _apexGap`, `arrowOffsetX = (_width - _arrowWidth) / 2`. Note: **`Positioned.bottom`, not `Positioned.top`** — the Column grows UPWARD from the apex point so the card's actual content height (1- vs 2-line title, button vs no-button) doesn't affect notch placement. The old `_heightWithButton = 144` / `_heightNoButton = 80` math constants were dropped — they were estimates that diverged from actual rendered height by ~28 px, leaving a phantom gap between the math-computed bottom and the visible bottom.
- **Hover-to-open removed**: D138's web hover affordance (150 ms dwell to open) is gone. Panning the camera on every cursor sweep is jarring, so PROD-2046 made click the only way to open a tooltip on every platform. The hover-exit / tooltip-hover handlers stay as inert safety nets (early-return when `_selectionFromHover` is false; nothing sets it now).
- **Tooltip opens AFTER the ease, not during**: a `Timer(_pinEaseDurationMs = 250)` defers the `onMarkerTap` callback to the shell until the camera has settled — the tooltip never renders at a mid-animation position. A subsequent tap cancels the pending timer so a fast double-tap on different pins surfaces only the most recent one's tooltip.
- **Old tooltip closes BEFORE the new ease starts**: on a pin-to-pin tap (existing tooltip already open), the platform layer calls `widget.onMapTapOutside?.call()` (which the shell wires to `_closeTooltip`) before kicking off the new `easeTo`/`flyTo`. Otherwise the user sees the old tooltip floating in place with stale content during the ~250 ms pan, then snapping to the new content — read as a glitch.
- **Move-suppress tail**: `_suppressMoveCallback` is held for an extra 100 ms (`_moveSuppressTailMs`) after the tooltip-show timer fires. Mapbox's trailing `moveend` can land a few ms past the nominal ease duration; clearing the suppress flag in the same callback that opens the tooltip created a race where the trailing event dismissed the tooltip we just opened.
- **Pin-centre projection**: as a sibling fix, the `pixel` forwarded via `onMarkerTap` is no longer the click point. Web: `_map.project([match.lng, match.lat])`. Native: `mapboxMap.pixelForCoordinate(Point(coordinates: Position(lng, lat)))`. Same anchor for every click within a pin's hit area — which combined with the camera-pan above means a tap is fully deterministic from "I tapped pin X" to "tooltip shows at fixed location". See learning [`learnings/mapbox-click-anchor-is-not-pin-centre.md`](../learnings/mapbox-click-anchor-is-not-pin-centre.md).
- **Hub map `onItemTap` wiring**: the `/lists` ("Yours") hub now passes `openItemDetail(context, ref, item)` ([D152](#d152-shared-openitemdetail-helper-decides-list-scoped-vs-standalone-routes-prod-2018)) as `onItemTap` — the canonical helper that routes list-scoped when the item carries a `listId` and standalone otherwise. This is the same helper the calendar agenda and hub calendar use, so the four hub-tappable surfaces share one destination rule. Hub items are deduped across the user's lists by `(lat, lng)` so the backing `UserListItem`'s `list_id` is whichever list happened to be first in iteration order — accepted: matches the rest of the codebase rather than diverging into a standalone-only hub path. PROD-2046 originally implemented this as a standalone-only helper (`openStandaloneItemDetail`) but rebase against develop's PROD-2018 follow-up surfaced the canonical helper and the orphan was dropped.
- **A tooltip-rendering quirk surfaced and fixed**: `_TooltipArrow` no longer carries a `pointsDown` flag (always points down — card is always above). `_TooltipArrowPainter` is a 4-line `CustomPainter` that draws a solid triangle (no separate shadow — the card's `BoxShadow` provides enough visual anchoring; a blurred shadow on a tiny triangle read as noise).
- **Trade-off accepted**: a 250 ms `easeTo` animation runs on every pin tap. Mild and faster than the typical "open a panel" interaction, but a noticeable departure from the original "instant-tooltip-where-the-pin-is" feel. The consistency win (tooltip looks identical for every pin) is the load-bearing reason — confirmed with the user during PROD-2046 implementation.
- **Where it lives**:
  - Platform layer: `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_web.dart` (`_unclusteredClickCb` — `easeTo` with `offset`, `_pendingTooltipShow` timer, `_suppressMoveCallback`) and `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_native.dart` (`_easeAndShowTooltip` — geographic-centre math via `pixelForCoordinate` + `coordinateForPixel`, then `flyTo`).
  - Shell: `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_widget.dart::_PinTooltipOverlay` (`_resolvePosition`, `_TooltipArrow`, `_TooltipArrowPainter`).
  - Hub wiring: `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (`onItemTap` on the `ListMapView`) → `lib/core/router/list_item_routes.dart::openItemDetail`.
  - Constants in shell: `_apexGap = 10`, `_arrowSize = 8`, `_arrowWidth = 16`, `_width = 240`. Constants in platform layers: `_pinTargetYFraction = 0.55`, `_pinEaseDurationMs = 250`, `_moveSuppressTailMs = 100`.

### D155: Zine/Lista view changer is an icon-only side-by-side segmented control (PROD-2029)
- **Choice**: the view-mode switcher on the list page header is two 40×40 icon-only pills (`book_open` for Zine, `list` for Lista) inside a radius-6 Soko/Shade5 outer chrome. Both options are always visible; the active pill is filled Soko/Ink with a Soko/Paper icon and bleeds **edge-to-edge** with the outer chrome (no inner padding). The inactive pill is transparent with the inactive Soko/Ink icon at 60 % alpha. Tapping the inactive pill flips the mode; tapping the active pill is a no-op.
- **Why**: the previous implementation was a single button that hid the inactive option — clicking flipped to the other mode, but a first-time user couldn't tell that "Lista" existed without tapping. A segmented control with both options always visible makes the affordance discoverable. Icon-only (no text labels) keeps the control compact enough to sit alongside the other 40 px header action chips without crowding the action row.
- **Edge-to-edge active fill**: the outer chrome (`ClipRRect` + `Container(color: sokoShade5)`) has NO padding. Each pill is a 40×40 child of the Row; the active pill's Soko/Ink fill extends to the outer rounded corners. Earlier iterations had a 4 px outer padding + 4 px gap between pills (visible breathing room around the active pill) — user feedback was that the brown should fill the whole half.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine_lista_toggle.dart` — `ZineListaToggle` (now `StatelessWidget`) + `_ModePill`. Localisation keys `listPageHeaderToggleZine` / `listPageHeaderToggleLista` remain in use as `Icon.semanticLabel`s.

### D156: Visibility chip on the list-page action row is owner-tappable to flip public ↔ private (PROD-2029)
- **Choice**: the public/private chip rendered between the view-mode toggle and the right-side action buttons in `_ActionRow` is now interactive for owners. Tapping invokes the same `onToggleVisibility` handler the pencil dropdown uses, which routes through `list_page_screen.dart::_handleToggleVisibility` → `_setVisibility` → optimistic update + PATCH + the existing `listVisibilityChangedPublic` / `listVisibilityChangedPrivate` snackbar. Press-scale animation (0.96 / 150 ms) matches `_HeaderActionButton`.
- **Why**: previously the chip was a read-only status indicator and flipping visibility was buried two surfaces deep — either via edit-mode's full `ListVisibilityToggle` or via the pencil dropdown's "Make zine public/private" item. Promoting the flip to a direct tap on the chip cuts the most common action down to one tap. The chip is still owner-only — non-owners see no `onTap` wired and the chip stays a passive label.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart` — `_VisibilityChip` (now `StatefulWidget` with `onTap` + press-scale state). The flip flow itself is unchanged.

### D157: Event/venue accent = the OTHER entity's surface colour (PROD-2042 Wave 2)
- **Choice**: in `AppColors`, the semantic accent aliases re-point so an entity's accent is the colour of the OTHER entity's surface:
  - `sokoEventAccent = sokoVenue` (= `sokoBlue`) — event tags / chips now use the venue's surface blue. Visually unchanged (it already resolved to `sokoBlue` via a direct reference), but the alias makes the swap rule explicit.
  - `sokoVenueAccent = sokoEvent` (= `sokoGreen`) — venue tags / chips flip from purple to green. **Visible change** on every venue tag-row / chip / map-pin tooltip across the app.
- **Why**: the previous binding (`sokoVenueAccent = sokoPurple`) had no semantic rationale — it was a leftover from earlier iterations. The new rule visually links the two entity types ("an event card carries a venue-coloured chip and vice-versa") without merging them, and makes the alias structure trivially memorable.
- **Affected venue surfaces**: venue detail page tag-row (`venue_tag_row.dart`), chat venue cards (`venue_card.dart`), chat place cards when venue (`place_card_with_letter.dart`), shared card tag-row (`card_tag_row.dart`), map-pin tooltip tag chip (`mapbox_map_widget.dart::_tagBackground`).
- **`sokoPurple` retained**: still used by `zine_cover_recipe.dart` for the "purple" zine-cover variant — the token survives, just no longer aliased as a semantic venue accent. Comment on `sokoPurple` updated to flag the retired binding.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/core/theme/app_colors.dart` (the `sokoEvent` / `sokoEventAccent` / `sokoVenue` / `sokoVenueAccent` alias block).

### D158: Modal place-card chrome (selected border + view-details pill) uses Soko/Ink, not the per-type accent (PROD-2042 Wave 2)
- **Choice**: in `PlaceCardWithLetter` (the chat place-card used by the places-modal carousel), two chrome affordances use `AppColors.sokoInk` regardless of the card's per-type accent:
  - **Selected card border + shadow tint**: `Border.all(color: AppColors.sokoInk, width: 2)` + `BoxShadow(color: AppColors.sokoInk.withValues(alpha: 0.2))` when `isSelected` is true. Previously used the per-type `primaryColor` (blue on green event surfaces; green on blue venue surfaces post-D157).
  - **View-Details pill** (the `Icons.open_in_new` button on the card): pill background `AppColors.sokoInk.withValues(alpha: 0.1)` + icon `AppColors.sokoInk`. Previously used the per-type accent.
- **Why**: D157's accent swap exposed a contrast problem — a blue border on a green event surface (or a green border on a blue venue surface) reads as visual noise. These two affordances are CHROME, not type-coded: they answer "is this card selected?" and "open the page". Soko/Ink reads cleanly on both event-green and venue-blue surfaces and matches the bookmark button's existing ink tint, so the two right-edge controls visually rhyme.
- **Not changed**: the letter-badge circle (top-left, "A"/"B"/...) still uses the per-type accent — it's a type-coded affordance (matches the pin colour on the map), not chrome. The bookmark button keeps its existing tint logic (`primaryColor` when saved, `sokoInk` when not).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/chat/widgets/place_card_with_letter.dart` — `Border.all` + `BoxShadow.color` in the outer `AnimatedContainer` decoration; pill `decoration.color` + `Icon.color` on the View-Details `GestureDetector` block.

### D159: Menu is a real GoRouter page (`/menu`), not a drawer (PROD-2019, PROD-2020)
- **Choice**: PROD-2019 converts the right-side `ProfileDrawer` + scrim into a top-level shell route at `/menu`, peer to `/`, `/chat`, `/yours`. PROD-2020 follow-up: `/menu` is a true top-level tab — the bottom-nav button uses `context.go(AppRoutes.menu)` (not `push`) and the route is mounted as `NoTransitionPage` (not `_detailPage`), symmetric with `/chat` and `/yours`. The right-edge swipe handler that previously opened the drawer is removed entirely — Menu is reached only via the bottom-nav tap. The page paints its own `sokoPaper` background + header so no entry in `_pageBgForRouteName` or `PinnedPageChrome` is needed. The header back arrow falls back via `popOrFallback` for deep-link / cold-load cases where there's nothing on the navigator stack.
- **Figma source**: node `6453:11389` (file `d4BCnyUHe2705J7ecQtaIH`) — was rate-limit-unreachable during PROD-2019 implementation (View seat). Implementation works from the soko token system + the top-level-page pattern; once Figma access returns, the screen needs a side-by-side comparison and may need polish.
- **Daily-Drop interaction deferred**: the ticket's "dont block discovery, blur daily drop" phrasing has two readings (translucent overlay vs blur-on-re-entry). Both deferred in favour of the simpler peer-page model. Revisit if Figma confirms a non-standard interaction.
- **Migration window (closed)**: the 6 sub-screens — Account (PROD-2020), Preferences (PROD-2021), Business Connections (PROD-2022 + deep-link migration), Merge (PROD-2023), Support (PROD-2024), About (PROD-2025) — each live at their own `/menu/X` route.
