PROD-2025 deleted the legacy drawer machinery entirely: `lib/features/profile/screens/profile_sheet.dart`, `lib/features/profile/widgets/profile_drawer.dart`, `lib/providers/profile_drawer_provider.dart` (`profileDrawerOpenProvider`), and the `ProfileDrawer`/`ProfileSheet` mount + `isProfileDrawerOpen` plumbing in `discovery_shell.dart`. The `/profile/business-connections` deep-link is now a top-level redirect to `/menu/business-connections` in `app_router.dart`.
- **Where it lives**: route at `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` (`AppRoutes.menu`, alongside `/chat` / `/yours`); page name constant `menuPageName` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart`; page widget at `lib/features/profile/screens/menu_screen.dart`; bottom-nav wiring + `isMenuSelected` derivation in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_bottom_nav.dart`; `popOrFallback` (discovery_shell.dart) handles the in-header back arrow. `lib/features/profile/helpers/profile_navigation.dart` (the `showProfileSheet` / `closeProfileSheet` helpers) deleted — the two call sites (`chat_screen.dart` `_openProfile`, `memories_screen.dart` `_openProfile`) now `context.push(AppRoutes.menu)` directly.

### D160: "Primary" chip lives on the section header for collections that may repeat (PROD-2020)
- **Choice**: For sections that render a collection of like records (Connected Accounts), the "Primary" pill chip sits next to the **section title**, not next to the row that is `isPrimary: true`. The section header reads "Connected Accounts [Primary]" when one of the methods is the primary auth, and rows are styled identically regardless. Phone / Email sections (where each row has a distinct human-readable identifier) keep the chip at row level.
- **Why**: when the backend returns two records for the same provider (we saw the case with a real user — one row stored the Google `sub` as `people/<sub>`, another as the raw `<sub>`, so two visually-identical "Google" rows render), a per-row chip arbitrarily flags one of the apparent duplicates as primary. That hides the duplicate problem behind a misleading "this one is real, that one isn't" cue. Moving the chip up to the section keeps the rows uniform and surfaces the data issue rather than masking it. The chip widget (`_PrimaryChip` in `account_screen.dart`) is the same `sokoPink` pill shape either way; only its host changes.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/account/screens/account_screen.dart` — `_AccountSocialMethodsSection.build` computes `hasPrimary = methods.any((m) => m.isPrimary)` and conditionally renders the chip beside the "Connected Accounts" text; `_AccountAuthMethodTile` (phone/email) keeps the inline chip for per-row primary marking. `_PrimaryChip` is the shared widget.

### D161: Shared chat-bar composer shrinks below the cached Figma spec
- **Choice**: `ChatBarTopRow` (in `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart`) drops placeholder + input from **18 px / -0.36 tracking** to **15 px / -0.30 tracking**, the input column's `minHeight` from **40** to **36** with the mic / send buttons matched at **36 × 36** (icons 18 → 16, send pill radius 20 → 18), and outer `Container` padding from **10** to **8**. Net top-row height: 60 → 52 px. Input column and button heights are now equal, so `CrossAxisAlignment.start` produces a vertically-centered single-line placeholder/text (the bug surfaced after the first pass shrunk only the input column).
- **Why**: the 18 px placeholder + 40 × 40 button row felt too imposing on both the Discovery home composer and inside `/chat`. Matching the input column height to the button height fixes vertical centering at the same time.
- **Trade-off — bottom row stays at the old size**: `ChatBarBottomRow` (Create-list pill) and `ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow` (Adiciona à Soko + history + WhatsApp) keep their 60 h chrome and 40 h pills / 40 × 40 WhatsApp icon. The two rows render as separate `BackdropFilter` capsules and the visual mismatch is acceptable; if it reads poorly in-app, shrink those pills to 36 in a follow-up. Discovery → Chat Hero (`kChatBarHeroTag`) is still lossless — only the top row participates in the Hero.
- **Diverges from**: `docs/ui/figma-cache/screens/discovery/header-chat-bar.md` (Mobile/B1 Reg 18 px placeholder, 40 × 40 buttons). Intentional product call, not a Figma fidelity issue.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart::ChatBarTopRow` + `ChatBarCircleButton` + `ChatBarSendButton` — single shared primitive used by `MessageInput` (chat), `DiscoveryChatBar` (discovery home), and `DiscoveryTypeaheadOverlay` (search typeahead composer). Editing it once keeps all three surfaces in sync.

### D162: Canonical section-header typography across `/menu/*` screens
- **Choice**: every section header inside a `/menu/*` sub-screen renders as **`fontSize: 14`**, **`fontWeight: FontWeight.w500`**, `color: AppColors.sokoInk`, with **12 px** vertical gap to the first row below it. The leading icon (when present) is `size: 20` with `SizedBox(width: 8)` between icon and text. Page title in `_*Header` stays at **18 / w600** (already consistent — `_AccountHeader`, `_PreferencesHeader`, `_BCHeader`, `_MergeHeader`).
- **Why**: the PROD-2020 soko-rebrand (`account_screen.dart`) set the pattern at 14 / w500, but PROD-2021 (`preferences_screen.dart`) shipped 16 / w600, PROD-2022 (`business_connections_screen.dart`) shipped 18 / w600, and `merge_accounts_screen.dart` had its `_ConflictSelector` label at 15 / w600. Result: every menu sub-screen had a visibly different section-header weight + size, making the rebrand feel uneven. Aligning to the account-screen values restores a single hierarchy: page title (18/w600) > section header (14/w500) > row label (14/w500, also with icon) > input/value text (14/w300).
- **Where applied**: `preferences_screen.dart` (`_SectionHeader._label`, `_LocationSharingHeaderToggle`), `business_connections_screen.dart` (`_InstagramSection` title + spacing + `_ConnectionTile` username), `merge_accounts_screen.dart` (`_ConflictSelector.label` + spacing). The four `_*Header` page-title widgets are duplicated structurally across files but typographically already match — kept inline rather than lifted to a shared widget in this pass. Lift opportunity flagged for a future cleanup.
- **Out of scope this pass**: hero-style headings (`mergeDetectedHeading` 22/w700) and content inside bottom sheets (`_DisconnectInstagramSheet` 18/w700) are not menu section headers — they keep their distinct typography.

### D163: `SokoCtaButton` is the canonical full-width primary CTA across `/menu/*`
- **Choice**: a new shared widget `lib/shared/widgets/soko_cta_button.dart` codifies the "add-to-soko" Save-button shape: **height 44 px, radius 6, elevation 0, padding `horizontal: 24`, Zalando Sans 14 / w400 / line-height 1.2 / letter-spacing -0.14, icon size 14 with an 8 px gap to the label**. Three variants — `pink` (default, `sokoPink` bg + `sokoInk` fg), `red` (destructive: `sokoRed` bg + `sokoInk` fg), `ink` (`sokoInk` bg + `sokoPaper` fg, used by the guest Sign-in CTA inside `/menu`). Optional leading `icon`, `loading: true` swaps the icon for an inline 14 × 14 spinner and disables `onPressed`, `expand: false` makes the button hug its content instead of taking the full available width (default `true`).
- **Adopted by**: `_SokoSignOutButton` (`menu_screen.dart`), `_GuestMenuBody` Sign-In CTA (`menu_screen.dart`), `_DeleteAccountSection`'s Delete button (`account_screen.dart`, `red` variant), Connect-Instagram CTA (`business_connections_screen.dart`), the `_SaveFooter` Save button inside the chat feedback bottom sheet (`feedback_sheet.dart`). Before the lift, each call-site declared a bespoke `ElevatedButton.styleFrom(...)` with slightly different radius (12 vs 14), font weight (w500 vs w600), and minimumSize — visible drift the user called out as "sizing, font don't match the rest of the app."
- **What still uses `BtSqIco`, not `SokoCtaButton`**: `BtSqIco` (`lib/shared/widgets/bt_sq_ico.dart`) is the 40-px sticky-footer pair primitive — Cancel + Confirm rows inside bottom sheets (`_RemoveMethodSheet`, `_DisconnectInstagramSheet`, `_DeleteAccountSheet`, `_DisconnectSuccessSheet`, OTP sheet, `MergeAccountsScreen` action row, the soko-rebranded `AddPhoneInline` / `AddEmailInline` cancel + send-code pair). `SokoCtaButton` is for screen-level single CTAs; `BtSqIco` is for sheet-row pairs. Both share the same Zalando Sans / 6 px radius vocabulary; the size split is intentional (44 vs 40).

### D164: Account display rows mirror the chat-input visual language (`sokoShade5` fill, 48 px pill)
- **Choice**: every read-only row in `/menu/account` (Display Name, Username, Phone, Email, Connected Accounts, empty-state "Add Phone/Email") uses **`sokoShade5` fill, no border, 6 px radius, fixed 48 px height** — the same chrome the chat composer pill paints. Replaces the previous outlined `Border.all(sokoInk8)` / 8 px radius pattern. Implemented as the shared `_AccountFieldRow` widget inside `account_screen.dart`; takes `value` (simple text) or `leading` (richer tile content like provider icon + label + primary chip), plus an optional `trailing` affordance (pencil / lock / verified check / plus). When tapped, the row swaps in-place with a `SokoTextField` at the same 48 px height so the field doesn't visibly resize on focus.
- **Why**: image #2 → image #3 user request — "all these (and bottom sheets) need to be like [the chat input]." The chat input pill (`ChatBarTopRow`) is the canonical Soko input visual; aligning the account display rows to the same fill + radius + height removes the visible split between the chat surface and the rest of the app's text affordances.
- **Height-locking trick (48 px in every state)**: the SokoTextField swap-in mode hosts a `_FieldSuffixButton` that ALWAYS returns a 48 × 48 box — even when idle (invisible placeholder), checking (centered spinner), available (tappable check), unavailable/invalid (validation glyph). Returning `null` from the suffix in the idle state collapses the field to its content-padding-driven height (~45 px) and produces a visible "shrinks when tapped" jitter. See `flutter-textfield-isdense-height-collapse.md` § "Alternative fix" for the underlying mechanism.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/account/screens/account_screen.dart` — `_AccountFieldRow` (the shared row widget), `_FieldSuffixButton` (the always-48 suffix slot), used by `_AccountNameSection`, `_AccountHandleSection` (both editable + locked-handle variants), `_AccountAuthMethodTile` (phone/email rows), `_AccountSocialMethodTile` (Google/Apple/Facebook rows), and the empty-state add-method row inside `_AccountAuthMethodSection`.

### D165: Popups in `/menu/*` are bottom sheets, not `AlertDialog`s
- **Choice**: every confirm / info popup reachable from `/menu/*` renders as a `DSSheetShell` bottom sheet via `showBottomSheetWithHiddenNav`, not a Material `AlertDialog`. Covers: remove-auth-method confirm (`_RemoveMethodSheet`), delete-account confirm (`_DeleteAccountSheet` — was an `AlertDialog` with a "DELETE" text-input gate; sheet preserves the gate via `IgnorePointer + Opacity(0.5)` on the destructive `BtSqIco` until the user types `DELETE`), Instagram disconnect confirm + post-disconnect info (`_DisconnectInstagramSheet`, `_DisconnectSuccessSheet`), OTP verification (`_OtpVerificationSheet` — was an `AlertDialog` with six raw `TextField` boxes; now a sheet with soko-styled `_OtpDigitBox`es and `isDismissible: false` + `enableDrag: false` to preserve the original lock-down behaviour), in-chat feedback (`feedback_sheet.dart` — was a `DraggableScrollableSheet` with a `TextField` form; now `DSSheetShell` with a `SokoCtaButton` Save in the sticky footer).
- **Why**: bottom sheets share the same `sokoPaper` background + drag-handle chrome as every other modal surface in the app (lists sheets, instagram-share, etc.); `AlertDialog` defaults to Material's surface colour and rounded-rect chrome that reads as a different design system. Forcing every popup through `DSSheetShell` unifies the modal visual language. Sheets that have focused text inputs wrap in `AnimatedPadding(viewInsets)` so the action row rides the keyboard.
- **Action-row pattern**: confirm/cancel pairs use `Row(Expanded(BtSqIco normal), SizedBox(12), Expanded(BtSqIco selected))`. Destructive primary uses `selectedBackgroundOverride: AppColors.sokoRed`. The 12 px gap and 14 px bottom-of-sheet inset match every other DS sticky footer (`share_list_sheet`, `import_list_sheet`, `instagram_share_sheet`).
- **What still uses `AlertDialog`**: nothing inside `/menu/*` as of PROD-2023. Other surfaces (`browser_instructions_sheet.dart` outside the menu surface, framework-supplied dialogs like permission prompts) are out of scope.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/account/screens/account_screen.dart` (`_RemoveMethodSheet`, `_DeleteAccountSheet`, the `showBottomSheetWithHiddenNav` call inside `_DeleteAccountSectionState._showDeleteConfirmation`), `lib/features/account/widgets/otp_verification_dialog.dart` (renamed sheet, same public `showOtpVerificationDialog` entry-point preserved so call sites in `AddPhoneInline` / `AddEmailInline` keep working), `lib/features/business_connections/screens/business_connections_screen.dart` (`_DisconnectInstagramSheet`, `_DisconnectSuccessSheet`), `lib/features/chat/widgets/feedback_sheet.dart`.

### D166: Primary-auth chip falls back to earliest `createdAt` when the backend forgets to flag one
- **Choice**: `/menu/account` derives the auth-method primary id through `AccountState.effectivePrimaryId` (`lib/providers/account_provider.dart`) rather than reading `method.isPrimary` directly. The getter prefers a backend-flagged primary (`primaryMethod?.id`) but falls back to the **earliest-created** auth method when the response carries `is_primary: false` for every record. Sections (`_AccountAuthMethodSection`, `_AccountSocialMethodsSection`) thread the derived id down to the tiles as a plain `isPrimary: bool` prop — tiles no longer reach into the model.
- **Why**: in production we saw accounts where every auth row came back with `is_primary: false`, leaving the user with no visible "Principal" chip anywhere on the page (image #2 from the user). The chip + the delete-affordance guards (`_showDeleteIcon`, the no-op `Clickable.onTap` on the primary row) all depend on the same flag — silently missing primary meant the user could in principle delete the very method they use to log in. Falling back to the earliest-created method is deterministic, matches the natural "I signed up with this" mental model, and works even on legacy accounts the backend never wrote `is_primary` for.
- **Backend gap to close**: the fallback masks a server-side data bug, it does not fix it. The OpenAPI spec at `open-api/heyl-webapp-v1.openapi.yaml:5103` and the schema at `:10571` both document `is_primary` as a real field, and the backend should be marking exactly one auth method as primary per account. Track + fix the data side separately; until then this widget is the safety net.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/providers/account_provider.dart::AccountState.effectivePrimaryId` (the derivation), `lib/features/account/screens/account_screen.dart` (`_AccountAuthMethodSection`, `_AccountAuthMethodTile`, `_AccountSocialMethodsSection`, `_AccountSocialMethodTile` — all take an `isPrimary: bool` / `effectivePrimaryId: String?` prop and stop reading `widget.method.isPrimary` directly).

<!-- STALE(2026-05-26): PROD-2179 removed the web `_MarketingSwitchRow` branch entirely. The notification row is now mobile-only — see D173 for the current state. The native `_NotificationsHeaderToggle` + Klaviyo wiring described below still applies; only the `if (kIsWeb) … else …` web branch was deleted. -->
### D167: Notifications toggle in `/menu/preferences` reads OS push state on native, server-stored `pn_optin` on web
- **Choice**: `_NotificationsHeaderToggle` (in `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart`) is the bell-icon row beneath Location Sharing on native. It reads/writes the OS push-notification authorization through `KlaviyoService` rather than going through a Riverpod state notifier. On web — where there's no Klaviyo push wiring — the same slot renders a `_MarketingSwitchRow` bound to the server-stored `pn_optin` preference instead (`preferences?.pnOptin ?? false`), so the toggle still represents the user's stated push intent (the same value Siga captures on Continue per PROD-2154). The `if (kIsWeb) … else …` branch lives in `_PreferencesBodyState.build`.
- **State sync on resume**: the toggle is a `ConsumerStatefulWidget` with `WidgetsBindingObserver`. On `AppLifecycleState.resumed` it re-reads `KlaviyoService.isPushAuthorized` and rebuilds — so when the user returns from iOS / Android Settings after granting or revoking the permission, the switch flips automatically. Same UX as the Location toggle, just without an intermediate `StateNotifier` since there's no app-side preference to persist — the OS is the source of truth.
- **Toggle behaviour**: ON → `requestPushPermissionAndRegister()` (existing Klaviyo wrapper around `FirebaseMessaging.requestPermission` + `registerForPushNotifications`); if the resulting status isn't authorized (e.g. previously denied, so iOS won't re-prompt), open Settings. OFF → open Settings, since neither iOS nor Android exposes a programmatic "revoke notification permission" API. Settings opens via `permission_handler.openAppSettings()` — the package was already in `pubspec.yaml` for the Location flow.
- **`KlaviyoService` surface additions**: two new methods on both the mobile (`klaviyo_service_mobile.dart`) and stub (`klaviyo_service_stub.dart`) variants — `Future<bool> get isPushAuthorized` (reads `FirebaseMessaging.getNotificationSettings`, treats `authorized` + `provisional` as ON) and `Future<void> openNotificationSettings()`. Stub returns `false` / no-ops so web compiles unchanged.
- **What's out of scope**: backend-side notification preferences. The OpenAPI spec has no notifications endpoints, so there's no per-category opt-in (chat / weekly digest / etc.) — just the single OS-level toggle. If finer-grained controls are needed later, that requires a backend addition first; the OS-level toggle stays as the master switch above any future per-channel rows.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart::_NotificationsHeaderToggle` (the row), `lib/core/services/klaviyo_service_mobile.dart` + `klaviyo_service_stub.dart` (the two new helpers).

### D168: Discovery inline search overlay does not render its own scallop — the page-level divider already provides one
- **Choice**: on `/discovery`, when the magnifier swaps the `CityActionBar`'s idle row for `DiscoverySearchOverlay`, the overlay renders only the search input pill and the category tabs — it does NOT prepend its own `ScallopDivider`. The Soko-Ink scallop the user sees above the search input is the page-level `DiscoverySectionDivider` that `DiscoveryScreen` always renders 30 px above the action bar (Figma `6346:12056`).
- **Why**: the overlay used to add its own inline `ScallopDivider` + 12 px spacer per the original [D148](#d148-soko-ink-scallop-motif-rendered-above-the-chat-composer-surfaces). On `/discovery` that produced two scallops on screen at the same time when the search panel opened — the page-level one sitting 30 px above, then a second one sitting just above the input. Only one is needed; removing the inline copy keeps the existing 30 px Figma-spec gap intact and matches the idle action-bar layout exactly.
- **Why not Lists**: on `/yours` the section divider sits BELOW the action bar (PROD-2026 reordered the header — see `features/lists.md` changelog 2026-05-20 and the STALE flag on [D119](#d119-lists-header-rhythm--scallop-divider-sits-above-the-action-bar-not-below)), so swapping the action bar for the lists-scoped search overlay never produced a duplicate scallop — `ListsSearchOverlay` was already scallop-free and stays that way.
- **Why not the other chat composer surfaces**: the `/chat` welcome state, `/chat` conversation state, and `DiscoveryTypeaheadOverlay` are full-screen surfaces with no page-level `DiscoverySectionDivider` competing for the same visual slot. Each is the only scallop on its surface and stays as D148 specifies.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_search_overlay.dart` (now a thin wrapper around `SearchOverlay` with no inline divider). The always-on page-level scallop lives at `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_section_divider.dart`, mounted at `lib/features/discovery/screens/discovery_screen.dart` above the `CityActionBar`.

### D169: `CityActionBar` location pill is conditional per host surface (PROD-2145)
- **Amendment 2026-05-27 — narrow-viewport behaviour**: the "With pill" layout described below is the wide-viewport baseline. On iPhone-SE-class widths the location pill yields (icon drop → city truncation preserving ", ISO2") so the row doesn't overflow `Expanded` on the create-zine pill — see [D177](#d177-mobile-overflow-yielding-for-cityactionbar-location-pill--search-tab-row--btsqico-stays-content-sized-and-dumb).
- **Choice**: `CityActionBar` exposes a `showLocationPill: bool = true` prop. When `false`, the location `BtSqIco` is omitted from `_IdleRow`. `/yours` passes `false`; `/discovery` defaults to `true`.
- **Why**: `/yours` stopped scoping its content to a single city (PROD-2145 + backend PROD-2151) — the pill had nothing to drive. Search and create-zine are still needed on the page (item search is the only entry point post-PROD-2128; create-zine is the primary CTA), so dropping the entire bar would have regressed both. A per-host prop is the minimum surface change that satisfies the product ask.
- **Layout per host** (revised 2026-05-25):
  - **With pill** (`/discovery`): magnifier (28 px circle) + 6 px gap + `Expanded` create-zine pill + 6 px gap + location pill. The create-zine pill stretches between the two fixed-width chips so the row visually fills the width.
  - **Without pill** (`/yours`, non-guest): magnifier + `Spacer` + content-sized create-zine pill anchored to the right edge. No `Expanded` — `BtSqIco` defaults to `MainAxisSize.min` so it hugs its label.
  - **Without pill + guest** (degenerate; `/yours` is router-gated to authenticated users): magnifier alone.
- **Why right-aligned (not stretched)**: a full-width create-zine pill on `/yours` reads as "primary action across the whole header" — but the magnifier is co-equal (search is the only entry point to `/yours` item search post-PROD-2128). Anchoring the create-zine pill to the right preserves the visual hierarchy of two equally-weighted affordances and avoids the "lonely stretched button" look that a single-pill row would have.
- **Why not refactor toward a slot-based bar**: D114's YAGNI clause explicitly anticipated this — "when a real need arises to differ per host, the refactor is cheap because all callsites go through one widget." A boolean flag is the cheapest call that doesn't fan complexity into the other call sites; revisit if a third surface ever needs a different subset.
- **`cityScopeProvider` stays**: the provider is unchanged and still drives `/discovery` shelves, the location-pill label on `/discovery`, and any future surface that opts back into city scoping. Only the read path on `/yours` was severed.
- **Where it lives**: `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/city_action_bar.dart` (prop + conditional `_IdleRow`), `heyl_app/lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (mounts with `showLocationPill: false`).

### D170: Legal acceptance dates live on `/menu/about`, not `/menu/preferences`
- **Choice**: `/menu/about` is the canonical place where the user sees which documents they agreed to. The Terms of Service and Privacy Policy rows in `_AboutBody` (in `lib/features/about/screens/about_screen.dart`) carry an "Accepted on {date}" subtitle sourced from `preferencesProvider` (`termsAcceptedAt` / `privacyAcceptedAt` on `UserPreferences`). `/menu/preferences` no longer has a Legal section — the previous duplicate listing under that page was removed along with the `_SectionLabel.legal` enum value and the local `_formatAcceptedOn` helper (now lives on `_AboutBodyState`).
- **Why**: About already lists the two documents as outbound links, so the date belongs next to the document name — splitting "what you accepted" (About) from "when you accepted it" (Preferences) made the user scan two pages to answer one question. Preferences is for things the user can change; legal acceptance is a record they can only read, which is the About page's role.
- **Loading**: `_AboutBody` switched from `ConsumerWidget` to `ConsumerStatefulWidget` so it can trigger `preferencesProvider.notifier.load()` in `initState`. Without the trigger the dates would only appear if the user had already opened Preferences in the session (which loaded the same provider). The trigger is idempotent — repeat `load()` calls are cheap and consistent with the same pattern used by `_PreferencesBodyState`.
- **Row rendering**: `_AboutLinkRow` (the canonical About row visual, `sokoShade5` fill / 6 px radius / `external_link` glyph) gained an optional `subtitle` parameter. When provided the row expands vertically (10 px vertical padding) instead of locking to the 48 px pill — preserves the original look for the Instagram row, makes room for the date on the legal rows.
- **ARB**: keeps using `preferencesAcceptedOn(date)` and `aboutTermsOfService` / `aboutPrivacyPolicy` (no new keys). The orphaned `preferencesLegalSection` / `preferencesTermsOfService` / `preferencesPrivacyPolicy` ARB keys were left in place since nothing else references them — clean up opportunistically.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/about/screens/about_screen.dart::_AboutBodyState` (the two `_AboutLinkRow` calls with `subtitle: _formatAcceptedOn(...)`), `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart` (legal section removed).

### D171: WhatsApp line picker is a navigation row → detail page, matching the Language picker
- **Choice**: `/menu/preferences` shows WhatsApp as a single `_NavigationRow` (icon + title + currently-selected region as subtitle + trailing chevron), not an inline list of `PickerSelectRow`s. Tapping pushes `/menu/preferences/whatsapp`, a detail page (`WhatsappSettingsScreen`, `lib/features/preferences/screens/whatsapp_settings_screen.dart`) that hosts the actual region picker. Mirrors how Language works (`/menu/preferences/language` → `LanguageSettingsScreen`) and uses the same `_detailPage` (CupertinoPage on iOS, NoTransitionPage on Android/web) chrome.
- **Why**: when a user has 3+ available WhatsApp regions, the inline list pushed all the other Preferences sections way below the fold. Collapsing it into a row matches the navigation rhythm of every other long picker on `/menu/*` (Language → detail page, Account → sub-flows) and keeps the preferences page scannable. The selected region shown as a subtitle gives the user the same at-a-glance answer the old inline section provided.
- **Routing pattern (replicable for future detail screens)**: a new picker detail screen needs three coordinated edits — (1) `AppRoutes.menu*` constant in `lib/core/router/app_router.dart`, (2) `menu*PageName` const in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart` (used by `_detailPage` for analytics + nav-observer page identity), (3) a `GoRoute` entry inside the menu route subtree in `app_router.dart` wired to the new screen via `_detailPage(name: …, child: …)`. All three must land together — missing the page-name constant compiles but breaks page-level analytics.
- **Detail screen shape**: same shell pattern as `LanguageSettingsScreen` — `ColoredBox(sokoPaper) → PageContent → SafeArea → Column(PreferencesHeader, Expanded(_Body))`. Reuses `PreferencesHeader` (back arrow + centered title) and `PickerSelectRow` (the existing Soko-pink selected-state picker chip). The header title comes from `preferencesWhatsApp` ("WhatsApp Line"), reused so we don't need a separate detail-title key.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/preferences/screens/whatsapp_settings_screen.dart` (new), `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` (constant + route), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart` (page name), `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart` (`_NavigationRow` replaces the inline section; `picker_select_row.dart` import removed).

<!-- STALE(2026-05-27): amended by D181 — the **highlight colour** branch of this decision is reverted. PROD-2205-followup dropped the `case selected → mapPinSelected (yellow)` wrap from the `circle-color` Mapbox expression on both web and native. Selected pins now keep their semantic fill (event-green / venue-blue) and are differentiated by radius (17 vs 10 px) + a 3 px stroke in a darker shade of the pin colour (per-category `AppColors.mapPin{Event,Venue,Default}SelectedStroke`). The `selectedMarkerId` mechanism still exists and still drives the radius/stroke/sort-key case expressions — only the fill-override branch is gone. `AppColors.mapPinSelected` (Soko/Yellow) is kept unused for a potential A/B re-enable. The rest of D172 (whole-list pins on the item-page map, sibling-tap routing via `openInListItemDetail`, single-item-list fallback) still applies. -->
### D172: Zine item-page map shows all list pins, with the current item highlighted (PROD-2159)
- **Choice**: when the user flips from a zine cover to any item page, the map below the item card renders the **entire list's pin set** (the same `state.mapPins` source the cover map reads), with the current item's pin painted in `AppColors.mapPinSelected` (Soko/Yellow) instead of its venue-blue / event-green semantic colour. Single-item lists keep the legacy single-pin look and skip the highlight (no other pins to differentiate from). Multi-venue events (PROD-2017, `occurrenceMarkers.length >= 2`) still render per-occurrence pins — that path is a different semantic ("one item across N venues") and is unchanged.
- **Why**: the bug report (PROD-2159) described the user perception as "the map only shows one place — the one I'm on" — flipping pages made every other place vanish, which read like the map was broken. The cover already shows the whole-list multi-pin map; making the item-page map a single-pin lookup created a jarring "shrink-to-one" transition mid-zine. Showing every pin everywhere with the current item highlighted matches the cover's mental model and gives the user a stable map of "where everything in this zine is" as they navigate. Yellow was picked because it's the existing `sokoListAccent` / zine accent token in the design system, contrasts strongly against both blue (venues) and green (events), and reads as a "you are here" pin in map vernacular.
- **Why yellow and not blue/green**: the original bug report suggested "blue vs green for the selected one." Rejected because venue (blue) and event (green) are already load-bearing semantic colours in the map system — repurposing one of them for "selected" would mean every selected venue reads as an event (or vice versa) and silently breaks the venue/event distinction on every other map surface that doesn't pass `selectedMarkerId`. A third accent (yellow, well-clear of both) preserves the existing venue/event language untouched.
- **Where the colour comes from**: new `AppColors.mapPinSelected = sokoYellow` token. The `circle-color` Mapbox style expression in both `mapbox_map_web.dart` and `mapbox_map_native.dart` wraps the existing `match item_type` branch in `case ['get', 'selected'] → mapPinSelected, else <existing match>`. Reuses the `selected: true` GeoJSON property the shell already stamps for the radius/sort-key paths — zero new properties on the wire. Non-zine consumers (cover map, hub map, event/venue detail blocks) keep the legacy semantic palette by simply not passing `selectedMarkerId` to `MapboxMapWidget`.
- **Tap behaviour**: tapping a sibling pin opens its in-list detail via the shared `openInListItemDetail(...)` helper (resolves the heavy `UserListItem` from `state.items` first, since slim pins don't carry `eventId`). Same destination the cover map uses for tooltip "Ver detalhes" — keeps the two map surfaces in lockstep so future routing changes only need one site edit. Explicitly NOT a "flip the zine pager to that page" affordance — the user can come back to the cover and flip if they want the zine experience; the tap is for "show me this place's details right now."
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_item_page.dart::_ItemMap` (signature changed from `{item, listId, onViewDetails}` → `{item, state}`; renders `state.mapPins` instead of a single marker; passes `selectedMarkerId: pinItems.length > 1 ? item.id : null`). Cross-platform style branch in `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_web.dart` + `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_native.dart`. Colour token in `lib/core/theme/app_colors.dart`. See [`features/maps.md` § "Selected-pin enlargement and highlight color"](../features/maps.md#selected-pin-enlargement-and-highlight-color) for the cross-platform style expression and [`features/lists.md` 2026-05-25 changelog (PROD-2159)](../features/lists.md) for the bug shape and full fix narrative.

### D173: Chat composer hosts History in its bottom strip; profile avatar removed from the chat header
- **Choice**: the `/chat` composer's shaded bottom strip (the same styled box that hosts the Create-list pill — `ChatBarBottomRow` in `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart`) now also renders a History circle on the **right** side. The strip is rendered whenever either slot is wired: Create-list on the left when the conversation has saveable cards, History on the right unconditionally on the chat surfaces. The chat conversation header lost both its left-side History `IconButton` AND its right-side profile avatar — the header now hosts only the theme-toggle (kill-switched) and, for admins, the session-info chip.
- **Wiring**: `ChatBarBottomRow` (chat-side strip) gained an optional `onHistoryTap`. `MessageInput` (conversation composer) gained `showHistory` / `onHistory` props; `DiscoveryChatBar` (chat empty-state composer) gained a `showHistory` bool — when true and not on the Discovery surface, it renders the same `ChatBarBottomRow` instead of `ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow`. The discovery surface keeps `showDiscoveryActions: true` and its three-shortcut row unchanged.
- **Why**: the chat header had three competing affordances (History, Theme, Profile) that crowded the top of the screen on mobile; the profile entry is already redundant with `/menu` in the bottom nav (D159). Moving History below the input puts it on the same plane as Create-list — both are "after you've written your message, here's what else you can do" affordances — and matches the visual grammar the discovery composer uses for its three shortcuts. Sharing the existing `ChatBarBottomRow` (rather than rendering a separate widget below `MessageInput`) keeps the strip visually attached to the input on both surfaces, with no extra spacing breaking the bar silhouette.
- **Supersedes**: D117 (PROD-1894) — that decision removed the bottom row from the chat composer entirely. We now reinstate it with a different payload (History instead of Adiciona à Soko / WhatsApp).
- **Trade-off**: the empty-state composer (`DiscoveryChatBar` on `/chat`) now diverges visually from its discovery-surface twin — same component, different bottom row. The Hero animation (`kChatBarHeroTag`) still works across the Discovery → Chat send transition because both bars share the same top-row geometry; the bottom row just morphs.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart` (`ChatBarBottomRow` accepts `onHistoryTap` + `onCreateListTap`, asserts ≥1 wired), `lib/features/chat/widgets/message_input.dart` (`showHistory` + `onHistory`), `lib/features/chat/screens/chat_screen.dart` (`_buildConversationHeader` stripped to theme + admin chip; conversation `MessageInput` passes `showHistory: true`; empty state passes `DiscoveryChatBar(showHistory: true)`), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_chat_bar.dart` (`showHistory` bool, else-if chain after `showDiscoveryActions`, `mounted` guard added to `_handleHistoryTap` to survive Hero-flight / hot-restart cycles).

### D174: About page socials are icon-only coloured circles, aligned to the bottom of the screen
- **Choice**: `/menu/about` replaces the three labelled `_AboutLinkRow` rows (Instagram / TikTok / LinkedIn — `sokoShade5` fill, leading icon + handle + trailing `external_link`) with three 48 × 48 circular icon-only buttons in coloured fills: Instagram on **#8B7AE8** (lavender), TikTok on **#C68676** (dusty coral), LinkedIn on **#B4DBF4** (light blue). All three glyphs paint in `AppColors.sokoInk`. The circle row sits in the same bottom-aligned `Padding` slot the labelled rows previously occupied, above the `_AboutFooter`. The two non-social `_AboutLinkRow`s (Terms, Privacy) in the scrolling section above are unchanged.
- **Why**: the labelled rows treated the three social handles the same way Terms / Privacy are surfaced (full-width tappable rows with leading icon + trailing external-link glyph) — but the social handles are brand-link entry points, not legal-document targets. The labelled treatment over-weighted them. Brand-coloured circles read as social-link affordances at a glance, match the visual language of "footer social icons" on the brand's other surfaces, and clear vertical space so the rest of the page lands above the fold on shorter mobile viewports.
- **Trade-off**: the handle text (`@soko.fyi`) is no longer visible — users have to recognise the platform from the glyph + colour alone. Acceptable because the three platforms are visually unambiguous (Instagram's camera, TikTok's musical note, LinkedIn's "in") and the icons are large + coloured, not the legacy 18 px monotone version. Semantic labels (`l10n.aboutInstagramHandle` etc.) remain on each `_SocialCircle` for a11y readers.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/about/screens/about_screen.dart` — `_AboutBodyState.build` renders a `Row` of three `_SocialCircle`s with 16 px gaps in place of the three sequential `_AboutLinkRow`s. The `_SocialCircle` widget is a `Material(shape: CircleBorder) + InkWell` wrapping a 48-square `SizedBox`. The shared TikTok SVG (`assets/images/tiktok.svg`) is reused at 22 px with the `sokoInk` colour filter.

### D175: Web has no notification toggle anywhere; cross-device push prompt re-fires Siga via a widened opt-in gate (PROD-2179)
- **Choice**: the Flutter web build renders **no notification toggle in Siga and no notification row in `/menu/preferences`** — both surfaces hide the control. Siga on web shows only an email + SMS consent toggle (`authSokoCommunicationsDescWeb` copy) and its PATCH body omits `pn_optin`, so `pn_optin` stays `NULL` on the server for web-first accounts. To handle the cross-device case, `needsSokoIntroProvider` was widened from `sms_marketing_opt_in_at IS NULL` to `email_marketing_opt_in_at IS NULL OR sms_marketing_opt_in_at IS NULL OR pn_optin IS NULL` — so when a web user later opens iOS / Android and signs in, Siga re-fires (this time with the full "Push, email and SMS" copy + OS push prompt) and the user finally records their push intent. Same-device users on mobile see Siga once with `pn_optin` stamped, then never again. Replaces [D167](#d167-notifications-toggle-in-menupreferences-reads-os-push-state-on-native-server-stored-pn_optin-on-web).
- **Why**: the Klaviyo Flutter SDK is mobile-only, and the JS-SDK + service-worker path that would deliver push on the browser is explicitly deferred (see `docs/investigations/context/klaviyo-posthog-push-integration.md`, §1 "Out of scope" line 39). A toggle on web could only record a preference flag that has nowhere to deliver — flipping it ON would be silently inert, which is worse than not showing it. Hiding the surface is honest about the platform capability; widening the Siga gate is what guarantees a web-first user still gets the prompt the first time they reach a device that can deliver.
- **Why widen the gate (vs. add a separate push-only screen)**: a separate "push consent" screen on mobile would have been one more route + provider + redirect rule + L10n strings. Reusing Siga via the OR'd gate keeps the consent surface single — the existing screen already shows a bundled toggle whose semantics fit ("Push, email and SMS — pick and choose later"). If email + SMS are already stamped from a web Siga, the second Siga visit on mobile re-PATCHes them with the user's current toggle value (idempotent on the backend) plus the new `pn_optin`. No information is lost.
- **Pn_optin sentinel semantics**: `UserPreferences.pnOptin` is `bool?`; `null` means "client never reported" (the cross-device signal we need); `true` means push verified or stated-intent; `false` means denied/revoked. The gate widening relies on this — see `user_preferences.dart:13-15` for the PROD-2111 comment that documents the tri-state contract.
- **OpenAPI contract**: `PATCH /api/v1/app/users/me/preferences` accepts both `pn_optin` (legacy) and `push_notification_opt_in` (long-form alias added by PROD-2178) — interchangeable, `pn_optin` wins on conflict. The Dart client keeps using `pn_optin` for backwards-compat.
- **Where it lives**: gate widening in `lib/providers/preferences_provider.dart::needsSokoIntroProvider`; web PATCH omission + platform-aware copy in `lib/features/auth/screens/soko_welcome_screen.dart` (`_onContinue` + `_CommunicationsPermissionCard`); deleted preferences row in `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart` (the `if (kIsWeb) ...` branch was removed entirely — mobile `_NotificationsHeaderToggle` is unchanged); new L10n key `authSokoCommunicationsDescWeb` in `intl_en.arb` + `intl_pt.arb` + `intl_pt_BR.arb`; client-side Klaviyo writes stripped from `auth_provider.dart`, `klaviyo_service_mobile.dart`, `klaviyo_service_stub.dart`, `push_permission_service.dart` (backend PROD-2178 owns profile + subscription writes).

### D176: Siga gate + `/menu/preferences` marketing toggles respect each user's reachable channels (PROD-2179 follow-up)
- **Choice**: `needsSokoIntroProvider` skips marketing-consent checks for channels the user has no way to receive on, and `/menu/preferences` hides the matching marketing toggles. `hasReachableEmail` is true for users with an `email` auth method OR any social provider (Google / Apple / Facebook all surface an email at the identity-provider level, which the backend stores and uses as the marketing target). `hasReachablePhone` is true only for users with a `phone` auth method. `pn_optin` is checked regardless of *channel* availability, **but only on mobile** — on web the term is gated off (see **D178**); web can never set `pn_optin`, so an unconditional check looped web signups back to Siga forever. It remains the cross-device push-prompt contract from [D175](#d175-web-has-no-notification-toggle-anywhere-cross-device-push-prompt-re-fires-siga-via-a-widened-opt-in-gate-prod-2179). Concretely: a Google-only user with `email_marketing_opt_in_at` + `pn_optin` stamped and `sms_marketing_opt_in_at = NULL` no longer trips Siga, and the menu shows only the email row (no SMS toggle). Same for a phone-OTP user with no email — Siga ignores `email_marketing_opt_in_at = NULL` and the menu hides the email row.
- **Why**: prior to this, a phone-less user could be sent back to Siga every time their `sms_marketing_opt_in_at` was NULL on the server, even though SMS would never be delivered. The menu toggle for that channel was similarly inert — a Google-only user could flip an SMS row that had no phone number to target. Hiding what can't be acted on simplifies the consent surface without losing real consent capture; the gate and the toggle visibility share the same source-of-truth getters on `AccountState` so the two surfaces never diverge.
- **Where channel availability comes from**: `AccountState.hasReachableEmail` and `AccountState.hasReachablePhone` (in `lib/providers/account_provider.dart`), both derived from the auth methods returned by `GET /api/v1/auth/methods`. `accountProvider` auto-invalidates on `user.id` change so re-login as a different user gets fresh data on first read — see `learnings/auto-invalidate-on-identity-change.md`.
- **Loading UX**: the menu's marketing section shows a single spinner row until BOTH `preferencesProvider` and `accountProvider.authMethods` settle, so a phone-only user doesn't see both toggles render then watch the email one disappear. Fallback: if auth-methods load errors, both toggles render (don't hide consent affordances based on incomplete data).
- **Where it lives**: gate logic in `lib/providers/preferences_provider.dart::needsSokoIntroProvider`; getters on `lib/providers/account_provider.dart::AccountState`; menu toggle gating in `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart`; opt-out PATCH now best-effort includes the existing Klaviyo push token + platform in `lib/features/auth/screens/soko_welcome_screen.dart::_onContinue` so the backend can revoke the device's Klaviyo push subscription on opt-out; 13 unit tests pin the gate matrix in `test/providers/preferences_provider_test.dart` (phone-only, email-only, social-only, phone+email permutations × the three consent fields).

### D177: Mobile-overflow yielding for `CityActionBar` location pill + search-tab row — `BtSqIco` stays content-sized and dumb
- **Choice**: when the `/discovery` `CityActionBar` runs out of horizontal room (iPhone-SE-class widths with the long "Rio de Janeiro, BR" auto-label) the location `BtSqIco` yields in two steps: (1) drop its leading map-pin icon to reclaim 22 px (14 px icon + 8 px gap), (2) if the label still doesn't fit, truncate just the city portion with a `TextPainter` binary search so the country code stays visible ("Rio de Janei…, BR" rather than Flutter's default end-truncation "Rio de Janei…"). The `SearchCategoryTabs` row (Tudo / Zines / Eventos / Sítios) switched from a centered `Row(MainAxisSize.min)` to a `LayoutBuilder` + horizontal `SingleChildScrollView` + `ConstrainedBox(minWidth: viewport)` + `Row(MainAxisAlignment.center)` so the chips centre when they fit and scroll horizontally when they don't (previously the centre alignment pushed both edges past the viewport, clipping the trailing chip behind a Flutter render-overflow stripe).
- **Why this isn't in `BtSqIco`**: the natural temptation is "make the DS button shrink padding / hide its icon when constrained." Rejected. `BtSqIco` is a content-sized chip used in 20+ call sites (sticky-footer pairs, sheet rows, search tabs, action bars) — making it intrinsically responsive would (a) require every callsite to wrap it in a `LayoutBuilder` measurement pipeline, (b) break the DS invariant that two identical pills render at the same size regardless of host, (c) confuse callers that already wrap it in `Expanded` (where Flutter has already decided the width). The actual layout problem lives at the call site that *combines* a fixed-content pill with a variable-content one — fix it there.
- **What `BtSqIco` did get**: a defensive `Flexible` wrapper + `maxLines: 1` + `TextOverflow.ellipsis` on the label `Text`. Unconstrained behaviour is unchanged (the Row is still `MainAxisSize.min`); when a constrained parent hands it less room than its content wants, the label ellipsises gracefully rather than overflowing the chip. Same chip primitive, same Figma `4108:2975` contract, just doesn't paint past its constraints.
- **Where the responsive logic lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/city_action_bar.dart::_IdleRow.build` wraps the row in `LayoutBuilder`; `_buildResponsiveLocationPill` makes the icon-drop + truncate decision per build; `_truncateCityKeepingSuffix` runs the binary search against a `TextPainter` measurement that matches `BtSqIco`'s label `TextStyle`. `_resolveLocationLabelParts` returns `(prefix, suffix)` so the suffix (", ISO2") is preserved as a separate token rather than embedded in a single ellipsised string. Riverpod `ref.watch` calls stay in `build()` proper, NOT inside `LayoutBuilder.builder` (the callback runs during layout, after build, so watches there don't register dependencies on the consumer element).
- **Where the chip row lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/search_category_tabs.dart::build` — `LayoutBuilder` hands the viewport width to a `ConstrainedBox(minWidth:)` so the inner `Row` is always at least as wide as the viewport. Fits → row centres in the surplus; overflows → row sizes to content (still ≥ minWidth) and the outer `SingleChildScrollView` scrolls. `clipBehavior: Clip.none` keeps focus / press rings from getting clipped at the viewport edges.
- **Threshold logic for the icon drop**: implicit, not a hardcoded magic number. The pill measures its own content-width (icon + gap + label + horizontal padding) against the constraint it received and only drops the icon when the full-with-icon shape would overflow. Cities short enough to fit with the icon (e.g. "Porto, PT", "Lisboa, PT", "Madrid, ES") keep it.
- **Trade-off**: the location pill is the only place in the app that does this much measurement gymnastics during layout. Justified because it's the only chip in the app where (a) the parent uses `Expanded` on a sibling so the natural Flutter overflow goes uncaught, AND (b) the label is user-driven (city name varies in length wildly). The pattern is deliberately not extracted as a shared utility — if a second instance ever needs it, that's the right time to abstract.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/bt_sq_ico.dart` (Flexible + ellipsis on the label `Text`), `lib/shared/widgets/city_action_bar.dart` (LayoutBuilder + responsive location pill helpers), `lib/shared/widgets/search_category_tabs.dart` (LayoutBuilder + ConstrainedBox + horizontal scroll). Test: `test/shared/widgets/city_action_bar_test.dart` adds a 343-wide regression that asserts the row never throws a flex-overflow exception. Amends [D169](#d169-cityactionbar-location-pill-is-conditional-per-host-surface-prod-2145) — the "With pill" layout it describes is the wide-viewport baseline; D177 documents what that same row does when the viewport gets tight.

### D178: Siga gate's `pn_optin` term is platform-gated (`!isWeb`) so web signups don't loop on the welcome screen (PROD-2179 follow-up)
- **Choice**: `needsSokoIntroProvider`'s third OR term is now `(!isWeb && p.pnOptin == null)` instead of `p.pnOptin == null`. On web the push term never trips the gate; on mobile it is unchanged. `isWeb` is read from a new `isWebPlatformProvider` (defaults to `kIsWeb`) so the web branch is unit-testable. Lives in `lib/providers/preferences_provider.dart`.
- **Why**: [D175](#d175-web-has-no-notification-toggle-anywhere-cross-device-push-prompt-re-fires-siga-via-a-widened-opt-in-gate-prod-2179) added `pn_optin IS NULL` to the gate so a web-first user re-sees Siga on their first *mobile* login (to capture push). But the term was evaluated on **every** platform, including web — and web's Siga PATCH deliberately omits `pn_optin` (no Klaviyo push on web), so `pn_optin` stays NULL on web forever. Result: after a brand-new web signup (e.g. Google), the user granted location, tapped Continue, and the router immediately redirected back to `/auth/soko-welcome` because `needsSokoIntro` was still `true`. The screen never advanced — "Continue does nothing." Verified live: a fresh Google web user had `email_marketing_opt_in_at` + `sms_marketing_opt_in_at` stamped but `pn_optin = null`.
- **Cross-device intent preserved**: gating on `!isWeb` (not removing the term) means a web-first user with `pn_optin = null` still trips Siga the first time they open iOS/Android, exactly as D175 intended. Web simply stops evaluating a channel it can never fulfill — the same principle D176 applies to email/SMS via `hasReachableEmail`/`hasReachablePhone`.
- **Testing note**: `kIsWeb` is always `false` under `flutter test`, so the web branch is only reachable by overriding `isWebPlatformProvider`. Regression tests for both the web (no loop) and mobile (still trips) paths live in `test/providers/preferences_provider_test.dart`. See [`learnings/server-derived-router-gate.md`](../learnings/server-derived-router-gate.md).

### D179: Hide "Abrir no Maps" button on venue detail when `google_place_id` is null (PROD-2220)
- **Choice**: `VenueActionGrid.hasMap` now requires `venue.googlePlaceId` to be set. Coordinates and the backend `googleMapsUrl` field are no longer sufficient. When `googlePlaceId` is null, the Maps button drops out of the row entirely (the existing collapse-cleanly layout handles the gap); Save / Share / Ver site behaviour is unchanged. One-liner: `final hasMap = (venue.googlePlaceId ?? '').isNotEmpty;` in `lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_action_grid.dart`.
- **Why**: a class of ghost venues (Foursquare-only, never reconciled with Google — e.g. "Restaurante Casual" / "Restaurante Cascal" in São Domingos de Rana) was rendering the button and sending users to a Google Maps search that landed on unrelated places. The canonical-URL fix in [PROD-1673](../bugfixes/PROD-1673-abrir-no-maps-place-id-syntax.md) addresses URL format quirks but only works when we *have* a `place_id`; without one, the launcher's fallback (name + lat/lng search) silently lands on whatever Google's keyword index returns. Render-time gating is the only thing that prevents the broken experience — backend-side quality gates can be fooled by LLM-generated descriptions that lift ghost venues past the `quality_status='eligible'` threshold. See [`learnings/open-in-maps-requires-google-place-id.md`](../learnings/open-in-maps-requires-google-place-id.md).
- **Trade-off**: real venues without `google_place_id` (e.g. Gourmet da Maria — Foursquare-sourced, has phone + website, the place exists) **also lose the button**. Accepted until a backend backfill resolves those rows via the Google Places Search API (separate ticket, see learning doc).
- **Not extended to events yet**: `EventDetailResponse2` does not expose `venueGooglePlaceId`. Mirroring the rule on `EventActionGrid` requires an OpenAPI spec change + client regen — tracked as a follow-up on PROD-2220.

### D180: Yours/Following toggle gains a sliding scallop underline; page-level scallop divider retired on `/yours`
- **Choice**: `YoursFollowingToggleHeader` now renders the canonical Soko-Ink scallop motif (the same SVG tile `DiscoverySectionDivider` uses page-wide) directly under the active label, tinted to the active label's ink colour, sliding + resizing between Yours and Following as the toggle animates. The active font bumps from 42 → 52 px on this surface (inactive 22 → 24 px), `_labelGap` 12 → 16 px, the top page padding bumps 16 → 40 px, and the gap between the toggle and the action bar grows 24 → 36 px. The page-level `DiscoverySectionDivider` row that used to sit below the action bar (PROD-2026 placement, see [D119](#d119-lists-header-rhythm--scallop-divider-sits-above-the-action-bar-not-below) STALE) is **removed entirely** — the per-title scallop replaces it as the page's section break. Amends [D139](#d139-yoursfollowing-is-an-animated-h1-page-title-toggle-not-a-tab-bar); the core "animated H1 toggle, not a tab bar" decision stands.
- **Why a tab affordance was needed**: user feedback was unambiguous — the two-typography H1 read as "two titles with one emphasised", not "two switchable tabs". 35 % opacity + smaller font on the inactive label was a hierarchy hint, not a control affordance. The scallop carries the active-state signal without flipping the surface into a TabBar / pill row (D139's rationale still applies — no separate control above the content title). Adding a flat 2 px underline first proved the affordance worked; switching the underline to the scallop motif tied the tab indicator into the app's existing visual grammar.
- **Why bigger fonts**: with the underline doing the active-state work, the inactive label could stay quietly readable at a slightly larger 24 px while the active label needed to lift to assert page-H1 weight. 52 px diverges from `DiscoveryShelf`'s 42 px H1 elsewhere, intentionally — on `/yours` the in-shelf title is suppressed via `DiscoveryShelf.titleOverride = SizedBox.shrink()`, so there's no on-page peer to match. The guest static H1 in `_YoursPageHeader` mirrors 52 px to keep the guest state visually consistent with the authed toggle.
- **Why the page-level scallop went away**: with the per-title scallop carrying section-break weight, the row below the action bar became a duplicate motif — two scallops on one surface fought for attention. Dropping it shortens the header rhythm to `title → 36 → action bar → body`, and the body's own dotted divider (inside `_ListsHubDefaultBody`) still marks the shelf/body boundary cleanly.
- **Scallop tile-fit — ceil, not floor**: `ScallopDivider` natively does `floor(width / 18.19)` so the tiled row never overflows its container. For an underline the natural reading is the opposite — readers expect the underline to slightly *exceed* the text width, not fall short of it. The toggle computes `scallopCount = ceil(underlineWidth / 18.19)`, builds a `SizedBox(width: scallopCount × 18.19)`, and shifts left by `(scallopWidth - underlineWidth) / 2` via `Transform.translate` to keep the scallop centred on the active label. `Transform.translate` (not `Padding`) because the centring offset can be slightly negative when the label sits flush against the column edge (yours-active at t=0). The overhang is bounded by ~9 px on each side, well within the page's 16 px horizontal padding.
- **Locked outer height + baseline anchoring**: the toggle's outer `SizedBox` height is locked to `52 × 0.94 + 6 + 9 + 1` ≈ 65 px (active line box + underline gap + scallop tile + 1 px sub-pixel buffer). Without the lock, the labels' `Row` height dips by ~13 px at mid-tween (both labels rendering at intermediate size) and the rest of the page bounces up-and-down on every swap. `Column(mainAxisAlignment: MainAxisAlignment.end)` anchors the label row to the bottom of the locked box, so the active label's baseline stays put (drift ~2 px between stable and mid-tween, visually imperceptible). The +1 px buffer covers a Flutter text-painter sub-pixel rounding quirk that otherwise produced a "Bottom overflowed by 0.120 pixels" debug warning. See [`learnings/animated-text-locked-height-textpainter.md`](../learnings/animated-text-locked-height-textpainter.md).
- **ScallopDivider gains an `inverted` prop**: vertical-flip flag via `Transform.flip(flipY: true)` so the same tile can render with curves pointing up (flat edge on bottom — for an underline-style anchor) instead of the canonical curves-hanging-down section break. The toggle ended up using the default orientation, but the prop stays for future surfaces.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/widgets/yours_following_toggle_header.dart` (the toggle + scallop + locked-height layout); `lib/shared/widgets/scallop_divider.dart` (the new `inverted` prop); `lib/features/lists/screens/lists_hub_screen.dart` (header rhythm — `_YoursPageHeader` top pad, removed `DiscoverySectionDivider` row, 40/36 px gaps). Guest static H1 in `_YoursPageHeader` bumped to 52 px to match.

### D181: Zine + detail map polish — unified height, bigger pins, always-unlocked, semantic-colour selection (PROD-2205-followup)

- **Choice (four parallel changes, all driven from shared constants):**
  1. **Unified map height.** Cover map, list-view body map, and zine item-page map all read `kZineMapHeight = 360` in `lib/shared/widgets/map_marker_model.dart`. Previously cover/list-view = 500 px and item-page = 230 px; the asymmetry made paging into an item feel like the canvas shrunk. The `/lists` hub map, chat compact (104 px), places modal (viewport-fraction), and detail blocks (`AspectRatio: 1`) intentionally pick their own height and are unchanged.
  2. **Pins ~30 % bigger for touch.** New `MapPinTokens` colocated with `MapClusterTokens`: unselected radius 8 → 10 px, selected 12 → 17 px (the latter bumped in r2; +40 % total over baseline). Cluster bubbles 16/22/28 → 21/29/36. Selected stroke 0.5 → 3 px.
  3. **Always-unlocked maps.** All four list-map consumers (`list_map_view`, `list_zine_item_page._ItemMap`, `event_map_block`, `venue_map_block`) read `kListMapsStaticByDefault = false` instead of hardcoding `staticByDefault: true`. The "Tap to interact / Lock map" chip code path in `MapboxMapWidget` is **untouched** — flipping the const to `true` re-enables the chip everywhere in one move, ready for an A/B test or rollback. Trade-off accepted: scrolling past a map on touch devices now requires touching outside the map area. Web wheel-event isolation (PROD-1978's `PointerSignalResolver`) is unchanged.
  4. **Selected pin keeps semantic colour, differentiated by size + stroke.** Dropped the `case selected → mapPinSelected (yellow)` wrap from `circle-color` on both web and native. Selected pins now render in their event-green / venue-blue fill so the item-type signal isn't lost on the focused pin. Differentiation is radius (10 → 17 px) plus stroke width (0.5 → 3 px) plus stroke colour (Soko/Ink → darker shade of the pin's own fill — `AppColors.mapPin{Event,Venue,Default}SelectedStroke`, each ≈ `pin fill · 0.6`). Three new `case`-on-`selected` expressions (`circle-stroke-width`, `circle-stroke-color`) mirror the existing `circle-radius` one, each with a matching 150 ms transition. `AppColors.mapPinSelected` (Soko/Yellow) retained for a future A/B re-enable. Amends [D172](#d172-zine-item-page-map-shows-all-list-pins-with-the-current-item-highlighted-prod-2159) (its colour branch — see STALE note above).
- **Why drop the yellow fill (vs keep D172's `case selected → mapPinSelected`)**: dogfooding showed the yellow hid the event/venue distinction at the exact moment it mattered most — the user is reading about ONE place and can't see at a glance whether it's a venue or an event. The radius + stroke combination tells the eye "this one is the focus" without overriding the semantic colour. D172's "yellow because it doesn't clash with venue-blue / event-green" reasoning is correct in isolation but loses to the readability win of preserving the semantic colour on the focused pin. The token stays for A/B.
- **Why the per-category darker-shade stroke (vs flat white or the original Soko/Ink)**: white (r2) read like a cluster bubble — borrowed visual vocabulary from the wrong primitive. The per-category mix of `0.7 · pin + 0.3 · sokoPink` (r2 follow-on) shifted the hue too far. A flat darker shade of the pin's own fill (r3) ties the border back to the pin without changing colour family.
- **Why always-unlocked (vs keeping the chip)**: every interaction began with a chip tap, which read as friction more than as an affordance. Removing it surfaced the underlying scroll trade-off (a fully interactive map inside a column hijacks touch gestures over the map area), but that trade-off is recoverable by touching outside the map area. The chip is preserved behind a single flag so an A/B test can re-introduce it if the scroll behaviour proves disruptive at scale.
- **Detail-page parity**: `EventMapBlock` and `VenueMapBlock` now also pass `selectedMarkerIds: {detail_pin_id}` so the detail-page pin picks up the same selection treatment (radius 17, 3-px darker stroke). The detail-page pin IS the focus of the page. Multi-venue events on the event detail page still render only the primary venue — the powering endpoints (`getEventDetail`, legacy `getEvent`) don't carry per-occurrence coords. Parallel BE follow-up tracked alongside [PROD-2219](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-2219/slim-list-items-add-per-item-occurrence-locations-occurrence-scope) (which fixes the slim-endpoint version of the same gap for the zine item-page map).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/map_marker_model.dart` (constants); `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_web.dart` + `mapbox_map_native.dart` (style expressions); `lib/core/theme/app_colors.dart` (three new selected-stroke tokens); `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_map_block.dart` + `list_map_view.dart` + `zine/list_zine_item_page.dart` (heights + flag); `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_map_block.dart` + `lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_map_block.dart` (detail-page highlight + flag). Full narrative in [`features/maps.md` § 15 ticket history](../features/maps.md#15-ticket-history) (PROD-2205-followup r1–r4 entries).

### D182: Discovery search redesign — lilac "Descobre a cidade" CTA replaces magnifier circle; chip palette goes per-category; default-content grid takes over while the overlay is open (PROD-2221)
- **Choice — idle action bar**: the magnifier `CircleIconButton` is replaced by a labeled `BtSqIco` pill on `BtSqIcoVariant.lilac` (Soko/Lilac `#C588F2`) with the localized "Descobre a cidade" / "Descubra a cidade" / "Discover the city" label (new ARB key `discoveryActionBarDiscoverCity`). The pill is `Expanded` so it stretches between the left edge and the location pill on the right. The create-zine pill is dropped from `/discovery` entirely (new prop `CityActionBar.showCreateZinePill: bool = true` — `/yours` keeps it on). The location pill itself switches from `BtSqIcoVariant.selected` (Soko/Pink) to `BtSqIcoVariant.normal` (Soko/Ink @ 6 %, neutral gray) on every surface so the lilac CTA reads as the primary affordance.
- **Choice — search overlay**: the input pill no longer takes the full row width — `SearchOverlay` gained an `inputTrailingBuilder` slot, and `DiscoverySearchOverlay` plugs in a sibling `ActionBarLocationPill` so the city pill stays visible while typing. The "All" chip (PROD-2026) is removed from Discovery (`includeAllCategory: false`); `/yours` keeps it. Selected chips paint in a per-category Soko tint via `BtSqIco.selectedBackgroundOverride`: Zines → Soko/Yellow, Eventos → Soko/Green, Sítios → Soko/Blue. The "All" pill on `/yours` keeps Soko/Pink.
- **Choice — default content while overlay is open**: when the search overlay is open AND the debounced query is below `kDiscoverySearchMinQueryLength`, `_DiscoveryBody` switches from `_DiscoveryShelves` (Daily Drop, Weekly Bundle, etc.) to a new `DefaultContentSection` — a vertical 2-up `SearchResultsView` grid backed by the active filter's feed (Zines → `trendingShelfProvider` "Most followed", Eventos → `happeningShelfProvider` "A acontecer", Sítios → `nearYouPlacesShelfProvider` "Perto de ti"). Events + places are sorted client-side by `distanceKm` ascending so the closest item always leads regardless of how the BE ranked the page. Infinite scroll piggybacks on `ShellSliverScope.scrollController`. Daily Drop stays on idle Discovery — it's hidden only while the overlay is open.
- **Choice — guest-mode clamp**: unauthenticated viewers see the first 6 cards crisp, then the remainder rendered behind an `ImageFiltered(ImageFilter.blur(sigmaX: 8, sigmaY: 8))` + `IgnorePointer` curtain, with a centered `GuestBlurCtaCard` floating above and a tap routed via `navigateToLoginPreservingReturn(referrer: AuthReferrer.guestGateHome)`. `loadMore()` is short-circuited for guests so we don't burn bandwidth on cards they can't see. 6 = three paired rows in the 2-up grid; cleanly avoids a half-filled trailing row at the boundary.
- **Choice — chat-bar cleanup**: the "Recentes" pill is removed from `ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow`; the row collapses to Add-to-Soko (left) + WhatsApp brand glyph (right). The history drawer is still reachable from the chat conversation composer's bottom strip (D173), so no functional regression.
- **Why this isn't a fork of the DS primitives**: every per-category color decision flows through `BtSqIco.selectedBackgroundOverride` — no new variants needed for the chip palette. The lilac search pill does need a new `BtSqIcoVariant.lilac` (added; matches the existing `Soko/Lilac` token already used by the `discoveryHelpUsInstagramPost` CTA per §5 of the action-bar figma cache). Location pill recolor is a one-line variant flip in `ActionBarLocationPill`, applied uniformly across surfaces so future hosts pick it up.
- **Why the keep-alive on the three shelf providers**: idle Discovery's `TrendingShelf`/`HappeningShelf`/`NearYouPlacesShelf` and the overlay's `DefaultContentSection` are mutually exclusive subtrees of `_DiscoveryBody`. Without `ref.keepAlive()` the refCount transiently drops to 0 during the swap; Riverpod schedules disposal and the fresh notifier's first refresh races with the overlay's mount. See [`learnings/keepalive-for-shared-autodispose-across-mutually-exclusive-subtrees.md`](../learnings/keepalive-for-shared-autodispose-across-mutually-exclusive-subtrees.md) for the full diagnosis.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/screens/discovery_screen.dart` (3-state body switch + new action-bar props), `lib/shared/widgets/city_action_bar.dart` (idle row + search-pill plumbing), `lib/shared/widgets/action_bar_location_pill.dart` (extracted helper, default `BtSqIcoVariant.normal`), `lib/shared/widgets/search_overlay.dart` (`inputTrailing` / `inputTrailingBuilder` slots), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_search_overlay.dart` (location-pill wiring + `includeAllCategory: false`), `lib/shared/widgets/search_category_tabs.dart` (per-category `selectedBackgroundOverride`), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/default_content_section.dart` (new — grid + guest blur + distance sort + scroll listener), `lib/features/discovery/providers/trending_shelf_provider.dart` (converted to paginated `StateNotifier` with `refresh()` / `loadMore()`), `lib/features/discovery/providers/happening_shelf_provider.dart` + `near_you_places_shelf_provider.dart` (`ref.keepAlive()` added), `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart` (Recents pill dropped from `ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow`). Amends [D177](#d177-mobile-overflow-yielding-for-cityactionbar-location-pill--search-tab-row--btsqico-stays-content-sized-and-dumb) (location pill recolor) and [D169](#d169-cityactionbar-location-pill-is-conditional-per-host-surface-prod-2145) (new `showCreateZinePill` peer to `showLocationPill`).
- **Tests**: `test/shared/widgets/city_action_bar_test.dart` — added a `PROD-2221 Discovery action bar` group (idle layout, overlay-open layout with location pill + 3-chip row, `BtSqIcoVariant.lilac` assertion). `test/features/discovery/providers/trending_shelf_provider_pagination_test.dart` — new file covering `refresh`/`loadMore`/empty-list filter/error-clear (mirrors `happening_shelf_provider_test.dart`).

### D183: Discovery search overlay does NOT auto-focus its input on mobile — keeps the soft keyboard from covering the just-revealed default content (PROD-2240)
- **Choice**: the shared `SearchOverlay` widget keeps its "auto-focus on mount → soft keyboard pops" behavior as the default (`autoFocus: true`), but `DiscoverySearchOverlay` passes `autoFocus: !isMobile` where `isMobile = defaultTargetPlatform == TargetPlatform.iOS || .android`. On native iOS/Android **and** mobile-web (Flutter web maps the OS via user-agent, so the single check covers both), tapping the lilac "Descobre a cidade" pill now reveals the input + tabs + `DefaultContentSection` grid (per [D182](#d182-discovery-search-redesign--lilac-descobre-a-cidade-cta-replaces-magnifier-circle-chip-palette-goes-per-category-default-content-grid-takes-over-while-the-overlay-is-open-prod-2221)) without the OS keyboard sliding up to cover the bottom half of the screen. Desktop web continues to focus on reveal — no soft keyboard there, so "tap pill → type immediately" is still the right affordance.
- **Why only Discovery, not `/yours`**: PROD-2240 was scoped to the "Discover the city" CTA on `/discovery`. The shared `SearchOverlay` parameter default stays `true` so `ListsSearchOverlay` (`/yours`) keeps its current behavior. If a follow-up ticket reports the same UX bug on `/yours`, the fix is to copy the same `isMobile` block into `lists_search_overlay.dart` — no further plumbing needed.
- **Why `defaultTargetPlatform`, not a viewport-width threshold**: the signal we care about is "does this device have a soft keyboard," not "is the viewport narrow." Mirrors the existing pattern at `lib/core/services/push_permission_service.dart:84-86` (`_isMobile` derived the same way). A viewport-width threshold would mis-classify iPad landscape (soft keyboard, wide viewport) and a tiny resized desktop window (hardware keyboard, narrow viewport).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/search_overlay.dart` — added `final bool autoFocus = true` parameter; gated the `WidgetsBinding.instance.addPostFrameCallback((_) { _focusNode.requestFocus(); })` block on `widget.autoFocus`. `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_search_overlay.dart` — added the `defaultTargetPlatform` check and `autoFocus: !isMobile`. Builds on the `SearchOverlay` shared-widget split established in [D122](#d122-searchresultsview--searchoverlay-are-shared-widgets-each-surface-owns-data-routing-only).

### D184: Profiling onboarding renders through the canonical Soko design system, not bespoke screens
- **Choice**: Every screen in the `/user-profiling/*` flow (location step → 4 vibe steps → interests → loading → persona reveal → smart lists) was rewritten to consume the same Soko primitives the rest of the app uses: `AppTheme.display(Primary)/body` for typography, `SokoCtaButton` / `BtSqIco` for actions, `ListZineCover` for the smart-list cards, `showSoko(...)` for transient notifications, and the canonical character illustrations under `assets/images/illustrations/soko-*.webp`. Old bespoke pieces (`PersonaIllustration` widget, ad-hoc `OutlinedButton`+`ElevatedButton.icon` footers, `_cardPalette` of one-off hex colours, `SnackBar` calls) were deleted or migrated.
- **Why**: the original profiling flow shipped as a parallel visual world — inline `TextStyle(fontSize: 36, fontWeight: w400)` titles, hand-rolled radius-28 pills, raw `Color(0xFF…)` literals, `ScaffoldMessenger.showSnackBar` toasts. A QA walkthrough surfaced the drift screen-by-screen; each iteration confirmed that the right answer was always "there's a Soko primitive for this — use it." Routing through the design system makes future Soko brand tweaks (font, colour, button shape) propagate into onboarding for free instead of leaving it on its own visual island.
- **Cards (vibe steps, Figma `6614:15148`)**: `lib/features/user_profiling/widgets/vibe_card.dart` — radius **6**, **2 px `sokoInk` border always** (selection communicated by the chip, not the border), `EdgeInsets.all(20)`, `crossAxisAlignment: end` so the chip lands at the lower-right corner. Label uses Mobile/H2 — `AppTheme.displayPrimary(fontSize: 30, fontWeight: w400, color: sokoInk, height: 1.0)`. Selected-state indicator is a 24 px `_CheckCircle` filled with `sokoInk` containing a 16 px `Icons.check_rounded` (matches the save-to-list `_RowChip` glyph but not its 30 px/sokoPink chrome — that variant was explored and reverted because the user wanted the lighter chip to keep the card colour dominant).
- **Interest chips (hobbies step, Figma `6614:14997`)**: `lib/features/user_profiling/widgets/interest_chip_grid.dart` — radius **6**, fixed **40 px** height, `EdgeInsets.symmetric(14, 10)`, **1 px `AppColors.sokoInk8` border** (the canonical 8 %-alpha token, same constant the `add_to_list` `_RowChip` uses for its unselected state). Selected state fills with one of four canonical brand tokens rotated by index — `sokoGreen → sokoBlue → sokoYellow → sokoLilac` (`VibePalette._interestColors`). Layout is `Wrap(spacing: 6, runSpacing: 6, alignment: WrapAlignment.center)` with content-sized chips — explicitly NOT a strict 2-column grid (one iteration tried that with `Expanded` and long labels truncated; the user redirected to natural wrap so long chips like "Atividades para crianças" get their own solo centered row).
- **Smart-list cards (result page, Figma "Só para ti")**: `lib/features/user_profiling/screens/smart_lists_screen.dart::_SmartListCard` reuses the canonical **`ListZineCover`** (`lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_cover.dart`) instead of a bespoke coloured `Container`. Each card resolves its cover via `ZineCoverRecipe.fromFields(...)` from the BE-provided `cover_*` slots on `SmartList` (the OpenAPI submit response mirrors `ListIdentityOut`'s cover slots — PROD-2260). For profile-generated interest lists the BE ships `cover_type=uploaded_photo` + a curated `Zine-Profiling-*.jpg` URL, so the card renders that artwork directly; the persona-fallback list keeps the deterministic colour-mode cover. Cover is locked to `AspectRatio(4/5)` (canonical zine shape) and grid `childAspectRatio` is **0.50**, sized so the worst-case 3-line description fits below the cover without compressing the curated artwork — see [`learnings/gridcell-expanded-image-shrinks-under-variable-text.md`](../learnings/gridcell-expanded-image-shrinks-under-variable-text.md) for the trap and the rationale (the original 0.62 + `Expanded` cover let longer captions silently shrink the artwork on a single card while leaving its siblings full-size). See D185 for the systematic suppression of the title-text + Soko logo + paper-texture overlay on `from_profiling` covers.
- **Chrome subtitle (every step) — Mobile/B1 Reg**: the header label ("Let's create your perfect day" / "Your vibe feels:" / persona name) on `user_profiling_flow_screen.dart::_Header`, `persona_reveal_screen.dart::_RevealHeader`, and `smart_lists_screen.dart::_ListsHeader` all use `AppTheme.display(fontSize: 18, fontWeight: w300, color: sokoInk, height: 1.0)`. Note this uses `display()` (Zalando + display letter-spacing rate `-0.02` → `-0.36 px` at 18) rather than `body()` (`-0.01` → `-0.18 px`) so the Figma's `-0.36` tracking matches exactly.
- **H1 (page titles) and H0 (persona name)**: every page title — "Where do you live?", each vibe step prompt, "Só para ti" — uses `AppTheme.displayPrimary(fontSize: 42, fontWeight: w300, color: sokoInk, height: 0.94)` (SeasonMix, Mobile/H1, tracking `-0.84`). Persona reveal bumps to Mobile/H0: `fontSize: 52, height: 0.86, tracking -1.04` (still `displayPrimary`). Each title gets **40 px top padding** for breathing room below the chrome.
- **Footer button pairs**: every step that needs a Skip / Continue or Maybe-later / Continue row uses **`BtSqIco(expand: true)`** wrapped in `Expanded`, NOT `SokoCtaButton`. This matches the `SokoCtaButton` docstring: *"For sheet button-row pairs (Cancel + Confirm) keep using `BtSqIco` — same shape sized down to 40 px for sticky-footer rows."* Skip / Maybe later → `BtSqIcoVariant.idle` (outlined transparent), Continue → `BtSqIcoVariant.selected` (sokoPink fill + `Icons.north_east`). `BtSqIco.onTap` is non-nullable, so the profiling flow's disabled-Continue state wraps the chip in `Opacity(0.4) + IgnorePointer(ignoring: !canContinue)`.
- **Single-CTA footers**: `smart_lists_screen.dart::_ListsFooter` ("Explore the app") is `SokoCtaButton(variant: pink, icon: Icons.north_east)` since there's no paired secondary action. Since PROD-2963 it is no longer pinned to the bottom edge: it flows inline below the zine grid, centered and hug-content (`expand: false`) — see [D357](#d357-the-profiling-zines-explore-the-app-cta-flows-inline-below-the-cards-not-pinned-to-the-bottom-edge-2026-09-16-prod-2963).
