- **Soko characters per screen**: `assets/images/illustrations/soko-walking-and-reading.webp` powers `user_profiling_loading_screen.dart::_LoadingBody`, matching `activation_callback_screen._buildProcessingView` (both are async-wait surfaces — one shared character keeps the visual language coherent). `soko-with-camera.webp` (a new asset added in this redesign) powers `persona_reveal_screen.dart` — **one character for all personas, not per-persona**, because we only have a handful of characters and the "discovering your vibe" framing is universal. The deleted `PersonaIllustration` widget previously held a `persona.id → asset` map; deleted because the new design has no consumer (per CLAUDE.md "if you are certain that something is unused, you can delete it completely").
- **`SokoCtaVariant.green`**: a new variant was added to the canonical `SokoCtaButton` (`lib/shared/widgets/soko_cta_button.dart`) so the "Use my exact location" pill on the location step could be a real Soko CTA instead of a bespoke green container. Uses `AppColors.sokoGreen` bg + `sokoInk` fg, slots into the same hover/pressed colour-math as the other variants. Onboarding is the only consumer today; new green-pill surfaces should reuse the variant.
- **`Use my exact location` wiring (PROD-followup candidate)**: tap reads `cityAutoScopeProvider` (the same provider Discovery's "near me" shelves use — IP geolocation → `/geo/cities` autocomplete → best-match), then `setCity(scope.city.name)`. Works on web (no native geocoder needed). `showSoko` (success / error) replaces the previous `ScaffoldMessenger.showSnackBar`. The button text + icon are baked into `SokoCtaButton`, including the in-flight `loading` spinner.
- **Layout convention (every step except smart lists)**: title pinned to the top with 40 px margin, content vertically centered in the remaining space via `LayoutBuilder → SingleChildScrollView → ConstrainedBox(minHeight: constraints.maxHeight) → IntrinsicHeight → Column(mainAxisAlignment: center)`. Smart lists deliberately skips the IntrinsicHeight (its grid `shrinkWrap`s already) — see [`learnings/scrollview-constrainedbox-expanded-trap.md`](../learnings/scrollview-constrainedbox-expanded-trap.md) for the gotcha that produced this carve-out.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/user_profiling/{screens,widgets,dev}/*.dart`, `lib/shared/widgets/soko_cta_button.dart` (new green variant), `assets/images/illustrations/soko-with-camera.webp` (new). Branch: `gracaninja/redesign-profiling-screens`.

### D185: `from_profiling` lists render plain — no title overlay, no Soko logo, no paper texture — gated upstream in `ZineCoverRecipe.plain` (PROD-2260)
- **Choice**: lists with `system_kind=from_profiling` (the 5 starter zines seeded by `/user_profiling/submit`) render their covers as a clean photo only — no overlaid title text, no Soko-icon glyph, no bottom colour stripe, no paper-texture overlay. The curated `Zine-Profiling-*.jpg` assets ship with their own typography and would compete with any app-drawn chrome. The gating lives on the recipe (`ZineCoverRecipe.plain: bool`, default `false`), set upstream by `ZineCoverRecipe.fromUserList(list)` from `list.isFromProfiling`. `ListZineCover` honours `recipe.plain` by skipping the title, logo, colour-stripe, and texture layers — every surface that consumes `fromUserList` (lists hub `ListCard`, discovery shelves, zine view, search results) inherits the behaviour automatically. The profiling "Just for you" screen consumes the same recipe but routes through `fromFields(..., plain: true)` directly, because `SmartList` from the submit response doesn't carry `system_kind` (every list there is `from_profiling` by construction).
- **Why on the recipe, not per call site**: an earlier iteration threaded a `bool suppressOverlay` prop into `ListZineCoverCard` + each shelf card and read `list.isFromProfiling` at every wrapper. User flagged it ("make it a systematic implementation"). The recipe-level flag means future cover surfaces don't need to know the `from_profiling` rule exists — they just render `recipe`, and `fromUserList` makes the decision once. Mirrors the centralisation rationale in [D88]'s "Single recipe resolver" bullet (`fromUserList` is already the one place every surface goes through).
- **`UserList.isFromProfiling`**: new getter alongside the existing `isFromInstagramShare`. Returns `systemKind == 'from_profiling'`. The constant is the `system_kind` enum value the BE renamed from `from_onboarding` in PROD-2260.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/utils/zine_cover_recipe.dart` (recipe + factories), `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_cover.dart` (suppression logic — `effectiveShowTitle/Logo` derived from `recipe.plain`, texture layer wrapped in `if (!plain)`), `lib/data/models/user_list.dart` (`isFromProfiling` getter), `lib/features/user_profiling/screens/smart_lists_screen.dart` (explicit `plain: true` on `fromFields(...)`).
- **Successor (PROD-2300, 2026-05-31) — granular flags replace `recipe.plain`**: BE [PROD-2297](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-2297) shipped three independent per-list curator toggles (`cover_show_title`, `cover_show_texture`, `cover_show_logo`, BOOL NOT NULL DEFAULT TRUE on `user_lists`) so backoffice can hide individual chrome elements on any list — not only profile-zines. Flutter mirrors them as `ZineCoverRecipe.showTitle/showTexture/showLogo` (default `true`). `ListZineCover` ANDs each with the widget-level arg: `effectiveShowTitle = widgetArg && recipe.showTitle`, same for logo; `effectiveShowTexture = recipe.showTexture` (no widget counterpart). Either gate hiding wins. **`recipe.plain` and the `UserList.isFromProfiling` getter were retired in this same PR** — `from_profiling` lists get `cover_show_*=false` from the BE (both backfill and the `/user_profiling/submit` response), so the granular flag carries the same intent. The recipe-level centralisation rationale in this decision still holds — curator toggles live on the recipe, not as per-call-site widget args, so future cover surfaces just render the recipe and inherit the behaviour. `SmartList` (the model for the profiling-submit response) gained the three booleans so the "Just for you" screen drops its explicit `plain: true` shortcut. Wired through `PublicList` → `UserList` via `_convertPublicListToUserList` so unauthenticated viewers honour curator toggles. See [PROD-2300](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-2300).

### D186: Daily Drop profiling CTA card visual (PROD-1988) + Soko Textures catalog grows 4 → 29
- **Choice**: when the BE returns `status: cta_profiling` from `/daily`, the daily-drop slot renders a `sokoPink`-filled, 6 px-radius, 399×213 card (matches the regular `DiscoveryImageTemplateCard` footprint so adjacent shelves don't shift) layered as `Stack`: (1) `Container(color: sokoPink)`, (2) the new `cover-texture-25.webp` paper-texture overlay (curated dust texture — see catalog note below) drawn `BoxFit.cover` inside an `IgnorePointer`, (3) a `Column(spaceBetween)` holding the `assets/images/illustrations/soko-seating-and-reading.webp` character centred at the top and a SeasonMix display headline + Zalando body subtitle stacked at the bottom. Copy lives under two new ARB keys `discoveryDailyDropProfilingCtaHeadline` ("Ainda não te conheço bem" / "I don't know you well yet") and `discoveryDailyDropProfilingCtaSubtitle` ("Clica aqui para eu descobrir um pouco mais de ti" / "Tap here so I can get to know you a bit more"); the older single-line key `discoveryDailyDropProfilingCtaTitle` was renamed (not edited) to avoid the PT/PT-BR value-protection check. Tap routes to `AppRoutes.userProfilingFlow` and fires `trackDailyDropProfilingCtaTap`.
- **Why a renamed key, not an edited one**: the L10n integrity check (`scripts/check-l10n-integrity.py --check values`) blocks PT/PT-BR translation edits on feature branches (those go through `translations/<date>` branches and a linguist pass). When the copy materially changes (single CTA line → headline + subtitle pair), renaming sidesteps the check while still letting EN/PT/PT-BR land at the same time. The discarded old key has no live consumer once the widget is rewired.
- **Soko Textures catalog growth (D184 amendment)**: `kZineTextureCatalog` + `kZineTextureKeys` in `lib/features/lists/utils/zine_cover_recipe.dart` grew from 4 ids (`01..04`) to 29 (`01..29`). The 25 new ids correspond 1:1 with the designer's Soko Textures set imported from `Downloads/Soko-Textures/Soko-Textures-App/Soko-texture-{1..25}.png`, with destination index = source index + 4 (so source #21 — the curated dust texture used here — lands as `cover-texture-25.webp`). All 25 are 384×384 WebP with derived alpha (~30–175 KB each, ~2.7 MB total addition) produced via the `magick … png32: → cwebp -alpha_q 95 …` pipeline documented in [`learnings/imagemagick-webp-alpha-preservation.md`](../learnings/imagemagick-webp-alpha-preservation.md) (the obvious one-step `magick PNG → WebP` pipeline silently drops the alpha channel to palette mode). Existing `01..04` are unchanged — replacing them would silently rewrite the cover of every list with `cover_texture: '01'..'04'` in the BE. New ids are appended at the *end* of `kZineTextureKeys` so the deterministic-by-listId fallback (`stableHash(listId) % kZineTextureKeys.length`) only re-buckets when a hash lands beyond the original 4 — preserving most existing lists' visual identity.
- **Tests coupled to catalog size**: two recipe-resolver tests in `zine_cover_recipe_test.dart` asserted `r.texture == kZineDefaultTextureId`. Those assertions only passed because `stableHash('list-1') % 4 == 0`, which happened to equal `'01' == kZineDefaultTextureId`. The real contract `fromFields` promises is "a deterministic, catalog-resident texture id", not "the default". Both tests now assert `kZineTextureCatalog.containsKey(r.texture)` plus determinism across two calls — the brittle equality is gone. Lesson worth carrying: assertions of the form `== kZineDefaultX` on hash-based picks are silently coupled to catalog cardinality.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/daily_drop_section.dart::_DailyDropProfilingCtaCard` (the visual + `_buildBody` short-circuit), `lib/l10n/intl_{en,pt,pt_BR}.arb` (two new keys), `assets/images/textures/cover-texture-05.webp` … `cover-texture-29.webp` (the 25 new textures), `lib/features/lists/utils/zine_cover_recipe.dart` (catalog + keys), `test/features/lists/utils/zine_cover_recipe_test.dart` (fixed two coupled tests).

### D187: Chat-bar "Recents" button — matched 6 px-radius grey rectangle, not a dark true pill
- **Choice**: `ChatBarTextPill` (`lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart`) — the "Recents" CTA on the right of the chat composer's bottom strip — renders as a 40 px-tall, 6 px-radius rectangle in `sokoShade4` (warm grey) with `sokoInk` text. It now mirrors the shape and weight of `CreateListPill` on the left so the two read as a matched sibling pair (radius, height, horizontal padding all aligned). Hover/press overlays flipped to `sokoInk-alpha-on-sokoShade4` (dark-on-light overlay model, same one `BtSqIco` uses on its light variants); the previous `sokoPaper-alpha-on-sokoInk` model is gone.
- **Why**: the prior PROD-1805 design used a true pill (radius 20, `sokoInk` fill, `sokoPaper` text) to give visual contrast with the pink Create-list CTA. In session-level review, the contrast read as "different affordance" rather than "secondary action of equal weight" — Recents looked like a separate widget category. Squaring its shape and softening its fill (grey instead of dark) keeps the colour distinction (pink primary vs grey secondary) without breaking the visual rhythm.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart` — `ChatBarTextPill` widget. Docstring updated to describe the new shape/colour intent; PROD-1805 reference removed because that decision is now superseded for this surface.

### D188: Cold-launch splash uses the same pink + Soko illustration canvas as `SokoWelcomeScreen` (PROD-2263 follow-up)
- **Choice**: while auth is initializing on cold launch, both `_SplashGate` (in `heyl_app/lib/app.dart`, wired via `MaterialApp.builder`) and the fallback `_SplashScreen` (at `/splash` in `heyl_app/lib/core/router/app_router.dart`) render `AppColors.sokoPink` background with the `soko-seating-and-reading.webp` illustration at 140 px width, centered. No spinner. The illustration's position matches `SokoWelcomeScreen`'s headline-area illustration so once `authStateProvider.isInitialized` flips true, the welcome canvas takes over and the character stays in place while the headline + CTA fade in.
- **Why no spinner**: the splash window is transient (a few hundred ms typically); a `CircularProgressIndicator` flashes briefly which feels jarring against a content-first welcome screen. The character on the brand canvas conveys "the app is here" without strobing motion.
- **Why this matters for Apple ATT review**: PROD-2263 fires the native ATT system dialog ~700 ms after the first post-frame callback. During that window the splash is what sits visibly behind iOS's modal. A bare loading spinner is not app-attributable; the Soko illustration on `sokoPink` is. Apple's reviewer recording shape is "fresh launch → identifiable app → ATT prompt → flow" — the splash canvas is the "identifiable app" beat. See [`learnings/att-prompt-must-fire-on-cold-launch.md`](../learnings/att-prompt-must-fire-on-cold-launch.md).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/app.dart` (`_SplashGate`) and `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` (`_SplashScreen`). Both reference the same asset path so they stay in sync; if either drifts, the cold-launch → welcome handoff loses its seam.

### D189: In-list item detail card gets a 15 px bottom gutter so the map ends cleanly (PROD-2270)
- **Choice**: `ListInContextDetailScreen` (`lib/features/lists/screens/list_in_context_detail_screen.dart`) wraps the embedded `VenueDetailBody`/`EventDetailBody` in a `Padding(bottom: 15)` inside the card's `ColoredBox`. This frames the body's last element with the same 15 px the body already reserves on its left/right gutters. Unconditional — every in-list detail card gets it, whether or not an "Aparece em" shelf follows the map.
- **Why**: the detail bodies render in `embedded: true` mode, which sets `bottomPad = 0` on the assumption that the wrapper owns all card chrome. But the wrapper's existing bottom spacer (`SizedBox(MediaQuery.padding.bottom + 20)`) sits *outside* the coloured `ClipRRect` card, so it never gave the card's *content* any internal breathing room. When an item had no "Aparece em / Appears in" shelf (`socialProof.lists` empty — typical for items that only live in your own zine), the square `MapboxMapWidget` tile became the last child and bled flush to the card's bottom edge — the bug the reporter saw on `/yours` ("map view has no clear ending"). Items that *did* have the shelf looked fine only because the shelf, not the map, was the last element. Verified on staging: trailing gap below the map went 20 px → 35 px (15 px card gutter + 20 px outside-card spacer).
- **Why in the wrapper, not the body**: the bodies' documented contract is "embedded mode renders zero chrome; the embedding wrapper owns card bounds, background paint, and bottom clearance." Putting the gutter in `ListInContextDetailScreen` keeps that contract intact (the bodies' `embedded` `bottomPad` stays 0) and leaves the standalone full-screen `VenueDetailScreen`/`EventDetailScreen` untouched — they use `embedded: false` and already pad with `OrientationUtils.bottomNavClearance(context) + 16`.
- **Why unconditional**: the alternative (`hasAppearsIn ? 0 : 15`) would require plumbing `socialProof.lists` state from the body up into the wrapper for a sub-15 px cosmetic nuance, and the flush-shelf case was itself a minor oversight. A consistent "every card ends 15 px from its bottom edge" rule is simpler and matches the side gutters; the extra trailing space below an Aparece-em shelf is harmless.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/lists/screens/list_in_context_detail_screen.dart` (the `Padding` inside the card `ColoredBox`). The `embedded: true` mount points are the only consumers of that flag (lines ~162/174 of the same file). Existing embedded-body padding tests (`test/features/{venue,event}_detail/widgets/*_embedded_test.dart`) still assert the body's own `bottomPad == 0` and are unaffected — though note those tests currently fail on `develop` for an unrelated harness reason; see [`learnings/embedded-detail-body-test-overflow.md`](../learnings/embedded-detail-body-test-overflow.md).

### D190: Guest mode reaches parity on iOS/Android — `/login` first, then Discovery with `GuestBanner` (reverses D100)
- **Choice**: Native (iOS/Android) builds now mirror the web guest experience. Unauthenticated visitors land on `/login` first; if they tap "Continue as guest" the choice is persisted (`explicitGuestModeProvider` → SharedPreferences key `explicit_guest_mode`) and they land on `/` (Discovery) with `GuestBanner` mounted above the shelves. The `kIsWeb` gates on `showGuestChrome` (in `DiscoveryShell`), on the `Continue as guest` CTA (in `login_screen.dart` / `login_form_screen.dart`), and on the splash + post-onboarding redirects are removed. Two surfaces stay web-only: `GuestSessionPopup` (the install-the-app dialog — meaningless on native) and `GuestBanner`'s `[Open app]` universal-link pill (hidden but space-preserving on native so the wordmark stays centered).
- **Why revert D100**: Apple reviewed iOS 1.1.1 and flagged the login wall under Guideline 5.1.1(v) (sign-in must not be forced before the user sees what they're signing up for). The previous shape — native guests bounced to `/login` with no way to peek at the product — failed that check. D100's web-only stance was based on a 401-storm risk (the home grid used to hit auth-only endpoints) that PROD-1979's in-screen blur/CTA primitives already eliminated for web; the same primitives now serve native uniformly, so the original "conversion surface" justification no longer outweighs the App Store review risk. UX parity satisfies 5.1.1(v) without sacrificing the conversion ask — the `[Log in]` CTA in `GuestBanner` is the first thing a guest sees on Discovery.
- **Why persist the guest choice** (instead of asking every cold launch): unauthenticated visitors hit `/login` only on the *first* launch (or after logout). Once they tap "Continue as guest" the choice survives reloads and relaunches via SharedPreferences, so a returning guest goes straight to `/`. Without persistence the user re-affirms the choice every session, which reads as "are you SURE you don't want to sign in" badgering. Logout clears the flag so the next account on the device starts at `/login` cleanly.
- **Why widen the no-choice-yet redirect** (was previously gated on `hasEverLoggedIn`): the legacy "session loss" branch only redirected returning users with expired tokens; first-time visitors fell through into guest UI. That asymmetry hid the sign-in option from new users — exactly the cohort Apple's 5.1.1(v) is about. Dropping the `hasEverLoggedIn` constraint makes every unauthenticated visitor without an explicit guest choice see `/login` first, regardless of history. Existing `returnUrl` preservation is unchanged — deep links survive the round-trip.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` (splash + no-choice-yet redirects), `lib/features/auth/screens/login_screen.dart` + `login_form_screen.dart` (always-visible Continue-as-guest CTA), `lib/features/onboarding/providers/login_history_provider.dart` (`explicitGuestModeProvider` + `setExplicitGuestMode`), `lib/providers/auth_provider.dart` (clears the flag in `onIdentityCleared`), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart` (`showGuestChrome = isGuest`), `lib/features/guest/widgets/guest_banner.dart` (`[Open app]` pill `Visibility(maintainSize: true)` on native). Full architecture in [features/guest-mode.md](../features/guest-mode.md).

### D191: Settings — Location row collapses two side-by-side toggles into a single tri-state picker (PROD-2303 step 7)
- **Choice**: `lib/features/preferences/widgets/location_consent_row.dart` replaces the prior `_LocationSharingHeaderToggle` (OS permission `Switch`) + `_LocationConsentToggle` (server-consent `SwitchListTile`) pair. The new row reads `locationConsentStateProvider` (PROD-2303 step 4) and renders one of three state labels — Precise / Approximate / Off — with a contextual status line ("A usar GPS · Lisboa, Portugal", "A usar IP · …", "Recomendações por país"). Tapping the row opens `LocationConsentPickerSheet` (a modal bottom sheet with radio rows + Aplicar). The OS permission `Switch` is gone; revoking the system grant now lives in a conditional secondary link ("Revogar permissão no sistema →") shown only on native when state=Off and OS permission is still granted.
- **Why**: codex flagged the two-toggle layout as the core UX trap in the PROD-2301 design consult: server consent and OS permission can disagree (consent ON / permission denied = backend stores intent but data layer falls back to IP; consent OFF / permission granted = GPS available but server says no), and two side-by-side `Switch`es can never honestly describe that combined state. A single row whose label *is* the actual data-layer behaviour eliminates the divergence — the row can't lie because there's only one source of truth, derived from the state machine.
- **Per-platform radio set** (Q1, design review 2026-05-30): iOS shows Precise / Approximate / Off (mirrors Apple's "Precise location" toggle); Android shows Precise / Off only (no OS-level coarse permission to map "Approximate" to); web shows Precise / Approximate / Off (Approximate = consent ON, browser permission denied → IP fallback). Computed by `availableLocationConsentChoices()` in `lib/features/preferences/models/location_consent_choice.dart` and passed *into* the sheet, so the sheet itself never branches on platform.
- **Behaviour on Precise + OS denies** (B3): the sheet still PATCHes `location_opt_in: true` (the user's intent), pops with `LocationConsentApplyResult.downgradedToApprox`, and shows a snackbar "Sem permissão de GPS. A usar localização aproximada." This matches PROD-2285 Siga's "intent first, OS override accepted gracefully" semantic — the user's answer survives even when iOS overrides the GPS prompt.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/preferences/widgets/location_consent_row.dart` (the row + skeleton + conditional revoke link + web cache explainer), `lib/features/preferences/widgets/location_consent_picker_sheet.dart` (the modal sheet), `lib/features/preferences/models/location_consent_choice.dart` (the enum + per-platform helper), mounted at `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart` between the Language section and the Notifications section. Full design rationale + 11 interaction-state decisions in [`.context/2026-05-30-prod-2303-step7-settings-consolidation-plan.md`](../../.context/2026-05-30-prod-2303-step7-settings-consolidation-plan.md). Umbrella architecture in [docs/designs/prod-2301-location-consent-all-cohorts.md](../designs/prod-2301-location-consent-all-cohorts.md).

### D192: Add-to-list rows show a `previewImages` collage, not the list's recipe cover — recipe is only the empty-list fallback (PROD-2300 audit close-out)
- **Choice**: `ListThumbnailStack` (45 × 60 px tile in `AddToListSheet`) renders a collage of up to 4 of the list's `previewImages` (1 → full tile, 2 → halves, 3 → big-left + 2-right, 4 → 2×2 grid). The list's `ZineCoverRecipe` is consulted **only when `previewImages` is empty** — then `ListZineCover` (chrome off, per [thumbnail-chrome learning](../learnings/zinecover-chrome-off-at-thumbnail-sizes.md)) draws the recipe's background colour, texture, and photo. Legacy raw `coverImageUrl` remains as a last-ditch fallback for non-list callers that don't pass a recipe.
- **Why**: every *other* list-cover surface (hub cards, shelf cards, list detail hero, change-cover preview) shows the list's *identity* — title + texture + chrome — because the user is choosing between or looking at a list. The add-to-list picker is the opposite UX: the user already knows which list is which by name, and is asking "where would this fit?" — the collage of items currently in the list is a stronger signal for that decision than yet another rendering of the list's cover. Confirmed by the existing PROD-1861 design (Add-to-list sheet introduced the collage-only thumbnail).
- **Why not unify with `ListZineCover`**: an earlier audit pass flagged add-to-list as inconsistent with the recipe pipeline. That's correct in the narrow "every list surface goes through the recipe" sense, but wrong for UX. Recording this decision so future audits don't accidentally rip out the collage in the name of consistency.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/list_thumbnail_stack.dart` (the widget with the `previewImages > coverRecipe > coverImageUrl > placeholder` fallback chain), `lib/features/lists/widgets/add_to_list_sheet.dart:778` (the sole call site at this size — passes both `previewImages` and `coverRecipe`/`title` so the fallback works).
- **Related**: the audit also closed three other gaps (event/venue "Aparece em" shelves + typeahead list row) which *do* render the identity cover — those surfaces *don't* have a `previewImages` collage option and were just sending the raw URL. See the [PROD-1908 history bullet for 2026-05-31 (audit-gap closure)](../features/PROD-1908-zine-cover-renderer.md) and [cover-gap-audit learning](../learnings/cover-gap-audit-fe-deserialisation.md).

### D193: User-profiling skip-confirm popup is a `DSSheetShell` bottom sheet, not an `AlertDialog`
- **Choice**: the "Skip the survey?" confirmation triggered from the Skip CTA on every step of the `/user-profiling/flow` screens renders as a `DSSheetShell` bottom sheet via `showBottomSheetWithHiddenNav`, with a paired `BtSqIco` action row — left `BtSqIcoVariant.normal` Cancel (`LucideIcons.x`, "No") + right `BtSqIcoVariant.selected` confirm ("Skip anyway", `LucideIcons.chevrons_right`, default sokoPink fill). Title uses `ZalandoSans 18 / w700 / -0.36` and body `ZalandoSans 14 / w300 / -0.14`, both centred on `sokoInk`. Replaces the legacy Material `AlertDialog` (`sokoDark`-filled cancel + plain TextButton confirm) the [D184](#d184-profiling-onboarding-renders-through-the-canonical-soko-design-system-not-bespoke-screens) profiling rewrite left behind.
- **Why**: extends [D165](#d165-popups-in-menu-are-bottom-sheets-not-alertdialogs) beyond `/menu/*` and closes the gap in the D184 profiling rewrite that missed this popup. The `AlertDialog` defaulted to Material's surface colour and rounded-rect chrome — visibly a different design system from the rest of the profiling flow, which routes every other modal surface through `DSSheetShell`. Same rationale as D165 — `sokoPaper` chrome + paired `BtSqIco` row unifies the modal visual language.
- **Action-row pattern**: identical to D165 and the canonical `delete_list_sheet.dart` pair — `Row(Expanded(BtSqIco normal), SizedBox(12), Expanded(BtSqIco selected))` with `bodyPadding: EdgeInsets.fromLTRB(20, 20, 20, 14)`. Confirm side stays the default `BtSqIcoVariant.selected` sokoPink fill (no `selectedBackgroundOverride: AppColors.sokoRed`) because skipping isn't destructive in the data-loss sense Delete is — it's just an opt-out.
- **Where it lives**: `lib/features/user_profiling/screens/user_profiling_flow_screen.dart::_UserProfilingFlowScreenState._confirmSkip`. Strings still resolved via `UserProfilingDevStrings` (`personaOnboardingSkipConfirm*` keys, not yet promoted to ARB — same as the rest of the dev-strings catalogue D184 left in place).

<!-- Most recent: D194 -->

### D194: Discovery search overlay closes on every "back to home" gesture, and the X inside it only clears text (PROD-2280)
- **Choice (entry points):** The Discovery search overlay (`searchOpenProvider == true`) is treated as a logical sub-page even though it lives on `/`. Every gesture that means "take me home" must clear `searchOpenProvider` + `searchQueryProvider`, not just route to `/`: (1) bottom-nav Home tap (`DiscoveryBottomNav`), (2) Soko wordmark tap in `DiscoveryHeader` (newly tappable as part of this change), (3) system / browser back via the existing `PopScope` on `DiscoveryScreen`. The X inside the input pill (shared `SearchOverlay`) now does the opposite — it **only** clears the typed text + query, leaving the overlay open and the keyboard state untouched.
- **Bottom-nav visual state:** `isHomeSelected` in `DiscoveryBottomNav` is gated by `!searchOverlayOpen`, so while the overlay is up the Home cell reads as deselected — "Início" label visible, Soko brand mark at the small (22 px) size. Tapping Home then closes the overlay and the cell flips to selected, replaying the elastic stamp-bounce + wobble. This mirrors the affordance the user gets in every other tab (Library, Chat, +Create): Home is "selected" only when the actual homepage view is on screen.
- **Why:** the overlay reads to the user as a standalone "Descobre a cidade" page (full-bleed grid, dedicated input, distinct chrome). When tapping Home from /lists or /chat left the overlay visible, users reported being stuck on Discovery; when tapping Home from `/` with the overlay open was a no-op (`context.go('/')` collapses because we're already there), the only escape was the small X next to the search bar. Albino's report (cited in PROD-2280) framed this as "the homepage button doesn't work." Repurposing the X as a clear-input affordance (the iOS pattern) and routing dismissal through Home + wordmark + system back makes every "go home" surface behave consistently and removes the X-as-only-exit trap.
- **Why X-clears-only is safe for the product tour:** the tour controller (`product_tour_controller.dart::closeSearchOverlay`) writes directly to `searchOpenProvider`; it never simulates a tap on the X. Re-pointing the X handler away from `openProvider` did not break the tour's discoverCity → createSheet transition.
- **Shared-widget contract change:** `SearchOverlay._close` is renamed `_clearInput` and no longer touches `openProvider`. This is the contract for **every** consumer of the shared widget — Discovery and the Lists hub (`ListsSearchOverlay`). The hub leaves the same way: bottom nav, system back. Anything new that mounts `SearchOverlay` inherits this behaviour.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_bottom_nav.dart` (Home tap handler + `isHomeSelected` gate), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_header.dart` (the wordmark now wraps in `GestureDetector` + `MouseRegion`, runs the same handler, includes the `TourStep.profileMenu` guard so it can't race the last tour step), `lib/shared/widgets/search_overlay.dart` (`_clearInput`). The `PopScope` half of the contract is the existing one in `lib/features/discovery/screens/discovery_screen.dart`, unchanged — see [`learnings/popscope-for-page-local-overlay-state.md`](../learnings/popscope-for-page-local-overlay-state.md) (the "same-shell entry points" section is the sibling pattern).

<!-- Most recent: D194 -->

### D195: User-profiling picker drops the "Reset to auto-detected" affordance (and the auto-preselection hint)
- **Choice:** When the profiling-first-page input opens `showLocationScopePicker`, it passes `autoScope: null`. This suppresses two affordances inside the sheet that depend on a non-null `autoScope`: the "Repor localização automática" / "Reset to auto-detected" `OutlinedButton.icon` at the bottom of the body (gated by `hasAuto = autoScope != null` in `LocationScopePickerSheet`), and the "We pre-selected your current country and city" hint at the top (gated by `isOnAutoScope`). Discovery's pill (`ActionBarLocationPill._openPicker`) still passes a non-null `autoScope` and keeps both affordances. **(Superseded 2026-07-23, PROD-3204: the `autoScope` param and the sheet were removed; `MapLocationPickerScreen` has no reset-to-auto/preselection affordances. Kept for historical context.)**
- **Why:** the `autoScope` parameter that any caller has available is the **IP-cascade** scope from `cityAutoScopeProvider`. In profiling, the "Usar a minha localização exata" CTA on the same page uses **device GPS** via `locationProvider.requestPermissionOnly()` — a different (and more accurate) source. Surfacing "Reset to auto-detected" alongside the GPS CTA gives the user two affordances that look like the same intent but produce different cities (often the same city, sometimes the user's GPS coordinates resolved to a different city than ipapi.co's IP guess). Tapping "Repor" after the GPS path would silently replace the more-accurate pick with the less-accurate one. Dropping it removes the inconsistency without losing the auto-reset intent (the CTA already provides it, with the better source).
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/user_profiling/widgets/location_step.dart::_LocationStepState._openPicker` (passes `autoScope: null`). Picker code unchanged — its `null` branch already covers the absent-autoscope case via `hasAuto` and `isOnAutoScope`.

<!-- Most recent: D199 -->

### D196: Corner-peel hint runs on every list-cover surface with a deterministic banded pattern (card 0 + 1 per band of 4)
- **Choice:** Every surface that renders a list cover the user can tap to open the zine — Discovery shelves (Yours, Recommended, Editor Picks, Trending, City Guides, Following), event/venue "Aparece em" rows, search results (`HighlightedShelfCard` via `SearchResultsView` on Discovery and `/yours`), and the smart-lists onboarding grid (`_SmartListCard`) — overlays a slow corner-peel hint via the shared `ZineCoverPeelOverlay` widget. The gate is a deterministic banded pattern, not a probability roll: band 0 = indices [0, 1] (size 2) where card 0 always peels (first-impression guarantee); bands 1+ = size 4 with the picked position cycling `bandIndex % 4` across bands so peelers don't all line up in the same column.
- **Why:** the peel-hint reads as "this card opens like a magazine" and ties the shelf affordance to the page-turn flip the zine itself uses. The first-impression guarantee matters because users won't always scroll to discover the affordance; the rotating "1 in 4" cadence below avoids drowning the page in motion. A probability gate (the previous design) sometimes left card 0 and card 1 both flat for ~25 % of shelves, which broke the first-impression contract.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/zine_cover_peel_overlay.dart` (the shared overlay + the `_shouldPeel` banded logic); `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_tease_peel.dart` (the underlying looping `CustomPaint`, perf-hardened with `RepaintBoundary` + `VisibilityDetector` — see [`learnings/visibilitydetector-pause-ticker-in-listview.md`](../learnings/visibilitydetector-pause-ticker-in-listview.md)). Wired into `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/{canonical_shelf_card,highlighted_shelf_card}.dart`, all the shelf wrappers that pass `indexInShelf: index`, `lib/shared/widgets/search_results_view.dart` (`_SearchResultsGrid` → row → cell), `lib/features/user_profiling/screens/smart_lists_screen.dart::_SmartListCard`.

### D197: Zine cover entry plays one dramatic first-cycle peel that reveals the actual first item page; subsequent loops are subtle
- **Choice:** The `ListZineTeasePeel` on `ListZineCoverCard` is configured with `firstCyclePeakRealFlipProgress: 0.25` and `peakRealFlipProgress: 0.15`. The first ~6 s loop after entering the zine lifts the corner to 0.25 (≈ 25 % of the top edge and 50 % of the right edge); after a one-shot Timer fires at the cycle boundary, the peel drops to the steady-state 0.15 forever after. Behind the lift, the peel renders the ACTUAL `ListZineItemCard` widget for `state.items[0]` — same one `TurnPageView` would paint at index 1 — clipped to the lifted-away triangle via `ClipPath`. The card is rendered with `pageLabel: null`, `isActive: false`, `isBlurred: <guest-cap>`.
- **Why:** the first peel doubles as both a "this is a zine you swipe through" hint AND a content preview — users see the real first item's title, hero photo, buttons through the corner, not a solid neutral or a placeholder. After one cycle the loop continues subtly so it doesn't grab attention forever (the steady-state 0.15 matches what item pages already use). Item pages and shelf cards stay at one constant peak (`0.15` for items, `0.10` for shelves) — they don't need the "open this!" emphasis.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_tease_peel.dart` (the `firstCyclePeakRealFlipProgress` param + the Timer-driven `_firstCycleDone` swap; the `underlay` Widget param + `_PeelClipTriangleClipper` that shares geometry with the painter via `_PeelGeometry`); `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_cover_page.dart` (passes `firstCyclePeakRealFlipProgress: 0.25` + `teaseUnderlay`); `lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart::_buildFirstItemUnderlay` (builds the real `ListZineItemCard` for item 0).

<!-- STALE(2026-06-05): Superseded by D202 (PROD-2415 / PROD-2429). `_listFlipPage` no longer applies a TurnPageAnimation on Android/Web — it uses `CupertinoPage` on all platforms. The flip felt heavy on non-iOS surfaces. -->
### D198: Tapping a shelf card opens the zine with a `TurnPageAnimation` flip on web + Android; iOS keeps `CupertinoPage`
- **Choice:** The `/lists/:listId` GoRoute uses a `_listFlipPage` helper that branches on `defaultTargetPlatform`. On iOS it returns `CupertinoPage<T>` (system slide + interactive swipe-back gesture preserved); on Android and web it returns `CustomTransitionPage<T>` with `transitionsBuilder` driving a `TurnPageAnimation` (right-to-left, 420 ms forward / 320 ms reverse, overleaf `sokoShade5` + `sokoInk` border). All entries into the list detail — shelf taps, search results, deep links, breadcrumb back — share this transition.
- **Why:** the page-turn echoes the zine's own in-flip animation, so the route reads as "you flipped open the cover" — visual continuity between shelf card and zine page 1. Doing it everywhere (instead of per-card hero) keeps the transition consistent regardless of entry point. iOS branches off because `CustomTransitionPage` doesn't carry the interactive `_CupertinoBackGestureDetector` — replacing `CupertinoPage` there would silently kill swipe-back, which is a non-negotiable iOS affordance. See [`learnings/customtransitionpage-loses-ios-swipe-back.md`](../learnings/customtransitionpage-loses-ios-swipe-back.md) and [`learnings/turn-page-transition-gotchas.md § 12`](../learnings/turn-page-transition-gotchas.md#12-turnpageanimation-as-a-gorouter-route-transition-page-flip-on-navigation).
- **Where it lives:** `lib/core/router/app_router.dart::_listFlipPage` (the helper). The `/lists/:listId` route's `pageBuilder` calls it; everything else is unchanged.

### D199: User-profiling vibe questions are single-select with 250 ms auto-advance; hobbies (interests) stays multi-select with manual Continue
- **Choice:** Steps 1-4 of the profiling flow (the four vibe pages: morning / activity / food / night) switch from "pick 1-2 of 3" multi-select to single-select. Tapping a `VibeCard` calls `selectVibeOption(stepId, optionId)` (the new provider method that replaces, not toggles, the selection), then schedules `nextStep()` after a 250 ms delay so the selected state has time to register visually before the page turns. The step-index-at-tap is captured so a Back navigation during the 250 ms window cancels the deferred advance. The `_Footer`'s `_canContinue` gate is unchanged — vibe steps `step.min` is 1, and single-select always satisfies it. Step 5 (interests / hobbies) keeps multi-select (`InterestChipGrid` + `toggleInterest`) + manual Continue tap — that step is genuinely "pick 1-4 of 12."
- **Why:** the vibe questions read as binary preference picks ("morning person or night owl?"); forcing a manual Continue after a single tap was friction. Auto-advance with a brief snap delay produces a confident, brisk flow ("tap → see → next") without feeling like the page jumped before you knew you'd tapped. Interests / hobbies stays multi-select because the prompt is genuinely additive (multiple hobbies coexist) and the count affects downstream smart-list assignment.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/user_profiling/providers/user_profiling_provider.dart::selectVibeOption` (new method; the old `toggleVibeOption` is retained but no longer used by vibes); `lib/features/user_profiling/screens/user_profiling_flow_screen.dart::_CatalogStepBody._onVibeTapped` (the 250 ms `Future.delayed` advance with the step-index-at-tap guard). Updated upstream contract in [`features/onboarding-vibe-flow.md`](../features/onboarding-vibe-flow.md) (step descriptions).

### D200: Venue/event card image fallback — type-aware Soko brand-colour floor + deterministic texture (`SokoCardImage`) (PROD-2352)
- **Choice**: All venue/event card images render through `SokoCardImage` (`lib/shared/widgets/soko_card_image.dart`): a type-aware Soko brand-colour "floor" (place/venue → `sokoVenue` blue, event → `sokoEvent` green, unknown → `sokoShade5` neutral) overlaid with a deterministic paper texture (the zine `cover-texture-NN.webp` catalog, seeded by the entity id via `sokoTextureForSeed`), with the network photo on top. When `image_url` is null or the GET fails (404 / network / timeout), the floor + texture stays — never a broken-image icon, neutral chip, or empty box. A loaded opaque `BoxFit.cover` photo fully occludes the floor, so the texture is only ever visible on the no-image fallback.
- **Reveal timing**: photos render natively via `CachedImage` (cached → snaps instantly; uncached → fades in). The floor is a backdrop: on a seed's **first** appearance this session it fades in (url-cards hold ~0.75 s then fade so a fast photo never flashes colour first; no-image cards fade ~0.35 s with no hold); on **repeat** appearances (scrolled away and back) it snaps in with no animation, tracked via a session seen-seed set so behaviour is deterministic under carousel widget recycling. Honours the OS reduce-motion setting.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`**: the coloured + textured image area on an imageless venue/event card is **intentional**, not a rendering bug.
- **Supersedes**: the per-surface fallbacks in D42/D43 (`_ArtworkTile` now delegates to `SokoCardImage`; its grunge-overlay-placeholder TODO is retired), D66 (venue hero now uses `SokoCardImage`), and the "neutral Soko/Shade5 chip" fallback from the PROD-1521 History-grid decision. `SokoCardImage` is now the **canonical** venue/event card-image primitive — new card surfaces use it, not raw `Image.network` / bare `CachedImage`.
- **Migrated surfaces (~18)**: chat cards (`venue_card`, `place_card_with_letter`, `generic_card`), list cards/rows (`list_item_card`, `list_item_row`, `list_view_mode/list_view_item_row`, `suggestion_item_row`, `search_result_card`, `add_to_list_sheet`), discovery shelves (`_artwork_tile` → `near_you_card` + `canonical_shelf_card`; `spaces_shelf`/`happening_shelf`/`near_you_places_shelf` pass kind+seed), typeahead (`typeahead_result_row`), venue/event detail hero (`venue_hero_photo`), discovery `history_grid`. List/zine **cover** art keeps its own `ZineCoverRecipe` (not migrated).
- **Where it lives**: `lib/shared/widgets/soko_card_image.dart` (+ `SokoEntityKind`); texture catalog + `stableHash` in `lib/core/utils/soko_texture.dart` (re-exported from `zine_cover_recipe.dart`).
- **Don't gate the photo's opacity on a flag**: an earlier cut hid the photo behind an `_imageReady` flag set from a post-frame callback; under carousel recycling that left loaded photos invisible (only the floor showed). The photo must render natively via `CachedImage`. See [`learnings/soko-card-image-photo-renders-natively.md`](../learnings/soko-card-image-photo-renders-natively.md).
- **Backend dependency**: discovery *feed* endpoints often return `image_url: null` for venues whose image isn't warmed yet, so carousels show the floor on first view until the feed resolves images. Tracked as PROD-2370 (extend the `/places/search` opaque `?place_id=` proxy to the feed resolver). See [`learnings/discovery-image-cache-propagation.md`](../learnings/discovery-image-cache-propagation.md).

### D201: Product tour visual + flow refresh (PROD-2422)

- **Choice:** Batch of follow-up improvements to the onboarding product tour, layered on top of the PROD-2353 / PROD-2354 scrim/cutout fixes:
  - **Bigger cards.** Headline 34 → 40 px (Season Mix Light — closer to the 42 px Figma frame 9378 always specified in the code comment), body 12 → 14 px, step counter 12 → 14 px, pill labels 12 → 15 px, leading icon 12 → 15. Pill height 32 → 42, padding 11 → 14, corner radius 6 → 8, spacing 5 → 6. Card body padding 11 → 14, row gap 14 → 18. Outer Soko-Blue solid border stroke 6 → 8 px (`Positioned -8` insets, centre-line radius 10).
  - **Seguinte / Concluído pill flipped from Soko-Pink to Soko-Blue.** Diverges from Figma frame 9378's Soko-Pink Next pill so the primary CTA matches the surrounding Soko-Blue rectangle around the active tour target and the card's outer frame — single-colour "active" channel across the overlay, rather than three colours competing for attention.
  - **Animated `tour_hand.png` cursor on the Next pill.** New `_NextPillWithHand` widget wraps the pill in a `Stack(clipBehavior: Clip.none)` and overlays a 40×46 hand asset that rises in from below the pill (easeOut, 600 ms) after an 800 ms delay, then loops a gentle 3 px upward pulse (`sin(value*pi)`, 1400 ms / cycle). `IgnorePointer` so taps pass through to the pill underneath. Reuses `assets/images/tour_hand.png` — the same Soko hand `_CursorPointer` already uses for inter-step transitions (no new asset).
  - **Dashed picotado moved BEHIND the card content.** `_DashedBorderPainter` gained an optional `fillColor` parameter; the painter now draws the Soko-Paper fill + the dashed perimeter together, and `CustomPaint.foregroundPainter:` was switched to `painter:`. The Container dropped its `color: bg` / `borderRadius` decoration since the painter owns both. Fixes the bottom-edge dash drawing OVER the upper portion of the animated hand image — the children of the `CustomPaint` (including the hand) now render strictly on top of the painter's strokes.
  - **Welcome card (`TourStep.welcome`).** New first step rendered by `_TourWelcomeTooltip` — centred in the viewport with no anchor (no `Showcase.withWidget`), same dashed-ink + Soko-Blue outer border as every other step, no `N/total` step counter, `"Vamos" / "Vem comigo" / "Let's go"` CTA label (instead of `"Seguinte"`). `TourTooltip` gained `showCounter: bool = true` and `nextLabel: String?` props to support this. Controller's `start()` opens at `welcome` (was `searchBar`); `next()` advances `welcome → searchBar` with no cursor animation so the existing chat-bar → connector → card choreography plays cleanly afterwards. ARB additions: `productTourWelcomeHeadline / productTourWelcomeBody / productTourWelcomeCta` in EN / PT-PT / PT-BR (`"Come with me. / Two minutes — I'll show you how I work. / Let's go"` and equivalents).
  - **Tour-local scrim opacity 30 % → 50 %.** `_TourScrim._scrimColor` switches from `AppColors.sokoInkSecondary` (`0x4D44131D`, 30 %) to a hardcoded `Color(0x8044131D)` (50 %). Other app scrims (modal sheets via the shared colour) unchanged.
  - **Step copy refresh + compound step-4 headline.** All four numbered step bodies were rewritten across EN / PT-PT / PT-BR to match a more conversational first-person Soko voice; step 4 headline becomes `Cria/Guarda` (`Create/Save` / `Crie/Salve`) — intentionally breaks the one-word imperative pattern used by steps 1–3 + 5, to surface BOTH verbs the `/yours` page supports.

- **Amendment 2026-09-07 (PROD-4167) — step 4 lands on `/library`, and its secondary highlight is gone.** The bottom-nav Biblioteca button `go`s `AppRoutes.library`, but the tour's simulated tap of that same button still went to `AppRoutes.lists` (`/yours`) — the cursor clicked one screen and the app opened another. `RealTourSideEffects.navigateToYours` is now `navigateToLibrary` → `/library`. Three things that only made sense on the lists hub went with it: the Soko-blue frame on the "Criar nova zine" pill (`CityActionBar` isn't mounted on `/library`, so its `createZineButton` key had no reader left), the 1300 ms `_yoursIntroDelay` whose sole job was letting that frame draw before the card, and `resetListsModeToYours`, which wrote hub-only `yoursFollowingModeProvider`. **Step 4 therefore has no secondary highlight at all** and its card appears immediately, like every other step — the "rectangle first, then card" rhythm above no longer applies to step 4. The `Cria/Guarda` headline is kept as-is: both verbs are still true of `/library`, and the body copy ("O que guardas e crias vai para aqui.") already described the destination generically. `TourStep.yoursNav` keeps its name so `analyticsName` stays `'yours'` and the analytics contract doesn't drift.

- **Amendment 2026-09-07 (PROD-4167) — the final card no longer promises the profiling flow.** Step 5's body read *"That's it! Take 2 min to share your vibe, or skip and start exploring."* — written when completing the tour handed off to the profiling flow. It doesn't, so the card promised a form that never appears. English now reads **"That's it — you're set. Go explore. I'll learn your vibe as we go."**: it keeps "your vibe" so it reads as the successor to the old line, and inverts the promise — the user tells Soko nothing, Soko infers from use. ⚠️ **EN deliberately diverges from pt / pt-BR / es right now.** `scripts/check-l10n-integrity.py --check values` blocks edits to existing translated values on a feature branch, so the other three still carry the 2-minute line until `/translate-update` runs. Do not "fix" the inconsistency by hand-editing the ARBs — `intl_es.arb` in particular is generated from the production localization portal (CLAUDE.md § Localization). The wording is an engineer's stopgap, not a copy decision; [PROD-4242](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-4242) owns settling it (body + the `Make it yours` headline, which was written for a card that sent you to a form) and shipping all four locales together.

- **Amendment 2026-09-14 (PROD-4242) — final card copy settled, all four locales together.** The stopgap above is superseded. Headline `productTourStep5ProfileHeadline`: **You're set** / *Tudo a postos* / *Tudo pronto* / *Todo listo* — replaces "Make it yours / Personaliza", which was an instruction to go do something (true when a profile form followed, false now). Body `productTourStep5Profile`: **"That's it. Go explore. I'll learn what you like as we go."** / *"É isto. Vai explorar. Eu vou aprendendo o que gostas."* / *"É isso. Vá explorar. Eu vou aprendendo o que você gosta."* / *"Eso es todo. Ve a explorar. Iré aprendiendo lo que te gusta."* "Vibe" is dropped: it only existed to echo the old form's question. Like step 4, the last card breaks the one-word-imperative headline pattern on purpose — it is a landing, not another task. Shipped from a `translations/` branch so the value-protection check allows the pt / pt-BR / es edits; es is hand-written (EN *changed*, so per CLAUDE.md § Localization the Spanish is ours, and must be re-applied after the next portal export cut — PROD-4102).

  | Step | Headline (PT-PT) | Body (PT-PT) |
  |---|---|---|
  | 1 Procura | Procurar | Pergunta-me. Eventos, sítios, planos — eu respondo. |
  | 2 Descobre | Descobre | Zines, eventos, sítios — descobre tudo aqui. |
  | 3 Adiciona | Adiciona | Viste algo? Cola um link, larga uma imagem, cria uma Zine ou uma memória — eu adiciono. |
  | 4 Cria/Guarda | **Cria/Guarda** | O que guardas e crias vai para aqui. |

  Step 5 copy: see the 2026-09-14 amendment (PROD-4242) above.

- **Why:**
  - The previous card sizing (32 px pills, 12 px labels, 34 px headline) read as cramped on a backdrop now at 50 % opacity. The Figma-spec 42 px headline was always the intended size; this batch closes the gap to 40 px and bumps the surrounding chrome (pills / icon / counter) to match.
  - Soko-Pink Next pill against a Soko-Blue surrounding rectangle made the CTA look like a separate UI surface (foreign chrome). Flipping to Soko-Blue plus the rising hand cursor turns "where do I tap" into the single most visually obvious thing on the overlay.
  - Without the welcome card, the tour started with the chat-bar rectangle drawing in around a user who hadn't even been told what they were looking at. The welcome card establishes Soko as the narrator ("Anda comigo / Vem comigo / Come with me"), continuing the first-person voice from the Siga screen ("Olá! Eu sou a Soko / Vou ajudar-te").
  - 30 % scrim wasn't pulling enough focus once the cards got bigger — the under-page UI was still pulling the eye. 50 % is firmly "focus mode" without becoming a modal.
  - The previous step copy described each surface from the outside ("Os teus saves estão aqui"). The new copy frames each step as something Soko DOES with the user ("eu respondo", "eu adiciono", "o que guardas e crias vai para aqui") — matches the conversational discovery model Soko is positioned around.

- **Where it lives:**
  - `lib/features/product_tour/models/tour_step.dart` — `welcome` enum value (stepNumber 0, analyticsName `welcome`).
  - `lib/features/product_tour/providers/product_tour_controller.dart` — `start()` opens at `welcome`; `next()` switch + `back()` switch updated for the new step.
  - `lib/features/product_tour/widgets/product_tour_host.dart` — `_TourWelcomeTooltip`, `_scrimColor` constant, `welcome` cases in `_showcaseFor` / `_cardVisibleForStep` / `_cutoutSpecsForStep`, host Stack child list.
  - `lib/features/product_tour/widgets/tour_tooltip.dart` — typography + sizing, `_NextPillWithHand`, dashed border moved to `painter:` slot with `fillColor`, `showCounter` + `nextLabel` props on `TourTooltip`.
  - `lib/l10n/intl_en.arb`, `intl_pt.arb`, `intl_pt_BR.arb` — welcome strings + rewritten step 1/2/3 bodies + step 4 headline rename + step 4 body rewrite.

- **Considered + parked:** replacing the welcome card with a Siga-page hint ("A seguir, mostro-te a app em dois minutos.") so the tour opens straight on the chat-bar step. Discussed mid-session but kept the welcome card for now — the Siga hint is a possible follow-up if telemetry shows users dismissing the welcome too quickly. A persistent "maozinha" cursor that never leaves the screen (transitioning between demo targets and the active card's Next pill) was prototyped + reverted in the same session; tabled for a dedicated ticket if revisited.

- **Supersedes / amends:** the implicit Soko-Pink CTA + 30 % scrim choices baked into the original tour rollout. Earlier tour entries (PROD-2353 / PROD-2354 scrim/cutout fixes) are unaffected — this batch is additive on top of them.

<!-- STALE(2026-09-15): the `_listFlipPage` helper this entry names no longer exists. D353 deleted it — once the Library/feed thumbnail → zine-cover Hero was reverted it was byte-identical to `_detailPage` — and `/lists/:listId` now uses the shared `_slidePage`. The *decision* survives unchanged: iOS keeps `CupertinoPage` (slide + swipe-back), and Android/web get a light slide rather than the page-turn flip. Only the helper name is wrong. -->
### D202: List-detail route uses `CupertinoPage` on all platforms (drops the Android/Web page-turn flip) (PROD-2415 / PROD-2429)
- **Choice:** `_listFlipPage` in `lib/core/router/app_router.dart` now returns `CupertinoPage<T>` unconditionally. The previous platform branch (D198 — `TurnPageAnimation` peel on Android + Web with a 420 ms enter / 320 ms exit; `CupertinoPage` on iOS) is gone, and the `turn_page_transition` import was removed from `app_router.dart`. The function name is kept for callsite stability — the `/lists/:listId` GoRoute still calls it.
- **Why:** the page-turn flip read as heavy and distracting on Android and in desktop / mobile browsers; PROD-2415 explicitly called it out as "this animation shouldn't exist outside the zine itself." Cupertino's slide is the lightest non-empty transition and matches the rest of the app's detail-route entries; iOS additionally keeps the system swipe-back gesture (`_CupertinoBackGestureDetector`). The page-turn echo on shelf → zine entry was nice-to-have but not load-bearing for comprehension — the zine's own in-flip animation already establishes the metaphor once the user is inside.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/core/router/app_router.dart::_listFlipPage`.
- **Supersedes:** [D198](#d198-tapping-a-shelf-card-opens-the-zine-with-a-turnpageanimation-flip-on-web--android-ios-keeps-cupertinopage) (flagged STALE). The `TurnPageAnimation` route-transition technique is still documented in [`learnings/turn-page-transition-gotchas.md § 12`](../learnings/turn-page-transition-gotchas.md#12-turnpageanimation-as-a-gorouter-route-transition-page-flip-on-navigation) as reference for any future custom-transition use; the in-zine `TurnPageView` is unaffected.

### D203: Explicit `cover_color` suppresses the PROD-2040 preview-photo fallback (PROD-2425)
- **Choice:** `ZineCoverRecipe.fromFields` now applies the PROD-2040 most-recent-item-photo fallback only when `cover_color` is `null` (= no recipe ever set). Any explicit colour value — written via a swatch tap OR by the "Sem imagem" callback's new sentinel write — suppresses the fallback and renders the solid colour cover. To make "Sem imagem" actually feel like "no image", `onClearItemImage` in `change_cover_sheet.dart` now resolves the current effective colour (`list.coverColor ?? kZineColors[stableHash(list.id) % kZineColors.length].wire`) and writes it explicitly when clearing the item ref.
- **Why:** before the gate, tapping "Sem imagem" was a visual no-op. The state transitioned correctly (`cover_type=background_color`, `cover_item_id=null`, `cover_item_image_url=null`), but the resolver's PROD-2040 fallback re-rendered `previewImages[0]` as the cover, and the `_NoImageTile`'s `noImageSelected = !previewRecipe.hasPhoto` stayed false so the tile never flipped to its selected state. The cleanest persistent signal already on the wire is `cover_color`. Picking ANY swatch is also "an explicit recipe", so this incidentally changes the colour-pick flow too: a list with items where the user picks yellow now shows solid yellow, not the preview photo with yellow underneath. The previous behaviour (always prefer "show content" via the preview photo even with an explicit colour) was the intent of PROD-2040 — but it conflicted with the user's direct picker action and produced surprise. The new gate keeps PROD-2040 for the *truly* unconfigured case (new zine, first render) while letting any explicit pick win.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** if a list with items shows a solid-colour cover instead of a preview-item photo, that's **intentional** — the user (or "Sem imagem") wrote an explicit `cover_color`. Don't flag.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/lists/utils/zine_cover_recipe.dart::fromFields` (background_color branch — the `coverColor == null &&` clause), `lib/features/lists/widgets/change_cover_sheet.dart::onClearItemImage` (the effective-colour resolution + explicit write), `test/features/lists/utils/zine_cover_recipe_test.dart` (the PROD-2425 regression test). The companion change that fixed item-image covers propagating to `/yours` lives in [`features/PROD-1908-zine-cover-renderer.md` § Changelog 2026-06-08](../features/PROD-1908-zine-cover-renderer.md#9-changelog) and in [`learnings/optimistic-clear-strands-listening-shelves.md`](../learnings/optimistic-clear-strands-listening-shelves.md).

### D204: Left-edge swipe opens chat history on the chat surface and pops home everywhere else (PROD-2531)
- **Choice:** `_onLeftEdgeSwipe` in `discovery_shell.dart` now branches the other way from PROD-1804. A left-edge swipe (finger starts in the 40 px left-edge zone, drags right — the iOS-style "back" gesture) resolves to: **on `/chat` or `/chat/<id>`** → open the chat-history sidebar (`sidebarOpenProvider.state = true`); **on every other DiscoveryShell route** (Discovery, `/yours`, lists hub, memories) → back-pop toward home via `popOrFallback` (falls back to `/` on cold-load). Detail routes still short-circuit earlier (Cupertino's own back gesture wins the arena there) and are unaffected.
- **Why:** PROD-1804 had made the chat surface read as a sub-page of Discovery, so its left-swipe retreated to Discovery while every other surface opened the chat-history sidebar. In practice that felt inverted: the chat screen is where reaching for past conversations is natural (so the swipe should surface history there), and on the non-chat surfaces the swipe reads as a plain "back" toward home. PROD-2531 swaps the two actions so the gesture matches that expectation on each surface.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a left-edge swipe on the chat screen opening the history sidebar (rather than navigating home) is **intentional**, and the same swipe on Discovery/lists/memories going home (rather than opening the sidebar) is **intentional**. Don't flag either as a regression. History remains reachable off-chat via the chat-history button in the message-input bottom row.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_shell.dart::_onLeftEdgeSwipe` (the `isChatRoute` branch + fall-through) and the gesture-handler comment block above it.
- **Supersedes:** the PROD-1804 chat-as-Discovery-subpage swipe mapping (which lived only in code comments, not a prior D-entry). The PROD-1804 tap-to-unfocus learning ([`learnings/duplicate-tap-to-unfocus-races-textfield-focus.md`](../learnings/duplicate-tap-to-unfocus-races-textfield-focus.md)) is a separate concern and is unchanged.

### D205: Discovery search filter chips — pink chrome, border-only selection, per-facet Lucide icons (no ticket, in-session)
- **Choice:** Both Discovery search filter strips (`event_filters_bar.dart`, `place_filters_bar.dart`) share one chip chrome: a light-pink `sokoLight3` fill with a `sokoPink` hairline border at rest. Selection no longer fills the chip with the accent colour — it recolours the **border only**, and the chip keeps its pink fill: Soko/Green for event categories, Soko/Blue for place facets, Soko/Lilac for the active time-range trigger. The ✕ clear chip matches the idle chrome. Each category/place facet chip also carries a leading Lucide glyph, mapped from the stable backend `label_key` via a new `_facetIcon(facet)` switch (e.g. `facet.music` → `LucideIcons.music`, `facet.eat_drink` → `utensils`, `facet.bars_nightlife` → `martini`), with a `null` fallback so an unmapped facet renders icon-less rather than guessing.
- **Why:** Product-owner-directed restyle in this session. The prior treatment filled the whole chip with the accent colour on a white idle chip; the pink + border-only direction keeps the strip calmer and reading as secondary chrome below the category selector, and the per-facet icons give a faster scan target. A fully-brown monochrome variant was tried and **rejected** (`sokoShade5` fill + `sokoInk` selected border): `sokoInk` is effectively black for a 1px border, and `sokoShade5` barely separated from the `sokoPaper` page so the chips lost affordance.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** pink filter chips whose **border** (not fill) carries the accent colour when selected are **intentional**, as are the per-facet leading icons. Do not flag the absence of a solid accent fill, the pink resting fill, or the icons as regressions. (Lucide is the canonical icon set per `docs/ui/design-system-rules.md`.)
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/discovery/widgets/event_filters_bar.dart` (`_FilterPill`, `_CategoryChip`, `_facetIcon`) and `lib/features/discovery/widgets/place_filters_bar.dart` (`_FacetChip`, `_facetIcon`).
- **Supersedes:** the filled-chip palette previously described in [`features/discovery.md` § Search filters](../features/discovery.md#search-filters) (now updated to match).

### D206: Add-to-zine sheet — destructive "Remover de todas" link (trashcan) + neutral "Confirmar" commit button (PROD-2138)
- **Choice:** Two copy/icon changes to `AddToListSheet`, from Diana's review of the PROD-2138 fix. **(1)** The "Already saved in" section's clear link (`addToListClearAll`) reads **"Remove all"** / **"Remover de todas"** with a leading `Icons.delete_outline_rounded` trashcan, replacing the neutral "Clear all" / "Limpar tudo". **(2)** The sticky-footer commit button (`addToListSaveAction`) reads **"Confirm"** / **"Confirmar"** with `Icons.check_rounded`, replacing "Save" / "Guardar" / "Salvar" + `Icons.bookmark`.
- **Why:** The button commits *staged changes* — which may be pure removals, or just a tip edit — not necessarily a save-to-zine, so a bookmark glyph + "save the item" wording misled (Diana: "the user is just deleting items"). "Confirm" is action-neutral across add / remove / edit-tip. Symmetrically, the clear link unticks every existing membership and removes the item from all those zines on Confirm — a destructive action that now reads as one (trashcan + "remove" verb) rather than a neutral "clear selection".
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the trashcan before "Remover de todas" and the checkmark (not bookmark) on the pink "Confirmar" button are **intentional**. Do not flag the absence of a bookmark glyph on the commit button as a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/lists/widgets/add_to_list_sheet.dart` — `_ClearAllLink` (trashcan + label in a `Row`), `_StickyFooter` (`icon: Icons.check_rounded`). ARB keys `addToListClearAll` + `addToListSaveAction`; EN values + `@`descriptions on the feature branch, PT/PT-BR values via a `translations/` branch (CI value-protection — see [`infrastructure/translation-guide.md`](../infrastructure/translation-guide.md)).
- **Refines:** [D144](#d144-add-to-list-sheet-batches-chip-toggles-into-a-single-save-time-commit) — the batched-commit mechanism is unchanged; only the button/link copy + glyphs changed.

<!-- STALE(2026-06-18): superseded by D208. The Discovery-header cluster (bell + avatar) was removed in PROD-2511 follow-up — inbox now lives on the chat bar, Menu header, and Discovery end-of-feed CTAs as a 36×36 inbox-glyph circle. -->
### D207: Notifications inbox entry — top-right cluster on `DiscoveryHeader` (bell + user-avatar) instead of a bottom-nav badge (PROD-2524)
- **Choice:** The notifications inbox entry point is a 40×40 cluster mounted top-right of `DiscoveryHeader` (`NotificationsTopButton` bell + unread badge, `MenuUserButton` small initial-tile avatar). The bell pushes `/menu/notifications`; the avatar goes to `/menu`. Mirrored as a secondary entry in the Menu page (the `LucideIcons.inbox` "Notifications" row between Account and Business Connections).
- **PROD-2524 ticket spec:** *"unread badge on bottom nav"*. The Discovery bottom nav (`DiscoveryBottomNav`) already holds 5 items (Chat · Zines · Home · Criar · Menu) and the Menu icon is the only conceptual home for an account-side action like the inbox. A 6th tab would crowd the bar; a badge on Menu would conflate "you have a new notification" with "you have something to do in settings".
- **Why:** The top-right header slot was empty before this work (just the centered Soko wordmark via a Stack overlay). Putting both icons there gives notifications and account access their own permanent, scannable affordance without disturbing nav semantics. The avatar surfaces the user's identity (initial-tile) without paying for the bigger 108-px `_AvatarTile` mounted inside the Menu screen itself.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the top-right bell + avatar pair on the Discovery header is **intentional**. The Discovery bottom nav stays at 5 items; do not flag the absence of a 6th Notifications tab or of a badge on the Menu icon as a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_header.dart` (Stack with `Center(SokoWordmark)` + `Positioned(right: 8, Row[NotificationsTopButton, SizedBox(4), MenuUserButton])`). The icon widgets are in `lib/features/notifications/widgets/notifications_top_button.dart`. Secondary entry: `lib/features/profile/screens/menu_screen.dart` `_AuthedMenuBody` row block.

### D208: Notifications inbox entry — three placements (chat bar · Menu header · Discovery end-of-feed), 36×36 inbox-glyph circle (PROD-2511 follow-up)
- **Choice:** Supersedes [D207](#d207-notifications-inbox-entry--top-right-cluster-on-discoveryheader-bell--user-avatar-instead-of-a-bottom-nav-badge-prod-2524). The Discovery header is back to a bare centered Soko wordmark — no top-right cluster. `NotificationsTopButton` is now a 36×36 `sokoInk8` circle with a 16 px `LucideIcons.inbox` glyph (was 40×40 transparent with a 22 px `LucideIcons.bell` glyph), mounted in three places:
  - **Chat bar** (`ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow`, right-side cluster next to the WhatsApp brand glyph) — primary entry across the Discovery surface.
  - **Menu header** (`_MenuHeader`, top-right slot opposite the back arrow) — only for authenticated users; replaces the static 48 px spacer. The in-list `_SokoMenuRow(icon: LucideIcons.inbox)` row between Account and Business Connections was removed (no longer a duplicate entry).
  - **Discovery end-of-feed CTAs** (`DiscoveryEndActions`) — bottom-of-list affordance after the user scrolls through everything.
  `MenuUserButton` was retired entirely; the avatar is no longer surfaced in the Discovery header.
- **Why:** The header-cluster placement was hard to discover (top-right of a wordmark a user already taps for "go home") and visually competed with the wordmark every time the Stack overlay rendered. The chat bar is where the user's hand is when reading Discovery, and the Menu header is a natural top-right slot users already scan for header actions. Moving to a chat-bar peer (same 36×36 brand-circle treatment as the mic, send, and history buttons) also fixed the size outlier — the bell was 40×40 + 22 px glyph, every other chat-bar button is 36×36 + 16 px glyph.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the Discovery header has **no** top-right cluster anymore — it's just a centered wordmark. The inbox-glyph circle on the chat bar (right of WhatsApp), in the Menu header, and at the end of the Discovery feed is **intentional**. Do not flag the absence of an inbox icon on the Discovery header (or the absence of a bell glyph) as a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_header.dart` (now just a centered wordmark — no Stack overlay), `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart` (`ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow`), `lib/features/profile/screens/menu_screen.dart` (`_MenuHeader`), `lib/features/discovery/widgets/discovery_end_actions.dart`. Glyph + sizing in `lib/features/notifications/widgets/notifications_top_button.dart`.
- **Supersedes:** [D207](#d207-notifications-inbox-entry--top-right-cluster-on-discoveryheader-bell--user-avatar-instead-of-a-bottom-nav-badge-prod-2524).

### D209: Menu screen sized to fit iPhone without scroll (PROD-2511 follow-up)

- **Choice:** The authed `/menu` body is sized so the six menu rows
  (Conta · Rede de Parceiros · Definições · Suporte · IA e dados ·
  Sobre) + the "Terminar sessão" CTA fit a standard iPhone viewport
  without engaging the body's `SingleChildScrollView`. Specific tweaks
  applied to `lib/features/profile/screens/menu_screen.dart`:
  - **`_AvatarTile`** mobile size 108 → **88**; initial-letter font
    44 → 36. Desktop tile stays 160.
  - **Avatar → first menu row** `SizedBox(height: 40)` → **16**.
  - **`_SokoMenuRow`** inner vertical padding 6 → **4**; icon tile
    56 → **48**; icon glyph 22 → **20**. Title/subtitle font sizes
    (17 / 13) unchanged — legibility is what makes the row read.
  - **Sign-out CTA** top padding 8 → 4 (bottom 24 unchanged).
- **Why:** The previous sizing overflowed every shipping iPhone (the
  "Sobre" row clipped behind the sticky sign-out button) and the
  body's scroll view engaged on a screen that has no business
  scrolling — six immovable rows, an avatar, and a CTA. The total
  reclaimed ~120 px is comfortably more than the worst-case overflow
  with the new chat-bar / Menu-header inbox button (D208) accounting
  for the inbox-badge surface area. The `SingleChildScrollView`
  wrapper stays as a safety net for iPhone SE 1st-gen and very-large
  accessibility text — it just doesn't engage at default sizes.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** The menu screen's tighter
  avatar (88 px, not 108 px in any Figma frame still showing 108),
  reduced avatar-to-menu gap, and smaller 48 px row icons are
  **intentional**. Do not flag against any Figma menu mockup that
  carries the older 108 / 40 / 56 sizing.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/menu_screen.dart`
  — `_AuthedMenuBody` (avatar → row gap), `_AvatarTile` (mobile
  sizing), `_SokoMenuRow.build` (icon tile + padding), `_MenuScreen`
  build (sign-out CTA padding).

### D210: Reminder bell in event-detail body inline-action cluster, next to Save + Report (PROD-2511 follow-up)

- **Amendment 2026-06-29 — cluster moved out of the top row of `_NameAndTagBlock` (D217):** the Choice below says the bell lives "in `_NameAndTagBlock`'s top row alongside `EventSaveButton`". After D217, the icon cluster (Save + Share + Bell) is no longer in the top row next to the title — it now sits in its **own Row directly beneath `EventTagRow`**, with a 30 px gap on both sides. Composition stays `[Save] [Share] [Bell]`, the body-mounted structural rationale (one mount covers both `/events/:id` and `/lists/:id/events/:id`) is unchanged. See D217 for full reasoning.
- **Amendment 2026-06-24 — cluster composition is now [Save] [Share] [Bell] (D214):** the original Choice below describes the trailing icon cluster as `[Save] [Report-flag] [Bell]`. After D214 (Report ↔ Share swap), the Report-flag chip has been retired and a Share icon now occupies the middle slot: `[Save] [Share] [Bell]`. The Bell's position, size, soko-dark fill behavior, and structural rationale (body-mounted so both `/events/:id` and `/lists/:id/events/:id` get it from one mount) are all unchanged. Reportar moved into the action grid Row 1 as a labeled pill — see D214. Where it lives: `_ShareIconButton` (next to `_ReminderBellButton`) in `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_detail_body.dart`.
- **Choice:** The event-reminder bell-ring affordance lives **inside the event-detail body**, <!-- STALE(2026-06-29): no longer "in the top row" — the cluster moved to a new Row beneath `EventTagRow`. See the Amendment 2026-06-29 above (D217). --> in `_NameAndTagBlock`'s top row alongside `EventSaveButton` (bookmark, 40 px) and <!-- STALE(2026-06-24): the Report-flag chip was replaced by `_ShareIconButton` in D214; Reportar moved to the action grid Row 1. See the Amendment 2026-06-24 above. --> `_ReportFlagButton` (flag, 40 px). 40 px circle, 14 px `LucideIcons.bell_ring` glyph. Filled state (`eventReminderStatusProvider(event.id)` is true) uses **soko-dark** (`sokoInk` bg + `sokoPaper` icon, matches `SokoCtaButton.ink`); outline state uses `sokoInk @ 6 %` + `sokoInk` glyph (same as the Save / Report peers). Implementation: `_ReminderBellButton` in `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_detail_body.dart`.
- **Why:** Two earlier placements failed visually. First attempt mounted the bell in `PinnedPageChrome`'s trailing slot (`_EventReminderTrailing`) and read `eventId` from `GoRouterState.of(context).pathParameters`. That worked on the standalone `/events/:eventId` route by coincidence but never on the in-list `/lists/:listId/events/:eventId` route — the chrome sits at the shell level, so `GoRouterState.of(context)` there returns the parent shell route's params and `eventId` is null. Three plumbing attempts (URL parse via `detailRouteFromLocation`, a `ChromeRouteArgs` record on `Page.arguments`, then matching against `discoveryNavObserver.topRouteArguments`) all failed visually for the in-list case. Body-mounting is structural: `EventDetailBody` is shared between `EventDetailScreen` and `ListInContextDetailScreen`, so the bell renders on both routes from one mount. The cluster (Save + Report + Bell) also reads as a coherent action row instead of a chrome-trailing outlier.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the bell sits **inside the event card**, right of the report-flag. The chrome's trailing slot is back to a plain `SizedBox(width: 48, height: 48)` symmetry spacer on every detail route — do not flag the absence of a bell in the header as a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_detail_body.dart` (`_NameAndTagBlock` top row + the `_ReminderBellButton` widget body). Filled-state source: `lib/features/event_detail/providers/event_reminders_provider.dart`. Past-only events' cheeky-toast guard: `_ReminderBellButton.onTap` (computes upcoming occurrences, shows `reminderPickerNoUpcomingToast` if empty instead of opening the picker).
- **Supersedes:** the prior chrome-trailing-slot placement (`_EventReminderTrailing` in `pinned_page_chrome.dart`, since deleted).

### D211: Notifications inbox hides read by default + two-pill "Por ler / Histórico" switch replaces the static title (PROD-2511 follow-up)

- **Choice:** `/menu/notifications` no longer shows already-read notifications in the default surface. The default fetch passes `include_read=false&include_dismissed=false`, the optimistic `NotificationsInboxState.visible` getter additionally filters `isUnread && !isDismissed` (so a freshly-tapped row disappears immediately instead of waiting for the next refresh), and a "Histórico" mode flips both flags to `true` and renders `items` as-is. The header drops the static `Notificações` title and mounts a **two-pill segmented switch** in its place: left pill **Por ler** (`notificationsTabUnread`), right pill **Histórico** (`notificationsTabHistory`). Selected pill takes the **soko-dark** treatment (`sokoInk` bg + `sokoPaper` text, w500 weight), unselected pill is transparent on a `sokoInk @ 6 %` rounded track (`borderRadius: 999`, 3 px inner padding). 160 ms `AnimatedContainer` swap. "Marcar todas" stays on the right and is hidden in history mode (everything there is already read). Card tap in history mode skips the `markRead` PATCH and just routes; swipe-to-dismiss still works there.
- **Why:** Mixing "unread, take action" with "already handled" in the same surface made the inbox feel cluttered after a few days of activity, and the demoted-read styling (paper bg + regular weight) wasn't strong enough to make the user feel "I'm done with these." A backend filter symmetric to the existing `include_dismissed=false` was the structurally correct fix — same `ix_notification_events_user_unread` partial index that `unread-count` and `read-all` already use, so the query stays bounded by the unread set rather than the total inbox. The Flutter-side `visible` filter shipped alongside it so the UX works immediately (the backend honoring `include_read` is a payload-size optimization, not a behavioral requirement — the client tolerates the param being ignored). Replacing the static title with the tabs reuses the slot the header already has: the tabs themselves name the section, the back arrow signals the screen, no information lost.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the inbox surface showing only unread + non-dismissed rows by default is **intentional**. The header carrying a two-pill segmented switch (`Por ler` / `Histórico`) **in place of** the static `Notificações` title is **intentional** — do not flag against any Figma frame that still shows the title.
