The "Marcar todas" text button disappearing on the history tab is **intentional**. Read cards (paper bg + regular-weight title) are only ever visible inside the history tab — flagging a Figma "read state" against the default inbox is a false positive.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/notifications/providers/notifications_provider.dart` (`NotificationsInboxMode` enum + `state.mode` + mode-aware `visible` getter + `refresh(mode:)` flag wiring), `lib/features/notifications/screens/notifications_screen.dart` (`_ModeTabs` + `_ModeTab` private widgets, header drops title and mounts `_ModeTabs` in the `Expanded` slot), `lib/data/datasources/api/notifications_api.dart` (`list(includeRead:)`), `open-api/heyl-webapp-v1.openapi.yaml` (`GET /api/v1/app/notifications` new `include_read` query param, `PATCH /notifications/{id}/dismiss` description points at the two-flag history view). ARB keys `notificationsTabUnread` + `notificationsTabHistory` + `notificationsHistoryEmptyTitle/Body`. Walkthrough: [`features/notifications-inbox.md § Inbox vs history view`](../features/notifications-inbox.md#inbox-vs-history-view).
- **Backend follow-up (out of this repo):** heyl-backend must extend the inbox query with `AND read_at IS NULL` when `include_read=false`. Until that lands the param is silently ignored server-side and the Flutter `visible` filter carries the UX on its own.

### D212: "Os teus lembretes" sheet rows as full sokoGreen event-card surfaces (PROD-2511 follow-up)

- **Choice:** `MyRemindersSheet` rows are no longer flat `InkWell` rows separated by `sokoInk8` hairlines — each row is now a self-contained rounded card with a full `AppColors.sokoGreen` (`#B0EF8B`) fill, the same surface family the canonical `EventCard` in the chat feed uses. Specifics: 6 px `BorderRadius`, **no border**, fixed 60 px height, 10 px horizontal inner padding, `Material` + `InkWell` with `Clip.antiAlias` so the splash clips to the rounded corners. **No trailing chevron** — the whole card is the tap target and the green colour signals "this is an event you can open." Dividers gone; cards stack with 10 px gaps inside an `EdgeInsets.symmetric(horizontal: 16, vertical: 12)` list padding. Title stays `sokoInk` w500/15; summary line (`"X lembretes · próximo Hoje, 18h"`) uses `AppColors.sokoInk.withValues(alpha: 0.70)` 13 (dark-ink at lower opacity preserves contrast on the saturated green where `sokoShade3`'s warm gray would muddy). Reuses `_Thumbnail` at 48×48 with its `sokoInk8` fallback well unchanged.
- **Why:** The flat-row treatment read as a generic list, not as a list of *events*. The product intent for "Os teus lembretes" is "the upcoming events you've asked Soko to nudge you about" — so the rows should look like the same green event cards the rest of the app renders for events. Borrowing the structural shape of `AddToListSheet._buildListRow` (6 px radius, 60 px height, 10 px inner padding) gives a familiar list-of-cards rhythm, and full-saturation `sokoGreen` (the existing event-surface token also used by `EventCard` and `NotificationCategory.social`) makes each row read as a mini event card without inventing a new chrome. No border — the saturated fill is its own definition and the AddToList border-on-selected pattern doesn't apply when there is no selection state. No chevron — the colour is the affordance.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** rows in `MyRemindersSheet` rendered with a full `sokoGreen` fill + 6 px radius + **no border** + **no trailing chevron** are **intentional**. Do not flag the absence of `sokoInk8` dividers, the absence of a border, the absence of a `>` chevron icon on the right edge, or the absence of a `_RowChip` (+/✓). The whole card is the tap target.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/notifications/widgets/my_reminders_sheet.dart` — outer `ListView.separated` padding + `SizedBox(height: 10)` separator + `_RemindersEventRow` widget (the `Material` + `InkWell` at the top of the build method). Structural reference: `lib/features/lists/widgets/add_to_list_sheet.dart:767–848` (`_buildListRow`). Surface colour reference: `lib/features/chat/widgets/event_card.dart` (the canonical event card). Walkthrough: [`features/notifications-inbox.md § Sheet UI — MyRemindersSheet`](../features/notifications-inbox.md#sheet-ui--myreminderssheet).

### D213: Marketing is a row inside the per-category "O que recebes de nós" list, not a master toggle inside the sub-card (PROD-2511 follow-up · PROD-2510)

- **Amendment 2026-06-22 — master toggle ON batch-enables every visible category, including `marketing.push`:** the original Choice below states the dark `_NotificationsHeaderToggle` is "OS push permission only, NOT a master enable-all of the categories below." That is no longer true on the ON-flip. After the OS push grant succeeds (`PushRegResult.registered` / `registeredTokenUnknown`), the handler now calls `_enableAllVisibleCategories`, which reuses `visibleNotificationCategories(ref.read(experimentServiceProvider))` from `notification_categories_section.dart` to build a single PATCH to `/api/v1/app/notifications/preferences` that sets every visible category to `true` — including the nested `{'marketing': {'push': true}}`. Reasoning: the user's mental model is "I turned notifications on, so notifications are on" — having marketing.push stay off because of its opt-IN default surprised users (Reminders/Discovery already default to `true` opt-OUT, so they always read as ON, but marketing.push silently stayed OFF). The legal model is preserved: the backend records a `marketing_consent_logs` row with `source='preferences'` for the marketing toggle, identical to a manual per-row tap, and `applicability` still gates web users out via the PROD-2464 silent server-side skip. The OFF-flip is unchanged — it just opens OS Settings and the lifecycle observer flips `pn_optin: false` on return; per-category toggles are NOT auto-disabled, preserving explicit opt-outs the user made later. Where it lives: `lib/features/preferences/screens/preferences_screen.dart::_NotificationsHeaderToggleState._enableAllVisibleCategories`.
- **Choice:** PROD-2510 unified the notification + marketing preferences under a single endpoint (`/api/v1/app/notifications/preferences` now returns `marketing: {email, sms, whatsapp, push}` alongside the six transactional categories). On the Flutter side, this surfaces as **one extra row** ("Marketing") inside the existing `NotificationCategoriesSection` list under the **`O que recebes de nós`** subheading, sitting next to Reminders / Descoberta / etc. The toggle controls the `marketing.push` channel specifically — promo / curated-tips push notifications. The four non-push marketing channels (email / SMS / WhatsApp) keep their existing standalone "Marketing" section with per-channel rows below; only `marketing.push` joins the per-category list. The top dark **"Notificações"** toggle (`_NotificationsHeaderToggle`) stays exactly as it was — OS push permission only, NOT a master enable-all of the categories below. <!-- STALE(2026-06-22): the dark toggle is now a master enable-all on the ON-flip. See the Amendment 2026-06-22 above. --> An earlier mid-iteration shape added a "Todas as notificações" enable-all row above `NotificationCategoriesSection`; **that row was removed** because the existing dark toggle already plays that role at the OS layer (disable it → no pushes; enable it → categories below choose which ones).
- **Why:** The user's mental model for the per-category list is "what types of push notifications you receive" — Reminders, Discovery, Marketing, etc. Adding `marketing.push` as a peer row matches that exactly. Hiding it inside a generic "all marketing" cluster (with email / SMS) would force the user to learn that the *push channel for marketing* is configured separately from the *push categories list*. Conversely, lifting it out of the categories list into a dedicated master would conflate per-category opt-in (six existing rows) with channel reachability (the OS push toggle), and there's no need for two masters when the OS toggle already gates everything below it. The simplest model: one OS master at the top, a unified categories list (now seven rows) below, separate Marketing section for non-push channels at the bottom.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the **Marketing** row inside "O que recebes de nós" is **intentional** — do not flag against any Figma frame that only shows the six original transactional categories. The Marketing section below the sub-card retains only email + SMS + WhatsApp rows (no push row there); that asymmetry between the two surfaces is **intentional**. There is **no** "Todas as notificações" / "Enable all" master row inside the sub-card; if such a row appears in a future Figma frame it is a regression of this decision, not a missing feature.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/notifications/widgets/notification_categories_section.dart` (`visibleNotificationCategories` extracted as a shared top-level helper; Marketing added as always-visible alongside Reminders + Discovery), `lib/data/models/notification_item.dart` (synthetic `NotificationCategory.marketing` enum value with no canonical `wireName` mapping — `fromWire` never returns it; analytics emits the literal `'marketing'`), `lib/data/models/notification_preferences.dart` (new `MarketingChannels` class + `marketing` field; `valueFor(NotificationCategory.marketing)` resolves to `marketing.push`), `lib/features/notifications/providers/notification_preferences_provider.dart` (`toggle` special-cases marketing to build the nested `{'marketing': {'push': value}}` request body), `lib/data/datasources/api/notifications_api.dart` (`updatePreferences` accepts `Map<String, dynamic>` so the nested marketing object can ride through), `open-api/heyl-webapp-v1.openapi.yaml` (`NotificationPreferencesOut` + `UpdateNotificationPreferencesRequest` carry the `marketing` object per PROD-2510). ARB keys `notificationCategoryMarketing` + `notificationCategoryMarketingSubtitle`. Walkthrough: [`features/notifications-inbox.md § Per-category preferences`](../features/notifications-inbox.md#per-category-preferences-prod-2526--t-l).

### D214: Report ↔ Share swap in event + venue detail, keep slot positions (no ticket, in-session)

- **Amendment 2026-06-29 — event-detail cluster moved below the tag row (D217):** the Choice below says the cluster sits "next to the title" on both event and venue detail. After D217 the **event-detail** cluster has been relocated to a new Row beneath `EventTagRow` (composition `[Save] [Share] [Bell]` unchanged). The **venue-detail** cluster is unchanged and still sits next to the title as described below. The Reportar pill in event/venue action grid Row 1 is unchanged.
- **Choice:** On event detail (`EventDetailBody`) and venue detail (`VenueDetailBody`), the trailing 40 px icon cluster <!-- STALE(2026-06-29): on event detail the cluster no longer sits next to the title — it now lives in its own Row beneath `EventTagRow`. Venue detail still matches this description. See the Amendment 2026-06-29 above (D217). --> next to the title is now `[Save] [Share] [Bell]` on event (no Bell on venue — venues aren't time-based) and `[Save] [Share]` on venue. The action grid Row 1 is now `[Guardar] [Reportar] [Ver site?]`. Share moved up from the grid to the icon cluster; Reportar moved down from the icon cluster (previously a 40 px `_ReportFlagButton` flag chip) into the grid pill slot Share used to own. Slot positions are unchanged — only which action each slot performs. Both context surfaces (standalone `/events/:id`, `/venues/:id` and in-list `/lists/:id/events/:id`, `/lists/:id/venues/:id`) covered by editing the shared body widgets. Share icon visuals: 40 px circular Soko/Ink @ 6 % surface, 14 px `LucideIcons.share_2` (Lucide is the cluster's convention — matches `_ReminderBellButton`'s `bell_ring`). Reportar pill: 14 px `LucideIcons.flag` + `l10n.moderationMenuReport` label, calls `showReportSheet(...)` directly with `ReportTargetType.event` / `ReportTargetType.place` (bypasses `ModerationMenuButton`'s `PopupMenuButton` because for events/places `authorUserId` is null → the popup was a 1-item Report-only menu; collapsed for one tap fewer). Tiny `_ActionButton` model refactor: `icon: String` (SVG path) → `iconWidget: Widget` so the pill can carry a Lucide glyph alongside the existing SVG-asset pills.
- **Why:** Share is the more discoverable, higher-frequency action of the two — promoting it to a permanent icon-cluster slot makes it reachable at the same eye-level as Save on every entry into the detail page. Reportar is a lower-frequency safety action; demoting it to a labeled grid pill is honest about its salience and unambiguous (the visible "Reportar" word removes the need for the user to recognize a flag icon). Calling `showReportSheet` directly from the pill removes a meaningless intermediate `PopupMenuButton` step that only ever showed a single "Report" item for these target types — see `moderation_menu_button.dart`'s `canBlock` gate (`authorUserId != null && isAuthenticatedProvider`).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the trailing icon cluster reading `[Save] [Share-icon] [Bell]` on event detail (and `[Save] [Share-icon]` on venue detail) is **intentional** — do not flag against any Figma frame that still shows a flag-icon in that cluster. **Per D217, on event detail the cluster now sits in its own row beneath the tag pill, not next to the title** — do not flag against Figma frames that show it next to the title. Venue detail still has the cluster next to the title (unchanged). The action grid Row 1 reading `[Guardar] [Reportar] [Ver site?]` (no Partilhar) is **intentional** — do not flag against any Figma frame that still shows Partilhar in the grid. The Reportar pill rendering Lucide `flag` (not an SVG asset like its grid siblings) is **intentional**; the cluster's Share rendering as a circular icon (not a labeled pill) is **intentional**.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_detail_body.dart` (`_ShareIconButton` widget + its inline mount in `_NameAndTagBlock`), `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_action_grid.dart` (Reportar `_ActionButton` in `rowOne` + `_onReport` method + `_ActionButton.iconWidget` model refactor), and the mirror pair in `lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_detail_body.dart` + `lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_action_grid.dart`. Reused utilities: `shareEvent`/`shareVenue` (existing share helpers), `showReportSheet` (existing moderation sheet), `LucideIcons.flag`/`LucideIcons.share_2`, `l10n.moderationMenuReport` (existing ARB key).
- **Amends:** [D210](#d210-reminder-bell-in-event-detail-body-inline-action-cluster-next-to-save--report-prod-2511-follow-up) — cluster composition swap (Report-flag chip → Share icon). Bell unchanged.

### D215: Reminder picker sheet shows web-only "delivered to mobile app" hint (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** The "Lembrar-me disto" picker sheet (`ReminderPickerSheet`, opened by the bell on event detail) renders an extra italic line on **web only** (`kIsWeb`) right beneath the subtitle: `reminderPickerWebMobileOnlyHint` ("Os lembretes chegam à tua app móvel (iOS/Android)." / "Reminders are delivered to your iOS or Android app." / BR `"Os lembretes chegam ao seu app móvel (iOS/Android)."`) with a leading 12 px `LucideIcons.info` glyph, both styled `AppColors.sokoShade3` (matches the subtitle family, one size down + italic so it reads as a meta-note distinct from the subtitle itself). Mobile builds skip the line entirely. The bell, the picker, the Save flow, and the offset/occurrence chips are unchanged on every platform.
- **Why:** Web has no FCM channel today — `FcmTokenService.initialise()`, `FcmHandlerService.initialise()`, and `PushPermissionService.refresh()` all early-return on `!_isMobile`; `heyl_app/web/` carries no service worker / VAPID key — so the browser will never display the push notification itself. The bell is **not** broken on web though: backend reminder delivery is user-scoped fanout (`CreateEventReminderRequest` is `{occurrence_id, offset_minutes}` only — no device selector), and the dispatcher pushes to every `device_push_tokens` row for the user, so a reminder scheduled on web genuinely fires on the user's iOS/Android device(s) if the mobile app is installed and registered. Keeping the bell visible preserves cross-device usefulness; the hint corrects the otherwise-misleading "Soko will notify you" subtitle by telling the user **where** the notification will actually land. Considered + rejected: (a) hiding the bell on web (matches existing `kIsWeb` pattern but loses cross-device setup), (b) building a backend device-introspection API to only show the hint when the user has at least one mobile device (medium effort, only solves the UI hint), (c) shipping real web push (the proper fix — tracked as deferred follow-up; see [notifications-inbox.md § Future work](../features/notifications-inbox.md#future-work)). Hint-only was the user's chosen scope.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the italic info row beneath the picker subtitle on web is **intentional** and only renders on web — do not flag its presence as a regression against a mobile Figma frame, and do not flag its absence as a regression on mobile. The hint must be removed when real web push lands (see § "Where it lives" below for the removal checklist).
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/reminder_picker_sheet.dart` (`kIsWeb`-gated `Row` block inside `build()`, right after the subtitle `Text`). ARB key `reminderPickerWebMobileOnlyHint` in all three locales (`intl_en.arb` carries the description). When web push ships: delete the `kIsWeb` block + the three ARB entries + the regenerated bindings.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/notifications-inbox.md § Picker sheet § Web-only delivery hint`](../features/notifications-inbox.md#web-only-delivery-hint).

<!-- Most recent: D226 -->

### D216: Hierarchical preference children hidden (not greyed) when master is off — master = OR of children (PROD-2510)

- **Choice:** In `NotificationCategoriesSection`, when a category has registered sub-types (today: Discovery → `weekly_bundle_ready` + `daily_drop_ready`), the indented sub-rows are **hidden entirely** when the category-wide toggle is off — not rendered as greyed-out switches. The master row IS the logical OR of its children: toggling the last enabled child off auto-flips the master off (and the children disappear); flipping the master on syncs both children to on. To re-enable a single child after the master auto-flipped off, the user flips the master back on (both children come on) and then individually re-disables. Marketing and any future no-sub-type category render their row unchanged (single switch, no children).
- **Why:** The in-flight pre-fix code greyed sub-rows out when the category was off, but kept the per-type override server-side. That created two visible-vs-real mismatches: (a) a user who turned a sub-type ON individually then turned the category OFF would still receive that sub-type because the override wins server-side (the sub-row showed greyed-off but the server kept delivering); (b) a user who turned both sub-types OFF individually would still see the category master showing ON, because `enabled` stayed true while the children were overridden off. Hiding children + bidirectional sync makes the displayed state and the server state agree: master off ⇒ children hidden + their overrides force-synced off; all children off ⇒ master auto-off + children hidden. Considered + rejected: (1) keep greying — preserves per-child state across master flips but the mismatches above are user-visible bugs; (2) clear-overrides on master toggle — would require a backend "null clears override" sentinel that doesn't exist today; (3) display-only sync that leaves `enabled` decoupled from children — would defer the bug instead of fixing it.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** when Discovery's master is OFF, expect **no** indented sub-rows under it (just the bare master row). When the master is ON, expect both `Weekly digest` + `Daily drop` sub-rows visible at full opacity. Switches with greyed-out children under an off master are a regression — flag them.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/notifications/widgets/notification_categories_section.dart` (`if (prefs.valueFor(entry.category))` guard around the `for ... in _subTypeRowsFor(...)` loop). `lib/features/notifications/providers/notification_preferences_provider.dart` (`toggle()` for down-sync, `toggleType()` for up-derive, `_deriveCategoryEnabled` helper, `_localKnownSubTypes` registry).
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/notifications-inbox.md § Hierarchical preferences`](../features/notifications-inbox.md#hierarchical-preferences-prod-2510).

### D217: Event-detail action cluster moves below the tag row, full-width title (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** On event detail (`EventDetailBody`), the trailing 40 px icon cluster `[Save] [Share] [Bell]` no longer sits to the right of the title inside `_NameAndTagBlock`'s top `Row`. The title is now a bare `Text` widget spanning the full content width; the cluster is rendered as its own `Row` directly beneath `EventTagRow`, with a **30 px** `SizedBox` gap above (matching the existing 30 px section gap below the block). Composition is unchanged (`[Save] [Share] [Bell]`, all 40 px circular chips with the same fills/glyphs as before — see D210 + D214). **Venue detail is intentionally NOT changed in the same pass** — `VenueDetailBody`'s `_NameAndTagBlock` still keeps the `[Save] [Share]` cluster next to the title. The asymmetry is on the to-do list; mirror to venue when explicitly requested.
- **Why:** Two reinforcing reasons: (1) **title legibility** — long event titles (e.g. "Pedro Ressano Garcia - Fluir – Espaço e desenho") wrap across multiple lines and the right-side cluster used to compress the wrapping column, producing awkward 4–5 line breaks; the full-width title resolves to 2–3 lines on the same content. (2) **closer alignment with Figma** — the Figma comp `6181:5701` puts a Save button in the row beneath the tags ("Inline 2-button row" `6144:4283` in the cached event-page reference). D210's original "Why" noted we kept the cluster up near the title because the inline-actions row only renders for list owners and Save needed a permanent home — that's solved here too, because the cluster's new row renders unconditionally below the tags, independent of `snapshot.canLeaveNote`. Padding chosen as 30 px to match the surrounding section rhythm (the same 30 px gap that already separates `_NameAndTagBlock` from `EventInlineActions`/`EventDetailsRow` below), giving the cluster symmetric breathing room on both sides instead of feeling crammed against the tag pill above.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on event detail the icon cluster `[Save] [Share] [Bell]` rendering as its **own row directly beneath the "Event" tag pill** is **intentional**. A `[Save]` or `[Save] [Share] [Bell]` cluster appearing **next to the title** on event detail is a regression of this decision. On **venue detail** the cluster still sits next to the title (unchanged) — flagging the venue page against the new event-page layout is a false positive. The 30 px vertical gap above and below the cluster row matches the existing inter-section rhythm.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_detail_body.dart` (`_NameAndTagBlock`: title is now a bare `Text`, the action `Row` sits after `EventTagRow` with a leading `SizedBox(height: 30)`). The three button widgets themselves (`EventSaveButton`, `_ShareIconButton`, `_ReminderBellButton`) are unchanged.
- **Amends:** [D210](#d210-reminder-bell-in-event-detail-body-inline-action-cluster-next-to-save--report-prod-2511-follow-up) (position of the bell within `_NameAndTagBlock`), [D214](#d214-report--share-swap-in-event--venue-detail-keep-slot-positions-no-ticket-in-session) (position of the cluster on event detail; venue-detail half of D214 is unchanged).

### D218: Map page — custom world-grid selection + `+k` overflow bubbles (map = grid = count) (PROD-2807)

- **Choice:** The dedicated **Map page** (custom `+k` selection web-only; the Discovery entry button is open to **all users** as of PROD-3133 — previously admin-only) does not use Mapbox's built-in clustering. It selects *which pins to show* with a pure-Dart **world-anchored Mercator grid**: cells ≈ one pin footprint at the current zoom, coarsened until the marker count ≤ the scope cap (`kSelectionCap` = 25 for `all`; `kSelectionCapBounded` = 50 for `yours`/`following`, PROD-2947); a single-pin cell shows an individual pin, a ≥2-pin cell collapses into a **`+k` overflow bubble** at the members' centroid (tap → zoom in ~2 levels → decluster on the next settle). A cell is *entirely* a pin or *entirely* a bubble, so no pool pin is silently dropped. The results grid hydrates **exactly** the shown pins ("map = grid") and the count line is the **shown** count, not the pool total ("count = shown") — overflow is conveyed by the bubbles + zoom-for-more. The selection consumes the per-pin `score` for ordering and **degrades to pure spatial** (nearest-first) when it's absent or uniform. (`score` is now **live** behind the backend `MAP_PINS_RELEVANCE_SCORING` flag — BE-2 / PROD-2942 — and, beyond ordering, PROD-2947 uses it to drive pin **size** (pool-normalized rank) and **caption placement priority** (`symbol-sort-key`); see `docs/features/maps.md`.) The map pins + count re-select **instantly on pan/zoom** (a throttled `onCameraMove` feeds a live-viewport provider the selection reads; the live zoom drives the floor gate); the **pool** and the results **grid** hydration stay **settle-gated** (grid reads a separate settled selection so it doesn't thrash mid-gesture). Native accepts `onCameraMove`/`customSelection` for signature parity but isn't wired (web-gated).
- **Why:** The PROD-2671 build rendered `/map/pins` via Mapbox `cluster:true`, which clusters everything with no relevance awareness, lazy-windows the grid across all ~300 nearest pins, and shows `counts.total`. That gives a dense, unreadable map, a grid that doesn't match what's on screen, and a count that overstates what the user can actually see. A footprint-sized world grid guarantees non-overlapping, spread pins; the finest-level-under-cap rule bounds the glyph count while turning genuinely dense pockets into honest `+k` bubbles; and the map=grid=count invariant makes the three surfaces agree. Building it to consume `score` but run correctly on today's center-biased, no-`score` pool means it works now and improves automatically when the backend pool + score land — no hard block. Considered + rejected: (1) keep Mapbox clustering — not relevance-aware, clusters everything, count/grid mismatch persists; (2) show each cell's top pin **and** a bubble for its remainder — two glyphs in one footprint overlap, defeating the spread; (3) screen-space (pixel) binning — simpler but not pan-stable (cells shift as you pan), which world-anchoring avoids.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on the Map page, expect **spread, non-overlapping pins** with dense areas rendered as **`+k` bubbles** (venue-only blue, event-only green, mixed pink — same palette as the old clusters), the results grid listing **exactly** the individually-shown pins, and "Ver N resultados" equal to that shown count (not a larger pool total). A map that plots every pin, or a count far larger than the visible pins, is a regression. Native still Mapbox-clusters — the Map page now **runs on native** (PROD-2671), but the custom `+k`/category-PNG rendering is web-only for now, so a native map showing Mapbox clusters + coloured circle pins is a **false positive**, not a regression.
- **Where it lives:** engine `lib/features/map/utils/map_grid_selection.dart`; `mapSelectionProvider` + `mapMarkersProvider` in `lib/features/map/providers/map_selection_provider.dart`; viewport bounds on `MapQuery` (`lib/features/map/models/map_query.dart`) fed by `onCameraSettled`; grid = shown in `map_grid_provider.dart`; count = shown in `map_results_sheet.dart`; render in `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_web.dart` behind `MapboxMapPlatform.customSelection` (+ `MapboxMapWidget.customSelection`, `MapMarker.overflowCount`, `MapPin.score`). Set on the Map page at `map_screen.dart` (`customSelection: true`).
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/maps.md § 4 "Custom world-grid selection"`](../features/maps.md#custom-world-grid-selection--overflow-bubbles-prod-2807--map-page-only).
- **Amendment (2026-07-10, PROD-3002 docs pass):** the "Native accepts `onCameraMove`/`customSelection` for signature parity but isn't wired (web-gated)" clause and the visual-audit note "Native still Mapbox-clusters … a native map showing Mapbox clusters + coloured circle pins is a false positive" are **superseded**: native has since wired the full custom rendering (PROD-2671 maps-improvements — world-grid selection, `+k` bubbles + stack-of-5, facet PNG teardrops, `onCameraMove` via `onCameraChanged`). The render now lives in **both** `mapbox_map_web.dart` and `mapbox_map_native.dart`. For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: a native map showing Mapbox clusters + coloured circle pins **would now be a regression**, not a false positive. (Also: the Map page's admin gating is a temporary pre-release measure — the page is intended for public release.) See [`features/maps.md § 4` "Native rendering parity"](../features/maps.md).

### D219: Map page — the zoom floor + "aproxima" hint are removed entirely (all scopes fetch/show at any zoom) (PROD-2807)

- **Choice:** The Map page has **no zoom floor and no hint**. Every scope — "Tudo na Soko" (`all`), "As tuas" (`yours`), "Que segues" (`following`) — fetches `/map/pins` and plots pins at **any** zoom. The `/map/pins` fetch is inherently bounded (radius clamped ≤50 km + 300-cap) and the custom grid selection caps the display (~25 markers + `+k` overflow bubbles), so even a wide/world view is readable and cheap — the floor no longer earns its keep. The "Aproxima para ver resultados" hint (`_ZoomFloorHint`), the `kMapZoomFloor` constant, `MapQuery.hasZoomFloor`, and the fetch/selection floor-gates are all removed.
- **Why:** the floor + hint existed (PROD-2737) to avoid querying/plotting the whole corpus at a too-wide view. Once PROD-2807 added the world-grid selection (top-per-cell, ≤25 markers, overflow bubbles) on top of the existing radius+300 caps, a wide view is just a handful of bubbles — readable and bounded — so blocking the fetch and telling the user to "zoom in" is pure friction. **Principle that settled it:** the hint may only exist while the floor is actually blocking the fetch; since we no longer want to block the fetch at any zoom, the hint has no valid state → both go. This evolved same-day through a brief "floor for unbounded only" intermediate (superseded before it mattered).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** pins visible on the Map page when zoomed **far out** (world/continent) are **intentional** on **every** scope — not a regression. A **"Aproxima para ver resultados" hint is a regression** (it should never render). An empty map with no pins + no hint when zoomed out is also wrong (pins should fetch + show).
- **Where it lived (now removed):** the floor gates were in `map_markers_provider.dart` (fetch), `map_selection_provider.dart` (`mapSelectionProvider` + `mapSettledSelectionProvider`), and the hint in `map_screen.dart` (`_ZoomFloorHint`); `MapQuery.hasZoomFloor` + `kMapZoomFloor` are deleted. The `mapZoomHint` ARB string is now orphaned (harmless).
- **Follow-up (PROD-2851):** removing the floor exposed that the ≤50 km radius still hard-gated bounded scopes on the backend — saved/followed items beyond 50 km from centre never came back, so they "disappeared" when zooming out. The FE now sends `ignore_radius: true` on `/map/pins` for `scope=yours`/`following` (backend PROD-2849 skips its 50 km distance gate; nearest-centre order + 300-cap kept). No-op for `scope=all` (discovery stays local). So the "radius clamped ≤50 km" bound above holds **only for `all`** now; bounded scopes are bounded by the 300-cap alone. Free-text + bounded-scope >50 km venue caveat tracked in PROD-2854. See [`features/maps.md § 4` "Bounded scopes ignore the radius"](../features/maps.md#custom-world-grid-selection--overflow-bubbles-prod-2807--map-page-only).
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/maps.md § 4`](../features/maps.md#custom-world-grid-selection--overflow-bubbles-prod-2807--map-page-only). Builds on [D218](#d218-map-page--custom-world-grid-selection--k-overflow-bubbles-map--grid--count-prod-2807).

### D220: Direct-to-Instagram-Story shortcut button on shareable surfaces uses the same chip look as sibling actions (unfilled, Soko/Ink @ 6 % bg), NOT the IG-gradient pill (PROD-2785)

- **Choice:** The dedicated Instagram-story shortcut (`IgDirectShareButton` — see [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md)) sits next to the normal share button on event / venue / list-page-header (zine detail) / zine per-card viewer / weekly-bundle overlay. On the **detail surfaces** (event, venue, list-page header, zine per-card viewer) the button renders in the **unfilled** variant: 40 px circle, `AppColors.sokoInk.withValues(alpha: 0.06)` bg, 14 px `LucideIcons.instagram` glyph in `AppColors.sokoInk` — visually identical to the sibling Save / Share / Reminder chips. Inside the **share sheet's channel row** (and on the **weekly-bundle overlay's dark backdrop**) the button stays in the **filled** variant: solid IG gradient (`instagramGradient`, top-left→bottom-right `FEDA75 · FA7E1E · D62976 · 962FBF · 4F5BD5`), 20 px white glyph — the loud "brand pill" IG uses in its own product surfaces.
- **Why:** A gradient IG pill in a row of Soko/Ink @ 6 % chips visually shouts and breaks the "row of equally-weighted action chips" rhythm those clusters rely on (D210, D214, D217). The unfilled variant preserves the row's read as a single strip of action chips while the IG glyph (uniquely recognisable at 14 px) still signals "this is the Instagram shortcut". Inside the share sheet the brand pill is on-tone because the whole row is meant to be brand-forward (WhatsApp green tile next to IG gradient next to Copy link neutral) — and on the WB overlay's dark backdrop the gradient reads correctly against `Colors.white @ 15 %` siblings. Considered + rejected: (1) always-gradient — see above; (2) always-unfilled — dilutes the shortcut's discoverability inside the sheet, where the picker row is the surface the user came for; (3) transparent bg + `ShaderMask` gradient-tinted glyph — tried mid-session and rejected as visually inconsistent with the flat sokoInk sibling glyphs (all Save / Share / Reminder chips use a solid glyph colour).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on **event / venue / list-page header / zine per-card viewer** expect a 40 px Soko/Ink @ 6 % circle with a black IG glyph, positioned **immediately after** the normal share button. A gradient IG pill on those surfaces is a regression. Inside the **share sheet's channel row** (below the preview card) AND on the **weekly-bundle overlay's top-right cluster** (dark backdrop) expect the gradient variant — that's still correct.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/share/widgets/ig_direct_share_button.dart` (`filled: bool` param; icon size auto-adjusts — 20 px filled, 14 px unfilled — to match the sibling glyph size). Wired unfilled on `event_detail_body.dart::_IgDirectShareButtonWrapper`, `venue_detail_body.dart::_IgDirectShareButtonWrapper`, `list_page_header.dart::_ActionRow`, `list_zine_item_page.dart` (row of `BtSqIco` chips). Wired filled in `soko_share_sheet.dart` (channel row IG tile via `ShareChannelTile.iconBackgroundGradient`) and `weekly_bundle_overlay.dart`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md) § "What ships" (two entry points table) and § "Direct-IG entry point".

### D221: IG-Story preview card image gets 16 px vertical padding so the BE gradient shows on all four sides (PROD-2785)

- **Choice:** In `SokoShareSheet`'s preview tile (`_LivePreviewBackground`, `soko_share_sheet.dart`), the BE-rendered PNG is wrapped in `Padding(EdgeInsets.symmetric(vertical: 16), child: Image.file(..., fit: BoxFit.contain))`. Previously the `Image.file` painted directly against the container's top and bottom.
- **Why:** The BE returns a portrait 1080×1920 asset. `BoxFit.contain` inside a landscape 240 px tile letterboxes it: image fills the full 240 px height, leaves ~100 px of gradient on each side. Top and bottom edges of the image met the container flush — the burgundy gradient framed the image left/right but not top/bottom, and the asymmetry reads as a UI bug ("why does it only show on the sides?"). 16 px vertical padding matches the sheet's `bodyPadding: EdgeInsets.fromLTRB(16, 16, 16, 24)` and the tile's 16 px `ClipRRect.radius` so the framing reads as intentional and visually balanced with the rounded card corners. Considered + rejected: (1) bump `bodyPadding` top/bottom — affects the cream sheet-background around the card, not the burgundy gradient inside it; (2) wrap `_PreviewTile` in a `Padding` — same as (1); the fix belongs inside `_LivePreviewBackground` where the gradient container lives.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on any share sheet preview tile expect a visible band of the BE-supplied gradient (`background_top_color` / `background_bottom_color`) **above and below** the letterboxed image, mirroring the left/right letterbox. A flush image against the top or bottom of the burgundy card is a regression of this decision.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/share/widgets/soko_share_sheet.dart::_LivePreviewBackground.build`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md § Live preview`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md#live-preview).

### D222: Direct-Instagram-Story shortcut opens a lightweight preview + auto-launch bottom sheet — no channel picker, no Continue button, no paste-link instructions (PROD-2785)

<!-- STALE(2026-07-01): SUPERSEDED by D223 — the `IgDirectPreviewSheet` was removed and the direct chip now fires the handoff sheetlessly, with feedback carried by an in-button spinner (D226). The Why sections of both D222 and D223 read as one narrative arc: D222 chose a preview sheet as the middle path between "no feedback" and "full instructions view"; D223 collapsed that further after user feedback that even the sheet felt like extra chrome for a chip that's meant to be one tap. -->

- **Choice:** Tapping the direct-IG button (see D220 for the button visual — that decision is unchanged) opens `IgDirectPreviewSheet` (`lib/features/share/widgets/soko_share_sheet.dart::IgDirectPreviewSheet`), a slim bottom sheet whose body is the shared `_PreviewTile` + a **two-phase caption** (`shareIgDirectPreparing` → `shareIgDirectOpening`). The sheet auto-fires the IG handoff via `notifier.share(channel: instagramStoryDirect, shareUrl)` once the BE asset resolves (dwell 300 ms so the user registers the preview PNG), and auto-dismisses on terminal state via a `ref.listenManual` on `shareControllerProvider(key)`. **No channel picker** (the picker is for the normal share button), **no Continue button** (the sheet is a feedback affordance, not a decision surface), and **no paste-link instructions view** (`IgStoryInstructionsView` is picker-path-only — see D220's cross-ref for the picker's contract).
- **Why:** The prior sheetless shortcut (`shareDirectlyToInstagramStory` awaits `copyIgAttributionLink()` before *any* visible feedback) meant users saw nothing for the ~1-3 s that the BE spent rendering the 1080×1920 PNG — the "Link copied" toast only fired *after* the fetch resolved, right before IG opens. User feedback ("no feedback, user doesn't get what's going on") was clear. The alternative extreme — reintroducing the full instructions view + Continue button from the picker path — was rejected because the direct button's whole point is fewer taps. The lightweight preview sheet is the middle path: **immediate slide-up on tap** (feedback), **shows the preview being generated** (context), **auto-launches** (no extra tap). "Link copied" pink `SokoVariant.success` toast still fires when the clipboard write resolves so the user has visible confirmation the paste-buffer is primed. Considered + rejected: (1) sticky `SokoVariant.loading` toast — smaller diff, but no preview and reads as a generic system spinner, not an intentional share affordance; (2) preview sheet with a manual Continue button — reintroduces the tap the shortcut was designed to skip; (3) inline surface-level feedback (spinner over the button) — awkward while the sheet slides up over the surface anyway.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on any direct-IG button tap expect a bottom sheet to slide up **immediately** (< 100 ms) showing the preview tile's loading skeleton + caption "Preparing your Instagram Story…". As the BE-rendered PNG resolves the tile flips to show it and the caption changes to "Opening Instagram…". IG opens shortly after and the sheet auto-dismisses. A tap that produces zero visible feedback for > 250 ms is a regression of this decision. The pink "Link copied" toast should also appear once during the sequence.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/share/widgets/soko_share_sheet.dart` — the top-level `shareDirectlyToInstagramStory(...)` helper (thin `showBottomSheetWithHiddenNav` wrapper) and the `IgDirectPreviewSheet` widget class + state.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md § Direct-IG entry point`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md#direct-ig-entry-point). Related [D220](#d220-direct-to-instagram-story-shortcut-button-on-shareable-surfaces-uses-the-same-chip-look-as-sibling-actions-unfilled-sokoink--6-bg-not-the-ig-gradient-pill-prod-2785) (button visual is unchanged — this decision is about what the button *opens*, not what it looks like).

### D223: Direct-IG chip is sheetless; picker's IG tile also fires without an instructions view (PROD-2785)

- **Choice:** Tapping the direct-IG shortcut chip (see D220 for the visual) calls `shareDirectlyToInstagramStory(...)`, which fires `notifier.share(channel: instagramStoryDirect, shareUrl)` **directly** and attaches a one-shot `ref.listenManual` on `shareControllerProvider(key)` to surface terminal-state toasts. **No sheet slides up.** The BE-rendered PNG resolves in-place; feedback lives on the chip itself (see D226). Inside the generic `SokoShareSheet`, tapping the **Instagram Story** channel tile now behaves the same way — the `_fire(instagramStory)` path dispatches straight to `notifier.share(...)`, not to a paste-link instructions view. `IgStoryInstructionsView` and its 6 ARB keys (`shareIgInstructionsTitle` / `Subtitle` / `Step1..3` / `ContinueCta`) were deleted. `_SheetView` enum and its `_view` state field on `_SokoShareSheetState` were also deleted — the sheet is a single view.
- **Why:** After D222 shipped the preview + auto-launch sheet for the direct chip, user feedback pushed further: "revert the direct share, it should open IG immediately". D222's sheet was the middle path between "no feedback" and "full instructions view"; keeping any bottom sheet on the direct chip contradicted the "one tap" mental model users expected. The picker path's paste-link view had a legitimate rationale — Instagram drops our `contentURL` sticker for non-Meta-verified apps ([learning](../learnings/instagram-stories-strips-content-url-for-non-verified-apps.md)) so we told the user "we copied it, paste it in the Link Sticker" — but the same feedback ("just open IG") argued against the extra tap surface. Collapsing both paths to fire immediately makes the two entry points behave identically (only analytics attribution differs) and eliminates the whole clipboard-write side effect that the instructions view was justifying (see also D224). Users who want a tappable URL in the story now paste it manually if they know the workaround — the extra chrome for that minority case wasn't worth the friction cost on every tap.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** tapping either the direct-IG chip on a shareable surface OR the Instagram Story tile inside the generic share sheet should produce **no additional sheet**. The direct chip should show its in-button spinner (D226) while the BE renders; the sheet's IG tile should show its tile-level busy spinner. A paste-link/instructions view appearing after tapping IG on either entry point is a regression. A "Link copied" toast firing on either entry point is a regression (see D224).
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/share/widgets/soko_share_sheet.dart` — `shareDirectlyToInstagramStory(...)` helper (now sheetless) + `_SokoShareSheetState._fire(...)` (no IG-tile special case). `lib/features/share/widgets/ig_story_instructions_sheet.dart` and the `_SheetView` enum are deleted.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md § IG-Story picker path`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md#ig-story-picker-path) and § "Direct-IG entry point". Supersedes [D222](#d222-direct-instagram-story-shortcut-opens-a-lightweight-preview--auto-launch-bottom-sheet--no-channel-picker-no-continue-button-no-paste-link-instructions-prod-2785).

### D224: No clipboard writes on any IG-Story flow — the pasteboard is off-limits (PROD-2785)

- **Choice:** Neither the direct-IG chip nor the picker path's Instagram Story tile writes to the pasteboard. `_shareInstagramStory` in `ShareController` no longer calls `sideEffects.copyToClipboard(...)` — pre-handoff or delayed. `ShareController.copyIgAttributionLink()` is deleted. The iOS `UIPasteboard.setItems([...])` clobber inside `social_share` is now irrelevant on our paths — we don't write a plain-text URL to clobber. The main `instagramStory` controller test asserts `clipboardContent` remains empty as an invariant.
- **Why:** With D223 removing the paste-link instructions view, the clipboard write lost its rationale — there was no in-app "we copied it for you" callout to justify touching the pasteboard, so a silent write reads as an unexplained side effect. Removing the writes also eliminates the whole delayed-re-copy iOS workaround (see D223's Why for why we no longer need it) and shrinks the controller substantially. Users who want a tappable link in the story either accept that IG strips the `contentURL` sticker for non-Meta-verified apps (see the linked learning) or paste the URL manually from another source. Considered + rejected: (1) keep the clipboard write on the picker only (asymmetric behavior between entry points that behave identically otherwise — leaks side effects users can't predict); (2) surface an explicit "Copy link before sharing?" toggle in the sheet (extra chrome for a minority case, adds analytics complexity).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** tapping the direct-IG chip or the IG tile in the sheet should NOT modify the system clipboard. A "Link copied" toast on either path is a regression. Pasting into any text field after tapping IG should yield whatever was in the clipboard before.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/share/providers/share_controller.dart::_shareInstagramStory` (no `copyToClipboard` calls). Test invariant lives at `test/features/share/providers/share_controller_test.dart` — the `success: idle → loading → handingOff → success` test asserts `expect(clipboardContent, isEmpty)`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md § No clipboard writes on any IG flow`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md#no-clipboard-writes-on-any-ig-flow). Historical iOS-mechanics context: [`learnings/ios-uipasteboard-setitems-clobbers-clipboard-setdata.md`](../learnings/ios-uipasteboard-setitems-clobbers-clipboard-setdata.md).

### D225: Preview loading state uses a cycling caption, not a spinner (PROD-2785)

- **Choice:** `SokoShareSheet`'s preview tile (`_PreviewTile`), while `instagramStoryAssetProvider` is `AsyncLoading`, renders a neutral grey skeleton (no spinner) with a `_CyclingCaption` widget below it. The caption rotates through 3 localised phrases every 1.2 s using an `AnimatedSwitcher` with a 400 ms crossfade: `shareIgDirectPreparing` ("Preparing composition…") → `sharePreviewLoadingComposing` ("Composing your Story…") → `sharePreviewLoadingAlmostReady` ("Almost ready…"). Loading captions and their cycle timing are the same across every entry point that opens the sheet (event / venue / list / zine / weekly-bundle / daily-drop).
- **Why:** The 1–3 s BE render was previously masked by a small centered `CircularProgressIndicator`, which reads as a generic system spinner rather than a deliberate Soko share affordance and doesn't convey progress meaningfully. User feedback asked for "text alternating (preparing composition…) instead of just loading animation". A cycling caption carries the "still working" signal via motion while also giving the user narrative context (preparing → composing → almost ready) that maps to what the BE is actually doing under the hood. `AnimatedSwitcher` crossfade at 400 ms matches the Soko brand's low-motion tone (no bouncy transitions). Combining the spinner AND the moving caption read as noisy in prototype — the caption alone carries enough signal, so `_PreviewSkeleton` lost its `showSpinner` param.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** while the preview PNG is being rendered by the BE, expect the grey skeleton with a text caption cycling underneath (not a spinning wheel). Once the PNG arrives, the caption disappears and the preview renders normally. The `AsyncError` state stays silent (no caption, no spinner) so failure reads distinctly from loading.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/share/widgets/soko_share_sheet.dart` — `_PreviewTile.build` (Column with skeleton + caption during `AsyncValue.loading`), `_PreviewSkeleton` (grey block only, no spinner), `_CyclingCaption` (`Timer.periodic` rotating index + `AnimatedSwitcher`). ARB keys: `shareIgDirectPreparing` (rescoped from the deleted direct-flow sheet), `sharePreviewLoadingComposing`, `sharePreviewLoadingAlmostReady`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md § Live preview`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md#live-preview).

### D226: Direct-IG chip self-derives busy state from the share controller and shows an in-glyph spinner (PROD-2785)

- **Choice:** `IgDirectShareButton` is a `ConsumerWidget` that takes `shareContext` + `entityId` as required params, keys them into `shareControllerProvider((shareContext, entityId))`, and derives `busy = state is ShareLoading || state is ShareHandingOff`. When busy, the button renders a small `CircularProgressIndicator` (stroke 2, coloured to match the glyph — white in the filled/gradient variant, `AppColors.sokoInk` in the unfilled variant) at the same size as the glyph, inside the same 40 px circle. Taps are swallowed while busy and the mouse cursor flips to `SystemMouseCursors.progress` on web.
- **Why:** Once D223 collapsed the direct-IG flow to sheetless, the button had zero visible feedback during the 1–3 s BE render. Users could tap and see nothing happen, tempting them to double-tap and enqueue a duplicate share. Moving feedback onto the button itself preserves the "no sheet" contract of D223 while giving the tap immediate acknowledgment. Self-watching (via required `shareContext` + `entityId` params) avoids threading a `busy` bool through 5 surfaces of prop-drilling (list-page header needed 3 layers) and keeps the busy semantics keyed to the specific surface's share controller — a sibling surface's share can't accidentally light the chip. `SystemMouseCursors.progress` is a small web-only affordance that matches the disabled-tap behaviour without needing an aria-busy attribute.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** immediately after tapping the direct-IG chip on any surface, expect the IG glyph to swap to a small spinner inside the same circle. On terminal state (IG opens, error toast, install prompt) the glyph returns. A chip that stays as the IG glyph for > 250 ms after tap without any visual change is a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/share/widgets/ig_direct_share_button.dart` (`ConsumerWidget` build reads the state and renders the spinner or glyph). Callers pass `shareContext` + `entityId`: `event_detail_body.dart::_IgDirectShareButtonWrapper`, `venue_detail_body.dart::_IgDirectShareButtonWrapper`, `list_page_header.dart::_ActionRow`, `list_zine_item_page.dart` (per-card), `weekly_bundle_overlay.dart`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/share-to-instagram-story.md § Direct-IG button — busy-state self-watch`](../features/share-to-instagram-story.md#direct-ig-button--busy-state-self-watch). Complements [D223](#d223-direct-ig-chip-is-sheetless-pickers-ig-tile-also-fires-without-an-instructions-view-prod-2785) which explains why no sheet.

### D227: Map page — Tema "Ok" is a floating, change-highlighted `BtSqIco` (not a sticky footer) (PROD-2671)

- **Choice:** The Tema facet drawer's apply button is a small **`BtSqIco`** (`Icons.check` + "Ok") that **floats bottom-centred over the facet list** (Figma `6917-21106`) instead of the old full-width `SokoCtaButton` sticky footer. It sits on an opaque `sokoPaper` backing with a soft `ink@12%` shadow so it stays legible as the list scrolls behind it (the list carries 72 px bottom padding so its last row clears the button). The button is `idle` (ink@6% border) while the selection matches the drawer-open state and flips to `selected` (**Soko/Pink**) the moment any facet is added/removed vs. that snapshot (toggling back to the opening state returns it to idle).
- **Why:** the floating treatment matches the Figma comp and lets the facet list use the full sheet height; the pink-on-change highlight signals a pending change to apply (mirrors the main filter buttons' touched→pink language). The soft shadow is a **deliberate divergence** from the flat Figma spec ("paper + Ink8 border, r6", no shadow) — a button floating over scrolling content needs lift/separation so it doesn't read as a stray chip.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on the Tema drawer, the "Ok" button **floating over the bottom of the list with a subtle shadow** is intentional; a **full-width footer button** is a regression. The button turning **pink after the user changes a facet** is intentional; staying grey/idle after a change is a regression. The shadow (absent from Figma) is an **approved divergence**, not a bug.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/map/widgets/tema_facet_sheet.dart` (`_FloatingApplyButton` + the `Stack` in the `data` branch; `_initialSel` / `_hasChanges` drive the pink highlight).
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/maps.md`](../features/maps.md); tracked during build as D7 in `docs/ui/PROD-2671/visual-audit-2026-07-01.md`.

### D228: Map page — pin captions render in Open Sans, not the design's Zalando Sans (PROD-2671)

- **Choice:** The Map page's inline pin caption (item name over its secondary facet) and the cluster "X venues / Y events" label render in **Open Sans** (Mapbox's stock glyph set), NOT the design's **Zalando Sans** (Figma `6949-21685`: name = B2 Bold / Zalando Medium 14, facet = B2 Reg / Zalando Light 14). Colour (Soko/Ink), sizes, per-section weights, and the top-left / ~6px-right layout all match; only the font family diverges.
- **Why:** the captions are drawn by a Mapbox **symbol-layer `text-field`**, and symbol text can only use fonts present in the map style's **glyph set**. Stock Mapbox styles ship Open Sans / Arial Unicode MS — Zalando Sans isn't among them, so a `text-font: ['Zalando …']` would silently fall back. Shipping the real font needs a **custom Mapbox Studio style** with the Zalando glyphs uploaded (a separate infra task); until then Open Sans is the closest stock match.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on the Map page, pin/cluster captions in **Open Sans** are an **approved divergence** (not a bug) pending the custom style. The layout (caption to the right of the teardrop, name top-aligned over the facet, ~6px gap) and Soko/Ink colour should still match the comp.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/shared/widgets/mapbox_map_web.dart` + `mapbox_map_native.dart` (the `_unclusteredIconLayerId` symbol layer's `text-field` / `text-font`).
- **Cross-ref:** [`learnings/mapbox-native-addstyleimage-wants-png-bytes.md`](../learnings/mapbox-native-addstyleimage-wants-png-bytes.md); context doc `docs/investigations/context/prod-2668-maps-page-v0.md`.

### D229: Profiling-CTA panel — headline shrinks to stay 1 line; subtitle balances its 2-line wrap (PROD-2796)

- **Choice:** The discovery "Ainda não te conheço bem" profiling-CTA card (`_DailyDropProfilingCtaCard`, shown for signed-in unprofiled users) makes its text responsive rather than fixed-size. The **headline** uses `AutoSizeText` at `maxLines: 1`, `minFontSize: 16`, max 28 — it **shrinks the font to stay on one line** instead of ellipsising when the string doesn't fit (narrow phones / longer locales). The **subtitle** uses `AutoSizeText(maxLines: 2)` fed through `balancedTwoLineBreak()`, so when it wraps it splits in the **middle of the sentence** ("Clica aqui para eu descobrir" / "um pouco mais de ti") rather than dropping a lone trailing word onto line 2.
- **Why:** the card is a fixed-proportion box (`AspectRatio 399/213`); the design's nominal 28px headline overflows on narrower viewports and in longer locales, so it truncated ("Ainda não te conheço b…"). Shrinking to one line keeps the single-line design intent (the reporter's own suggestion was "decrease the font size") over wrapping to two lines. The balanced subtitle wrap is purely a legibility/aesthetic choice.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on this panel, a headline rendered **smaller than 28px** on a narrow device is **intentional** (shrink-to-fit), not a bug — a truncated/ellipsised headline is the regression. The subtitle breaking at a **balanced mid-point** (not after the last word that fit) is **intentional**.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/daily_drop_section.dart` (`_DailyDropProfilingCtaCard`); helper `lib/core/utils/text_line_break.dart`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`learnings/balanced-two-line-wrap-nbsp.md`](../learnings/balanced-two-line-wrap-nbsp.md), [`learnings/fixed-height-container-autosizetext-pattern.md`](../learnings/fixed-height-container-autosizetext-pattern.md).

### D230: OS font scaling is honored but clamped app-wide to 1.3× (PROD-2875)

- **Choice:** `app.dart` wraps the `MaterialApp.router` builder in `MediaQuery.withClampedTextScaling(maxScaleFactor: 1.3)`. The app **honors** the OS "Larger Text" / Dynamic Type / Android font-size setting (text, buttons, cards and headers all grow with it) but **caps the ceiling at 1.3×**. No floor — users who shrink text keep their setting. Fixed-height shelf cards (`NearYouCard`, `CanonicalShelfCard`) and the Lists toggle header **grow** with the scale rather than clipping; chat text (via `flutter_linkify`) is fed the clamped factor explicitly.
- **Why:** unclamped OS scaling clipped buttons/cards/headers across the app (PROD-2875). Clamping caps the worst blowups while staying accessible; disabling scaling entirely is user-hostile and can fail store accessibility review.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on a device with **Larger Text enabled**, text/buttons/cards rendered **bigger than the Figma nominal** (up to 1.3×), and **taller shelf cards / header**, are **intentional** — not bugs. Text scaled **beyond 1.3×** would be the regression (the clamp failed). Screenshots taken at large font are not fidelity bugs.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/app.dart` (builder clamp); `lib/core/utils/text_scale.dart`; `discovery_shelf.dart` + `near_you_card.dart` + `canonical_shelf_card.dart` (cards grow); `yours_following_toggle_header.dart` (header grows); `message_bubble.dart` + `rich_message_renderer.dart` (chat scales).
- **Cross-ref:** [`learnings/os-font-scale-clamp-and-grow.md`](../learnings/os-font-scale-clamp-and-grow.md), [`learnings/flutter-linkify-ignores-textscaler.md`](../learnings/flutter-linkify-ignores-textscaler.md).

### D231: Lists "Tuas / A seguir" header scales its label-row + scallop to fit (PROD-2904)

- **Choice:** the animated `YoursFollowingToggleHeader` on `/yours` wraps its label row + scallop underline in `FittedBox(scaleDown, bottomLeft)`. When the two big display labels + scallop would exceed the page width (large OS font, mid-tween where both labels are near active size, or long locales), the whole unit **scales down together** to fit; when there's room it renders at full size (no-op). A sub-pixel slack on the scallop box suppresses a pre-existing float-ULP overflow banner.
- **Why:** the header is bounded by screen width, so it can't grow past the edge — scaling the unit down is the only way to keep the scallop non-truncated and the title centred under it without clipping. Fixes a preexisting horizontal overflow (surfaced by the PROD-2875 large-font work).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on `/yours` with **Larger Text** or a narrow/long-locale layout, the "Tuas / A seguir" header rendered **smaller than nominal** (scaled to fit) is **intentional** — clipped labels or a truncated scallop would be the regression. At normal width there is no change.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/lists/widgets/yours_following_toggle_header.dart`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`learnings/animated-text-locked-height-textpainter.md`](../learnings/animated-text-locked-height-textpainter.md); [D230](#d230-os-font-scaling-is-honored-but-clamped-app-wide-to-13-prod-2875).

### D232: Chat "Histórico de Conversas" sidebar shows no session-count badge (PROD-2914)

- **Choice:** the chat history sidebar header (`chat_sidebar_drawer.dart`) renders just the title + close button — **no count badge**. The badge previously showed `SessionsState.total` (from the sessions-list API's `SessionListResponse.total`).
- **Why:** the backend `total` was **unreliable** — it read `9` while the list clearly held 13+ conversations (the count didn't match the sessions actually returned). Rather than show a wrong number, the badge is removed until the backend count is trustworthy. **Do not re-add it without confirming the API total is correct** — backend investigation/fix tracked in **PROD-2914**.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the absence of a number next to "Histórico de Conversas" is **intentional**, not a missing element vs. an older mock.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/sessions/widgets/chat_sidebar_drawer.dart` (header row).

### D233: Feature-spotlight visual voice — hand + card, split roles (PROD-2808)

- **Choice:** In-app feature spotlights (`SpotlightTooltip` + `SpotlightHandOverlay`) render as **two separate elements** rather than a single unified tooltip: (1) a Soko Paper card with an `SokoCtaButton` primary + optional dismiss text link, mimicking the `OnboardingScreen` voice; (2) an animated `tour_hand.png` (46×52) rendered in its own `OverlayEntry`, positioned adjacent to the highlighted button on the OPPOSITE side from where `showcaseview` places the card, fingertip touching the target's near edge. Card gets a single primary action button when the CTA is a pure acknowledgment (message-feedback's "Percebi"); dismiss link renders only when `onCtaAction != null` (reminders' "Experimenta" opens the picker, IG share's "Partilhar agora" fires the direct share).
- **Why:** Baking the hand into the tooltip container broke on real device screenshots — `showcaseview` auto-flips the tooltip when the target sits low in the viewport, and centres the tooltip on the target's normal axis rather than its exact rect, so a hand glued to the tooltip pointed at empty sky (bell case) or empty space beside the button (IG-share case). A dedicated overlay positioned via `_targetKey.currentContext.findRenderObject()` breaks the coupling. The card visual borrows from onboarding rather than the product tour's editorial dashed-border card because feature spotlights teach a *specific* button — the tour is a multi-step introduction; feature spotlights are a one-shot "look here". Duplicate-button drop (`showDismissLink: onCtaAction != null`) avoids the accidental "Percebi / Percebi" UX where CTA and dismiss share their translation.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the hand sitting on the opposite side of the target from the tooltip (with the fingertip pointing back at the button) is intentional. A hand baked into the top/bottom of the tooltip card is a regression. Message-feedback rendering a **single primary CTA with no small dismiss link below** is intentional (this decision's "pure ack" case); adding a second "Percebi" link is a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/feature_spotlight/widgets/{spotlight_tooltip.dart, spotlight_hand_overlay.dart, spotlight_trigger.dart}`.
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/feature-spotlights.md`](../features/feature-spotlights.md); [`learnings/spotlight-hand-must-live-in-separate-overlay.md`](../learnings/spotlight-hand-must-live-in-separate-overlay.md); [`learnings/multiple-showcase-triggers-need-cross-widget-lock.md`](../learnings/multiple-showcase-triggers-need-cross-widget-lock.md); companion to D201 (product tour visual refresh).

### D236: A card reserves a subtitle slot only when it's given a subtitle — and D57's 2-line name stays (PROD-3090)

- **Choice:** `HighlightedShelfCard.heightForWidth` now takes `hasSubtitle` (default `true`, so existing callers are unchanged). The Map page's results grid passes `false`. The card's height reserves a **2-line subtitle block** (`subtitleBlockHeight` = a 2 px gap + 2 × 14 px @ 1.2 ≈ 36 px), but `build` omits the subtitle widgets entirely when `subtitle` is null — and the map grid is the **only** one of the card's three callers that never passes one (`highlighted_shelf.dart` and `search_results_view.dart` both do). So on the map that block was reserved for something that can never render.
- **Why it only mattered on the map:** on a scrolling shelf the slack disappears into scroll padding and reads as breathing room. The Map drawer's `half` snap is **exactly one card row tall** (PROD-3090), so the slack became a visible gap between the cards and the filter buttons — and, worse, *paid for in map*: it inflated the snap. Same widget, same slack, but the map is the one surface where card height is load-bearing. Card row at 393 pt: 318 → 282 px; `half`: 418 → 382 px.
- **D57 re-tested and upheld.** The remaining ~18 px is the reserved **second name line** ([D57](#d57-card-titles-wrap-to-2-lines-with-ellipsis-prod-1520)). PROD-3090 challenged it in the one context where D57's "the cost is whitespace that reads as breathing room" no longer holds — here the whitespace costs *map*. Decision (José, 2026-07-14): **keep the 2 lines.** D57's rejection of variable card height still stands and is in fact the crux — a `SliverGrid` has a uniform `mainAxisExtent`, so the row must reserve for the tallest name in the **list**, not the tallest in the row you happen to be looking at. Ellipsising real names ("Visitas ao Templo Radha Krishna") to reclaim 18 px was judged the worse trade.
- **Alternatives rejected:** capping the height used for the snap only (would clip the card at the snap — worse than the gap); measuring the first row and sizing to it (a grid can't do per-row heights, and rows 2+ would clip).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on the Map drawer at `half`, cards sitting directly on the footer's 12 px band is intentional. A large empty gap under the cards is a regression (the subtitle slot has crept back). Whitespace under a *short* name is intentional (D57).
- **Where it lives:** `heightForWidth(width, {hasSubtitle})` + `subtitleBlockHeight` in `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/highlighted_shelf_card.dart`; `MapResultsGrid.cardRowHeightFor` in `lib/features/map/widgets/map_results_grid.dart` (pinned by tests in `test/features/map/widgets/map_results_grid_test.dart`).
- **Cross-ref:** [`features/maps.md`](../features/maps.md) § "Results drawer"; D57.

### D235: Persona card renders the real Figma card image + localized text overlays (PROD-2863, extended by PROD-3263)

- **Choice:** The persona "shareable card" on the social profile is a **bundled Figma raster** (`assets/images/personas/cards/{persona_id}.png`, one per persona), not a native Flutter reconstruction and not the backend-rendered story PNG. The baked description on the image is Portuguese placeholder; at runtime a `Container` in the persona's exact cover colour is positioned over that region (fractional geometry: `left 0.145w`, `top 0.732h`, `width 0.71w`, `height 0.158h`) and paints the **localized** description on top, with a redrawn dashed rule above it. Shown at three sizes (mini ~78 px in the "A tua memória" section, ~230 px on the memory screen, tap-to-enlarge ~480 px) and for visited profiles too. Lives in `lib/features/profile/widgets/persona_card.dart`.
- **Why:** The card must match Figma **exactly** (stacked tilted cards behind, exact cover colour, dashed rule) — a hand-rolled reconstruction was rejected for not being pixel-identical. The backend renders the canonical story PNG but needs GCS creds for the persona illustration (`gs://…/personas/{id}.png`), so it 500s on localhost. A raster can't be translated, so the fix is: ship the real image, cover only the baked text, and overlay translated copy. Description text uses a fixed `0.026·w` font (not `AutoSizeText`) with newlines collapsed, so all personas render at the same size. The dark-maroon Figma background is chroma-keyed to transparent; footer (www.soko.fyi) is cropped out.
- **Asset weight:** re-exported clean from the Figma story frame at **880×994, 256-colour palette, edge-bled** (~45–64 KB each). The earlier 24-colour crush (91 KB total) collapsed the edge anti-aliasing and speckled an "ink-spill" fringe on the tap-to-enlarge zoom — see [`learnings/flat-figma-card-png-palette-quantization.md`](../learnings/flat-figma-card-png-palette-quantization.md). Same framing (aspect 0.885), so the fractional overlay geometry above is unchanged.
- **Extended to the header — kicker + persona name (PROD-3263):** the description was only *one* of the baked text blocks. The **persona name** was baked in English and the **kicker** in Portuguese, so a PT user read "GOLDEN AGE" and an EN user read "A tua personalidade" — the same bug in both directions. A second overlay now covers the header band (`top 0.500h` down to `_descTop`, same `left 0.145w` / `width 0.71w`), redrawing kicker → dashed rule → name from the ARB. No l10n work was needed: `personaOnboardingPersona*Name` and `personaCardKicker` already existed in en/pt/pt_BR, the latter referenced by no widget. Between them the two overlays now cover ~39% of the card. Baked bands, measured identical to the pixel on all five PNGs: kicker `0.5141..0.5392h`, rule `0.5644..0.5664h`, name `0.5956..0.7264h`.
- **Name is set one word per line** (`AutoSizeText`, `wrapWords: false`, `maxLines` = word count) — the same treatment as `persona_reveal_screen.dart`. The baked art always wrapped to two lines, but the real names run 2-3 words and vary by locale ("Golden Age" / "Era Dourada" against "Local at Heart" / "Só Coisas Boas" / "Ar Livre Consciente"), so a fixed size overflows the band on the three-word ones. The description keeps its fixed `0.026·w` font — that consistency is deliberate (above) and unaffected.
- **`_personaFront` holds the colour Flutter DRAWS, not the Figma value.** The constants were Figma's, but the shipped PNGs are 256-colour quantized (1-2 units off) and their palette entries are **not opaque** (alpha 253-254), which shifts `quality_seeker` and `curious_cosmopolitan` a further +1 on decode. The mismatch showed as a faint visible rectangle on the flat card — on the *description* overlay too, since PROD-2863. Re-measure from a render, never from Figma or the palette, if the cards are re-exported. Full recipe + the two adjacent traps (ink-vs-line-box sizing, cross-locale measurement): [`learnings/overlaying-localized-text-on-baked-raster-art.md`](../learnings/overlaying-localized-text-on-baked-raster-art.md).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the small on-screen size (not stretched to the margins), the dashed rules above the name and above the description, and the exact cover colour behind the localized text are intentional. A persona card showing a name or kicker in the *wrong language*, visible Portuguese placeholder text, a missing dashed rule, descriptions at inconsistent font sizes, or a visible seam where an overlay meets the card, is a regression.
- **Future direction — rebuild natively, drop the card PNGs (not scheduled):** raised after PROD-3263. The feared cost of the raster ("4 locales will explode the image count") is **not** what bites: the count stays at 5 regardless of locale, because the overlays carry the text. What bites is that every baked pixel is a translation that can never work, and layout changes need a Figma re-export. A native rebuild is smaller than it looks — `_miniFront` (`persona_card.dart`) is already the template at 78 px, and **every** element left in the PNG has a native equivalent in the code today: tilted stack (`_personaStack` + `_miniBack`), colour/border (`_personaFront`), "Soko" wordmark (`Text`, SeasonMix), illustration (a separate standalone asset, `persona_icon.dart`), dashed rules (`_dashedRule`), name/description (`profiling_strings.dart`). It would delete ~254 KB of card PNGs, the fractional geometry, and the alpha/quantization colour-matching problem by construction. Open questions before doing it: confirm the wordmark and title faces against Figma (`SeasonMix` vs `SunsetSerial`), and note the IG-Story share render is a **separate** path (`persona_icon.dart` comment) that a native in-app template does not cover.
- **Cross-ref:** the `export-and-optimise-image-from-figma` skill (Phase 4 optimisation).

### D234: Map page shortcut chips — colourful filled tags, no leading icon (PROD-2996)

- **Choice:** The Map page's quick-filter shortcut chips (`MapShortcutChips` → new `_ShortcutChip` in `map_filter_bar.dart`) render as **solid-colour filled tags** (Figma `7102-20592`/`7102-20593`, radius 6, height 30) with a `sokoInk` label and **no leading icon** — even though the Figma "Tag" shows one. Each chip is a mapped palette colour: Restaurantes = `sokoRed` (the restaurants/`eat_drink` facet colour), Meus = `sokoLilac`, Esta/Próxima semana = `sokoBlue`, Este fim de semana = `sokoYellow`. The "Reset all filters" collapse state reuses the same tag shape with a **white** fill. Only Restaurantes' colour is semantic; the other three are an arbitrary palette assignment (the shortcuts don't each filter a single facet).
- **Why:** Product wanted the colour to make the shortcuts pop but explicitly did **not** want the icons (they add clutter and imply a per-facet meaning the shortcuts don't have). Dropping the icon is a deliberate divergence, not an oversight. The chip is a **new** `_ShortcutChip` rather than a restyle of `_SmallChip` because `_SmallChip` is shared with the De quem / O quê / Quando filter-option panels, which keep their own h24/r2 paper/pink styling.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the **absence of a leading icon** on the coloured Map shortcut chips is intentional vs. the Figma "Tag". A white "Reset all filters" chip (same shape as the coloured ones) is the intended collapsed state. (As of PROD-2981 the white chip also carries a soft drop shadow the coloured chips lack — see [D239](#d239-map-white-shortcut-chip-variant-carries-a-soft-drop-shadow-to-stay-visible-over-the-map-prod-2981).)
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/map/widgets/map_filter_bar.dart` (`_ShortcutChip`, `MapShortcutChips`).

### D237: Map dot hints — category-coloured density dots beneath the pins, fixed 80/20 with no backfill (PROD-3124, v0)

- **Choice:** Retrieved-but-not-pinned `/map/pins` pool results render as **dot hints** — 10 px circles (1 px `sokoInk` outline, whole dot at 60% opacity) in the pin PNGs' body colours, drawn **beneath** every pin layer, scaled by their own zoom curve (0.2× at zoom ≤ 11 → 0.3× plateau 14–17 → 0.4× ≥ 18: dots *shrink* zoomed out, unlike pins, keeping the map pins-first), fading in ~400 ms after each settle's pins land. Dots are **tappable** (lowest priority): a tap flies the camera to the dot at zoom 17 and promotes the item to a full captioned pin even when the relevance selection wouldn't show it (`mapPromotedPinProvider` + `withPromotedPin`). Not tappable, never counted in "Ver N resultados". Allowance: a flat 200 (band structure kept for re-tuning), split **events 85% / venues 15% — computed independently, with NO backfill** (a starved leg means fewer total dots, by explicit product decision). Spread: at least one dot per occupied world-grid cell, remainder proportional to cell density.
- **Why:** The ~8-pin relevance cap makes a zoomed-out map look empty; dots whisper "there's more here — zoom in" without competing with the pins (opacity, size, z-order, and the delayed fade all keep them subordinate). The no-backfill rule keeps the event/venue balance stable and predictable for tuning; v0 is frontend-only (pool-density proxy, ceiling ≈ 140 dots) so the team + beta testers can react before any backend contract is designed.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** dozens of small translucent coloured dots under the pins are intentional, NOT clutter/regression; art + music dots share the same yellow (asset palette); null-facet dots are pink (`#FFB9CC`, the `pin-default` body); dots may be visibly sparse at high zoom on event-light areas (no-backfill).
- **Where it lives:** engine `lib/features/map/utils/map_dot_hints.dart`; render tokens/builders `lib/shared/utils/map_dot_hints_render.dart`; providers `lib/features/map/providers/map_dot_hints_provider.dart`; layers in both `mapbox_map_web.dart` / `mapbox_map_native.dart` (`heyl-dot-hints`). Spec + evidence: `docs/investigations/context/prod-3124-map-dot-hints-v0.md`, `docs/features/maps.md` §4.

### D238: Map results-grid type tags wear the OPPOSITE accents from the chat cards (PROD-3124)

- **Choice:** The "Evento"/"Sítio" type pill inset on the Map results-grid card images (`HighlightedShelfCard.itemType`) uses the accents **swapped** relative to the chat suggestion cards' `CardTagRow`: on the grid, **Evento = green** (`sokoVenueAccent` → `sokoGreen`) and **Sítio = blue** (`sokoEventAccent` → `sokoBlue`); chat cards keep the original mapping (Evento blue / Sítio green). Same `SokoTag` primitive, same l10n labels, 8 px inset top-right over the image.
- **Why:** Explicit product request (Zé, 2026-07-15) scoped to the grid tags only. Note the accent tokens are themselves cross-wired at the token level (`sokoEventAccent = sokoVenue`, `sokoVenueAccent = sokoEvent` in `app_colors.dart`), so the grid now effectively uses the RAW entity surface colours while chat uses the crossed accents.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the grid-vs-chat tag colour mismatch is intentional, NOT a bug. Do not "fix" either side to match the other without a product decision.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/highlighted_shelf_card.dart` (overlay + swap comment); chat mapping in `lib/shared/widgets/card_tag_row.dart` (untouched).

### D239: Map white shortcut-chip variant carries a soft drop shadow to stay visible over the map (PROD-2981)
