- **Choice:** The **white** `_ShortcutChip` variant — the "Limpar filtros" / "Reset all filters" collapse pill, and any future white pill in the same top shortcut row (e.g. a "search this area" CTA) — carries a soft ink drop shadow (`sokoInk` @ 18%, `blurRadius: 10`, `Offset(0, 2)`), scoped strictly to the white fill via a `color == Colors.white` check. The **coloured** shortcut chips (D234) stay flat. Shape/size/fill are otherwise unchanged from D234.
- **Why:** A flat white pill washes out against the Mapbox canvas (low contrast over roads/buildings) — the reported visibility problem. A drop shadow lifts it off the map regardless of what's behind it, which a 1 px hairline border can't (a border disappears over dark map features). The token deliberately matches — and is slightly stronger than — the on-map floating back button's shadow (`soko_back_button.dart`: `sokoInk` @ 12%, blur 8), because a 30 px pill needs more lift than the 44 px circle; the point is that the white pill reads as the same family of floating map controls. Deriving the shadow from the white fill (not an explicit flag) means a future white "search this area" pill inherits it for free.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the white reset pill having a drop shadow the coloured chips lack is **intentional** (this refines D234, which predates the shadow). A *flat* white reset pill is the regression; coloured chips staying flat is correct.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/map/widgets/map_filter_bar.dart` (`_ShortcutChip`, the `color == Colors.white` branch). Companion to [D234](#d234-map-page-shortcut-chips--colourful-filled-tags-no-leading-icon-prod-2996).

### D240: Chat composer bottom row leads with a read-only search-location indicator (pin + city)

- **Choice:** The `/chat` conversation composer's bottom strip (`ChatBarBottomRow`, the same shaded box that hosts the Create-list pill on the left and History/"Recents" on the right) now **leads** its left slot with a read-only location indicator — `ChatBarLocationLabel`: a 14 px `LucideIcons.map_pin` + city name in plain `sokoInk` text (14/w300/-0.14, matching the sibling pills' text tokens), no container. It shows where chat is currently searching (the per-conversation center **C**). When the Create-list pill is also present (unsaved cards, input unfocused) the indicator leads and the pink pill follows; the city label ellipsizes so "Recents" is never clipped.
- **Why:** Users had no visual cue where chat was searching (Lisbon-the-picker vs a place they named vs their GPS). A passive label — chosen over an outlined chip or a "Searching · <city>" prefix — reads unmistakably as context, not a tappable control, and stays out of the way of the two real action pills. Requested by João (2026-07-16). It is deliberately **not** a picker (no tap/gesture target, not a semantics button); a picker is a possible future iteration.
- **Data:** `chatSearchLocationLabelProvider` (`lib/providers/chat_search_label.dart`) resolves **label-first**: `activeSession.searchCenter.label` → the picker scope's **display label** (`cityScopeProvider`/`cityAutoScopeProvider` per `SearchScope` variant; matches the pill exactly — city → "City, ISO2", area → the leaf place name `boundaryPlaceLabel` (e.g. "Arroios"; full hierarchy until 2026-07-23), country → country name) → hidden when unresolved (no naked pin). Updates live as C moves.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the pin + city label left of "Recents" in the **chat conversation** composer is intentional, NOT clutter/regression. It is absent from the **Discovery** home composer (`DiscoveryChatBar`) by design. It is read-only — do NOT "wire it up" as a tappable picker without a product decision.
- **Where it lives:** `ChatBarLocationLabel` + `ChatBarBottomRow.searchLocationLabel` in `lib/shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart`; wired from `lib/features/chat/widgets/message_input.dart`. Design + tests: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-07-16-chat-search-location-indicator-design.md`. Feature context: `docs/features/user-location.md` § Chat search center.

### D241: Own profile grows a "Connections" tab (Locals · Followers · Following sub-tabs, Locals leads + pre-selected); Locals is demoted from a top-level tab (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** On **your own** profile, the top-level **Locals** tab is gone. A single **Connections** tab (`LucideIcons.users`) now hosts three segmented sub-tabs, ordered **Locals · Followers · Following**, with **Locals leading and pre-selected** (`_ConnectionsTabState._filter` defaults to 0 = Locals) — via `_ConnectionsTab(handle, includeLocals: isSelf)`. The `user_plus` glyph is on the **first** (Locals) segment (`_SegmentedTabs(icons: [LucideIcons.user_plus, null, null])`). On a **visited** profile the same Connections tab shows only Followers/Following (`includeLocals: false`), Followers first. On your own profile each Followers/Following list also **leads with an ownership header** — "People who follow you" / "People you follow" (`profileConnectionsFollowersHeader` / `profileConnectionsFollowingHeader`, `Pt.b2`/`pInk50`), shown only for self so the user reads it as *their own* list; visitors see no such header.
- **Why:** Connections previously appeared only on visited profiles while your own profile surfaced Locals at top level — two different tab sets for the same screen. Unifying them under one Connections tab makes the profile shape consistent for self vs. visitor, and folds Locals in as "people near you" alongside the follow graph rather than as a peer of Zines/Saved/Activity. Locals leads + is pre-selected because "people near you" is the primary discovery surface on your own Connections; the ownership headers disambiguate whose follow lists you're looking at. Product-driven, in-session (João).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the absence of a top-level Locals tab on your own profile is intentional — Locals lives **inside** Connections now, as the **first** sub-tab (pre-selected). A visited profile legitimately has no Locals sub-tab (Followers leads there). The "People who follow you" / "People you follow" header line on your own follow lists is intentional, not clutter.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_iconTab` for Connections, `_ConnectionsTab`/`_ConnectionsTabState`, `_SegmentedTabs`); header keys in the three `intl_*.arb`.

### D242: Followers / Following lists adopt the Locals-suggested row style — borderless, textured rounded-rect avatars (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** The Followers and Following lists (`FollowListBody` → `_UserRow` in `follow_list_screen.dart`) render **exactly like the "Locals suggested" cards**: borderless rows sitting flat on the paper, a `TexturedAvatar` **rounded-rectangle** (50×63, radius 2, paper-grain texture, `sokoPink` initial placeholder, `initialFontScale: 0.34`), name in `Pt.b1Bold`, `@handle` in `Pt.b2`/`pInk50`, follow button trailing. The previous style — a **white card** (`Colors.white`, radius 12, ink-8 border) with a **circular** 40 px avatar (`_MiniAvatar`, now deleted) — is gone.
- **Superseded in part by [D263](#d263-profile-avatars-are-circles-everywhere-2026-08-13), amended by [D277](#d277-one-rounded-square-for-every-avatar-the-locals-shelf-becomes-the-follow-card-no-ticket-user-directed-2026-08-26) (2026-08-26):** the avatar is a **rounded square of 50** (D263 made it a circle of 50; D277 reshaped it). Everything else about the row — borderless, flat on the paper, same type and trailing button — still holds.
- **Why:** The two surfaces list the same kind of thing (people you can follow) but looked unrelated — white circular-avatar cards vs. borderless textured rounded-rect rows. Matching them is a coherence fix, requested in-session (João: "as listas … estão com preenchimento branco e perfil circulo … é incoerente"). `_SuggestedCard` in `discover_people_screen.dart` is the canonical row shape both now share.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** borderless follow-list rows with textured **rounded-rectangle** avatars are intentional — a white card with a **circular** avatar is the regression.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/follow_list_screen.dart` (`_UserRow`); canonical shape in `discover_people_screen.dart` (`_SuggestedCard`); avatar in `lib/features/profile/widgets/textured_avatar.dart`.

### D243: "Find people" entry bar — outlined chrome + Soko-purple person-plus tile, lives only in the Locals sub-tab (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** The `_FindPeopleEntry` shortcut bar is styled to match the **outlined "Edit profile" button** (`_SqButton`, `filled: false`): transparent fill, `pInk8` hairline outline, radius 6. Its leading tile is a **Soko-purple** (`sokoPurple`) rounded square holding a `user_plus` glyph. The bar **only** leads the **Locals sub-tab** list (top of `_LocalsTab`) — an earlier placement directly under the Edit/Share action row on your own profile header was **removed** (round 2). Several tile treatments were tried and reverted in-session (solid `sokoYellow` bar; a white-square binoculars-illustration tile) — the final is the purple `user_plus` tile. The binoculars illustration (`soko-binoculars.png`) is **not** on this bar; it appears on the Find-contacts intro (`contact_matches_screen.dart`) and beside the **Find people page title** (`discover_people_screen.dart` header row).
- **Why:** A plain white bar read as filler ("está só bracha"); matching the outlined Edit-profile button makes the shortcut read as a peer control with the purple tile as the only accent. Placing it in the header under Edit/Share was tried but felt redundant with the top-bar `+`, so it was pulled back to just the Locals sub-tab. Product-driven, in-session (João).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the Find-people bar (outlined chrome + purple `user_plus` tile) appearing **only** at the top of the Locals sub-tab — and **not** under Edit/Share in the profile header — is intentional. A binoculars tile on this bar, or a filled yellow/pink bar, is the regression. The binoculars beside the Find-people **page title** is intentional (that's a different surface).
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_FindPeopleEntry`, `_LocalsTab`); page-title illustration in `discover_people_screen.dart`.

### D244: Own-profile "Share" button = Find-people "Invite friends" (system share of the profile link), not the persona share sheet (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** The own-profile action-row **Share** button (`_SqButton`, `filled: true`, in `_ActionButtons`) now fires the **same** flow as Find people's "Invite friends" (`_inviteFriends`): `shareItem(...)` — a system share of the canonical profile link (`profileShareUrl(handle)`) carrying the invite message (`inviteShareMessageTitle`). It **no longer** opens the persona share sheet (`showSokoShareSheet`, `shareContext: 'persona'`, `entityId: 'me'`). Consequence: the persona-card share (the IG-story preview + channel grid) is **no longer reachable from the profile**; `showSokoShareSheet` remains used only by the **visited**-profile top-bar share action (`shareContext: 'profile'`, `showPersonaCard: false` — a plain link share, no persona card).
- **Why:** Product wanted "Share" on your own profile to just share your profile link — one consistent "share my profile" action identical to Invite friends — rather than the persona-card share sheet. In-session (João).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** tapping own-profile Share opening the **system share sheet** (or the web share sheet) with your profile link — not the Soko persona share sheet with the card preview — is intentional.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_ActionButtons` Share `_SqButton`); helper `shareItem` in `lib/shared/utils/share_helpers.dart`; parallel `_inviteFriends` in `discover_people_screen.dart`.

> **Stale-flagging caveat (PROD-1866):** D64 (`requireCity`) is still accurate — the behaviour and entry points are unchanged. Only the chrome migrated.

### D245: Zines tab shows ALL zines in one grid, each with a public/private badge — the public/private sub-tab is gone (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** The profile **Zines** tab no longer splits into a Public/Private sub-tab (`_SegmentedTabs`). It renders a **single grid of every zine** (`_ZinesTab` → one `_ZineGrid`, no `visibilityFilter`), and each cover carries a small **visibility badge in its top-left corner** — 🌐 public / 🔒 private (`_VisBadge` + the shared `visibilityIcon`). The legacy `followers`-only tier is retired, so any leftover `followers` zine shows the **public** badge. Visitors still only receive their viewer-visible subset from the backend, so the same grid is correct for self and others.
- **Why:** A profile is zine-first; a single scannable grid with a per-cover state badge reads faster than toggling a sub-tab to see "the other half" of your zines. Product-driven, in-session (João).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** one uninterrupted zines grid with a corner badge per cover is correct; a Public/Private segmented control above the grid is the regression. A `followers`/👥 badge is a regression (map it to public).
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_ZinesTab`, `_ZineGrid`, `_ZineCard`, `_VisBadge`); `visibilityIcon` in `lib/features/lists/widgets/visibility_menu_chip.dart`.

### D246: Saved tab leads with Zines and pre-selects it (Discovery keeps Events/Places) (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** On the profile **Saved** tab the category pills are ordered **Zines · Events · Places** and **Zines is pre-selected** (`_SavedTabState._cat = zines`, `_savedOrder`). This is a profile-only override: `SearchCategoryTabs` gained an optional `categoryOrder` param; Discovery/Lists pass nothing and keep their existing **Events · Places · Zines** order with their own default.
- **Why:** A profile's saves are zine-centric, so Zines should lead and open by default — unlike Discovery, where Events/Places are the primary filters. Product-driven, in-session (João).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on the profile Saved tab, Zines first + selected on entry is correct; Places-first (the old default) is the regression. Discovery's category order is unchanged — don't "fix" it to match.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_SavedTabState`); `lib/shared/widgets/search_category_tabs.dart` (`categoryOrder`).

### D247: Profile chrome + people-search polish — white Find-people bar, settings menu icon, borderless textured search rows, `@`-tolerant search, illustrated empty follow lists (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** A batch of small profile/discovery tweaks:
  - **Find-people bar** is now **white fill** (was transparent/outlined) — updates the fill aspect of **D243** (the outlined chrome + purple `user_plus` tile is otherwise unchanged).
  - **Own-profile menu icon** is the **settings gear** (`LucideIcons.settings`), not the hamburger (`LucideIcons.menu`).
  - **People-search results row** is **borderless + `TexturedAvatar` (50×63, r2)** — matching the suggestions list and the followers/following rows — instead of a white `Container` card + circular `_MiniAvatar` (that mini-avatar widget was removed).
  - **Superseded in part by [D263](#d263-profile-avatars-are-circles-everywhere-2026-08-13), amended by [D277](#d277-one-rounded-square-for-every-avatar-the-locals-shelf-becomes-the-follow-card-no-ticket-user-directed-2026-08-26) (2026-08-26):** the row is still borderless, but the avatar is a **rounded square of 50**, not a 50×63 rounded rectangle (D263 made it a circle; D277 reshaped it). The `_MiniAvatar` this replaced was circular too — D263 is not a return to it; that one was a white card with a 40 px avatar, this is the same borderless row at 50.
  - **People search accepts a leading `@`**: `@handle` searches the same as `handle` (`peopleSearchProvider` strips a leading `@`; the screen's 2-char search gate mirrors it).
  - **Followers/Following empty state** ("no one here yet") gains the **`soko-reading` illustration**, matching the zines/saved empty states (`_EmptyList`).
- **Why:** Visual consistency across the profile's user-list surfaces + a friendlier empty state; the `@` tolerance matches how users type handles. Product-driven, in-session (João).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** white Find-people bar, gear (not hamburger) menu icon, borderless textured search rows, and an illustrated (not text-only) empty follow list are all intentional.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_FindPeopleEntry`, header `_HeaderAction`), `discover_people_screen.dart` (`_PersonRow`), `people_providers.dart` (`peopleSearchProvider`), `follow_list_screen.dart` (`_EmptyList`).

### D248: Social profile opened to ALL users; Memory stays admin-only (GA of the social pilot, in-session)

- **Choice:** The admin-only social-profile pilot goes GA — **every** user now gets the Profile hub (5th bottom-nav slot), find-people, contacts, the follow graph, followers/following, Connections + Locals, and social notifications. **Memory stays admin-only.** The split is enforced in **both** layers:
  - **Webapp** — the router redirect (`app_router.dart`) no longer bounces `/profile`, `/profile/edit`, `/find-people`, `/u/…`, `/@…` for non-admins; it now bounces **`/menu/memory`** instead. The bottom-nav 5th slot (`discovery_bottom_nav.dart`) is the Profile hub for everyone (was `isAdmin ? Profile : Menu`). The memory surfaces that live *inside* the now-open profile are gated on the **viewer being admin**: the header **brain** icon, the **tastes/twin** section ("O que a X gosta", `_TastesSection`), and the **bio-memories toggle** in Edit profile. Menu Back → `/profile` for signed-in users.
  - **Backend** — `require_admin` dropped from `public_profile_router` + `follows_router` (`api.py`); the two memory surfaces under those routers keep their own gate: `GET /{handle}/tastes` gets `Depends(require_admin)`, and the `memory_bio*` fields on the by-handle profile payload are nulled for non-admin viewers. (The `/users/me/memory*` hub stays on its own admin-gated path.)
- **Why:** Launch the social layer to everyone while Memory (bio memories, tastes, the "bio evolves" surface) remains an admin-only capability. Both layers must ship — webapp-only would 403 on the profile; backend-only would still be hidden by the nav. Product-driven GA, in-session (João).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a **non-admin** seeing the Profile hub + social surfaces is correct; a non-admin seeing the **brain icon, the tastes section, or the bio-memories toggle** is the regression (those are admin-only). `/menu/memory` must redirect a non-admin to home.
- **Where it lives:** webapp — `app_router.dart` (redirect), `discovery_bottom_nav.dart` (slot), `public_profile_screen.dart` (`_TopBar` brain, `_ProfileBody` tastes), `edit_profile_screen.dart` (`_body` bio toggle), `menu_screen.dart` (Back). Backend — `heyl-backend` `api.py`, `api_profile.py`.

### D249: "A tua bio evoluiu" banner inherits the lilac tokens of the "conta-nos sobre ti" surface (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** The owner-only pending-bio banner (`MemoryBioPendingBanner`) moved off its old pink chrome onto the **same lilac family as the "conta-nos sobre ti" entry point**: `sokoLilac` @22% fill, `sokoLilac` @40% hairline, radius 12, padding 14 — the exact tokens of the collapsed `MemoryTellUsCard`. Its button pair is now the canonical **`BtSqIco`** (`idle` outlined for "Manter atual", `lilac` filled for "Usar esta versão") instead of bare Material `TextButton`/`ElevatedButton`, and the title steps up from `Pt.b2Bold` (14) to **`Pt.b1Bold` (18)** so it out-ranks the proposed-bio prose beneath it. Both of the card's `SnackBar`s became `showSoko` calls.
- **Why:** The two memory-authoring surfaces sit in the same card and should read as one family — the banner's pink chrome made it look like an unrelated alert. Product-driven, in-session (João). The banner has no Figma frame of its own (it's an owner-only state of an admin-gated surface, see **D248**), so its tokens are derived from its nearest sibling surface rather than from a design.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a **lilac-tinted** banner with an 18 px title and a `BtSqIco` button pair is intentional. Pink fill (`sokoPink` @30%), an ink-filled stadium "Usar esta versão" button, or a 14 px title are the pre-change state, not the target. The banner only renders for **admins viewing their own profile** when the backend has a regenerated bio pending.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/features/profile/widgets/memory_bio_section.dart` (`MemoryBioPendingBanner`); token source `lib/features/memory/widgets/memory_tell_us_card.dart` (`_buildCollapsed`); dev harness `lib/dev/memory_bio_preview.dart` (scenario "Dono · versão pendente").

### D250: Recurring-events phase chips — "Primeiros dias" = Soko/Yellow, "Últimos dias" = Soko/Red (FE colour choice, no Figma; PROD-3379)

- **Choice:** The recurring-events chips (`RecurrencePhaseChip`, built on `SokoTag`) render **`recurrence_phase: "new"` → "Primeiros dias" on `AppColors.sokoYellow`** (0xFFEDE77D, novelty/highlight; label is "first days of the run", deliberately NOT "Novo"/newly-added) and **`"last_days"` → "Últimos dias" on `AppColors.sokoRed`** (0xFFF68686, urgency/last-chance). `null`/absent/unknown → **no chip**. One colour per phase, baked into the widget so every surface renders identically (map drawer cards + event detail sheet now; "This Week" / Happening later).
- **Why:** The ticket explicitly delegates visual treatment to FE ("pin vs card vs both is FE's call") and there is **no Figma comp**. Colours picked for legibility + distinctness: both read on the drawer-card artwork **and** on the green (`sokoEvent`) detail-sheet background, and neither collides with the blue type pill. `sokoRed` is also the saves-tag fill, so on the detail sheet "Últimos dias" can sit near a red saves pill — accepted (different text/icon, and urgency ⇒ red is semantically right); easy to swap if it reads as too much red. The map **pin caption** was deliberately **not** chipped (Mapbox symbol layer can't host a styled pill; deferred).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a **yellow "Primeiros dias"** or **red "Últimos dias"** pill on an event card / detail sheet is intentional. Absence of a chip on venue cards, on non-recurring events, and on mid-run events is correct (the backend only emits a value when highly confident — "silence over speculation"). A chip on the raw on-map pin is **not** expected this round.
- **Where it lives:** `lib/shared/widgets/recurrence_phase_chip.dart` (the chip), `lib/data/models/recurrence_phase.dart` (`RecurrencePhase` enum + `recurrencePhaseFromWire`), l10n keys `recurrencePhaseNew` / `recurrencePhaseLastDays`. **Surfaces wired:** the map drawer cards read the phase from `ItemSuggestion.recurrencePhase` (hydrated `EventSearchResult.recurrence_phase`) → `HighlightedShelfCard` (chip in the top-right badge stack, **below** the "Evento"/"Sítio" type pill in a right-aligned Column — a wide "Primeiros dias" chip drops to its own line rather than colliding with the type pill). **The event detail header does NOT show the chip** — it was tried in `EventTagRow` then removed (Zé 2026-07-23): the phase is a per-occurrence property and `EventDetailOut.recurrence_phase` anchors on only the next-future occurrence, which is ambiguous on a by-event view whose "Datas" section lists many occurrences. `EventDetailResponse2.recurrencePhase` stays parsed-but-unused; a proper detail treatment would annotate the "Datas" ranges per-occurrence (needs BE per-occurrence phase). Data source: `recurrence_phase` on `/map/pins` event pins (BE-6 / PROD-3378, flag-gated), and `EventSearchResult` (`/map/hydrate`) + `EventDetailOut` (BE-7 / PROD-3412, **ungated**). Detail chip anchors on the event's next-future occurrence, so it can differ from a tapped occurrence's card/pin chip for multi-occurrence events.

### D252: Signals action row — five equal-weight chips (Save · Reminder · Share · 👍 · 👎), binary sentiment decoupled from attendance (PROD-2939)

<!-- Renumbered from D251 (collided with the Leitores-tab D251) 2026-07-27. -->

- **Choice:** When the `entity-signals` flag is on, the venue/event detail action row renders **five full-width, equally-weighted captioned chips**: Guarda (Save) · Lembrete (Reminder) · Partilha (Share) · 👍 (Gosto) · 👎 (Não é para mim / "Not for me"). Flag off → the legacy compact circle chips (Save · Share · bell), production look unchanged. Extends the action-chip-row pattern (D210, D214, D217) with the two sentiment thumbs, rendered as loose captioned cells (`detail_action_button.dart`) so the parent row spaces all five evenly with `px-20` insets (Figma `7213-29087`).
- **Sentiment is binary and decoupled from attendance.** A thumb writes pure taste (`like`/`dislike`); there is **no standalone "Went" button** and no tri-state (`loved` dropped). Attendance (`went`) is its own axis, set elsewhere — a thumb never asserts it. This **superseded** the earlier 2026-07-13 design (a premium "Went" button opening a sentiment+review popover, a "…" overflow menu with Not-for-me / algo-exclude / Report, and tri-state sentiment). All of that FE surface — `putReview`, `setExcludeFromAlgorithm`, `canReview`, the review field, the overflow menu — was removed. Report moved to the bottom action grid; algo-exclude is hidden (no FE for now). Backend decoupling tracked in PROD-3443 (`sentiment` wire field replaces `taste`).
- **Filled state is unified (2026-07-27) — every active button gets Save's treatment: pink fill (`#FFB8CB`) + brown outline (`#44131D`), baked into the SVG assets.** 👍/👎 = `thumb-up-fill.svg` when held (👎 is 👍 rotated 180°); Save = `bookmark-fill.svg` when saved; Reminder bell = `bell-filled.svg` native colours when lit (the code drops the `sokoInk` recolor on the lit state — the asset already carries pink+brown). Unheld = thin brown outline. Thumbs render immediately in the unheld state (no gate on the initial signal GET) so they don't pop in a beat after the others. (Superseded the earlier mix: thumbs were paper-fill/pink-outline, bell was recolored solid brown.)
- **Captions go past-tense in the held state where the verb allows it (2026-07-27):** Save → Saved, Like → Liked (`detailActionSaved` / `signalLiked`, all 4 locales). 👎 stays **"Não é para mim" / "Not for me"** (a held stance, no natural past tense — renamed from "Isto não"); Reminder and Share stay static (a set reminder hasn't fired, so "reminded" would be wrong; "remind" also doesn't nominalize cleanly across pt/es — its fill alone signals the state; share is a one-shot). Covered by `event_save_button_test` / `soko_save_button_test` / `signal_thumb_cells_test` under the event + venue detail test dirs.
- **Where it lives:** `signal_chips_section.dart::signalThumbCells` (the two thumbs), `detail_action_button.dart` (the captioned cell), gated forks in `event_detail_body.dart` / `venue_detail_body.dart`. Admin + auth gated (`enableEntitySignals` + `isAuthenticatedProvider`). Server owns the toggle — re-sending the held sentiment clears it; the controller reconciles to the returned state and fires no client analytics (the write endpoints log server-side).
- **Why:** The premium always-present "Went" button overweighted attendance, an axis most users don't act on. Giving sentiment its own always-visible thumbs — and letting a thumb mean "I like this" independent of whether the user attended — is the lighter, more-used affordance. Decoupling also lets 👎 double as weak evidence of attendance ("I know because I went") without a dedicated control.


### D253: No-photo avatars — deterministic per-person colour + handle-initial fallback; shared PersonDot curator "bolinha" (no ticket, in-session 2026-07-28)

- **Choice:** `TexturedAvatar`'s no-photo placeholder derives its card tint from a stable hash of the person's **user id** over a 5-colour Soko palette (pink / yellow / green / blue / lilac) instead of the previous fixed `sokoYellow` — same person, same colour, on every surface and across sessions (hand-rolled hash; `String.hashCode` isn't stable across Dart embedders). The initial falls back to the **handle** when there's no display name (generated `userXXXX` handles thus read "U"), and renders NO letter at all (clean tinted card) when neither exists — never a "?". With no seed and no name the fallback tint is `sokoPink`, not yellow. The official Soko profile keeps its explicit white card. A shared `PersonDot` (`lib/shared/widgets/person_dot.dart`) renders the small round curator marker — photo when set, else initial+seeded colour — next to curator names on: every Discovery zine shelf (`CanonicalShelfCard` / `HighlightedShelfCard` via `attributionAvatar*` params, composing with the D-PROD-3336 Expert badge), the shelf see-more pages, Discovery/Yours search results (`SearchResultCard`), and the list-page header curator cluster (which now shows the curator's display NAME, falling back to the handle only when no name is set).
- **Why:** a grid of no-photo locals rendered as a wall of identical yellow cards with "?" initials — no individual identity, and "?" reads as an error. Deterministic colour gives each person a recognisable identity pre-photo (Google-style), reusing only palette colours already on brand surfaces.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** varied placeholder colours across people are intentional; the same person must keep one colour everywhere. A 14–16 px dot before "Edt. por X" on shelf cards/list header is intentional, photo or not.

### D254: Chat-bar bottom row — location pill (pink) replaces Adiciona à Soko; "Descobre a cidade" pill spans its full row (no ticket, in-session 2026-07-28)

- **Choice:** `ChatBarDiscoveryBottomRow` now leads with `ActionBarLocationPill` (new `variant` param). ~~Rendered `BtSqIcoVariant.selected` = Soko/Pink fill.~~ **Reverted 2026-07-29 to `BtSqIcoVariant.normal` (grey, Soko/Ink @ 6%)** — João's call to keep the slot neutral rather than pink (still reads on the row's dark blur, unlike the transparent `idle` pill). The `CityActionBar` on Discovery passes `showLocationPill: false`, so the lilac "Descobre a cidade" search pill occupies its entire row. Add-to-Soko keeps its entry point in `DiscoveryEndActions` at the end of the feed (D135's single-fill rule now applies there only).
- **Why:** PM request — a bigger discover-the-city bar, with the location control promoted into the chat-bar row it freed.
- **Caveat:** the product tour's `createSheet` step still targets `tourKeys.addToSoko`, which no longer attaches to any widget in the chat bar — the tour degrades to its hardcoded fallback offsets (no crash) but highlights an imprecise spot. Tour retarget (e.g. to the end-of-feed Add-to-Soko or a reworked step) is an open follow-up.

### D255: Map v2 search bar sized/filled to match the floating back button, not the Figma pill spec (PROD-3496)

- **Choice:** The Map page's v2 search bar (`map_search_bar.dart`, flag `map-search-v2`) renders at **height 44 with a `Colors.white` fill**, matching its row-mate — the floating `SokoBackButton` circle (44 px, white per PROD-2996). The retired v1 keyword field followed Figma `6917-18150`: a 40 px `sokoPaper` pill. The Ink8 hairline border + radius-40 pill shape are kept from the v1 spec.
- **Why:** Zé's call (2026-07-28, device review): the bar and the back button share one row and should read as one chrome unit — same height, same fill. The final designer pass for the whole focused-search UI is still pending (umbrella Decision #4: implement now, restyle later), so this is the interim canonical look.
- **Where:** `heyl_app/lib/features/map/widgets/map_search_bar.dart`. Row geometry unchanged — the 44 px back button already set the row height, so `mapTopChromeInset` / `mapDrawerFullTopInset` are untouched.

### D256: Discovery shelf title is a see-more tap target with an inline baseline-aligned chevron (no ticket, in-session 2026-07-29)

- **Choice:** On a Discovery shelf that has a see-more page (`showSeeMore` — more than `kDiscoveryShelfMaxVisibleItems` = 10 items), the H1 title grows a **thin hand-drawn chevron** (`_ThinChevronPainter`, 1.6 px stroke — the icon-font `chevron_right` can't be thinned) and the whole title becomes tappable; both open the shelf's see-more page with the same `discovery_shelf_see_more_clicked` + route push as the trailing `ShelfSeeMoreTile`. Driven by the new `DiscoveryShelf.onSeeMore` callback, wired into all eight see-more shelves. Shelves without a see-more page (≤10 items) show no chevron — the arrow is an affordance for a real destination, not decoration.
- **Alignment:** the chevron is a `WidgetSpan` **inside the title's own text run**, `PlaceholderAlignment.baseline` (bottom on the alphabetic baseline) sized to roughly the font's x-height, so it sits in the lowercase band on the same line as the title's last letter — not floating against the taller line box (which read "too high"). 10 px gap. `titleOverride` (the Yours/Following toggle) is excluded (it's its own interactive widget with no see-more).
- **Layout-path gotcha (the subtle part):** the chevron looked correctly aligned on Happening / Near-you but off on the other shelves. Cause: Happening/Near-you have a `titleTrailing` (show-hidden button), so their title runs through `Row([Expanded(title), trailing])` — the `Expanded` gives the `Text.rich` a tight width constraint, under which the baseline-aligned `WidgetSpan` lays out at a slightly different height than the bare `return titleWidget` path the other shelves used. Fix: route **every** chevron title through the same `Expanded`-in-`Row` layout so the alignment is identical everywhere. All current PT shelf titles end in x-height letters (s/r/i/o/e), so the baseline alignment reads centred on all of them; a future title ending in a descender (g/p/y) would sit a touch high, but none do today.
- **Where:** `heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/widgets/shelves/discovery_shelf.dart` (`_buildTitleLine`, `_ThinChevronPainter`); each shelf passes `onSeeMore`.

### D257: The map search bar's active state reuses the hint slot and the leading glyph — no new chip (PROD-3498)

- **Choice:** After a selection executes, the Map page's v2 search bar (`map_search_bar.dart`) shows the active search **inside the existing pill** rather than adding a chip or a second row: (1) the leading glyph swaps from `LucideIcons.search` to the selection's **type icon** — the same icons the suggestion rows use (`store` / `calendar` / `map_pin` / `book_open`), so the bar visibly echoes the row that was picked; (2) the label renders in the **hint slot** at full opacity (`alpha: 1.0` instead of the placeholder's `0.5`), because it is a state, not a placeholder; (3) the clear-X stays visible with the field empty, and its meaning is **mode-dependent** — focused it clears the text, idle it un-searches (restores the default pins, camera untouched).
- **Why:** the umbrella specced "bar shows the selection's label + type icon + clear-X" (Decision #29/D8) without a visual. A chip would have needed row geometry the 44 px pill doesn't have (D255 fixed the height to match the back button), and a second row would move the shortcut chips — which PROD-3496 deliberately kept still (Decision #24, no top-chrome reflow). Reusing the two slots the pill already has costs zero layout change. Designer pass for the whole focused-search UI is still pending (umbrella Decision #4: implement now, restyle later), so this is interim-canonical.
- **Caveat:** an idle bar showing a label looks identical to one showing a placeholder except for the opacity and the glyph. If the designer's pass introduces a chip, the swap is local to `map_search_bar.dart` — the state (`MapSearchState.active`) is already modelled separately from the text.
- **Where:** `heyl_app/lib/features/map/widgets/map_search_bar.dart`; state in `features/map/models/map_active_search.dart`. Behaviour documented in [`features/maps.md`](../features/maps.md) § "v2 search — selection execution".

### D258: Saved-tab row markers ride the subtitle line; the two social-proof lines get a leading glyph (PROD-3779)

- **Choice:** Two related placements. (1) On the profile's own Saved tab, the marker saying *why* a row is there — bookmark for saved, thumb for liked, **both when both** — sits at the end of the **second (subtitle) line**, right-aligned, 16 px, using the same `bookmark-fill.svg` / `thumb-up-fill.svg` the detail page shows in its selected state. (2) On the detail page, the "Followed by …" and "Liked by …" lines each gain a **leading** glyph via `MutualAvatarsRow.leading` — bookmark and thumb respectively.
- **Why (1):** a trailing slot on the *title* line costs every title ~40 px and ellipsises names sooner — and in the merged list **every** row carries at least one marker, so that tax is permanent, not occasional. The subtitle ("Bar · Lisboa") has slack to spare and is the right thing to truncate. It costs no height: the row is already 56 px because of the thumbnail, while two text lines come to ~38 px. Both markers show rather than one winning, because saving ("I want to go") and liking ("I liked it") are independent axes and hiding either hides a fact the user stated.
- **Why (2):** "Followed by A, B and 12 others" and "Liked by A, B and 12 others" are the same shape, the same avatar cluster and the same type — you had to read them to tell which was which. The glyph goes on the shared `MutualAvatarsRow` rather than being wrapped at each call site, so the two cannot drift apart.
- **Superseded in part by [D262](#d262-one-interested-list-replaces-the-liked-by-row-on-venues-and-events-2026-08-11):** half (2) no longer applies to the detail page's likes line — that line became the merged "… têm interesse" row, which carries **no** leading glyph (it mixes both signals, so any one glyph would misdescribe half the people in it). The zines' "Seguido por" line keeps its bookmark, and half (1) — the Saved-tab row markers — is untouched.
- **Caveat:** the markers are labels, not buttons — no `Clickable`, no ripple, no button padding; the whole row already opens the detail page. A future designer pass that wants a trailing slot should shorten the title deliberately, not by accident.
- **Where:** `heyl_app/lib/features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_SaveRowCard`); `heyl_app/lib/features/profile/widgets/mutual_avatars_row.dart` (`leading`, `SocialProofGlyph`); merge rule in `features/profile/utils/saved_liked_merge.dart`. Behaviour documented in [`features/entity-signals.md`](../features/entity-signals.md).

### D259: The Zine chip's ✕ keeps a 24×24 tap target, not 44 pt (PROD-3653)

- **Choice:** The `✕` inside the selected Zine chip in the map's "De quem?" panel gets a full-chip-height **24×24** opaque hit box (`Clickable` wrapping a `SizedBox(24, 24)` around a 14 px glyph), not the ~44 pt the ticket asked for. The chip body stays inert; only the ✕ acts.
- **Why:** `_SmallChip` is **24 px tall and shared across all three option panels** — 6 instantiations: 3 in `_WhoPanel` (the scope loop + the Zine chip's two faces), 2 in `_WhatPanel`, 1 in `_WhenPanel`. Growing the target means either restyling all three panels, or making one chip taller than its three neighbours in the same `Wrap`. The `Wrap` also uses `runSpacing: 8`, so a 44 pt box would overflow into the row above and start stealing taps from it — a worse accessibility outcome than a small-but-honest target. 24×24 is the largest box that is still *inside* the control the user can see.
- **Consistency argument:** every chip in this row is 24 px and every one of them is tappable, so the ✕ is not an outlier — it matches the touch affordance of the three scope chips beside it.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a sub-44 pt tap target on this specific control is **intentional**, not a regression. Flag it again only if `_SmallChip`'s height changes or the row stops being a `Wrap`.
- **Revisit:** **PROD-3649** owns the design pass across all eight search surfaces and is the right place to grow the whole row if the target is judged too small in device QA.
- **Where:** `heyl_app/lib/features/map/widgets/map_filter_bar.dart` (`_SmallChip.trailing` / `onTrailingTap`, `_WhoPanel`). Behaviour documented in [`features/maps.md` § "the Zine chip: one chip, two states"](../features/maps.md).

### D260: Profile chrome tidy-up — header icons join the tinted-circle family, Activity becomes the library Calendar, the memory card leaves the profile (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** Four coupled changes to the social profile, all product-driven in-session (João, 2026-08-07):
  1. **Header buttons sit on the app's tinted circle.** The five header actions (back arrow, find-people, brain, inbox, settings) were bare glyphs; they now draw the canonical **36 px `Soko/Ink @8%` circle with a 16 px glyph** — the chrome `ChatBarCircleButton` and `NotificationsTopButton` already use. `SokoBackButton`'s `floating` bool became `SokoBackButtonVariant { bare, shaded, floating }` (`shaded` is the new one; the two Map callsites moved to `variant: floating`, unchanged visually), the three Lucide actions route through `CircleIconButton`, and `NotificationsTopButton` **lost its `filled: false` bare variant** — that variant existed only for this header, so there is now one inbox chrome everywhere. Row gap 6 px, matching the action bar.
  2. **The Activity tab is replaced by a Calendar tab** (`LucideIcons.calendar`, `profileTabCalendar`), still self-only and still behind the "only visible to you" notice. It renders the **library's** calendar — see the `lists.md` note; the tab is a second consumer of the hub providers, not a copy.
  3. **The library's create-zine CTA heads your own Zines grid** — the same yellow `BtSqIco` the action bar carries, right-aligned, **inside the scrollable** so it scrolls with the cards. Iterated in-session: bar-style (like `_FindPeopleEntry`) → yellow bar → back to the library's pill, left → right.
  4. **The admin-only "A tua memória" card is gone from the profile** (`_TastesSection` + `_TellUsPill` + `_TasteChipView` deleted), and the **Memory page dropped its template narrative + activity count pills** (`MemoryNarrative` unmounted). Rationale below.
- **Why:** (1) the profile header was the only icon row in the app rendering bare glyphs, so it read as a different family; (2) Activity duplicated what the sections below already showed, while the calendar — the one genuinely useful cross-zine view — was buried in the library; (4) the memory card repeated, on the profile, content the Memory page already owns (`MemoryBioProse` is literally the same widget), and the count pills repeated the sections beneath them.
- **Rejected in-session:** making the tab icons **fill** (outline → solid) on selection. `flutter_lucide` ships no filled variants — see [`learnings/lucide-ships-no-filled-icon-variants.md`](../learnings/lucide-ships-no-filled-icon-variants.md) — so this needs hand-made assets; a generated-from-source attempt was built and reverted. The tabs keep the select-to-scale animation only.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** tinted circles behind the profile header glyphs are **intentional** (bare glyphs are the pre-change state). A calendar glyph as the third tab on your own profile is intentional — Activity no longer exists; supersedes the "Zines/Saved/Activity" tab set described in [D241](#d241-own-profile-grows-a-connections-tab-locals--followers--following-sub-tabs-locals-leads--pre-selected-locals-is-demoted-from-a-top-level-tab-no-ticket-in-session)'s rationale. The absence of a tastes/memory card on the profile is intentional, **not** a regression of the admin gate.
- **Where it lives:** `features/profile/screens/public_profile_screen.dart` (`_TopBar`, `_HeaderAction`, `_iconTab`, `_CalendarTab`, `_CreateZineEntry`, `_ZineGrid`), `shared/widgets/soko_back_button.dart`, `features/notifications/widgets/notifications_top_button.dart`, `features/memory/screens/memory_screen.dart`. ARBs: `profileTabActivity` / `profileNoActivity` / `profileActivityLoadError` out, `profileTabCalendar` / `profileNoCalendarEvents` / `profileCalendarLoadError` in.
- **Left unused, deliberately not deleted:** `profileActivityProvider`, `profileTastesProvider` and `features/memory/widgets/memory_narrative.dart` (+ its `memoryNarrative*` ARB keys) now have no consumer. Kept so the surfaces can be remounted; delete them if the removal sticks.

### D261: The avatar cropper is a fixed pink frame over a moving photo, in the avatar's own portrait shape (no ticket, in-session)

- **Choice:** `CropAvatarScreen` switched interaction models. Was: a draggable/resizable 1:1 square with pink corner dots over a static photo. Now: a **fixed frame, moving photo** — drag to pan, pinch or scroll to zoom, and the frame never moves (`interactive: true`, `fixCropRect: true` on `crop_your_image`). The corner dots are gone, replaced by a 2 px `AppColors.sokoPink` outline drawn in `overlayBuilder` behind an `IgnorePointer`. The frame is **portrait 100 × 125** (`_avatarAspect`), not square, with a 12 px corner radius.
- **Why:** the old model wasn't a design choice, it was broken, and reported as such ("está muito bugado… nem consigo mexer o quadrado, e as bolinhas rosas não funcionam"). `interactive` gates panning **and** the rect-drag layer (`crop.dart:669`) but **not** the pointer-signal handler (`crop.dart:621`), so a trackpad scroll over the crop area zoomed the photo unasked and nothing could pan it back. The square then had no room to move and the corner dots, ratio-locked to 1:1, had none either — hence all three symptoms from one cause. Fixed-frame/moving-photo is also what Instagram and WhatsApp do, so it needs no learning.
  Separately, the frame's **shape** was wrong: the avatar renders as a portrait squircle (`_ProfileAvatar` at 100 × 125, the edit preview at 104 × 130), so cropping 1:1 framed a shape the app never shows and the app re-cropped on render — whatever the user lined up was only a suggestion.
- **Rejected:** keeping the dots as decoration. A handle that cannot be dragged is worse than no handle; the outline says "this is the photo" without promising an interaction. One gesture to learn instead of three.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the pink outline with **no corner handles** is intentional. The 24/16 px padding around the frame is deliberate too: flush against the screen edge, a pan drag starts the platform's back-swipe instead.
- **Superseded in part by [D263](#d263-profile-avatars-are-circles-everywhere-2026-08-13), then by [D277](#d277-one-rounded-square-for-every-avatar-the-locals-shelf-becomes-the-follow-card-no-ticket-user-directed-2026-08-26) (2026-08-26):** the frame followed the avatar to a circle and then to a **rounded square** (`aspectRatio: 1` plus a painted rounded overlay). The interaction model above — fixed frame, moving photo, no handles — is unchanged through both.
- **Correction (2026-08-13):** this entry claimed the corner dots were "gone". They never were. `fixCropRect` removes only the drag handler; `crop_your_image` paints `const DotControl()` whenever `cornerDotBuilder` is null (`crop.dart:698`), so the dots were drawn the whole time — invisible enough on a rectangle, obvious on a circle. D263 passes an empty `cornerDotBuilder`, which is what actually removes them.
- **Where it lives:** `features/profile/screens/crop_avatar_screen.dart`.

### D262: One "interested" list replaces the "Liked by" row on venues and events (2026-08-11)

- **Choice:** The venue/event detail page shows **one** social-proof line for signals — "A, B e mais N têm interesse" (`InterestedRow`) — covering everyone who liked **or** saved the item, instead of the "Liked by" row plus the saver list that never existed. Tapping opens a **full screen** (`/venues/:id/interested`, `/events/:id/interested`) with the standard people-row and a Follow / A seguir / Requested button per person.
- **Why a screen, not the bottom sheet the "Liked by" row used:** it is a paged list of people, and the app already has that exact screen for zine followers (`ListFollowersScreen`). Matching it gets a shareable URL, browser back, and rows built from the same pieces — `TexturedAvatar` 50×63, `Pt.b1Bold` / `Pt.b2`, `ExpertBadge`, `CompactFollowButton`. Every list of people in the app should read as the same list.
- **No per-row glyph, and no leading glyph on the line.** Both were built and then removed on review. The line mixes two signals, so a single leading glyph would misdescribe half the people in it; and per-row bookmark/thumb markers turned every row into a small puzzle for a distinction the reader does not act on. `liked` / `saved` still travel per person on the wire — that is what makes it one list rather than two — it is simply not drawn. (This is the part of [D258](#d258-saved-tab-row-markers-ride-the-subtitle-line-the-two-social-proof-lines-get-a-leading-glyph-prod-3779) that no longer holds; the Saved-tab markers there are unaffected.)
- **Self always sorts first**, ahead even of people the viewer follows — done in SQL, not pinned client-side the way `ListFollowersScreen` does it, because this list pages and the viewer could otherwise sit on page 4. Before that rule the viewer landed *second*, below someone they followed, which reads as a bug.
- **Known inconsistency, accepted:** the "Partilhado por" row on the same page still opens a **bottom sheet**. It answers a different question (who created/contributed the item) and was deliberately left out of scope; the two rows now sit next to each other and open differently. Worth a follow-up.
- **Where:** `heyl_app/lib/features/entity_signals/widgets/interested_row.dart`, `.../screens/interested_screen.dart`, routes in `core/router/app_router.dart`. Data contract and the privacy rules behind who appears: [`features/entity-signals.md`](../features/entity-signals.md).

### D263: Profile avatars are circles everywhere (2026-08-13)

- **Superseded by [D277](#d277-one-rounded-square-for-every-avatar-the-locals-shelf-becomes-the-follow-card-no-ticket-user-directed-2026-08-26) (2026-08-26): avatars are no longer circles.** They are rounded squares, and the radius moved off the call sites into `TexturedAvatar.kRadiusRatio`. The reasoning below — that a person's avatar should not read like a content card, and that the crop screen must show the final shape — still holds; only the shape changed. `imageInset`, added by the follow-up at the bottom of this entry, no longer exists.

- **Choice:** Every avatar showing a **person** is a circle. Thirteen call sites across ten screens: the people rows (followers, following, find-people, contact matches, Discovery readers, the "interested" list) go from a 50×63 rounded rectangle to a **circle of 50**; the profile header from 100×125 to a **circle of 100**; the edit-profile preview from 104×130 to **104**; the Locals shelf card from 105×140 to a **circle of 105**; plus the 48 and 40 px small rows. Each box became square and its radius half the side.
- **Why:** a circle reads as a person the way a card reads as a place, and the app shows people in a dozen surfaces that all looked like content shelves. The rows also lose 13 px of height, so more people fit on screen.
- **The crop screen had to follow, and this is the part that would have been a bug if left behind.** `CropAvatarScreen` is the only surface where the user decides their own framing. A frame that is not the final shape means the app re-crops on render and whatever they lined up was only a suggestion — the exact defect [D261](#d261-the-avatar-cropper-is-a-fixed-pink-frame-over-a-moving-photo-in-the-avatars-own-portrait-shape-no-ticket-in-session) fixed in the other direction. It now uses `crop_your_image`'s `withCircleUi`, which fixes the ratio to 1:1 itself (the package has no oval support) and masks a circle.
- **The corner dots are now actually removed.** They were never removed before, despite D261 saying so: `fixCropRect` drops only the drag handler, and the package paints `const DotControl()` whenever `cornerDotBuilder` is null. On a rectangle they passed for chrome; on a circle they mark corners that do not exist. Fixed with an empty `cornerDotBuilder`.
- **Follow-up (2026-08-13): the official Soko avatar needed an inset.** It is the one avatar drawn with `BoxFit.contain` rather than `cover`, because the mark must show whole — and that mark fills **83% of its own file** (`soko-avatar.png`, 115 px square), so in a circle it sat against the rim and read as cramped. `TexturedAvatar` gained an optional `imageInset` (fraction of height, 0 by default); only the official avatar passes it, at `0.14`. A user's own photo is deliberately NOT inset — insetting a portrait letterboxes somebody's face.
  Left alone on purpose: the artwork is ~2 px off-centre inside its own PNG (left margin 12, right 7). Under 2 px at render size, and compensating with lopsided padding in code would hide a defect that belongs in the asset.
- **Caveat, unresolved:** the tap-to-enlarge lightbox on the public profile is circular too, for consistency. It is the one place where the shape arguably hurts — it is a zoom-to-4× viewer, and a person opening it wants the whole photo, not the avatar crop. Flagged, not changed.
- **`TexturedAvatar` itself is untouched.** The shape lives at each call site. That is deliberate: the widget is being rewritten on `joaocardoso/soko-figure-avatars` (illustrations replacing the initial letter), and putting a `circle` flag inside it would have guaranteed a conflict between two branches that are otherwise independent.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** circular people-avatars are intentional everywhere, including in the crop viewfinder. Entity thumbnails (places, events, zines) are unaffected and stay rectangular.
- **Where it lives:** the eleven files in `feat(profile): profile avatars are circles everywhere`.

### D264: The onboarding saved-zine affordance is Soko/Pink, not discovery yellow (PROD-3882, 2026-08-22)

- **Choice:** In chat onboarding, both the whole-zine **Save/Saved** CTA (`_SaveBar` in `onboarding_zine_page.dart`) and the cover **quick-save bookmark chip** (`_QuickSaveButton` in `onboarding_zines_carousel.dart`) paint their *saved* state Soko/Pink — the CTA via `BtSqIcoVariant.selected`, the chip via `AppColors.sokoPink`. Previously they used yellow (`BtSqIcoVariant.yellow` / `AppColors.sokoYellow`).
- **Why:** Soko/Pink (`0xFFFFB8CB`) is the design system's canonical "Save selected/hover" colour — the in-app saved/following affordance (`ListPageHeader`'s "Seguir → A seguir" bookmark) already uses the `selected` (pink) variant, and the design-system Bookmark component defines a `Selected` Soko/Pink-fill state (see the plus-circle divergence note above). Yellow is the *discovery* accent ("Cria nova zine" pill), a different meaning. Onboarding was the odd one out; aligning it makes "saved" read consistently across every surface.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a pink saved-zine button/bookmark in onboarding is intentional and matches in-app; a yellow saved state there is a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `fix(onboarding): saved-zine state pink, matching in-app affordance` (commit `6e7217ef`).

### D265: The onboarding name turn is a two-field form, not a chat message (PROD-3888, 2026-08-22)

- **Choice:** The identity step's **name** sub-turn (`identity.name`) captures **First name + Surname** in two stacked `SokoTextField`s (`onboarding_name_composer.dart`) with a dimmed `OnboardingContinueButton` that enables only when both are filled — instead of the single free-text chat bar (`ChatBarTopRow`) the rest of the flow uses. The single-field chat bar remains as a fallback for foundation callers that don't supply the `firstNamePlaceholder`/`surnamePlaceholder` copy.
- **Why:** A person's name is first + surname, and a reporter found that asking for it as one chat message was ambiguous (people typed only a first name). A structured two-field capture makes the expectation explicit. The two fields are still combined into a single `display_name` string on submit, so the backend contract (`identity.name` → `display_name`) is unchanged.
- **Divergence from Figma:** the chat-name Figma frame shows a single chat input; this is a deliberate product override of that design for clarity. City/interests/extra turns keep their existing composers.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a two-field First name/Surname form on the onboarding name turn is intentional; a single "Type your name…" chat bar there is the old behaviour.
- **Where it lives:** `heyl_app/lib/features/onboarding_chat/widgets/onboarding_name_composer.dart` and the `showNameComposer` branch in `onboarding_identity_name_screen.dart`.

### D266: The onboarding transcript is bottom-anchored, not top-anchored (PROD-3888, 2026-08-22)

- **Choice:** `OnboardingTranscript` now lays its turns out bottom-up — messages hug the pinned bottom composer and grow upward, scrolling only once they exceed the viewport — instead of a plain top-aligned `ListView.builder`. Implemented with the standard `LayoutBuilder` → `SingleChildScrollView` → `ConstrainedBox(minHeight: viewport)` → `Column(mainAxisAlignment: end)` idiom.
- **Why:** a chat reads bottom-up (iMessage/WhatsApp): a short transcript should sit near the input, not pin two bubbles to the top with a large empty gap down to the composer. A reporter hit exactly that on the name step (only two bubbles → big gap; worse with the keyboard open). The widget's own doc already described "bottom-follow behavior," but a top-aligned ListView only bottom-follows once content overflows.
- **Behaviour preserved:** the existing follow-bottom logic (auto-scroll on new turns, pause while reading history, resume at bottom), per-turn entrance animations, the empty-turn spacing skip, and the `onboarding-transcript-list` scroll key are all unchanged. Overflowing transcripts (vibe/zines/rituals) scroll exactly as before — only short content moves from top- to bottom-anchored.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** short onboarding steps with content resting near the bottom (empty space above) are intentional; content pinned to the top with a gap beneath is the old behaviour.
- **Where it lives:** `heyl_app/lib/features/onboarding_chat/widgets/onboarding_transcript.dart`.

### D267: Chat/onboarding loading + typing indicators are bubble-free (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-24)

- **Choice:** The two transient Soko-side states — the typing "···" dots (`TypingIndicator`) and the search/loading status pill (`SearchStatusIndicator`, e.g. "Finding places for you…", "Curating your zines…") — no longer render inside the grey rounded pill (`AppColors.sokoShade5` + `BorderRadius.circular(16)`). They now sit as plain content on the paper surface, left-aligned flush with the Soko text, with only a small vertical padding. Animations (bouncing dots, pulsing dot, label crossfade) are unchanged.
- **Why:** Both chat and onboarding chat moved Soko messages to typewriter-on-plain-text with **no bubble** (Claude-style). The two loading/typing states were the last surfaces still in the old grey pill, which read as inconsistent against the bubble-free messages. Both indicators are shared widgets imported by both features, so the single edit updates chat and onboarding together.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a bubble-free "···" typing indicator and a bubble-free "Finding places for you…" status (dot + label on paper, no grey rounded background) are intentional in both chat and onboarding; the grey pill is the old behaviour.
- **Where it lives:** `heyl_app/lib/features/chat/widgets/typing_indicator.dart`, `heyl_app/lib/features/chat/widgets/search_status_indicator.dart`.

### D268: Every onboarding action CTA scrolls inline in the transcript, not pinned to a fixed bottom bar (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-24)

<!-- STALE(2026-08-24): reversed same day by D269 — the vibe Continue was moved BACK to the pinned bottom bar. Inline scrolling did not give a *truly* constant bottom distance (it moves as content grows), which is what the user actually wanted. See D269. -->

- **Choice:** The vibe.taste "Continuar" now renders **inline as the transcript's last turn** (via `_VibeContinueGate` in `_buildContent`'s `vibeContinueContentId` case), exactly like every other step's action row (not-local, vibe.share, zines gate, profile edit/follows, rituals exit/finish). It is no longer rendered in the pinned `_composerShell` bottom bar. Because the transcript is bottom-anchored ([D266](#d266-the-onboarding-transcript-is-bottom-anchored-not-top-anchored-prod-3888-2026-08-22)), an inline last turn rests at a **constant distance from the bottom** (`~24px turn gap + transcript padding`) and **scrolls away** with the content when the step overflows.
- **Why:** vibe.taste was the one action CTA still pinned to the fixed bottom bar (~8px from the safe area, never scrolling), so its Continue sat at a different, closer distance than every other step's — most visibly against vibe.share's inline Continue right after it. The user wants every action button at the same distance from the bottom, allowed to scroll away rather than float fixed. Only the **input composers** (name/location/interests/extra/consent), which hold text fields / choices and must stay above the keyboard, remain in the pinned `_composerShell`.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** an onboarding action button (Continue etc.) resting at the same bottom offset across steps and scrolling with the transcript is intentional; a vibe Continue clamped tighter to the bottom than the other steps is the old behaviour.
- **Where it lives:** `heyl_app/lib/features/onboarding_chat/screens/onboarding_identity_name_screen.dart` (`_buildContent` `vibeContinueContentId` case; the removed `vibeTaste` branch in the composer chain).

### D269: The vibe Continue is pinned to the bottom bar again — reverses D268 (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-24)

- **Choice:** The vibe.taste "Continuar" moves back OUT of the inline transcript and into the pinned `_composerShell` bottom bar. Its `vibeContinueContentId` case in `_buildContent` renders `SizedBox.shrink()`, and a `current == vibeTaste && composerReady` branch in the composer chain renders the `_VibeContinueGate` in the fixed bottom bar (like the input composers). This directly reverses [D268](#d268-every-onboarding-action-cta-scrolls-inline-in-the-transcript-not-pinned-to-a-fixed-bottom-bar-no-ticket-user-directed-2026-08-24).
- **Why:** "Same distance from the bottom" (the goal in D268) is only truly satisfied when the button is *pinned* — an inline, bottom-anchored CTA still drifts as the transcript grows/scrolls, so its distance from the bottom is not actually constant. The user re-asked for a constant bottom distance; pinning delivers it. Scope is the vibe Continue only; the other steps' inline CTAs were left as-is this pass.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on the vibe step, a Continue clamped to the bottom bar (not scrolling with the carousels/search above it) is the intended current behaviour; the inline-scrolling Continue from D268 is superseded.
- **Where it lives:** `heyl_app/lib/features/onboarding_chat/screens/onboarding_identity_name_screen.dart` (`_buildContent` `vibeContinueContentId` → shrink; the `current == vibeTaste && composerReady` `_composerShell` branch).

### D270: User chat bubbles — 10px corners with a squared bottom-right tail; grouping squares the seam (Figma 7285:23397, 2026-08-24)

- **Choice:** The pink user bubble (`sokoUserBubbleRadius`, shared by onboarding + main chat via `SokoChatBubbleShell`) uses **10px** outer corners, with the **bottom-right corner squared (`Radius.zero`) as a tail** — squared on a single/first/middle bubble, rounded only on the *last* bubble of a stack; the **top-right** squares off whenever another user bubble sits directly above. Left corners always rounded. Inner padding is **14×8** (horizontal×vertical).
- **Why:** Matches Figma `7285:23397`, whose standalone bubble shows `br=0`. Grouping (`prevIsUser`/`nextIsUser`) squares the seam corners so a run of replies reads as one connected column down the right edge. **Divergence:** Figma specs 14×**12** padding, but the tighter **14×8** vertical reads better for the short one-line onboarding replies (user-directed) — a deliberate, documented divergence.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a single user bubble with three rounded corners + a squared bottom-right, and ~14×8 padding, is intentional; fully-rounded 16px corners are the old behaviour.
- **Where it lives:** `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_chat_bubble_shell.dart` (`sokoUserBubbleRadius`); padding at the two call sites in `onboarding_transcript.dart` and `message_bubble.dart`.

### D271: Chat and onboarding cards are one shared poster (`SokoEntityCard`) with thumbs (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-24)

- **Choice:** The main-chat place/event carousel and the onboarding vibe shelves now render the **same** card — `heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_entity_card.dart` (`SokoEntityCard`): a portrait poster (image on top, radius 8) with 👍/👎 overlaid top-right, a 2-line name and a 2-line "type · location" subtitle, **no background chrome**. Previously chat used a wholly separate `VenueCard`/`EventCard` (big horizontal card, `sokoVenue`/`sokoEvent` fill, bookmark, tag chip, social proof, action buttons) while onboarding used `OnboardingVibeCard`. `OnboardingVibeCard` is now a thin `VibeCandidate → SokoEntityCard` adapter; the chat carousel (`place_cards_row.dart`) maps `ItemSuggestion → SokoEntityCard`.
- **Thumbs replace the chat bookmark.** Chat cards wire 👍/👎 to the app-wide `signalControllerProvider` with a new `SignalProvenance.chat` (free-form string — no backend/OpenAPI change). Save-to-list is **removed from the card**; it stays reachable one tap deeper on the venue/event detail page the card opens. Generic/idless cards render the poster with **no** thumbs (no signal target).
- **Kept:** the chat dismiss-X (per-session hide + PROD-3209 "not this" recommender signal — a distinct axis from taste) and the "Show more" terminator. **Added:** a carousel-level "Places" (`sokoVenue`) / "Events" (`sokoEvent`) tag pill above the row, mirroring onboarding's shelf tag (l10n `chatCardsTagPlaces`/`chatCardsTagEvents`).
- **Event cards lose the date line** on the card (subtitle is now "type · location", matching onboarding); the date remains on the detail page. Chat poster size is 150×200 vs onboarding's 105×140.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** chat place/event cards as bubble-free portrait posters with 👍/👎 (no blue background, no bookmark) and a Places/Events tag above the carousel are intentional; the old horizontal blue `VenueCard`/`EventCard` with a bookmark is the pre-change state.
- **Follow-up (not done here):** `venue_card.dart`, `event_card.dart`, `generic_card.dart` (and their `card_tag_row.dart`/`card_action_buttons.dart` helpers) are now **unused** but left in place — `event_card.dart` still has a passing date-precision test; delete them and that test together in a dedicated cleanup.
- **Where it lives:** `soko_entity_card.dart` (new), `onboarding_vibe_card.dart`, `place_cards_row.dart`, `chat_screen.dart` (call site), `entity_signal.dart` (`chat` provenance).

### D272: A fresh quicksave on a card/chat/map opens no drawer at all — the haptic is the confirmation (PROD-3983, 2026-08-24)

- **Choice:** On cards, chat, the in-chat map modal, list rows and the zine item page, tapping an unsaved bookmark now quicksaves and opens **nothing**. Previously (PROD-3873) it saved and then presented the add-to-list drawer half-open at `0.45` as an optional "also file it in a zine" nudge. The nudge arrived *after* the action had already completed, so it interrupted a finished gesture rather than extending it.
- **The confirmation is now the filled bookmark plus `HapticFeedback.lightImpact()`**, fired once on `QuickSaveSuccess` at the `showAddToListSheet` chokepoint so all nine surfaces get it from one line. **Deliberately not `selectionClick`** — on Android that maps to `HapticFeedbackConstants.CLOCK_TICK`, the picker-detent tick, which is too faint to carry a confirmation when it is the only one present. `lightImpact` is `VIRTUAL_KEY` / a light `UIImpactFeedbackGenerator`. The map keeps `selectionClick` for pin selection, correctly: picking a pin genuinely *is* a selection change. No `VIBRATE` permission is involved — this path goes through `View.performHapticFeedback`, not the `Vibrator` service. No-op on web.
- **Nothing is lost.** Tapping an **already-saved** bookmark still opens the full drawer, so filing into a zine is one extra tap away rather than removed.
- **The in-chat map modal is included** on purpose — it is itself a modal, so opening a drawer over it would be a drawer on a drawer.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a card bookmark tap that fills the icon and shows no sheet is intentional; the half-open peek is the pre-change state.
- **Where it lives:** `add_to_list_sheet.dart` (`skipDrawerWhenSaving`, renamed from `peekWhenSaving`), 7 call sites, `bottom_sheet_utils.dart`.
- **AMENDED 2026-08-27** (PROD-4029 + branch `prod-quicksave-fixes-2`) — the decision above stands; two mechanics under it have moved. **(1) Timing:** the buzz fires on the **tap**, not on `QuickSaveSuccess` — waiting out the POST read as laggy. A save that then fails still buzzed, accepted because every failure path raises its own error toast. **(2) Level:** `lightImpact` → `mediumImpact`, tied on both platforms, and it now lives behind `SokoHaptics.saveConfirm()` (`core/utils/soko_haptics.dart`) — tune it there, never at a call site. The level has moved twice: PROD-4029 raised Android alone on the theory that iOS Medium is a thud, and device testing found the opposite, so iOS moved up to match. The `selectionClick` reasoning above is unaffected. **Still unfelt on physical hardware** — emulators have no motor.
- **AMENDED 2026-08-27** — the zine item page's bookmark did **not** fill on tap when this decision shipped: its icon was a hardcoded `LucideIcons.bookmark`, so "the confirmation is the filled bookmark" was untrue on that one surface and the silent quicksave was reported as "you have to press it twice". Now derived from `isItemSavedProvider` like every other bookmark. For `/compare-ui`: a filled `bookmark_check` on a zine card is correct.

### D273: The detail-page quicksave sheet opens at a third and folds its own chrome away (PROD-3983, 2026-08-24)

- **Choice:** A fresh quicksave from a venue / event / daily-drop detail page opens the add-to-list drawer at `initialChildSize: 0.40`, snapping `[0.40, 0.95]`, showing **only** a drag handle, a compact strip (32 px thumbnail + item name + a yellow "New zine" pill floated right) and three zine rows. The pink item banner, the search field, the "Already saved in" / "Save to" section headers and the tip + Confirm footer are all **hidden** at that height and return as the sheet is dragged up. Previously it opened full-height.
- **Why the chrome folds rather than just shrinking:** at `0.33` the sheet's fixed chrome exceeded the detent outright and produced `BOTTOM OVERFLOWED BY 8.4 PIXELS` with zero visible rows — see [`learnings/short-sheet-detent-must-budget-for-fixed-chrome.md`](../learnings/short-sheet-detent-must-budget-for-fixed-chrome.md). A short sheet is a height budget; the rows have to be bought before the chrome.
- **The fold tracks the live extent**, via a `DraggableScrollableController` shared by the sheet and its body, with a `0.5` cutoff and `AnimatedSize` on each folding region — not a constructor flag, or the banner stays shrunk at full height.
- **Ticking a zine while short grows the sheet to `0.95`** in the same gesture, so the row and the Confirm button arrive together instead of the row being squeezed out by the footer it just summoned.
- **Managing an already-saved item still opens full height**, unchanged.
- **The title on the strip is the bare item name, not "Save <name>"** — the save already happened by the time this sheet exists, so a verb would describe something the user has done. The full banner keeps its existing "Save <name>" string.
- **The create-list CTA moved onto the search row** as a labelled yellow pill with a document-plus glyph (`Icons.post_add_rounded`), reclaiming ~54 px that was a full-width row of its own. It carries a **short** label (`addToListCreateNewShort` — "New zine" / "Nova zine" / "Nuevo zine") rather than reusing "Create new zine": at 361 dp of row width the long label pushes the pt search hint (`Pesquisar nas tuas zines`) into an ellipsis. A bare `+` beside a search field was rejected as ambiguous — add? filter?
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a detail-page save sheet with no banner and no search field at rest is intentional; drag it up to see the full composition.
- **Where it lives:** `add_to_list_sheet.dart` (`compact`, `collapsed`, `_isShort`, `_NewZinePill`, `_SearchRow`), `bottom_sheet_utils.dart` (`showDraggableSheetWithHiddenNav` gained a `controller` param), `soko_save_button.dart`, `event_save_button.dart`, l10n.

### D274: The venue page gets a FOURTH tag — the backend-localized venue type (PROD-3978, 2026-08-25)

- **Choice:** `VenueTagRow` now renders a fourth `SokoTag` carrying `VenueDetailOut.primary_tag` — the backend-resolved venue type in the caller's locale ("Restaurante chinês" / "Chinese restaurant") — in **Soko/Lilac**, placed immediately after the existing "Sítio" kind chip and before rating and saves. A venue with no usable primary type (`primary_tag == null`) renders the row exactly as it was before.
- **Deliberate divergence from Figma `6144:4129`, which specifies three tags.** The comp has no slot for a taxonomy label because, until PROD-3960, `VenueDetailOut` carried none. Zé chose the extra chip over the two alternatives on 2026-08-25:
  - *Replacing the "Sítio" chip* was rejected: the venue page's green "Sítio" and the event page's blue "Evento" are a matched pair, and a null `primary_tag` would leave the row with no leading chip at all — needing a fallback rule anyway.
  - *A subtitle line under the name* was rejected: it would sit directly above `description_short` ("Restaurante chinês" over "Pizzaria artesanal"), which says nearly the same thing in better words. `list_view_item_row.dart` already treats those two as **alternatives, not companions** — it prefers the blurb and falls back to the category.
- **Soko/Lilac is chosen because the row's other three fills are taken** (venue-accent green, rating yellow, saves red) and lilac is not otherwise bound on this surface.
- **The Daily Drop detail page gets the same value in the other shape** — a new `type · city` subtitle line for venue drops, mirroring the event drops' `category · city · date`. That page has no tag row to extend and no details grid repeating the city, so the subtitle is the right home there.
- **Render verbatim.** `primary_tag` has been a localized label since PROD-2142; the client-side slug prettifiers (`_humanize` × 2, `_humanTag`) were deleted in the same change. Never humanize, title-case, or map this value. *Verbatim is about the label, not about whitespace* — both render sites `.trim()`, because stray surrounding space would paint as a visible gap inside the chip and the subtitle. Codex flagged the trim as a contract violation in review; the contract wording was tightened rather than the trim removed, and the two sites were made to agree (the Daily Drop side was rendering untrimmed).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a venue page whose tag row shows four chips is intentional; three chips means the venue has no primary type, not a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `venue_tag_row.dart`, `daily_drop_detail_screen.dart` (`_VenueSubtitle`), `social_proof.dart` (`VenueDetailResponse.primaryTag`).

### D275: The location picker's "Load more results" row is a quiet tint, not an accent (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-25)

The deep `POST /geo/search` tier was reachable only by the keyboard's Search key and users never
found it, so the picker dropdown gained a tappable row below the last result.

First build used `AppColors.sokoPink` — a real accent — on the reasoning that the *existing*
affordance for the same action (the map search bar's `mapSuggestExpandLocations` expander) is
styled muted and that is precisely what nobody noticed. Rejected on review as too loud for a row
that sits inside a list of results: **`AppColors.sokoShade5`** (#F1E5E6) with `AppColors.sokoInk`
text, hover stepping to `sokoShade45`. Enough tint to separate it from the white result rows
without competing with them.

A leading chevron was also dropped on review — the label now starts at the same left margin as
the result rows above it, so the row reads as part of the list rather than as separate chrome.

Foreground is `sokoInk`, never white, per the standing contrast rule for Soko fills. The
`Material` carries `clipBehavior: Clip.antiAlias` in both picker variants: the row has a
background colour, so without clipping it squares off the dropdown's rounded bottom corners.

### D276: The picker's search dropdown floats over the map instead of displacing it (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-25)

In `LocationScopeSheet` the results dropdown was a child of `DSSheetShell`'s `header` slot, so
opening it grew the header by up to 280 px and pushed the map card **and** the footer down. It is
now a `Positioned` overlay in the `body` Stack, aligned to the map card's own 16 px insets so it
still reads as attached to the field above; the map and footer hold a fixed position whether or
not results are showing.

The zoom hint below the field is **no longer hidden** while the dropdown is open. Hiding it would
shrink the header by its own height the instant results appeared — the same class of jump this
change removes — so the slot stays occupied and the dropdown simply covers the area below it.

**Amended 2026-08-25:** covering it required moving it. While the hint was the last child of the
*header*, the dropdown — which overlays the *body* — could only ever start below it, leaving a
stripe of hint text between the search field and the first result. The hint now renders at the top
of the body `Column` (above the map, under the dropdown), so the header ends at the search field
and its bottom padding (`_kDropdownGap` = 6 px) is the entire gap. The hint is still laid out
normally rather than at a fixed height, so a large-font or two-line translation shortens the map
instead of clipping the text or mispositioning the dropdown.

The legacy full-screen `MapLocationPickerScreen` never had the displacement (its map is
full-bleed with the field floating above it) and was not restructured.

### D277: One rounded square for every avatar; the Locals shelf becomes the follow card (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-26)

- **Choice:** supersedes [D263](#d263-profile-avatars-are-circles-everywhere-2026-08-13). Every avatar showing a **person** is a **rounded square** — the shape Figma's onboarding follow card (`7304:24157`) already used: 80×80 with a 16 px radius. Nothing keeps a circle, and nothing keeps a portrait crop.
- **The radius stopped living at the call site.** `TexturedAvatar` now owns it as `kRadiusRatio = 0.20` (a fraction of the shortest side) and `radius` became an optional override. Fifteen per-site overrides were deleted.
