- **Why a ratio, not a flat 16 px.** 16 on the 80-px design card is 20%. Held as a constant instead, a 24-px list thumbnail would be a near circle and a 360-px full-screen viewer a near square — the same number reading as two different shapes. The ratio makes a 50-px row (r10), a 100-px header (r20) and the lightbox (r up to 72) read as one shape at three sizes.
- **Why the widget, and not each screen, now owns it.** D263 deliberately left the shape at each call site to stay clear of the `joaocardoso/soko-figure-avatars` branch, which rewrites `TexturedAvatar`. The bill came due within two weeks: the app was showing people in **three** shapes at once — D263's circles, portrait cards D263 missed (the onboarding profile card at 96×120), and the onboarding follow card's rounded square. A shape spread across fifteen call sites is a shape that drifts; there is no version of "the same everywhere" that fifteen independent numbers can hold.
- **The crop viewfinder followed, for the third time.** `CropAvatarScreen` is still the only surface where the user picks their own framing, so it still has to show the final shape (the rule from [D261](#d261-the-avatar-cropper-is-a-fixed-pink-frame-over-a-moving-photo-in-the-avatars-own-portrait-shape-no-ticket-in-session), applied again). `withCircleUi` is out; the ratio is `aspectRatio: 1` and the rounded corners are **painted**, not masked — `crop_your_image` offers a rectangular mask or a circular one and nothing between, so `_RoundedFrameOverlay` fills the four corner slivers with the mask colour over the package's square hole and strokes the rounded outline in Soko/Pink. See [`learnings/crop-your-image-has-no-rounded-rect-mask.md`](../learnings/crop-your-image-has-no-rounded-rect-mask.md).
- **`TexturedAvatar.imageInset` is gone.** D263 added it for one caller: the official Soko mark, drawn `BoxFit.contain`, filled 83% of its own file and so sat against the rim of a *circle*. A rounded square leaves that mark ~8% of clear box on each side by itself, so the 14% inset was only shrinking it. The parameter had exactly one user and no remaining reason to exist.
- **The Discovery "Locals" shelf now renders the onboarding follow card**, not a shelf card with a text Follow button. Two widgets came out of `features/onboarding_chat/` to make that possible and now live in `features/profile/widgets/`: `FollowAddBadge` (the circled-"+" disc that straddles the avatar's right edge, `sokoShade45` → `sokoPink`-with-check once following, same optimistic follow write as `CompactFollowButton` — `followStateProvider.toggle(...)`; the shared `applyFollowToggle` helper it used to call was absorbed into that store on 2026-09-17) and `FollowSuggestionCard` (the whole card — avatar, badge, name, @handle). Everything in the card derives from one `avatarSize`, so a shelf can scale it down without the badge sliding off the edge. The onboarding carousel is now a thin wrapper supplying its own tap target (a sandbox profile) and funnel step.
- **The shelf's `visibleCardsHint` drops 3.5 → 3.3.** At 3.5 a phone-width viewport scales the cell to ~93 px, which drags the avatar to ~63 and the badge with it to ~31 — under its tap target. 3.3 keeps the card near design size and still peeks a third of the next card, so the row still reads as scrollable.
- **Names on that shelf are regular weight**, not the card's semibold (`nameWeight` param, user-directed). The onboarding carousel keeps Figma's `w600`.
- **What this change deliberately does NOT touch: the small overlapping person markers stay circles.** `MutualAvatarsRow`'s 16 px "N in common" cluster, `PersonDot` (the 14 px curator dot) and `SharedByAttribution`'s 20 px avatar were converted in-session and then reverted at the user's direction. At those sizes a 20% radius is 3 px and reads as neither shape, and the cluster's paper ring has to grow its own radius to match — cost with no legibility gain.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** rounded-square people-avatars are intentional everywhere, including the crop viewfinder and the tap-to-enlarge lightbox; circles are the pre-change state. The small overlapping markers listed above staying **round** is also intentional, not a missed call site. Entity thumbnails (places, events, zines) are unaffected. The Locals shelf having no text Follow button is intentional — the "+" badge replaces it.
- **Where it lives:** `features/profile/widgets/textured_avatar.dart` (`kRadiusRatio`), `features/profile/widgets/follow_add_badge.dart` + `follow_suggestion_card.dart` (new), `features/discovery/widgets/shelves/locals_shelf.dart`, `features/profile/screens/crop_avatar_screen.dart`, plus the eleven call sites that dropped their `radius:`.
### D278: The feed hero's eyebrow substitutes uppercase Mobile/B2 for ABC ROM Compressed (PROD-4006, 2026-08-26)

Figma's `event_hero` frame (`7304:23467`) sets the eyebrow — "EM DESTAQUE" — in **ABC ROM
Compressed Medium**. That family is not in `pubspec.yaml` (the app bundles `ZalandoSans`,
`SeasonMix`, `SunsetSerial`, `UnJamoBatang`) and does not appear in `docs/ui/design-tokens.md`,
so it is not a token that was missed — it is a face used on one label.

The eyebrow renders as **uppercase `Mobile/B2` at 14 px, weight 500, letter-spacing +0.14** — the
tracking the frame itself specifies, which reads as the compressed-caps treatment it was reaching
for. Bundling a font file for one string would add weight to every build, on every platform, for a
label that is at most three words.

Revisit if ABC ROM shows up in the Figma variable collection as a real typography token, or if a
second surface starts asking for it. One label is a substitution; two is a missing font.

### D279: Server-supplied colours resolve wire → registry → default, per field (PROD-4006, 2026-08-26)

The feed banner takes its ground and button fills from the backend as raw hex
(`style.background`, `style.button_background`). Three sources feed one palette, in priority order:
the wire, then a client-side registry keyed on the banner's asset key, then a default.

**Per field, not per source.** A backend that sends only a background keeps the registry's button
rather than falling all the way to the default — so a partial `style` improves the banner instead
of degrading the half it did not mention.

**The label colours are derived, never sent.** Copy and button-label colour come from the fill's
relative luminance (`core/utils/hex_color.dart`, `foregroundOn`): light fill → `Soko/Ink`, dark →
`Soko/Paper`. If the backend can send any hex, a backoffice author can pick a dark ground, and
asking them for a matching text colour would only move the failure one field along. Deriving means
they cannot produce unreadable copy.

Related: **a colour read off a Figma *frame* is a trustworthy hex under an untrustworthy name.**
`get_design_context`'s trailing "styles contained in this design" list is a best-effort name match,
not a variable binding — one capture of the feed's pinned header named `#3B0F18` as `Soko/Ink` and
`#44131D` as `Soko/Ink 10` in the same response, which cannot both be true. `Soko/Ink` is
`#44131D`; `#3B0F18` is a real fill (the Daily Drop wordmark, the IG-Story background, the banner
body text) that is simply not that token. When a hex needs to be a named brand token, take it from
the variables panel — `docs/ui/design-tokens.md` — not from a frame.

### D280: The gated onboarding like-step nudges with a glowing 👍 after 1s (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-26)

- **Choice:** On the chat-onboarding `vibe.taste` step (the like-gated carousel: the transcript pauses and "Continuar" stays hidden until the user 👍 at least one card — a 👎 does not count), **~1 second after a carousel's cards appear, one randomly-chosen card that is currently on screen breathes its 👍** — a gentle scale pulse (1.0 ↔ 1.12) plus a soft `Soko/Pink` glow halo — to point users at the action that unblocks them. The glow **follows visibility**: if its card scrolls out of view it re-picks another visible card; and it **stops for good once that carousel has a like**. Only the 👍 pulses (never 👎), and only on the onboarding step. New users otherwise miss that a like is required to proceed.
- **Why:** User-directed. The gate is invisible until you happen to like something; the nudge makes the required action discoverable without a modal or copy change. A single moving focal point (one card, not all) reads as a hint, not noise.
- **How it stays off everywhere else:** the 👍/👎 buttons live in the **shared** `SokoEntityCard` → `SokoThumbCluster` (see [D271](#d271-chat-and-onboarding-cards-are-one-shared-poster-sokoentitycard-with-thumbs-no-ticket-user-directed-2026-08-24)), used by the main-chat carousel too. The glow is a new reusable `AttentionPulse` wrapper (`shared/widgets/attention_pulse.dart`) gated by an **opt-in `highlightLike` flag that defaults false** and is threaded only from the onboarding shelf — so main chat and every other caller render the plain thumb cluster, byte-for-byte unchanged. Honors reduce-motion (renders static, no timer). The card's `Stack` gained `clipBehavior: Clip.none` so the halo isn't clipped at the image corner; the thumb tap target stays inside the image bounds even scaled, so this does **not** trip [the Stack-Clip.none hit-test trap](../learnings/stack-clip-none-overflow-child-not-hit-testable.md).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a pulsing/glowing 👍 on the onboarding like step is **intentional**; it is not a rendering bug and not present on the main-chat carousel. A static card row (no glow) after ~1s on that step, or a glow that persists after the user has liked, is the regression.
- **Where it lives:** `attention_pulse.dart` (new), `soko_entity_card.dart` (`highlightLike` on card + cluster, `Clip.none`), `onboarding_vibe_card.dart` (forwards the flag), `onboarding_vibe_carousels.dart` (`visibility_detector`-driven random on-screen pick, 1s timer, stop-on-like). **Known limitation:** the stop is per-carousel (each shelf owns its `OnboardingVibeState`), so a like in *Sítios* doesn't stop *Eventos* glowing until it too gets a like — usually invisible since the carousels stack vertically and one is on screen at a time.

### D281: Weak memory chips fold behind a "+N more" outline chip; section summaries count chips, not observations (PROD-3766, 2026-08-27)

- **Choice:** On the Memory page, a dimension renders its `primary` chips (≥ 2 ticks) by default and hides `secondary` (1-tick) chips behind a quiet **outline chip** reading "+N more" / "show less" (`_FoldToggleChip`, `foldByTier` in `compact_family_section.dart`). An expanded chip is never hidden. The collapsed parent's subtitle ("venue types 12 · vibes 4") counts **distinct primary values**, not raw observations.
- **Why:** A heavy user's "your kind of place" showed ~30 chips, all at 4 ticks, and "vibes 597" — one observation per backing fact. With Memory v1's derived strength most of those become 1-tick chips; the fold keeps the section readable as *tastes* while the weak signal stays one tap away. Not in Figma — the page predates the tier concept.
- **How it stays off elsewhere:** the tier comes from the backend (`MEMORY_PAGE_RULES_ENABLED`); a payload without `tier` is all-primary, so the toggle never appears and older builds see today's page.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** an outline "+N more" chip at the end of a chip wrap is **intentional**; a filled chip with that text, or a "+N more" with nothing hidden, is the regression. Copy keys: `memoryChipsShowMore`, `memoryChipsShowLess`.
- **Where it lives:** `observation_view.dart` (`tier`, `isSecondary`), `compact_family_section.dart` (`foldByTier`, `_FoldToggleChip`, `_DimensionBlock` is now stateful, `_dedupeByValue` carries `tier`), `parent_section.dart` (`_buildSummary`). Feature doc: [`features/memory-page.md`](../features/memory-page.md).


### D282: The event hero's scrim ramps in on an eased curve, not a uniform blur (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-27)

Figma's content overlay (`7304:23466`) declares a **uniform** `backdrop-blur: 20` over a
bottom-anchored box, plus a `#0000 → #00000080` gradient. Shipped literally, that is a hard
sharp-to-blurred seam straight across the poster — a Figma background blur is not modulated by the
layer's own alpha, so what looks like a soft ramp in the frame is a step.

The card instead ramps: **zero effect at the scrim's top edge, full blur and full darkening where
the first line of copy begins, held from there down.** That end point is the scrim's own `pt: 60`,
so the two move together.

> **Amended 2026-08-28 (Zé, PROD-3998).** The ramp originally finished **15 px above** the copy and
> the darkening bottomed out at **65 %**. Both were eased back: the finish moved down onto the copy
> — the start did not move, so the transition got 15 px *longer* rather than sliding down — and the
> foot went to **55 %**. The two changes work together: a longer runway does some of the work the
> extra darkness was doing, and 65 % read as heavy once the fade had more room. `55 %` is still
> above the frame's 50 %, so the legibility divergence below survives; it is just smaller.

Two consequences worth carrying:

- **`BackdropFilter` cannot express this.** It cannot be gradient-masked, and faking a ramp with it
  means N stacked banded filters. The only thing behind the scrim is the poster, so the card paints
  the poster a *second* time, blurred, and masks that copy with the ramp. The image provider is
  shared, so it is a second paint, not a second fetch. An `OverflowBox` re-imposes the full card
  height on the copy so its `BoxFit.cover` crop matches the sharp layer's — without it the two
  layers show different parts of the image and the blur does not register with what is behind it.
- **The ramp is eased, not linear** — see `learnings/a-two-stop-gradient-bands-where-it-lands.md`.

Darkening also goes deeper than the frame: **55 % against Figma's 50 %** (65 % as first shipped —
see the amendment above), for legibility over bright posters. Both ramps read their stops from the box they are painted into, because the scrim's height
follows its copy — a constant fraction would land the ramp differently on a three-line title.

### D283: The half-circle notch edge takes its diameter per surface and fits count + gap to the width (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-28)

Circles centred on a surface's top edge so only the lower half shows. Two frames pin it and they
**disagree about size**: the event hero (`7304:23768`) is a 386 px run with Ø12 circles, the bundle
thumbnail (`7304:23923`) a 60 px run with Ø3.428. Circle-to-run is 0.031 against 0.057 — so the
diameter is **not** a function of the width; it is chosen per surface, the way a corner radius is.

What the two *do* share, to three decimals, is the proportion: `gap / circle = 10 / 12`.

So the rule is: **diameter in, count and gap out.** Fit the whole number of notches whose pitch
lands closest to the designed one, first and last circle flush to the ends. Both alternatives are
worse — scaling the diameter gives an 800 px card 24 px craters, a different design element rather
than the same one bigger; fixing the count stretches the gaps until the notches read as unrelated
dots.

**It clips rather than paints.** The frames draw filled `Soko/Paper` circles over the image, which
is quicker to author but bakes the page colour into the card: on any other background those circles
read as bumps *on* it rather than bites out of it, and nothing fails — it just looks wrong. The
corner radius is cut in the same path, because two clips cannot make one shape: an outer
`ClipRRect` would round a rectangle the notch clip had already bitten into.

`lib/shared/widgets/notched_edge.dart`. Supersedes the `ScallopDivider` wave on the event hero;
every other scallop surface is unchanged.

### D284: The Daily Drop and Weekly Bundle cards share one frame, told apart by colour and rule (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-27)

All three Daily Drop states plus the Weekly Bundle now build on one shell (mat → solid ink outline →
inset rule → content), so "looks like the other card" holds by construction rather than by two sets
of numbers agreeing.

- **The ready Daily Drop stopped being a photo card.** It was the odd one out: generating and
  timed-out already drew the coloured frame, while ready drew a cover photo with a title and byline,
  so the slot changed species as the drop resolved. The drop's content now lives behind the tap.
  Applies to **both** home pages — the section is shared.
- **Two rules, not one.** The frame draws a continuous ink outline at the field's edge *and* a
  dashed/dotted rule inset inside it. Daily Drop's inset rule is **dashed on a two-beat short/long
  pattern**; the Weekly Bundle's is **dotted**. That is the only structural difference between them.
- **PROD-1979's guest treatment survives as the tap, not the blur.** The blur existed to withhold a
  cover and title the branded card no longer shows; blurring it would obscure nothing.

### D285: The feed's top slot is an ordered list of ritual cards, one-plus-a-third when there are two (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-08-27; peek + fade amended 2026-08-28)

Nothing at zero cards, full width at one, a snapping carousel at two or more — the peek is what
signals there is more. Adding a third card is one list entry; every width mode derives from how many
slots report themselves visible.

**Amended 2026-08-28 (Zé) — the peek is a THIRD of a card, not a half, and the peeking card is
dimmed to 0.65.** At a half the peek stopped reading as a hint and read as two cards competing, which
cost the card the user is actually looking at the emphasis this slot exists to give it. A third is
still unmistakably a cut-off card. The dimming does the same job with light rather than geometry:
the settled card is 1.0, a card showing only its resting peek is **0.65** (0.8 first, then taken
down the same day — at 0.8 the two cards did not separate), and everything between the two rest
positions is interpolated — one card brightens by exactly as much as the other dims, so the
swap reads as a crossfade rather than a step.

- **The fade is driven by how much of the card is on screen, not by `offset / pitch`.** The last card
  can never reach its own pitch multiple: `CarouselSnapPhysics` clamps its snap target to
  `maxScrollExtent`, and with two cards that stop is well short of one full pitch. A page-index
  parameterisation therefore leaves the final card resting at ~0.93 instead of 1.0, with the card
  behind it *brighter* than the third of itself it is actually showing. Visibility has no end effect
  — the card the user settled on is by construction the fully visible one. Pinned by
  `feed_top_cards_test.dart` § "at rest on the LAST card ... despite the short travel", which fails
  under exactly that mutation.
- **The visible fraction is remapped so the resting peek is where `peekOpacity` lands**, not zero
  visibility. A plain `lerp` on visible fraction would put a peeking card at 0.88 and never reach
  `peekOpacity` at all.
- **No animation controller.** The carousel owns a `ScrollController` and each card's wrapper is an
  `AnimatedBuilder` on it, with the card itself passed as the un-rebuilt `child`. `Opacity` skips its
  save-layer at 1.0, so the settled card costs nothing.
- **Where it lives:** `feed_top_cards.dart` — `peekFraction` (1/3) and `peekOpacity` (0.65) are the
  two constants; `cardWidthFor` and `opacityFor` both derive from `peekFraction`, so the geometry and
  the fade cannot disagree about what "the peek" is.

**Membership is decided by the host, not the children.** A section that collapses to
`SizedBox.shrink()` still occupies its slot in a horizontal list, so "how wide is a card" cannot be
answered by the child. The status is derived from the same provider state the sections read, which
makes it a second copy of a decision — pinned by a test against each section it mirrors.

**Width mode counts visible + resolving, not visible alone.** A card still in flight is assumed to
be arriving, so the common case (both resolve visible) opens as a carousel and never changes width.
Holding everything until every slot is terminal would delay the most visible element on the page and
still jump at the end.

No caption under either card in this slot: the covers are self-titled, so the line under them was
saying it twice.

### D286: The feed draws a rule between every pair of blocks, never inside one (PROD-3998, user-directed, 2026-08-27)

- **Choice:** The server-driven Discovery feed puts a dotted `Soko/Shade4` rule between **adjacent block instances** — every pair, no exceptions — with `kFeedPageBlockGap` (30) above and below it. It never draws one *inside* a block: the v0 `event_hero` block carries two cards, and those two cards sit 30 apart with no rule between them. The rule spans the **content column** (x15 → x415 on a 430 px phone), so it starts and stops exactly where block content does and never runs under the 15 px page margin.
- **Why this is a divergence:** the full-page comp `7304-23414` draws a rule at only **8 of 18** block boundaries, and there is no rule to derive from which 8 — three consecutive boundaries carry one, then `banner → bundle → bundle` carries none, then two adjacent titled sections carry none between them. That comp deliberately mixes blocks from the Eventos, Sítios and Pessoas feeds, so its sequence is illustrative; the boundaries it happens to mark are not a specification. Zé's call: rule between blocks, and a block is whatever the backend sent as one block, however many cards it draws.
- **What this makes the gap mean:** before this, the two hero cards in one block were spaced 12 and blocks were spaced 24, so "tighter" was doing the work of saying "these belong together". Now both are 30 (the frame's one number) and the **rule** is the only thing that says "different block" — which is what makes it safe for the backend to change how many items a block carries.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a rule between the two cards of one hero block is a **regression**, not a match to the comp. A rule missing between two adjacent blocks of the same type (two bundles, say) is also a regression — the comp shows none there, and the comp is wrong.
- **Where it lives:** `feed_block_divider.dart` (new, wraps `SokoDottedRule`), placed as `SliverList.separated`'s `separatorBuilder` in `discovery_feed_v2_screen.dart`. `FeedBlockSurface` gave up its own bottom padding so the rule sits centred between two blocks rather than 24 from one and 30 from the other. Measured spec: `docs/ui/figma-cache/screens/discovery/feed-v2-blocks.md` §6.

### D287: A `banner` is its own separator — no rule against it (PROD-3998, user-directed, 2026-08-28)

- **Choice:** amends [D286](#d286-the-feed-draws-a-rule-between-every-pair-of-blocks-never-inside-one-prod-3998-user-directed-2026-08-27). A block boundary that touches a `banner` keeps the frame's 30 px gap and **drops the dotted rule**. Every other boundary still gets one.
- **Why:** a banner is a full-bleed coloured slab on a paper page, so it already reads as a break in the feed. A rule laid against its edge separates nothing and doubles the division (Zé). It also matches the comp, which draws no rule either side of either of its two banners — one of the few places `7304-23414`'s divider placement turns out to be deliberate rather than incidental.
- **Where the decision lives:** `FeedBlockSeparator` (`feed_block_divider.dart`), the `SliverList.separated` separator. It has to be there rather than in a block widget because the question is about the **pair** — a block cannot see its neighbours, and "am I next to a banner" is not a property of either block alone.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a dotted rule directly above or below a coloured banner block is a **regression**. Two adjacent non-banner blocks with no rule between them is also a regression.
- **Adding a second self-separating block type** is one line — `FeedBlockSeparator.isOwnSeparator` is the only place that decides.

### D288: Login page redesign — flat two-column tagline, unified outline social buttons, shortened terms (PROD-4071, Figma 7501-26908, 2026-08-28)

- **Choice:** rebuilt the single-screen login to Figma node `7501-26908`. Four visible changes: (1) the shared `SokoBrandHeader` tagline flattened from a bold two-line main ("Menos scroll.\nMais vida local.") + long secondary phrase into two equal light-weight columns — "Menos scroll" (left) · "Mais vida local" (right), Zalando Sans Light 14, `sokoInk`, −0.14 tracking; (2) the Google **and** Apple CTAs unified to one outline style — `sokoPaper` background + `sokoInk`@0.1 hairline border + `sokoInk` text/glyph — dropping the old white-Google / dark-Apple split and its `isDark` branches; (3) social button labels re-toned per locale (EN "Enter with", pt "Entra com a", pt-BR "Entre com"); (4) the "OU" divider lowercased to "ou".
- **Two deliberate divergences from the Figma pixels:** the social buttons stay at **44 px** height (design-system tap target, matches the primary `SokoCtaButton` above them) rather than the comp's 40 px; and the "ou" divider keeps `sokoShade3` rather than the comp's near-invisible `sokoInk`@0.1, for legibility. `/compare-ui` should treat both as intended, not regressions.
- **⚠️ Compliance caveat (terms shortened):** the same pass cut the auth terms to "…Terms of Service **and** Privacy Policy" on all three auth screens (`login_screen.dart`, `login_form_screen.dart`, `email_register_screen.dart`), **removing the inline EULA link and the zero-tolerance sentence**. That inline text was the mandated PROD-2264 fix for the 2026-05-28 Apple **Guideline 1.2** rejection (see `docs/features/ugc-moderation.md` §3 and `docs/platform/apple-app-store-audit.md` §7, both now STALE-flagged). `POST /eula/accept` still fires on auth success so acceptance is recorded, but the on-screen text Apple asked for is gone. Shipped deliberately by the ticket owner accepting the re-rejection risk — **re-confirm the 1.2 posture before the next iOS submission.**
- **es-MX not updated:** `intl_es.arb` is portal-generated (per `CLAUDE.md`), so its tagline/button string *values* still show the old copy until a localization-portal re-export. The terms shortening is a widget change, so it applies to es-MX immediately.

### D289: Detail hero is a photo collage; a lone photo is the big tile, left-aligned (PROD-4074, user-directed, 2026-08-28; alignment flipped to left 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** the venue + event detail hero is now [`SokoPhotoCollage`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_photo_collage.dart) (Figma `6621:15621`), replacing the single 16:9 `VenueHeroPhoto`. Layout scales with the photo count: **1 → the "big tile" at its natural 4:5 (264×330), left-aligned in the content column with the brand colour to its right — matching the big tile's left placement in the 2/3+ collages**; 2 → big + one side tile; 3+ → big + two stacked (`+N` badge on the 4th+). Tapping a secondary tile promotes it to the main slot; tapping the main tile opens a swipeable fullscreen gallery. This **supersedes [D66](#d66-hero-photo--169-cover-crop-no-letterbox)** (16:9 cover) for the detail pages.
- **Why single-and-left-aligned:** the backend ships one `image_url` today (multi-photo is [PROD-1676](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-1676)), so in practice production always renders the one-photo case. Zé's calls, in order: don't stretch the lone photo full-width (too dominant), don't replicate one photo to fake a 3-up collage (reads as broken — three identical posters), show it once. The alignment evolved: first centred, then right-aligned, and finally (2026-09-01) **left-aligned** so the lone photo sits exactly where the big tile lives in the 2-/3-up collage — a single image and a 2/3-up collage now share the same left anchor, with the brand colour showing to the tile's right. The `images` field is parsed forward-compatibly (falls back to `[image_url]`), so the 2-/3-up collage lights up with no app release once the backend sends real distinct photos.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on single-image detail pages, a full-width or centred hero is a **regression** — it should be the left-aligned big-tile at 4:5 (brand colour to its right). A replicated-photo collage (identical tiles) is also a regression. **This includes the Daily Drop detail page** (see the 2026-08-30 parity note below).
- **Parity — Daily Drop detail page (2026-08-30, user-directed):** the Daily Drop's own detail page ([`daily_drop_detail_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/daily_drop/screens/daily_drop_detail_screen.dart), step 5) adopted the same `SokoPhotoCollage` block, replacing its `VenueHeroPhoto`. Its photo is single-image in practice (the drop's own poster wins on frame one, preserving the no-swap rule; only a drop with no image of its own borrows the fetched entity's `images[]`), so it renders as the left-aligned big tile (the alignment is the widget's own, so this page inherited the 2026-09-01 flip automatically). This made **`VenueHeroPhoto` dead code** — it now instantiates nowhere in `lib/` (kept as a file for now; only doc-comment pattern references remain).
- **Where it lives:** `soko_photo_collage.dart` (new, `lib/shared/widgets/`); models `VenueDetailResponse` / `EventDetailResponse2` gained `images` (`data/models/social_proof.dart`). OpenAPI gap: [`openapi-gaps.md`](../openapi-gaps.md#venuedetailout--eventdetailout--no-images-array-for-the-multi-photo-detail-hero).

### D290: The bundle's see-all affordance is the title plus a hairline chevron, and it appears only when there is something behind it (PROD-4068, 2026-08-28)

- **Choice:** the home-page `bundle` renders only its **highlighted** items (1–4, typically 3) and grows a see-all affordance — the **block title and a thin right chevron, both tap targets** — rendered **only when `total_count` exceeds the number of highlights** (D87 in the design doc).
- **No Figma exists for this.** The arrow is **the see-all page's own back-arrow glyph, mirrored** (`SokoForwardArrow`, `Transform.scale(scaleX: -1)` over `kSokoArrowGlyph`) — so the two arrows of a see-all round trip, the one that opens the page and the one that leaves it, are visibly the same object. One asset constant, so swapping the art cannot leave them disagreeing. Drawn at the chrome header's 22×17 and faded to 35 % ink: the affordance is a hint, not a control, and at full ink it competes with the title.
- **Pinned to the right edge, in a 40 px box** — the same width as the `CircleIconButton` each bundle row puts at that edge, so the arrow and the save buttons below it share one vertical axis. Asserted as geometry (centres coincide), not as padding.
- **The title takes the remaining width and ellipsizes into it**, so a long title cannot push the arrow off the edge. The whole row is the tap target: title, arrow, and the gap between them.
- **Superseded on 2026-08-28** (Zé): this first shipped as an inline hairline chevron inside the title's text run — `ThinChevron`, lifted out of `discovery_shelf.dart`. That widget stays, and the legacy shelves still use it: they embed it in a 42 px title's run, which is a different layout and a different weight.
- **The one-rule design is the point.** The client holds no knowledge of which bundle types have a real source yet: a bundle whose full set *is* its highlights has nothing behind the tap and shows no chevron, and the day its source starts returning more, the chevron appears **with no app release**.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a chevron on a bundle whose rows are all it has is a regression; a bundle showing more than 4 rows on the home page is a regression (it is rendering `items` instead of the highlights, and `items` is now the full set, up to 30).

### D291: The see-all page mirrors `ShelfSeeMoreScreen`'s chrome but lists rather than grids, and degrades to the feed when opened cold (PROD-4068, 2026-08-28)

- **Choice:** same *pattern* as the shelf see-more page — centred title, back arrow, no search bar, no filter chips — with a **vertical list** rather than that screen's 2-up grid, and a count line under the heading.
- **`FeedBundleRow` is reused unchanged, not reimplemented.** The rows a user tapped through from must be recognisably the same rows; a lookalike drifts the first time either surface changes. The row gap comes from `FeedBundleBlock.rowGap`, the same constant, for the same reason.
- **The count reads `total_count`, never `items.length`.** `items` is capped by the backend and the two differing is the entire point — a page showing 6 of 30 still says 30.
- **No fetch and no spinner** (D80): the block arrives with the feed and is handed to the page as a route `extra`.
- **⚠️ Known consequence, accepted:** this is also a *web* app, so a browser refresh or a pasted link has no in-memory block. The route **redirects to the feed** rather than rendering an empty page. `extra` cannot survive serialization, which is exactly what makes it the right guard — *"has extra"* means *"live in-app push this session"*. Same shape as `/drop/detail/:id`. Real deep-linking would be a backend endpoint, not a client change.

### D292: Feed cards and rows open their entity, routed by `item_type` (PROD-4076, 2026-08-28)

- **Choice:** the hero card body, the bundle row body and the see-all row body all open the entity's detail page. The action buttons (save, like, dislike) keep acting — they are children and win the gesture arena.
- **Routed by `item_type`, never hardcoded to `/events/`.** v0 emits `event` only, but the bundle declares its type on the wire (D34) and venues follow; a hardcoded path would keep compiling and start opening the *wrong page* the day a venue bundle ships. An unknown type opens nothing rather than guessing.
- **A blurred block opens nothing — and there is no check in the tap handler.** `FeedBlockSurface` already wraps a blurred block's subtree in an `IgnorePointer`, so the tap never arrives. A second guard would be the divergent client-side gating policy D10 forbids. This is asserted as behaviour in tests, so nobody "fixes" it twice.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a feed card that does nothing when tapped is a regression; a card that navigates when its bookmark is tapped is a regression.

### D293: The pinned header is a slotted design-system component, and the back arrow inside it is a bare glyph (PROD-4080, user-directed, 2026-08-28)

- **Choice:** the feed's `FeedPinnedHeader` became [`SokoPinnedHeader`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_pinned_header.dart) in `lib/shared/widgets/` — three independently-settable slots (`leading` / `centre` / `trailing`) plus [`SokoPinnedHeaderHost`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_pinned_header_host.dart) for the three placement modes. `SokoLocationLine` (was `FeedLocationLine`) moved with it. **Nothing outside the feed adopted it in this ticket** — deliberately: chat, the public profile and the library are being reworked by their page owners, and the point of the move is that they reuse this header rather than each growing a sixth one.
- **`null` is a real slot value, not a TODO.** An absent side still reserves `kSokoPinnedHeaderHeight` so the centre stays optically centred — the feed already needed this for the non-admins who get no memories button, and generalising it is what lets a page ship with an empty side. A page that hasn't chosen its buttons should leave the slot empty: an empty slot is cheap to fill, whereas a plausible-looking wrong button has to be *noticed* before it can be removed.
- **The back arrow is a bare glyph on the 40 px slot — not a filled circle** (Zé, 2026-08-28). Going back is navigation, not an action on this page, so it must not read as a peer of the filled `sokoInk`@6 % circles beside it; it keeps the same slot width so the row holds one rhythm. Implemented as `SokoBackButton(variant: bare, size: 40)` — a new `size` override on the design-system button rather than a fourth variant. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: a back arrow rendered on a filled circle inside this header is a regression.**
- **Both sides always reserve the same width, so the centre is at the *bar's* centre.** The obvious shape — a `Row` with `Expanded(Center(...))` between the slots — centres the middle in whatever the sides left over, so unequal sides slide the title by half the difference (measured: 20 px for one extra button, silently). Every ready-made slot happens to be 40 wide, so nothing in the app tripped it; the first page owner to put two buttons on one side would have. The bar is laid out by a `MultiChildLayoutDelegate` reserving `max(leading, trailing, 40)` on both sides, with the centre constrained to what is left so a long title ellipsises instead of running under a button. **A page can put anything in either slot — one button, two, a back arrow, nothing — and the centre does not move.**
- **The centre has one voice.** `SokoHeaderSlot.title` uses the same `Mobile/B1 Reg` as `SokoHeaderSlot.location`, so swapping a title in for a location does not change the bar's weight — two pages using the component should not look like two components.
- **`static` is `fixed`.** The design doc (`heyl-controller/.../2026-08-24-server-driven-discovery-feed.md`, D119) names the modes `pinnedOverlay` / `static` / `scrollAway`. `static` is a Dart reserved word and cannot be an enum value, so the code says `fixed`.
- **`scrollAway` asks the caller to reserve its band.** A box-world overlay cannot hand its space back to a child whose viewport is fixed, so the spacer goes inside the scroll content — the same split `PinnedPageChrome` and `ShellSliverScope.chromeReserved` already use. Callers already building slivers should skip the host and put `SliverToBoxAdapter(child: SokoPinnedHeader(...))` first instead.
- **Boundary with [`PinnedPageChrome`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/widgets/pinned_page_chrome.dart)** — the app's *other* pinned header, and the reason to read this bullet before adding a third. That one is **route-resolved**: the shell decides from the route what chrome to draw, pages cannot mount it, and it renders a 32 px SeasonMix list title with its own height reservation, serving `/lists/*`, `/venues/*`, `/events/*`, daily-drop and create-zine. `SokoPinnedHeader` is **page-mounted** and renders the 40 px `Mobile/B1 Reg` bar. **If you are adding chrome to a page, you want `SokoPinnedHeader`.** Re-expressing `PinnedPageChrome`'s row through it is a sensible follow-up and was explicitly not done here.
  - **Update (PROD-4086, Zé, 2026-08-31): the two pinned headers are NOT converging. This boundary is permanent, not a staging post.** They are not the same bar and were never a duplicate of each other: 40 px flat with a single-line `Mobile/B1 Reg` centre, versus 60/81 px with a 2-line 32 px SeasonMix `AutoSizeText` title that becomes an editable `TextField` in PROD-1783 edit mode, bottom-anchored with its own gap and its own route-derived background. Composing one from the other would mean generalising `SokoPinnedHeader` with height, vertical alignment, bottom gap and per-side widths — turning a deliberately opinionated Figma bar (`7304-24495`) into a generic toolbar, precisely as chat, the public profile and the library are about to adopt its opinionated form. The reverse (adopting the 40 px bar on the detail routes) is a visible design change nobody has asked for. **So the rule stays the one above: route-resolved shell chrome is `PinnedPageChrome`, page-mounted chrome is `SokoPinnedHeader`, and "there are two pinned headers" is the intended state.** Do not file this as tech debt again.
  - **What they *do* share is the back arrow.** `PinnedPageChrome` hand-rolled a `Clickable > SizedBox(48) > Center > SvgPicture(back-arrow.svg, 22×17, sokoInk)` that was byte-for-byte `SokoBackButton(variant: bare)` at its default size. It now calls the design-system button, passing `onTap` so its richer `popOrFallback(parentListId:)` fallback survives. Geometry and art unchanged; one arrow, one tap policy, behind both headers. That is the whole of the convergence that was worth doing.
- **`FeedAltPinnedHeader` stays hand-rolled.** Its D40/D41 layout is a pink filter pill + "in" + a *left-aligned expanding* location line — not a centred three-slot bar. Forcing it through the slot API would distort the API for an admin-only comparison treatment.
  - **Superseded (PROD-4299, Zé, 2026-09-09): it is no longer hand-rolled, and it is no longer the alternative — it is the feed's ONLY pinned header.** The three-slot `FeedPinnedHeader` and the `feedAltHeaderEnabledProvider` toggle are both deleted. The pill is now a real `SokoTagChip` (label *and* icon from `kFeedFilterEntries`, so the header's button and the row's selected chip are one component); the memories button is `SokoHeaderSlot.memories`, which fixes a wrong glyph — it had been shipping `LucideIcons.brain` where the design system draws the Figma `Icon/Brain` SVG. The reasoning above still holds for the *shape*: it is a sentence, not a centred three-slot bar, so it does not go through `SokoPinnedHeader`. What changed is that the bar's two side slots now live in `FeedLocationBand`'s own delegate — see D305's amendment below.

### D294: A saved item's note follows the viewer's role — owner edits everywhere, non-owner reads it once (PROD-4072, 2026-08-28)

- **Choice:** the note (`tip`) on a saved item renders per the **viewer's relationship to the list**, consistently across the zine and the event/venue detail page:
  | Surface | Owner | Non-owner (incl. guest) |
  |---|---|---|
  | Detail page note | existing note **+ edit pencil**; empty → "Leave a note" pill | note **read-only** (no pencil, no pill); no note → nothing |
  | Zine in-card note | shown | shown read-only |
  | Zine note **below the pager** | shown / editable | **removed** |
- **Non-owners see the note read-only on the detail page.** Previously the whole "Deixar nota" section was owner-gated (`canLeaveNote = isOwner`), so a non-owner viewing a shared list saw nothing there. A curator's note is content worth showing to a visitor — the shared [`ListItemNote`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_item_note.dart) already renders all four states from `canEdit` + `tip`; the fix was to feed it `canEdit: isOwner` and mount it for non-owners too.
- **The below-pager note is dropped for non-owners, not just made read-only.** The tip already renders read-only **inside the collage card** (`list_zine_item_page.dart`), so the second callout below the pagination was a duplicate for a viewer who can't edit. Owners keep it (it's their editing affordance). **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: a "Leave a note" pill or an edit pencil on a list you don't own is a regression; a read-only note under someone else's pager is also a regression (it belongs in the card, not below the pager).**
- **Why gate on live ownership, not a frozen snapshot:** the detail note reads tip/element-id/ownership live from `unifiedListProvider` in `EventInlineActions`/`VenueInlineActions` — see [[async-provider-watch-after-await-freezes-against-partial-dependency]] for the owner-visible bug that motivated this (a frozen snapshot showed the owner "Leave a note" over an existing note).

### D296: Zine item card matches Figma — 66 px title band with a pinned dashed rule, half-width 4:5 poster, city over street address (PROD-4072, user-directed, 2026-08-31)

- **Title band is a fixed height, not shrink-wrapped.** The title is `SeasonMix` **66 px** (was 56) sitting in a **fixed-height band** (`contentWidth * 127/370`, clamped 80–140) with the solid rule pinned at the top and the **dashed rule pinned at the bottom** — so a short title leaves the Figma gap above the dashed rule instead of the rule hugging the title. `AutoSizeText` inside an `Expanded` shrinks long titles within the band (see [[fixed-height-container-autosizetext-pattern]]). Implemented in [`list_zine_item_page.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_item_page.dart).
- **The bottom row is two equal halves.** Left details column + a **half-width 4:5 poster** (`(contentWidth-10)/2`, so ~180×225 on a 400 px card), driven off a `LayoutBuilder` — not the old hard-coded 147×184. Everything is proportioned off the card's inner width so it holds at every screen size.
- **The pin row shows the location (city), never the full street address.** Events show the venue name, falling back to `item.city`; venues show `item.city`. Neighbourhood is not on the list-item payload (backend gap — only `city`/`address` ship), so city is the broadest area available today.
- **The note is attributed with its author's avatar.** The curator note (`item.tip`) is prefixed with a [`PersonDot`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/person_dot.dart) (the "liked by" bolinha) fed from `added_by` — the author's photo when present, else their seeded initial — instead of a generic person glyph. `added_by.avatar_url` is often null today (backend gap), so it degrades to the seeded initial and lights up when the avatar ships.
- **B2 line-height is 1.0**, matching the Soko `Mobile/B2 Reg` token (was 1.2 in `AppTheme.mobileB2Reg`).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a title that hugs its dashed rule (no gap under a short title), a poster narrower than half the card, a **street address** in the pin row, or a generic person glyph on the note are all regressions.

### D297: Event/venue subcategory shows as its own detail row, fed by a new backend `subcategory` field (PROD-4072, user-directed, 2026-08-31)

- **Choice:** the zine detail rows show the second-level type beneath the eyebrow category — e.g. eyebrow `CULTURA · EVENTO`, detail row **"Festival"** — as a tag-icon `_DetailRow` populated from a new `subcategory` field on the list-item `event`/`venue` payload. Omitted when absent, so it degrades cleanly before the backend ships.
- **The field is new backend work (cross-repo).** The saved-item payload only exposed the top-level `category`; the two-level taxonomy (`Event.sub_categories`, `TAXONOMY` parent→child) existed but wasn't serialized. Backend branch `rafael/event-venue-subcategory-labels` adds a full `SUB_CATEGORY_LABELS` map (100 slugs × en/pt-PT/pt-BR/es-MX), a `format_event_subcategory_label` formatter, `select_event_subcategory_label` (event `sub_categories[0]`) / `select_venue_subcategory_label` (venue `types[1]`), and emits `subcategory` from `_serialize_event` / `_serialize_venue`. Documented on the event/venue objects in [`open-api/heyl-webapp-v1.openapi.yaml`](../../open-api/heyl-webapp-v1.openapi.yaml).
- **Labels are the child concept alone** ("Festival", not "Culture · Festival") — the parent is already in the eyebrow, so the row must not repeat it.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the row is only expected once both repos deploy; its absence on a stale build is not a regression.

### D298: Chat like/dislike pops on tap, cards reveal one at a time, reopened chats don't re-type (user-directed, 2026-08-31)

- **Like/dislike in chat & onboarding cards use the detail row's tap pop.** The shared `SokoEntityCard._ThumbButton` previously only swapped its fill on hover; it now wraps the tap in [`PressPop`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/press_pop.dart) — the same press-shrink + release overshoot bounce the event/venue detail action row uses — so a tap feels physical instead of waiting on the network to fill the colour. One change covers both chat (`place_cards_row.dart`) and onboarding vibe cards.
- **The card carousel reveals one card at a time.** [`SokoTurnEntrance`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_turn_entrance.dart) gained a `startDelay`; `PlaceCardsRow` staggers each card by `index × 90ms` on first show, replacing the single-unit row fade. Cards hold their box at opacity 0 during their delay, so the row doesn't reflow. Runs once (guarded by `animateCardsReveal`), including on reopen.
- **Reopening an existing conversation renders whole — text does NOT type, cards DO animate.** The newest reply used to typewrite for a frame on reopen (the once-only guard was seeded asynchronously; a prefetched conversation painted un-seeded first). Now the history is seeded synchronously before the first bubble builds. Cards still play their (staggered) reveal on reopen — the user wanted motion on the cards, stillness on the text. See [[chat-listing-reveal-after-text-not-a-spinner]].
- **The chat place-card dismiss (X) shows the web pointer cursor** — swapped its bare `GestureDetector` for the canonical [`Clickable`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/clickable.dart).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a chat/onboarding like that doesn't pop on tap, a whole-row card fade (no per-card stagger), a reopened conversation that types its last reply, or an X with the default arrow cursor on web are all regressions.

### D299: Every like / dislike / save pops on tap with the one shared curve (user-directed, 2026-08-31)

- **One pop, everywhere.** Every user-facing like, dislike, and save/bookmark affordance plays the same tactile bounce — press-shrink (0.92) then release overshoot (1.0→1.18→1.0) — via the shared [`PressPop`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/press_pop.dart) / `popScale` curve. Covers: detail action rows (venue/event/daily-drop — like/dislike already popped, D295-era; **save** added here), feed hero + bundle (save/like/dislike, via `CircleIconButton.popOnTap`), chat + onboarding cards (D298), zine bookmark, search add/remove, list-view bookmark + add chips, list calendar-row bookmark, and the onboarding zine quick-save.
- **`PressPop` is the canonical tappable for these controls.** It now also sets the web pointer cursor (`MouseRegion`), so it's a full drop-in for [`Clickable`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/clickable.dart) — use `PressPop` (not a bare `GestureDetector`/`InkWell`/`Clickable`) for any new like/dislike/save glyph button. `CircleIconButton` keeps its own press/hover/bg but opts into the same release overshoot via `popOnTap`.
- **Passive/disabled states don't pop.** A save chip with no action, an already-saved "add" chip, or a control mid-save renders settled (no pop, no pointer) — the pop signals an actionable tap, not decoration.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a like/dislike/save that doesn't pop on tap (bare ripple or no feedback) is a regression; neutral nav/close buttons (e.g. the feed alt header) intentionally stay press-only.

### D300: A selected feed toggle is an OPAQUE paper circle with a filled ink glyph (user-directed, 2026-08-31)

- **The hero card's three overlay toggles (save · like · dislike) now show selection.** At rest each is the frame's translucent `Soko/Paper 30` circle with a paper **outline** glyph, so it reads over any photo. Selected, the circle goes **fully opaque `Soko/Paper`** and the glyph becomes the solid **`Soko/Ink`** fill. Hovering previews the filled glyph without committing the circle, matching the chat/onboarding thumbs (D298). Previously only like/dislike changed at all, and the save showed `LucideIcons.bookmark_check` — a checkmark reads "done", where the affordance means "kept".
- **Bundle rows get the filled bookmark, but keep their resting circle.** They sit on paper, not over a photo, so the ink-8 ground stays in both states and the fill alone carries the signal. The opaque-circle treatment is specific to the photo overlay, where a translucent circle has nothing stable to read against.
- **The glyph is shared; the circle is not.** Every like/dislike/save glyph in the app now comes from [`SokoToggleGlyph`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_toggle_glyph.dart) (`.thumbUp()` / `.bookmarkChip()` / `.bookmarkAction()`) — no call site spells an asset path. The *button* around it stays per-surface: a 30 px squared two-up half in chat/onboarding, a 40 px round `CircleIconButton` on the feed, a labelled action on detail. `CircleIconButton.glyphBuilder(hovered)` is what lets the feed host the shared glyph in its own chrome. See [`lucide-ships-no-filled-icon-variants.md`](../learnings/lucide-ships-no-filled-icon-variants.md).
- **Dislike is the 👍 glyph rotated 180°**, never a second asset — pinned by a test, because that is the property the shared glyph buys.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on the feed hero, a selected save/like/dislike whose circle is still translucent, or whose glyph is still an outline, is a regression — as is a saved bundle row showing an outline bookmark, or any of them rendering a Lucide icon instead of the Soko glyph.

### D301: The pinned header's paper block is a component, not a per-page convention (PROD-4101, 2026-08-31)

- **Choice:** the block a pinned header sits in — `SafeArea(top) → 15 → bar → [30 → row] → 15` on full-bleed paper — is [`SokoPinnedHeaderBlock`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_pinned_header_block.dart) in `lib/shared/widgets/`. [D293](#d293-the-pinned-header-is-a-slotted-design-system-component-and-the-back-arrow-inside-it-is-a-bare-glyph-prod-4080-user-directed-2026-08-28) made the **bar** a component; this makes the block one. A page picks slots and passes a `below:` row if it has one; it composes nothing.
- **Why, concretely:** the rhythm lived only inside the Discovery feed's own page. PROD-4081 built its header from this component's documentation — the "Adopting it" snippet plus the `fixed` safe-area note — followed both exactly, and landed **30 px shorter than the feed** (84 px against 114 at a 44 px notch; bar `dy` 44 vs 59). **The first independent adopter, following the docs, got it wrong on the first try.** The docs were correct about their subject (the `SafeArea` ordering) and silent about the margins, which is the failure this entry exists to prevent repeating.
- **The PROD-4085 ordering rule moves into the block.** `SafeArea` goes **inside** the `ColoredBox`, never around it: a pinned block overlays a scrollable, so the paper must reach y=0 while the content clears the notch. Around it, the paper starts below the notch and page content is visible sliding through the status bar. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: a transparent band above a pinned bar on a notched device is a regression.**
- **The block does no horizontal work.** `FeedPageContent` (the page margin + desktop column) stays at the call site. A header must not make a page-wide margin decision on the page's behalf — the same principle that stops it taking the left/right insets. The known 15-vs-16 split between the feed and Discovery is **deliberately untouched** here; it wants its own audit, not a smuggled fix.
- **`headerHeight` is required for `scrollAway` only**, and is no longer defaulted. 40 was right for a bare bar and silently wrong for every block; a `scrollAway` page would reserve 40 for ~114 and open its content under the bar. `fixed` provably never reads it, so requiring it there would buy the symmetry of one sentence and cost a mandatory wrong number. Compute it with `myBlock.heightFor(context)` — an **instance** method, so the block's own gap and margin cannot disagree with the number it reports — and **never a literal**: it carries `MediaQuery.padding.top`, so `114` is right on exactly one device.
- **One constructor per placement, and no `placement` parameter.** `SokoPinnedHeaderHost` has no generative constructor: it is [`.fixed(header:, child:)`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_pinned_header_host.dart) — which takes nothing else, having silently ignored a controller, `revealAfter` and `onVisibilityChanged` before — [`.pinnedOverlay(controller:, revealAfter:, onVisibilityChanged:, …)`], or [`.scrollAway(controller:, headerHeight:, …)`] with a **non-nullable** height. A `placement` argument meant `scrollAway` could be constructed without the height its `build` dereferences, caught only by an assert that release strips. Three build-time asserts and the `reservedHeight` getter went with it — that getter restated `headerHeight`, which a `scrollAway` caller necessarily already holds, and had no production caller while still crashing in release if read on the other two modes.
- **`heightFor` is asserted against the block's *measured* height**, at two insets × with/without `below`. A helper that computes what a widget builds is right the day it lands with no mechanism to stay right — the same description-is-not-the-thing shape as the three failures above. That parity group is the mechanism.

### D302: The zine page-turn overleaf is plain Soko paper with no fold outline (user-directed, 2026-08-31)

- **The back of the page being turned in the list/zine reader is now a clean sheet of `Soko/Paper` with no border.** This covers all three surfaces that render the turn: the live `TurnPageView` flip (`lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart` — `overleafColorBuilder: (_) => AppColors.sokoPaper`, transparent border, width 0), the looping corner-peel **tease** on cover + item cards, and the Discovery shelf list-cover peels (`ZineCoverPeelOverlay`). Previously the overleaf was tinted with the **next item's category colour** (`sokoEvent` green for events, `sokoVenue` blue for venues; shelf peels used `sokoShade5`) and stroked with a `Soko/Ink` 0.5 px fold border — a "coloured hint of what you're about to flip to." The user asked for the switching page to read as plain paper instead.
- **The page *underneath* the peel is unchanged.** The lifted-away triangle still shows the real next-item underlay on the cover (D197) or the `sokoShade5` clip-fill on item/shelf peels — only the *overleaf* (back of the lifting page) went to paper. Does not touch D196 (peel cadence) or D197 (first-cycle drama + underlay).
- **The colour plumbing is gone, not just re-pointed.** Because every tease call site funnels through `_TeasePeelPainter`, the paper fill + no-outline is now the painter's fixed behaviour. The vestigial `overleafColor` param (`ListZineTeasePeel`), the `teaseOverleafColor` fields on both cards, and the `_nextOverleafColorFor` helper in `list_zine_view.dart` were all removed. Separate from D198's reverted `_listFlipPage` route overleaf (`sokoShade5` + ink border, now `CupertinoPage` everywhere).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a category-coloured (green/blue) or ink-bordered overleaf on any list page-turn or corner-peel is now a regression — the overleaf is always plain Soko paper, borderless.

### D303: The in-app Business Connect portal renders in the app's own chrome, not the marketing landing's (PROD-4040, user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** the authed Business Home dashboard (`/business`, [`BusinessHomeScreen`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/business_home/screens/business_home_screen.dart)) uses the **app-native shell** — a standard back-arrow + centred "Business" app bar over the discovery bottom-nav — **not** the wireframe's marketing-portal chrome (dotted rules, centred Soko wordmark, language menu, "Get app", avatar dropdown). The wireframe (`soko-business-wireframes` frames 2c/2d) drew the same chrome as the public landing (2a/2b); we deliberately diverged. Mockup of the three options considered (A portal-faithful / **B app-native** / C hybrid) was rendered for the decision.
- **Why:** this ticket is the *in-app* authed flow, reached inside the Flutter app (behind the `business-ownership` flag, direct entry / deep link). The marketing chrome — wordmark bar, `Get app`, FAQs, language switcher — belongs to the **`soko-website` Astro landing** (Business Connect Phase 4, a different repo), and re-implementing it inside Flutter would duplicate that surface and read as foreign next to every other app screen on mobile. App-native reuses the existing `PageContent` + app-bar pattern (same as `BusinessConnectionsScreen`), ships fastest, and stays consistent.
- **The three numbered sections keep the wireframe's serif "N · Title" dividers.** The section content (phone-verify, Instagram, venue search→claim, owned/pending cards) matches the wireframe faithfully; only the outer frame changed. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: the marketing top bar (wordmark / Get-app / language menu) on the in-app `/business` screen is a regression — that chrome lives on the soko-website landing, not in the app.**

### D304: The filter row is one horizontally-scrolling Tag row across the feed, "Descobre a cidade" and Procura (PROD-4081, user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** the content-type selector on all three surfaces is [`SokoTagBar`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_tag_chip.dart) — content-sized `SokoTagChip`s in a horizontal `ListView` (the `standard` motion treatment; [D328](#d328-home-and-library-filters-share-the-complete-tag-design-system-user-directed-2026-09-03) later added a measured `reorder` one for Library), 30 tall, 6 apart, radius 6, 10 px padding, a 14 px icon 6 from the label, Zalando Light 14 (Figma `7598-24582`). The feed's row leads with a 30×30 **circular** icon-only chip for `Procura`, which navigates rather than filters.
- **Why, concretely — the layout is inverted.** The feed's row was five `Expanded` columns: equal fifths, each label `maxLines: 1, overflow: ellipsis`. Under pressure the *content* gave way, and "Pessoas" clipped on a narrow phone. Now the chips size to their content and the **row** gives way, by scrolling. `SokoTagChip` carries no `maxLines`/`overflow` at all, deliberately — the chip is sized by its text, so an ellipsis there would be dead code that re-enables truncation the day someone constrains it.
- **It is the same Figma component the Map's shortcut row already implements**, at identical metrics — `MapShortcutChip` is "Tag" (`7102-20592`) too. The two differ only in skin: the map paints a solid palette colour with no border and drops the Tag's icon; this one is `Soko/Paper` with a 1 px `Soko/Shade45` hairline and keeps it. Consolidating them is possible and deliberately **not** done: the map expresses selection through `color` and has no `selected` flag, so merging would change its model.
- **Selected is a `Soko/Pink` fill** (Zé, 2026-08-31). The Figma frame ships only the unselected variant, so the state was chosen rather than read: pink is the selection language the Discovery pills already used, so nothing is lost in the move. The selected chip drops its hairline — sampled off `7501-23715`, its border row is the same pink as its interior.
- **The row runs edge to edge; the margin lives INSIDE the scrollable.** It is the one deliberate exception to the feed's page margin. Padding a horizontal scroller from the outside clips its chips at the margin, which reads as the row being cut off; padding *within* it means content still begins on the margin at rest and scrolls to both viewport edges. On the feed the row is mounted in `PageContent` (desktop column cap, no horizontal margin) rather than `FeedPageContent`; the two search surfaces cannot be mounted outside their page's padding, so they grow back out of it via `escapeHorizontalPadding`. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: a filter row whose first chip sits at the viewport edge, or whose chips vanish at the margin, is a regression.**
- **Enablement is per surface, and the asymmetry is principled** (D131, Controller). `Pessoas` is live on the two search surfaces and greyed on the feed, in the same row: a search **category** needs a query endpoint, and people search has one; a feed **filter** needs a lead **block type**, and `/feed/home?filter=people` has none — which is what D15 defers. It is not a gap a ticket closes, and enabling the feed tile would ship a filter that returns nothing.
- **`SearchCategoryTabs` is NOT deleted.** The `/lists` hub and the profile's Saved tab keep the pills — they carry an "All" option this row has no concept of, and restyling them was not asked for. `SearchOverlay.categoryRowStyle` picks between the two.
- **`Leitores` is retired as a name.** Discovery was the only surface that ever rendered the category, so moving it onto this row makes **"Pessoas"** its single name rather than creating a second one.
- **A rule separates the ritual cards from the row** ([`FeedChromeRule`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_block_divider.dart), Figma `7598-24580`): solid `Soko/Ink`, 1 px, on the page margin — distinct from `FeedBlockDivider`, which is a dotted `Soko/Shade4` rule between two *content* blocks. It collapses with the cards, via the provider they both read: with an empty ritual slot it would land directly under the scallop, which is already a divider.

### D306: An empty near-you grid is not drawn, and the client is what drops it (PROD-4108, user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** when the Sítios feed's *Perto de ti* grid arrives with **zero items**, the whole block is removed before the list builder — [`renderableFeedBlocks`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_block_dispatcher.dart), the same function that already drops an unrenderable block type. Zé, 2026-09-01: *"if there are no results on the near you grid, we want to not display that block."*
- **Why it is the client's job and not the backend's.** The backend **cannot** omit it. The block is placed during composition, *before* the per-caller near-you query runs, and the cursor's layout digest is computed over that composition — dropping it server-side after the page was sliced would shorten a page the digest already covered, and a scroll would silently skip a block. So the backend ships it at zero items by design, and the removal has to happen here.
- **What the alternative looked like.** The backend's own guidance was to render it as *title + CTA, no tiles*. That is a **client-side empty state for a block**, which the contract explicitly rules out — `GET /feed/home`'s description says *"An empty block is omitted entirely — the feed just gets shorter. There are no client-side empty states for blocks."* A "Perto de ti" heading over a live chevron with nothing under it reads as a **loading failure**, not as a sparse city. The counter-argument (the CTA is still useful, since `/shelves/near-you` runs *unsuppressed* and may have venues) is real and was recorded, not taken: those venues stay reachable from the legacy shelf's own see-more tile.
- **⚠️ It must stay a filter, not an empty widget.** A `SizedBox.shrink()` returned from the builder still occupies a list index and shifts every key after it. And it costs paging nothing: rendering and paging read different lists — the ledger dedupes over the **parsed** blocks (`feed_home_provider.dart`), so an unrendered block still advances the feed. That is already what stops an unknown block type reading as "nothing new" and truncating the feed.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: a bare "Perto de ti" heading with no tiles under it is a regression, not an empty state.**

### D307: The venue grid tile is a NEW card — "reuse the near you" meant the data path, not `NearYouCard` (PROD-4108, user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** the *Perto de ti* grid cell is [`FeedVenueGridTile`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/blocks/feed_venue_grid_tile.dart), built from the **feed's own vocabulary** — `FeedMetaLine` at the bundle row's metrics, and the hero card's overlay-toggle shape at 30 px. It is **not** `NearYouCard`, which PROD-4108 explicitly told us to reuse.
- **Why — it is a different card, measured.** Against the live frame (`7675-38682`) the two differ on five counts: cover **4:5** vs the shelf card's 105×140 (**3:4**); a **30 px blurred save chip** on the cover, which the shelf card has none of; the name in **Light** (`Mobile/B1 Reg`) vs Medium; meta lines carrying **`map_pin` / `move_right` icons** vs plain text; and both meta lines in `Soko/Ink` vs the shelf card's muted-rose `#A88D92` category. Reusing it would have put the legacy shelf's look inside the new feed.
- **What "reuse" actually meant** (Zé, 2026-09-01): the **data path and the destination page**. The backend sources the grid from the *same query* `/shelves/near-you` uses, and "ver mais" opens that existing page **unchanged** — which is what makes the destination coherent by construction rather than by coincidence (D137). Both halves are delivered server-side; nothing about the card was ever the reusable part.
- **⚠️ The Figma node cited in PROD-4106, PROD-4107, PROD-4108 and design-doc D132/D137 does not exist.** `7598-25362` returns *"The provided node ID was not found in the file."* The live frame is **`7675-38682`**, cached at [`figma-cache/screens/discovery/feed-v2-venue-grid.md`](figma-cache/screens/discovery/feed-v2-venue-grid.md). Read the cache, not the tickets.
- **The venue BUNDLE row is deliberately provisional** — `FeedBundleRow`'s existing 80 px geometry with the meta line swapped to `neighbourhood · type`. **⚠️ The `neighbourhood · type` half of this is SUPERSEDED by D342 (2026-09-08)**: the two facts are now two meta lines, type first. The rest of this bullet still holds. There is no frame for it and none was wanted: Zé, *"we just need to build the first simple version. the final version is out of scope for this ticket. we will focus on changing all bundles in the future."* **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: do not file the venue bundle row against a design — it has none yet.**

### D305: The feed's header is a THREE-state progression, and the location is the thread through it (user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** scrolling the new home feed now passes through three states, not two. (1) The top header, in flow, as Figma draws it. (2) The **location band** — "Estás em ◎ X" plus the scallop — which stops instead of leaving, pinning to the top edge the moment it gets there. (3) The pinned bar. The location is on screen throughout and **slides** from hard right to the bar's centre; it is never re-created.
- **Why:** the bar only appeared once ALL the in-flow chrome had gone — top header, ritual cards, rule and inline filter row, ~300 px of scrolling. For that whole window the feed slid visibly through the status bar and the location was off screen. Reported from device (Zé, 2026-08-31/09-01).
- **The band pins where the in-flow copy arrives**, so the hand-over is invisible: the same [`FeedLocationBand`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_location_band.dart) at the same argument, landing exactly on top of itself. The trigger is the top header's **measured** trailing edge minus the band's height — a scroll extent read during layout, never a painted position, since a culled sliver reports y=0 forever.
- **An opaque strip over the status-bar inset, at every offset** ([`kFeedNotchBandKey`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/screens/discovery_feed_v2_screen.dart)), in the shell's own page colour so there is nothing to see at rest. **The wordmark is meant to slide under it and disappear with the scroll** — without the strip it was still visible inside the notch when the band pinned, and the block's paper cut it off in a single frame. Clearance is `kFeedPageBlockGap - kSokoPageMargin` = 15 px of scroll, and the notch cancels out of that, so it is the same on every device. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: any page content visible inside the status-bar strip at any scroll offset is a regression** — except the wordmark, before the band pins.
- **The bar arrives when it COVERS the inline filter row, not when that row leaves the viewport.** The invariant was always "never two filter bars on screen" (D31/D32), and a row behind opaque full-bleed paper is not on screen. Waiting for departure held the bar back by a whole block plus the 30 px gap. The threshold asks the block it builds for its own `heightFor`, so it cannot drift from what it is measuring; measured against the **bar's** block rather than the band's, because the rule is about what the bar hides when it arrives.
- **One block in two states, not two blocks that swap.** `FeedLocationBand` takes a `t`: 0 is the band, 1 is the bar, and between them the prefix and scallop give way to the two circular buttons while a **single** `SokoLocationLine` slides. Cross-fading two headers would put two locations on screen mid-transition; the hard swap it replaces put none there for a frame. `TweenAnimationBuilder`, 240 ms, `easeInOut`. See [`an-element-in-two-header-states-must-be-one-widget.md`](../learnings/an-element-in-two-header-states-must-be-one-widget.md).
- **The top header's location adopts the pinned bar's type** — icon 22 → 14, `Mobile/B1 Reg` for both the label and the "Estás em" beside it. Authorised by Zé as part of this change, and it is what keeps the slide a pure change of position rather than a lerp of the icon and the typeface as well. It narrows, but does not close, the divergence recorded in [`figma-cache/screens/discovery/feed-v2-headers.md`](figma-cache/screens/discovery/feed-v2-headers.md) §1: the frame's token is still `Mobile/H3` 26.
- ~~**The alternative admin treatment (D40/D42) keeps the swap it always had.** Its location sits after an "in" at 14 px, so there is no shared position to slide to; it gets a zero-length duration rather than a second animation nobody asked for.~~
  - **Reversed (PROD-4299, Zé, 2026-09-09).** That treatment is now the only pinned header, and its location was the reason it was excluded — so the exclusion was removed by removing its cause. The location takes `Mobile/B1 Reg` (18), identical to the band's, which makes the transition a pure change of position again; the "in" takes 18 with it, so the phrase is one voice while the chip keeps the Tag system's 14. `barChromeHasLocation` and the `Duration.zero` branch are both gone, and **there is no longer a second layout to be right about**: the bar's leading (`chip + "in"`) and trailing (memories) slots are laid out by `_BandToBarLayout` itself, so `SokoPinnedHeader._SlotLayout` and the band's delegate can no longer disagree about where the location sits. The delegate MEASURES the leading group rather than assuming a width — it has to, because the width changes with the chip's label, with the locale's `in`/`em`/`en`, and frame-by-frame during the reflow below.
- **The location SLIDES when the filter changes, not just when the bar arrives** (Zé, 2026-09-09) — `[Eventos] in X` → `[Sítios] in X` reflows over the Tag system's own 600 ms `easeOutCubic`, "just like the filters bar". ⚠️ **This does not come free with the chip**, which is the trap: `SokoTagChip` does not animate its own width (`_TagSurface` shrink-wraps and `Material` animates only colour and shape), and the filter bar's sliding lives in `_SokoTagAnimatedRow`, which never fires on a label change because those five chips never change label. An `AnimatedSize` in `FeedPinnedHeaderLeading` supplies it; everything downstream follows through ordinary layout. It is **omitted entirely under reduce motion rather than given `Duration.zero`** — `RenderAnimatedSize` re-dirties itself inside its own `performLayout` at zero and throws, unlike the implicit animations every other honouring site in the Tag system uses.
- **The delegate interpolates the location's BOX, not its width and left edge separately.** Lerping the two independently puts `right` at `lerp(a) + lerp(b)`, bounded by neither end — and with a long place name (the bar's room is narrower than the band's while the left edge travels right) the location overflowed the content column by 12 px at `t = 0.5`. Measured, not hypothetical. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: the location extending past the page margin at any point in the transition is a regression.**
- **Rejected:** centring the band's location so it would not have to move at all — perfect continuity, but it leaves a large empty gap down the right of the top header at rest, which is the most-seen state of the page.

### D306: Zine footer combines follow-proof + leave-note into one row above a pink pagination line (Figma 7660:27940, user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** below a zine card the follow social-proof (`_FollowedByLine`) and the item's leave-note affordance (`ListItemNote`) render as **one justified row** (`_FollowNoteRow` in [`list_zine_view.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_view.dart)) — proof left, note right — placed **above** the pagination line. Follow-proof shows on **every page** (cover + item pages), not cover-only. The pagination fill ([`zine_progress_line.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/zine_progress_line.dart)) is **Soko/Pink** `#FFB8CB`. The follow label is the compact **"{name} +N seguem"** (`listFollowersInline`): one name, an "+N" carrying the overflow count, 20-px avatars, 4-px overlap, no leading glyph, no avatar "+" badge.
- **Why:** matches the Figma frame, which draws the two clusters on one line above the line and a pink progress fill. Previously the progress line sat directly under the card with the follow/note as separate stacked blocks below it, and the follow-proof was cover-only.
- **Deliberate divergences from Figma (kept):**
  - **Cumulative fill, not single-active-segment.** Figma's static mockup highlights only the current segment; the code fills every segment up to the current page. Cumulative progress is a documented feature (PROD-1955 / D124) and a one-frame mockup can't disambiguate the two — color was the unambiguous signal, so only color changed.
  - **Visible inactive track.** Figma's isolated export shows a paper-on-paper (invisible) track; the code keeps the faint `sokoInk @ 12%` hairline so the track is visible over paper in-app.
- **Degradation:** when both proof and note are present the row splits (`Expanded` proof / `Flexible` note) so the note's expanded editor/callout can never overflow; proof-only left-aligns, note-only fills the width.
- **Shared-widget scope:** `MutualAvatarsRow` gained a non-breaking `overlap` param (default 6) — profile/locals/feed callers are unchanged. The pink fill and grey-10% leave-note ground (see D307-adjacent note) are applied globally as the design-system standard.

### D307: Card-overlay quick-save bookmark is paper-white in both states, like the thumbs (user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** the onboarding zines carousel quick-save bookmark ([`onboarding_zines_carousel.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/onboarding_chat/widgets/onboarding_zines_carousel.dart) `_QuickSaveButton`) renders **paper-white** in both states over its frosted grey chip — white outline when unsaved, solid white fill when saved — exactly like the like/dislike thumbs (`SokoThumbCluster`). Only the outline→fill swap signals selection; the color never changes.
- **Why:** the bookmark previously used the default Soko-Ink glyph, which read as a dark mark over the photo and was inconsistent with the white thumbs sitting on the same kind of frosted ground. Reported on the "Curated for you" carousel card.
- **Scope:** applied to the onboarding carousel bookmark only. The same `SokoToggleGlyph.bookmarkChip` (ink) is still used on other card overlays (`feed_hero_card`, `feed_bundle_row`, `list_zine_item_page`); extending the white treatment there is a possible follow-up, not done here.

### D308: The two ritual cards are printed on paper, and the Daily Drop lockup sits at the foot of the card (user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** four corrections to the Daily Drop and Weekly Bundle cards, against the frames Zé linked — `7675:37837` (Daily Drop) and `7675:37872` (Weekly Bundle). Both cards gain the printed grain; the Daily Drop lockup moves from centred to the foot of the card; the Weekly Bundle gains the rule between its thumbnail strip and its lockup, and is finally set in the typeface the frame asks for.
- **The two cards use DIFFERENT sheets, and it is not a detail.** Daily Drop is `Gstaik Textures (5) 3` — creased and folded; Weekly Bundle is `Texturelabs_Grunge_340S 2` — a fine even grain.
