Both at `opacity-40` / `mix-blend-hard-light`, over the WHOLE card (mat, field, inset rule and content alike, which is where Figma stacks them: last child of the frame). They reach Flutter through [`BlendMask`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_grunge_surface.dart) — `mix-blend-mode` needs a `saveLayer`, which no `DecorationImage` or `colorBlendMode` can express.
- **The Gstaik sheet was already bundled; the Texturelabs one was not.** Identified by correlating each Figma export against all 30 bundled textures under squash, centre-crop, every 90° rotation and mirroring: Gstaik matches `cover-texture-03.webp` at **0.97** (runner-up 0.25), while the Texturelabs search tops out at **0.13** — a negative result trusted only because the same search, run as a control, finds the Gstaik one. So `grunge-texture-340s.webp` is a new 87 KB asset. ⚠️ `SokoGrungeSurface` had *named* `avatar-texture.png` "Texturelabs Grunge 340S" for months; it correlates **0.04** with the real sheet and is actually the Gstaik grain. That comment is corrected — it is what sent the first search to the wrong file.
- **A quarter turn on the Daily Drop's sheet is load-bearing, not decoration.** Figma's layer is `rotate(-90deg)`. Upright, the sheet's diagonal crease crosses the card and reads as a scratch on the screen; turned, the grain runs with the short edge and reads as paper. Verified by compositing both orientations at 40 % Hard Light over the field before choosing. The Weekly Bundle's sheet is unturned.
- **Each sheet is a WINDOW onto paper larger than the card, never a fit** — [`RitualCardTexture`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/widgets/sections/_daily_drop_status_cards.dart) carries the rect, in fractions of the card. Daily Drop hangs a sheet **1.58 × 2.37 cards** off the top-left (Figma nests a 551 × 380 box holding a `-rotate-90` child that over-fills it 116.66 % × 100.55 %; those collapse to one rect); Weekly Bundle is **1.00 × 1.16**, centred. Cover-fitting the sheet to the card instead — the obvious first draft, and what shipped in the first commit here — packs every crease into 399 px and makes the grain ~1.7× too fine. **Measured, not judged:** correlating the grain against Figma's own 4× render of the Daily Drop card scores **+0.88** for the derived rect and **+0.05** for cover-fitting, and our Flutter render then correlates **+0.73** with Figma's. The Weekly Bundle's rect is read off the same CSS but is *not* render-verified — that node refuses to export from Figma at any scale.
- **The Daily Drop lockup is inset equally from left, right and bottom** — Zé, 2026-09-01: *"the same padding on the right, left and bottom"*. The frame puts all three at ≈27.5 on its 350-wide frame, i.e. **31.3** on our 399. It was vertically centred in whatever the header row left, which put a wide band of bare field under it and a narrow one over it. `DailyDropReadyCard` carries only the **remainder** over what `DailyDropCardShell` already pads, because the shell owns the mat, the field stroke and the rule gap and should keep owning them.
- **The Weekly Bundle's rule between the strip and the lockup** (`7675:37875` — a 0.877 stroke with `stroke-dasharray: 0.06 3` and a round cap, i.e. dots) had simply never been drawn. It reuses the ritual card's own dotted rule, now public as `RitualCardDottedRule`, rather than a second painter with its own numbers. It is absent when there are no thumbnails — a rule under nothing.
- **The Weekly Bundle lockup was in the wrong typeface, since it was built.** `AppTheme.display` is ZalandoSans; the frame sets both words in `Season Mix TRIAL: Regular`, so the card had been reading as a heading rather than as a masthead. Now `AppTheme.displayPrimary` at **w400** (`displayPrimary` defaults to w300), tracking −3 %, base size 58.0 off `7675:37885` — replacing 63.083 from the superseded frame `3841:2326`. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: a sans-serif "Weekly Bundle" on this card is a regression.**
- **The italic half is a synthesised slant, and that is correct.** Figma names the face `Season Mix TRIAL: Regular_Italic`, but the trial family we bundle ships four upright cuts and no italic — Figma is skewing the upright cut and so are we. No fifth font file is owed.
- **Not changed, deliberately:** the palette. The frames draw Daily Drop as purple-on-pink and we ship blue-on-green — a pairing chosen on device from six candidates (2026-08-10) and untouched here. The Weekly Bundle's inner rule also stays dotted where the frame's CSS says dashed, per the 2026-08-27 decision that the rule style is what tells the two ritual cards apart.
- **The Daily Drop's grain ships at ~a fifth of Figma's strength, knowingly** (Zé, 2026-09-01, when he spotted it looked fainter than the frame). **The compositing was never the cause** — at the frames' own `opacity-40` our render sat within measurement noise of the CSS formula. The cause is **asset resolution**: `cover-texture-03.webp` is 371 × 512 against a 2173 × 3000 source, and the card magnifies it ~5×, so the fine grain averages away and only the coarse creases survive. Measured amplitude (sd of high-frequency detail, 0–255 luminance, on the bare field): Figma **4.77**, ours **0.95**. Raising the opacity would not restore it — the high frequencies are gone from the file, so it would only deepen the blotches. The only fix is a sharper sheet, and grain is noise, so it does not compress: shipping just the window the card shows costs **152 KB → 53 %**, **357 KB → 78 %**, **918 KB → 91 %** of Figma's grain, against the 36 KB reuse we have. Zé chose to stay at 36 KB rather than add one of the app's largest assets for a background texture, and instead to **raise the opacity to 0.55, over the frames' `opacity-40`** — *"put the texture slightly less transparent … so that it is more noticeable"*. That does not bring the fine grain back (it is gone from the file); it deepens the coarse creases that survived, which is enough to make the paper read. Daily Drop 1.09 → **1.45**, Weekly Bundle 2.06 → **2.81**. ⚠️ **Do not "correct" the opacity back to 0.4 to match the frames** — the divergence is the decision, and a test pins 0.55 as a literal for exactly that reason. The Weekly Bundle's sheet is **not** affected — 960 × 595 is the original upload and the card magnifies it only 1.25×, so it ships at full available fidelity.
- **Testing note — the placement rects are asserted as LITERALS, not against the constants.** The first version of the placement test compared the rendered geometry to `kDailyDropCardTexture.rect`, which reads better and tests nothing: the constant agrees with itself whatever it says, and a mutation to `Rect.fromLTWH(0, 0, 1, 1)` passed. The numbers in the test come off the Figma CSS independently, so a disagreement means one of the two is wrong. Likewise the quarter turn is pinned by value — dropping it changes no rect, so no geometry assertion can see it.

### D309: Detail date rows always show the absolute date; the relative timing moves to a rotated tag over the title (user-directed, 2026-09-01, Figma `7660-27981` / `7674-37412`)

- **Choice:** on item detail the date row/section shows the **clean absolute date** — "Sex 12 Jul, 21h", never "Today" / "This weekend" / "Past event". The relative status becomes a **rotated sticker over the title** ([`RotatedDateTag`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/rotated_date_tag.dart)): +5° tilt, radius 6, 30 tall, 10 px horizontal padding, Zalando Light 14 / Soko/Ink, overlapping the title's lower-right. It shows Past event / Today / Tomorrow / This weekend / This week / Next week, or **nothing** when the date is far enough out that only the absolute date matters.
- **Two new helpers**, both in [`event_when_formatter.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/core/utils/event_when_formatter.dart): `formatEventDateAbsolute` (always absolute, never a relative bucket) and `eventRelativeTagLabel` (the tag label, or null). They **reuse the existing `mapDate*` + `listZineEventPast` ARB strings** — no new keys, so no l10n parity/contamination risk.
- **The sticker fill is a shade of the page background, not an accent** (user: "don't make it red — the bg color a little darker/lighter"; and "can't be the same color as the page background"). `RotatedDateTag.fillFrom(bg)` nudges the page colour ~10% darker (or lighter on a dark page), same hue. The base differs per surface: **zine item page** → the per-item `bgColor` (poster-derived); **full event detail** → Soko/Paper; **map pin sheet** (compact header) → Soko/Event green.
- **Applies to three surfaces**, all reusing the one widget: the zine item page ([`list_zine_item_page.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/zine/list_zine_item_page.dart)), the full event detail and the map pin sheet's compact header (both in [`event_detail_body.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_detail_body.dart)). Venues carry no date, so they show no tag. The compact map-sheet already computed the label — it just wasn't rendered before.
- **Past events now show their real date.** Previously the detail's "Dates" section (and the zine detail row) replaced the date with a muted "Past event" marker; that marker is gone, the real date renders, and "Past event" lives only in the tag.
- **Superseded in part, same day, by D312** (placement + fill only). The label set, the absolute-date-in-the-row, and the "past events show their real date" rules all still hold. What changed: the sticker moved off the title onto the **image** (top-right), the fill became a fixed **Soko/Red** (past) / **Soko/Paper** (upcoming) accent — reversing the "shade of the page, don't make it red" choice above — the padding hugs the text (Figma `7660:27990`, 10×2, not a fixed 30 tall), and it now stamps on. See D312.

### D310: Chat/onboarding card thumbs (and the feed hero buttons) are frosted-glass buttons whose selection swaps only the glyph, never the ground (user-directed, Figma `7674-37412`) — revises D295

- **Choice:** the paired 👍/👎 control on the shared card ([`SokoThumbCluster`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_entity_card.dart)) is now **two 30 px frosted-glass circular buttons at the image's bottom-right** (was two square paper buttons flush at the top-center per D295). Each is a translucent Soko/Ink-30 ground (`Color(0x4D797979)`) under a 4 px `BackdropFilter` blur, with a **white** thumb glyph. Selection swaps the glyph white-outline → white-fill; **the frosted ground is held constant** — no background change on select. 👎 is the 👍 glyph rotated 180°.
- **The feed hero card's save/like/dislike (`_OverlayToggleButton` in [`feed_hero_card.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/blocks/feed_hero_card.dart)) got the same "no bg change on select" rule:** the circle no longer flips to opaque paper on selection — the ground stays the resting translucent fill and the icon stays white; only the glyph outline→fill carries the state.
- **This supersedes D295 for these two surfaces** — D295's "solid Soko/Ink active glyph on a paper button" is replaced here by a white glyph on a constant frosted/translucent ground. The detail-page save chips D295 also covers are unchanged.
- **Extended to the venue grid tile and consolidated into one widget (2026-09-02, PROD-4160).** The `venue_grid` tile's save chip ([`feed_venue_grid_tile.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/blocks/feed_venue_grid_tile.dart)) was still on the pre-D310 treatment — opaque `Soko/Paper` circle with a `Soko/Ink` bookmark when saved — because D310 named only the two surfaces above and the tile shipped separately under PROD-4108. Reported by Zé from the designer's updated frame. The rule now lives once, in [`SokoOverlayToggle`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_overlay_toggle.dart), which all three surfaces use; per-surface differences are its `size` / `ground` / `blurSigma` arguments (hero: 40 px, `Soko/Paper 30`, unblurred — thumbnail chips: 30 px, `Soko/Ink 30`, 4 px blur). **This is a card-overlay component only** — a control on the page's own paper, like the bundle row's save button, keeps its ink-on-light `CircleIconButton` and is deliberately out of scope.
- **Toggles now announce their state.** With the ground constant, the glyph outline→fill is the only visual signal, and a screen reader cannot see it — so `CircleIconButton` gained an optional `selected` flag that `SokoOverlayToggle` always passes. Before this the hero's three controls and the tile's chip announced identically saved or not.

### D311: The owner's zine visibility selector is a tap-to-toggle animated chip, not a dropdown (user-directed, Figma `7660-28041`)

- **Choice:** on the owner's zine detail (non-edit) the visibility selector ([`VisibilityMenuChip`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/visibility_menu_chip.dart)) **flips straight between Public and Private on tap** — no menu, no chevron. It's the canonical [`BtSqIco`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/bt_sq_ico.dart) `normal` chip (light DS pill, radius 6) with a 🔓 `lock_open` / 🔒 `lock` icon, replacing the old dark `sokoInk` pill that opened a `DSDropdownMenu`.
- **The icon + label cross-fade on toggle** via an `AnimatedSwitcher` keyed on the visibility value (220 ms fade + scale), so the change reads as a state flip rather than a silent relabel. The tap drives the existing optimistic-update + revert-on-error handler, so it animates immediately and rolls back if the PATCH fails.
- **Only Public↔Private** (Followers was already retired); the enum value is kept for back-compat parsing but never surfaced. The edit-mode segmented `ListVisibilityToggle` is unchanged.

### D312: The relative-date sticker rides the image (not the title), is a fixed Soko/Red-past / Soko/Paper-upcoming accent, and stamps on (user-directed, 2026-09-01, Figma `7660-27990`) — revises D309

Same-day iteration on D309, all user-directed:

- **On the image, not the title.** The [`RotatedDateTag`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/rotated_date_tag.dart) rides the **hero/poster image's top-right corner**, tilted, across the full event detail (on the [`SokoPhotoCollage`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_photo_collage.dart) main tile via a new `cornerBadge` slot — layered outside the `Hero` + `ClipRRect` so it doesn't fly into the gallery and can bleed past the rounded corner), the **map compact sheet** (on the 96×120 thumbnail), and the **zine poster**. It falls back over the title only when the event has **no hero image**. A single-image collage left-aligns the photo to ~⅔ width, so a container-level `right` anchor lands in the brand margin — hence the badge must ride the tile itself, not the container.
- **Fixed accent fill, not a page shade — reverses D309.** Past events → **Soko/Red** (`#F68686`, Figma `7660:27990`); upcoming → **Soko/Paper**; text stays Soko/Ink. Past-ness is derived from the **same start date as the label** (shared `eventRelativeTagIsPast` in [`event_when_formatter.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/core/utils/event_when_formatter.dart)), never a model `isPast` flag, so colour and label can never disagree (they did: an event with a past start but non-past `isPast` showed a paper "Past event").
- **Hugs the text.** Figma `7660:27990` padding (10 px horizontal / 2 px vertical), sized to the label via `UnconstrainedBox` — not a fixed 30 px height. Without the `UnconstrainedBox` the padded `Container`'s `alignment` made it balloon to fill the zine title band under `Positioned.fill`→`Align` (see [`container-with-alignment-expands-to-fill-bounded-constraints`](../learnings/container-with-alignment-expands-to-fill-bounded-constraints.md)).
- **Stamps on.** A one-shot entrance: an oversized ghost slams to size (`easeOutBack` overshoot) with a fade. Armed on first visibility, or — on the zine, whose `TurnPageView` stacks all pages in one on-screen slot — on the card turning to the front (`active`/`isActive`), since geometric visibility fires for every page at once (see [`visibility-gated-entrance-animation-misfires-in-a-stacked-pager`](../learnings/visibility-gated-entrance-animation-misfires-in-a-stacked-pager.md)).

### D313: Zine detail action row — labeled view toggle, per-role button sets, no IG-share, and a contextual reminder bell (user-directed, Figma `7660-28038` / `7660-28232`)

- **View toggle is a single labeled pill, not a two-icon segmented control.** [`ZineListaToggle`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/zine_lista_toggle.dart) is now a [`BtSqIco`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/bt_sq_ico.dart) `normal` chip showing the **current** mode (📖 `book_open` + "Zine" / ☰ `list` + "Lista"); tapping flips to the other mode with a 220 ms icon+label cross-fade (same `AnimatedSwitcher` treatment as the sibling `VisibilityMenuChip`, D311). The old edge-to-edge icon-only two-pill track is gone.
- **Right-cluster button sets by role** (`_ActionRow` in [`list_page_header.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_page_header.dart)): **owner** = `[toggle] … [Público] [+] [Share] [edit-menu]` — the `VisibilityMenuChip` moved from a centered slot into the right cluster; **non-owner** = `[toggle] … [Seguir] [Bell?] [Share] [Flag]`.
- **IG-direct-share dropped from both rows.** The `IgDirectShareButton` + its `SpotlightTrigger` (and the screen's `onIgDirectShare`/`_handleIgDirectShare` plumbing) were removed from the list header — the plain Share chip remains (IG-direct still lives on other surfaces). Per the user's call the owner keeps the web Share; only the IG shortcut went.
- **Contextual reminder Bell** (non-owner only): renders **only when the foregrounded zine page is an upcoming event**, and **animates in/out** (horizontal size + opacity, `_ReminderBellSlot`) as the viewer pages onto/off an event. It reuses the event-detail reminder stack wholesale ([`showReminderPickerSheet`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/event_detail/widgets/reminder_picker_sheet.dart) + `eventReminderListProvider` + `signalControllerProvider` lit state); on tap it lazily fetches the event's occurrences via `eventDetailProvider` (the zine item carries only a single `eventDate`) and opens the picker. This supersedes the "bell intentionally hidden in v1" note (there was no subscribe-to-list endpoint; this bell is a per-event **reminder**, which the backend does support). The header ↔ pager bridge is a new autoDispose `currentZinePageItemProvider(listId)` the pager writes on page-settle and the header reads — the header sliver is built separately from the pager and otherwise can't see the current page.

### D314: A zine's non-owner header subtitle carries the followers cluster inline; the owner's does not (user-directed, Figma `7660-28322`)

- **Choice:** on a **non-owner** zine the "{name} +N seguem" followers cluster (overlapping avatars + label) now sits **inline in the header subtitle** after the save-count/bookmark (`Edt. Soko · 10p · 45 🔖 · [avatars] JJ +3 seguem`), in addition to the below-card social-proof row. The **owner** subtitle keeps only the plain count — the followers cluster stays below the card next to the leave-note affordance.
- The cluster was extracted from `list_zine_view.dart`'s private `_FollowedByLine` into a shared [`ListFollowersInline`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_followers_inline.dart) so the header (16 px avatars + the stats text style) and the below-card slot (20 px + `Pt.b2`) render the same widget. Same gating as before: hidden while loading/on error and when the viewer is the only follower; tap opens the followers screen.

### D315: The saved-item note editor is a chat-composer capsule with an explicit rectangular send button — autosave-on-blur retained (user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice.** `ListItemNote._Editor` (the inline note editor mounted on the zine per-page slot, `VenueInlineActions`, `EventInlineActions`, and the list-view edit-mode rows) was restyled from the grey **Soko/Ink @ 6 % filled** `TextField` (6-px radius, hairline border — the treatment D94 describes) to the **chat-composer capsule**: **Soko/Paper** fill, 22-px radius, 1-px **Soko/Ink @ 12 %** hairline border — the same chrome as the `outlined` `ChatBarTopRow` (`shared/widgets/chat_bar.dart`). The inner `TextField` is borderless / unfilled; the capsule is the visible frame. A **rectangular icon-only send button** (`BtSqIco`, `LucideIcons.send`, `selected` / Soko-Pink variant) sits bottom-right beside the counter and commits the note explicitly (`_submit` → `_save()` + `unfocus`); it renders **disabled** while the trimmed text equals `_lastCommittedText` or a save is in flight (spinner via `loading`).
- **Autosave is retained, not replaced.** The button is *additive* — blur-autosave (D96) still fires as the safety net. `_save`'s `_saving` guard + the `_lastCommittedText` comparison collapse a submit-tap-then-blur into a single save, never a double.
- **Counter moved out of the Stack.** The `0/500` counter (`kUserListItemTipMaxLength`) was a `Positioned` / `IgnorePointer` overlay in a `Stack`; it now sits in a `Column`'s bottom `Row` (`[Spacer, counter, send button]`), still driven by a `ValueListenableBuilder<TextEditingValue>` on the controller.
- **Scope.** Only the **note** editor changed; the **description** editor (`ListDescription` editable variant) still uses the old grey-fill panel, so its "chrome matching the note editor" lineage no longer holds.
- **Where it lives.** `lib/features/lists/widgets/list_item_note.dart::_Editor` + `_ListItemNoteState._submit`. Reuses `shared/widgets/bt_sq_ico.dart`.
- **Known pre-existing follow-ups** (surfaced while building this, not introduced by it): on **web**, tapping the pencil to open the editor does not reliably land keyboard focus — programmatic `requestFocus` fires in a post-frame callback, outside the tap gesture, and the browser won't open the keyboard for it. A latent `ConcurrentModificationError` also exists (`TextEditingController` mutated during `notifyListeners`, from `didUpdateWidget` reseeding `_controller.text` while the parent rebuilds). Both predate this restyle.

### D316: A feed filter change holds the scroll where the reader can still see the filter row, and otherwise lands on the top of the feed (user-directed, 2026-09-01)

Changing the entity filter throws away the whole block list, so there is a
moment with nothing to render. What the page does with the reader's scroll
position in that moment splits on **one question: can they still see the inline
filter row?** That boundary is not a guess — it is the offset at which the
pinned bar covers the row, which the page already computes for D32.

| Reader is | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| Above the boundary (the inline row is on screen) | **Hold the offset exactly.** The area below simply goes empty while the new feed loads. Nothing moves under their thumb. |
| At or past it (the bar is covering the row) | **Instant jump to the boundary** — the top of the feed. The old content goes, the bar is up before and after, and it never blinks. |

**Instant, never animated** (Zé): animating a page whose content has just been
emptied reads as a glitch rather than as motion.

The jump target is the bar's own reveal threshold rather than "offset 0" or
"the first block", because it is the only offset that satisfies both halves of
the ask — the feed starts directly under the chrome, and the pinned header keeps
being displayed in the state it was already in.

Holding any offset over an emptied feed needs the extent to still be there, so
the page reserves a viewport of blank while the new feed is in the air. That is
the load-bearing half: without it `maxScrollExtent` collapses and the reader is
clamped to the top whatever the page intends. It also means the page is
deliberately blank below the filter row during the change — "the page below
should simply be empty while the new feed is loading" (Zé).

> **Superseded on that last point by D338 (2026-09-07).** The reserve stays and is still
> load-bearing for the scroll, but it is no longer blank: it now carries the feed's loading
> state, because the backend's compose can take ~10 s and the blank read as a broken page.

⚠️ A feed that loads back **shorter than the target** will still settle lower —
there is genuinely nowhere to scroll. Real page-1 feeds are three blocks of
cards and comfortably taller, so this is a boundary case rather than a
behaviour; it is not worth padding the page to hide.

See [`features/discovery.md`](../features/discovery.md) and
[`learnings/a-scroll-threshold-must-be-re-evaluated-after-layout.md`](../learnings/a-scroll-threshold-must-be-re-evaluated-after-layout.md).

### D317: The Zines grid's chevron is conditional, and its empty state is the backend's job (PROD-4118, Figma `7675-38867`)

- **Choice:** the `zine_grid` block renders its "ver mais" chevron **only when the block carries a `button`** — unlike `venue_grid` (D137), where the affordance is unconditional. `grid-featured` replaces the old Highlighted shelf, which ships no see-all by design, so the backend sends `button: null` and a chevron there would dead-end the tap. The other two grids open `/shelves/editor-picks` and `/shelves/recommended`, pages that already exist.
- **Two columns, always** — the client owns the column count (D8), so the widget lays out `ceil(items.length / 2)` rows and a **ragged last row is expected**: three items give a full row plus one card at column width, left-aligned rather than stretched across the page. The pair layout is lifted from `search_results_view.dart`, which already renders zines this way, rather than authored a third time.
- **⚠️ An empty `zine_grid` is RENDERED, not dropped** — the inverse of D306, and the thing most likely to be "fixed" by someone porting the venue guard across. The venue grid needs a client-side drop because it is *placed* during composition and *filled* per caller afterwards, so it can genuinely arrive empty; every zine source runs inside composition, so the backend omits an empty zine grid entirely. Confirmed against staging: a guest page returns five blocks with `grid-featured` and `grid-editor-picks` **absent**, not empty. `feed_zine_grid_block_test.dart` pins the deliberate absence of a guard.
- **The card is `HighlightedShelfCard` fed a recipe**, never a URL. `FeedZineItem.imageUrl` is `null` by design so no surface can accidentally paint a flat cover by reading the sealed base's getter — the cover composes through the shared `ZineCoverRecipe.fromFields`, which is what makes a feed card and the zine page it opens show the same artwork.
- **The bundle row's control is follow, not save**, drawn as the same bookmark that fills when active, and labelled with the existing "Guardar/Guardada" copy — the app has always presented zine-follow to users as saving, so coining a "Seguir" string here would introduce an API distinction to the user in one row of one feed and nowhere else.

> ⚠️ **Built without the two layout frames.** Figma nodes `7675-38867` (grid) and `7675-38094` (bundle row) were not readable during implementation — the MCP resolved to a different document, and neither node is in `docs/ui/figma-cache/`. Spacing and type were derived from the adjacent shipped surfaces (`HighlightedShelfCard`'s own `6144:5096` measurements, and `FeedBundleRow`'s existing 80/64/12/6 geometry). **`/compare-ui` this page against both frames before treating the layout as verified.** The corner frame `7686-43803` *was* read, and D318 records it.

### D318: The zine cover's turned corner is static, and its page colour is receded twice (PROD-4118, Figma `7686-43803`)

- **Choice:** every zine cover on the new home feed carries a **static** turned top-right corner ([`SokoZineCornerFold`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_zine_corner_fold.dart)) — grid cards and bundle-row thumbs alike. It replaces `ZineCoverPeelOverlay` on that surface only, via an opt-in `cornerFoldColor` on `HighlightedShelfCard`; search results, the shelves, the smart-list grid and the onboarding carousel keep the animated peel.
- **Why not reuse the peel.** The two answer different questions. The peel is an intermittent *hint* that a card opens into a page-turn, gated to roughly one card per band — on a four-card grid that decorates one card and leaves three looking unfinished. The fold is **part of the cover**: it is what makes a zine read as a zine beside an event and a venue in one scroll, so it must be on all of them and must not move.
- **Geometry is proportional, from the frame.** `43/194` of the cover's width, aspect `35/43`, bottom-left radius `6/43`, and the flap's apex `2.05469/43` **above** the box — that overshoot is what sets the diagonal's slope (37 over 43, not 35) and gives the flap width at the card's top edge instead of a point. Ratios rather than pixels because the same cover renders at 194 in a grid cell and 64 in a row thumb.
- **⚠️ The corner curve is a quadratic bezier, not `arcToPoint`.** `arcToPoint` needs a sweep direction and the wrong one takes the long way round — which bites a chunk out of the flap's left edge and reads as "the top-left is clipped weirdly" while the bottom-left never looks round. Caught on device, not by a test; `soko_zine_corner_fold_test.dart` now pins it with three assertions, because any two alone are satisfiable by a wrong shape.
- **The page colour is receded twice, and the split matters.** A raw brand pastel in the corner out-competes the artwork it frames. Two candidates were built and compared on device side by side: an ink shadow along the fold line (hue stays true, but goes murky over a dark cover photo) and mixing the colour toward ink (flat, prints the same at any size). **The mix won.** Shipping: `mixTowardInk` **0.22** — down from the 0.38 tried first, which read as a different, muddier colour rather than a shaded one — then 0.10 toward white to take the saturation off, with the ink shadow kept at **0.12** as a barely-visible hint that the cover is casting onto the page. If a later change lightens the mix, do **not** compensate by raising the shadow; that reintroduces the murk the shadow-only variant showed.
- **The colour is deterministic from the zine id and excludes the cover's own colour.** The true "first page" colour would be that item's derived palette colour (`zineItemColorProvider`, PROD-4072), which needs an item id and image the feed payload does not carry. Excluding the cover colour is not a preference: a solid `background_color` cover in the same brand colour makes the fold vanish, which reads as a rendering bug.

### D319: Memory sub-category chips sit under a category header, derived from the slug prefix (PROD-3766, 2026-09-02)

- **Choice:** on the Memory page, `event_sub_categories` chips no longer render as one flat list under the mechanical family title. They are bucketed by their **category** and each bucket gets a header: *Música* above `[Blues] [Jazz]`, *Cultura* above `[Cosplay]`. `sport-*` sub-categories move out of Events entirely and render in the **Sport & Outdoors** section — the same per-chip routing `venue_types` already gets (`splitVenueTypesByGroup`, PROD-2799) — and there they carry **no** group header, because the section title already says "sport".
- **Why:** a chip reading "Blues" on its own says nothing about where it came from. The open sub-category vocabulary (ADR-052) multiplies these chips, so the flat list gets less legible as the vocabulary grows, not more.
- **The grouping is free — it is already in the slug.** Sub-category slugs are `category-name` (`music-blues`, `sport-climbing`), so the prefix before the first hyphen **is** the category and the app derives it at render time: no new backend field, and LLM-proposed slugs group exactly like canonical ones. The header text comes from `humanizeMemoryValue(..., family: 'event_categories')` — the display map the event-category chips already use — so **PT/BR/EN/ES work with no new ARB keys**.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a category header above a run of sub-category chips inside Events is **intentional** and is not in Figma. The regressions to look for are the inverse: sub-category chips flat under one header, a `sport-*` chip appearing in Events, a group header rendered in Sport & Outdoors, or a header printed in lowercase — group headers keep their own casing, unlike the Soko-voice dimension headers they replace, which stay lowercased.
- **Where it lives:** `memory_hierarchy.dart` (`subcategoryGroupPrefix`, `subcategoryGroupCategoryKey`, `splitEventSubCategoriesByParent`), `compact_family_section.dart` (`_DimGroup`, `headerOverride` on `_DimensionBlock` — an **empty** override hides the header row, `null` falls back to the dimension header), `memory_screen.dart` (routes the family through the split). Feature doc: [`features/memory-page.md`](../features/memory-page.md).

### D320: The self profile is the Memory surface; visited profiles keep tabs (PROD-4164, user-directed, 2026-09-02)

- **Choice:** your own profile drops the Zines / Guardados / Calendário / Ligações tab bar entirely and renders a single centred **Memória** section under the identity block. A **visited** profile keeps a tab bar, but as words — **Zines · Guardados · Pessoas** — not the four icon tabs. The official Soko account's three tabs became words too, so no profile mixes the two idioms.
- **Why the old tabs are not lost:** they moved to the Library. The calendar in particular was already rendering there (`lists_hub_screen.dart`), so the profile's copy was redundant rather than removed. `Guardados` and `Zines` in the stats row open the Library — **but only on your own profile**. On someone else's, the Library is *yours, not theirs*, so the counters `animateTo` that person's tab instead.
- **"the Library" here means `/library`, not `/yours` (corrected 2026-09-04).** While both hubs existed, that sentence was ambiguous and the code took the other reading: the counters shipped `go`ing `AppRoutes.lists`, so both taps opened the **old** hub `/library` had replaced. They now go to `/library`, and each names the shelf it wants — Guardados `?tag=all`, Zines `?tag=zines` — because the Library's tab is sticky across visits and bare `/library` would land on whatever was left there last. Contract: [`features/library.md`](../features/library.md) § "Entry points and `?tag=`".
- **Emblemas is drawn in the mock and deliberately not built.** Badges do not exist, so there is nothing to switch between. **(updated 2026-09-09)** The self surface now omits the tab header entirely — `_SelfProfileBody` renders `MemoryTabView` directly under the dotted rule. It previously kept a fake one-item "Memória" heading styled with the tab bar's underline, but a single-item selector is just noise: a tab bar you cannot tab is not a control. Reinstate a real `TabBar` here when Emblemas ships. (`public_profile_screen.dart` → `_SelfProfileBody`; the `_MemoryTabHeader` widget was removed.)
- **The mock shows a bell and a settings gear on a *visited* profile.** Not implemented: that is the self header pasted onto the frame, and it would leave a pushed route with no way back plus a gear pointing at the viewer's own settings. Visited profiles keep back + share. **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`: this divergence is intentional.**

### D321: Memory values render as tunable bars with no provenance, not chips (PROD-4164, user-directed, 2026-09-02)

- **Choice:** on the profile/`/memory` surface each value is a row — `[bin] [ label ····· level ] [−] [+]` — filling `level/4` in `Soko/Pink`. Tapping the row does **nothing**: the `ProvenancePanel` ("where Soko learned this") is unreachable here by design, so the page reads as something you *tune*, not something you *inspect*. The legacy `/menu/memory` page keeps chips + provenance.
- **The fold folds on the number the user can see.** `foldByLevel` hides level-1 rows, then caps what remains at 5. PROD-3766's `foldByTier` used the backend's `tier` flag, which is computed separately from the certainty the meter renders — so a bar could display "1" and still be tagged `primary` and stay in the list. A nudged value is pinned past both rules so it cannot vanish under the finger that moved it.
- **Ungraded rows omit the meter and the −/+ entirely** rather than disabling them. The home location is a fact, not a preference, and a full bar on an avoided value would read as a strong *like*; two permanently greyed circles on every such row read as broken, not as "not applicable".
- **Avoids are their own section of chips at the foot**, with the same circular bin the bars use, stacked vertically. A chip, not a bar, because an avoided value has no magnitude — you do not dislike paella 3 out of 4. Its confirm sheet is separate copy: removing an avoid *un-blocks* a value, so the shared "Soko will stop using X when suggesting places" was exactly backwards.
- **Social is withheld from this surface.** Its values are people; a 1–4 interest meter on a person is meaningless, and the level-1 fold would then hide people for a reason nothing on screen explains.

### D322: Icon geometry measured from the Figma export by alpha coverage, not eyeballed (PROD-4164, 2026-09-02)

- **Choice:** the circled `+`, `−` and section arrow are drawn in a `CustomPainter` ([`memory_glyph.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/memory/widgets/memory_glyph.dart)) from ratios measured off the 26×26 Figma exports, **not** bundled as the supplied PNGs. Those exports are 1× — they blur at 2×/3× and cannot be tinted for a disabled state. `Icon/Brain`, which *was* available as a vector, ships as an SVG asset instead; drawing is the fallback, not the preference.
- **Measure by summing alpha, not by counting solid pixels.** Eyeballing the raster over-read every dimension: ring stroke read as 1.2/26 and the mark as 1.5/26, when the summed coverage is **1.0/26 for both**. The bar measures 10.75 px end-to-end *including* round caps, so the geometric half-length is `(10.75 − 1.0)/2 = 4.875`, not 5.
- **The ring is full-bleed and full-opacity** — it touches the box edge and paints `#3B0F18` at 100 %, unlike Lucide's `circle_plus`, whose ring is inset 2/24. An inset hairline at 35 % was visibly wrong against the design.
- **The trash and the pencil are NOT part of this set** and keep the quieter `ink @ 35 %` hairline ring on purpose — they were not in the export set. Do not "unify" them into the glyph treatment.

### D323: Library Date tag opens a calendar sheet, not a sort (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** On `/library`, the Eventos sub-filter **Data** presents a `DSSheetShell` calendar (`CalendarDatePicker` themed with Soko/Pink + Soko/Ink). Confirming a day keeps only saved events whose `start_at` falls on that local calendar day. The joined pill then shows the compact date (`14 Ago` / `Aug 14`) instead of the word "Data". Tapping that half reopens the sheet; tapping the Eventos half still clears.
- **Why:** Figma `7501:23705` draws the tag and no results state. A first pass treated Data as an ascending sort of the current page. Product: it is a date picker. `/users/me/saved` still has no `from_date`/`to_date` (PROD-4103), so the filter is client-side until that lands.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** Data that reorders the same list without a sheet is a regression. A Material `showDatePicker` dialog (not `DSSheetShell`) is also a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `library_date_menu.dart`, `library_month_calendar.dart`, `library_filter.dart` (`selectedDate`), `library_filter_bar.dart`, `library_screen.dart`.
- **Superseded by D326** (dropdown calendar, not a sheet).

### D324: `/library` lands on the unfiltered merged feed, not Eventos (PROD-4103, user-directed, 2026-09-01)

- **Choice:** `/library` opens with **no category selected** — Figma `7501:24414` — and loads `GET /users/me/library`. Eventos is an opt-in tab, not the default. Sub-filters (Futuros / Passados / Data, Tuas / Seguidas) appear only after a category is picked. The × control is hidden on the landing.
- **Why:** the first cut skipped the no-tab frame and defaulted to Eventos because the merged endpoint did not exist yet. The endpoint shipped; the landing must match the frame.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a pink Eventos pill and an events-only list on first paint is a **regression**.
- **Where it lives:** `library_filter.dart` (`LibraryFilter.initial`), `library_screen.dart` (`_ServerFeedBody`), `library_api.dart`.

### D325: Library pin is a pink meta-line glyph; sort is a bare strip under the chips (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-09-02)

- **Choice:** Pin is **display only**. Live Figma `7660:30340` (`get_design_context` 2026-09-02) is a 14×14 filled thumbtack, `#EF8EA8` (Soko/Dark Pink / [AppColors.sokoPinkMiddle]), first item on the metadata line, 6px before the 20px curator avatar, 12px below the title. Unpinned rows show no pin (crop is one pinned row). Sort lives under the chips (`7660:30312`): 20px gap, `justify-between`, left `Icon/ArrowDown` 14×14 stroke ink + current sort label, right `Icon/List` + "Lista". Both groups are bare (no fill, no border). Sort opens the existing sheet. **Lista is drawn, not tappable** — Figma has no second view. Category tabs hide the strip. Eventos / Sítios / Pessoas still load `GET /users/me/library?types=`. Zines stay on the lists endpoints.
- **Why:** Earlier cuts used a 40px ink circle (wrong slot) or Lucide stand-ins. These are the file's own SVGs.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** A pin circle on a library row is a regression. Header sliders are a regression. A tappable "Lista" view switch is **not** expected.
- **Where it lives:** `library_glyph.dart`, `library_pin_button.dart` (`LibraryPinMark`), `library_sort_bar.dart`, `library_item_row.dart` (`showPin`), assets `heyl_app/assets/images/icons/library/`.
- **Superseded in part by D326** (sort and list/grid are dropdowns; Lista is tappable).

### D326: Library date, sort, and list/grid are dropdowns (no ticket, user-directed, 2026-09-02)

- **Choice:** Date, sort, and list/grid open as **anchored dropdowns**, not `DSSheetShell` drawers. Date is a squarish month grid (`LibraryMonthCalendar`) under the Data chip: today outlined in ink, selected day ink-filled, days with saved events filled Soko/Pink (same language as `ListCalendarView` / PROD-2018). Tapping a day applies the filter and closes. Sort and list/grid use `DSDropdownMenu` (Figma Overlay/Edit chrome on page `2843:984`): icon + label + check on the current value. Grid is two columns of cover+title tiles.
- **Why:** Drawers were a stand-in. Product: these controls should sit on the strip like the rest of the Soko dropdowns, and the calendar should show which days have events.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** A bottom sheet titled "Pick a date" / "Sort by" is a regression. Date that does not highlight event days is a miss. Lista that does nothing is a regression.
- **Where it lives:** `library_date_menu.dart`, `library_month_calendar.dart`, `library_sort_bar.dart`, `library_item_row.dart`, `glassmorphic_popup_menu.dart` (`panelBuilder`).

### D327: The zine bundle row shows the curator's photo, and falls back to the person glyph rather than an initial (user-directed, 2026-09-02, PROD-4160)

- **Choice:** the curator half of a zine row's meta line ([`feed_bundle_row.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/blocks/feed_bundle_row.dart) `_zineContent`) shows the curator's actual photo via [`PersonDot`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/person_dot.dart) when one exists, and `LucideIcons.user` as its **no-photo fallback**. The count half keeps `LucideIcons.book_open`.
- **Why not the seeded initial-letter dot**, which is what the zine page header and the Descobrir shelf cards fall back to: those surfaces have a URL to *try*, and degrade to a letter only for a curator who uploaded nothing. This row has no URL on the wire at all, so a letter would be a permanent stand-in rather than a fallback — and a column of unrelated coloured letters reads as noise where one uniform glyph reads as "a person". Zé ruled on it after seeing both rendered side by side.
- **The gap underneath it, closed the same day:** `FeedZineItem` projected only `curator_name` from the owner record, while `UserListOut.owner` carries `avatar_url` (`PublicListOwnerOut`) — the two surfaces read different projections of the same person, which is why the zine page header showed isabella's picture and the feed row could not. The backend added **`curator_avatar_url`** in spec **v1.123.0**, sourced from the dict the item builder already had in hand: a field, not a query.
- **No seed, and no `curator_id`.** The backend's pre-merge branch carried one, and it was **dropped before landing** — correctly: `PersonDot`'s seed only tints its *name* fallback, and this branch runs only when there IS a photo, so the seed could never show. `is_expert` was left out on the same reasoning (2 experts, 13 public zines, and a 14 px dot has no badge slot). Both are additive later at the same cost. ⚠️ We had wired `curator_id` against the pre-merge branch; the merge removed it and our side was corrected to match. **Read the merged `develop`, not the branch commit an agent cites** — the two differed here.
- **The avatar URL is rendered raw.** The stored value is already an absolute backend image-proxy URL (`/api/v1/image/gcs/...`) — the upload handler is its only writer — and every other owner projection emits it unwrapped, so rebasing it here would make the feed the only surface that does.
- **An empty string is not a photo.** The guard tests `isNotEmpty`, not just non-null: `PersonDot` handed an empty url falls through to its *name* fallback, which is the initial letter this decision rules out. Pinned by a test.
- **Nothing was parsed speculatively.** While the field name was asked-for but unagreed there was no parse and no `leading` slot — a name guessed wrong would silently never populate, the exact shape of [`a-field-nothing-reads-is-a-field-nothing-validates`](../learnings/a-field-nothing-reads-is-a-field-nothing-validates.md); both landed only once the contract did. The parse is absent-safe, so a backend predating v1.122.0 still renders the glyph rather than degrading the block.
- ⚠️ **The claim the first version of this entry made — "owner avatars are absent backend-wide (PROD-1703)" — was wrong.** It came from `docs/openapi-gaps.md` and two Dart doc comments that had all stayed stale long after the backend closed that gap. All four were corrected, along with the spec, which was missing `avatar_url` on `PublicListOwnerOut`.

### D328: Library category pills share the home-feed Tag metrics (user-directed, 2026-09-03)

- **Choice:** `/library` filter pills ([`LibraryTag`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/widgets/library_tag.dart) / [`LibraryCircleTag`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/widgets/library_tag.dart)) use the same numbers as the home-feed Tag ([D304](#d304-the-filter-row-is-one-horizontally-scrolling-tag-row-across-the-feed-descobre-a-cidade-and-procura-prod-4081-user-directed-2026-09-01)): 30 tall, radius 6, 10 px horizontal padding, a 14 px icon 6 from the label, Zalando Light 14 with height 1.2 and tracking −0.14, paper fill + 1 px `Soko/Shade45` hairline when unselected. The type lives in one helper, [`sokoTagChipLabelStyle`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_tag_chip.dart), so the three surfaces cannot drift.
- **What stays Library-only:** joined category + sub-filter (squared facing corners, category accent on the right half), search/clear as 30×30 circles, and the presence/reorder animation on `AnimatedTagRow`. Those behaviours have no feed equivalent; this decision is metrics, not a widget merge.
- **Why:** the pills shipped on `Mobile/B1 Reg` (18 Light) and 12 px padding — a size larger than the Tag the new home feed already uses. Product: they should read as the same control.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** 18 px labels, `sokoInk10` hairlines, or 12 px chip padding on `/library` are a regression. Joined split-pills and the × circle are still expected.
- **Where it lives:** ~~`library_tag.dart`~~, `soko_tag_chip.dart` (`sokoTagChipLabelStyle` + shared metric constants).
- ⚠️ **Superseded by [D333](#d333-home-and-library-filters-share-the-complete-tag-design-system-user-directed-2026-09-03) (2026-09-04).** This entry converged the metrics while keeping `LibraryTag`/`LibraryCircleTag` as separate widgets; D333 finished the job by deleting them and rendering Library through `SokoTagChip`/`SokoTagIconChip` itself. **The visual end state is identical** — same 14 px `sokoTagChipLabelStyle`, same `Soko/Shade45` hairline, same 10 px padding, same icon metrics, all still sourced from this file's shared constants. Only the widget boundary moved, so the `/compare-ui` regressions listed here still hold; look for them on the shared Tag.

### D329: Library end-of-list is four create rows, not an empty illustration (user-directed, 2026-09-03)

- **Choice:** `/library` closes with four full-width `spot-list` rows from Figma `7660:30304` / `7660:30620`–`7660:30641` — Instagram (purple), add place (blue), add event (green), create zine (red). They replace the walking illustration + "Can't find what you're looking for?" + pink "See more options" (which went home). Same destinations as the Create menu: Instagram share sheet, Google Maps list import, photo→event sheet, `/discovery/lists/new`. Guests hit `requireAuth`. Copy is the Figma labels, not the Create-menu sentences.
- **Not a 2×2 grid.** Each row is 64×80 thumb + wrapping Mobile/B1 title, 10 px between rows — including after the last feed item. Grid view still stacks these as a column under the wrap.
- **Grain is four regular-ish sheets, one per create row**, so product can pick (2026-09-03). Instagram = Texturelabs 340S (cardstock). Place = catalog `06` (fine speckle). Event = `14` (pebbled paper). Zine = `16` (sandpaper). Contacts (Pessoas) = `10` (mesh). Hard Light, fill-scaled, ritual-card opacity. Gstaik creases are a miss.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the illustration + home CTA at the foot of `/library` is a regression. A 2×2 of these cards is a miss. Create-menu copy ("Share Instagram link", "Upload a Google Maps list") on these rows is a miss. Untextured add-card thumbs, or Gstaik creases on them, are a miss.
- **Where it lives:** `library_end_actions.dart`, `library_screen.dart` (`_LibraryRows`).

### D330: Library end rows follow the selected tab (user-directed, 2026-09-03)

- **Choice:** the foot rows are **per tab**, not always the four create cards. Instagram on every tab **except Pessoas**. Eventos adds "Add an event", Sítios "Add a place", Zines "Create a zine". Landing (no category) still shows all four. Pessoas is a single yellow `spot-list` row from Figma `7660:31485` — User glyph, "Import contacts" — wired to the same contacts flow as Find people (`/find-people/contacts`; web keeps the on-app sheet).
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** IG on Pessoas is a regression. All four create rows on Eventos/Sítios/Zines is a miss. Invite-friends / QR rows on Pessoas are not in this frame.
- **Where it lives:** `libraryEndActionsFor` in `library_end_actions.dart`.

### D331: Library thumbs reuse the feed cutouts — ticket events, rectangle places, squircle people (user-directed, 2026-09-03)

- **Choice:** `/library` photo wells are the same cutouts as the home-feed bundle row (PROD-4118), not one shared ticket clip. **Event** = `FeedBundleRow.eventPhotoThumb` (`NotchedTopEdgeClip`). **Place** = `FeedBundleRow.placePhotoThumb` (square top, 2 px bottom corners). **Person** = [`TexturedAvatar`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/profile/widgets/textured_avatar.dart) 64×64 squircle (`kRadiusRatio`). Grid view scales that widget; it does not wrap a second `ClipRRect` over it.
- **Why:** Library landed `photoThumb` as always-notched because the row started event-only. Places and people then inherited a ticket silhouette.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a notched top on a place or person in `/library` is a regression. A circular person thumb is a miss.
- **Where it lives:** `feed_bundle_row.dart` (`eventPhotoThumb` / `placePhotoThumb`), `library_screen.dart` row builders, `library_item_row.dart` (`_GridTile`).

### D332: Library zine thumbs keep the static fold (user-directed, 2026-09-03)

- **Choice:** dense library zine thumbs (64×80) use the static home [`SokoZineCornerFold`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_zine_corner_fold.dart), not a looping peel. `ZineCoverPeelOverlay.alwaysPeel` exists for larger covers; do not enable it on this row.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a looping peel on a library list thumb is a miss at this size. A zine with no fold at all is a miss.
- **Where it lives:** `library_zine_thumb.dart`.


### D333: Home and Library filters share the complete Tag design system (user-directed, 2026-09-03)

- **Choice:** the new home feed and `/library` render their filters through the same shared [`SokoTagBar`, `SokoTagChip`, and `SokoTagIconChip`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_tag_chip.dart). The feed's metrics are canonical: 30 px height, 6 px radius, 10 px horizontal padding, 14 px icon, 6 px icon-to-label gap, Mobile/B2 Reg typography, `Soko/Shade45` hairline, and content-sized labels with no ellipsis. The feature-local Library button and animated-row renderers were deleted.
- **Library keeps composition, not visual mechanics.** `LibraryFilterBar` still decides which category and sub-filter entries exist, but declares them as stable `SokoTagBarItem`s and a `SokoTagGroup`. The group owns zero internal gaps and joined corners; the bar's named `reorder` treatment owns insertion/removal/reorder, selected-fill, and joined-edge motion. The standard treatment retains the feed/search row's lazy `ListView`, so a shared façade does not make stable rows pay for measurement and positioning.
- **Visual extensions are controlled variants.** Pink remains the default selection. Library's joined level-two half chooses a `SokoTagTone` (blue/green/purple/yellow) rather than passing an arbitrary `Color`; motion is selected as a named treatment — `standard`, `reveal` or `reorder` — not as feature-owned durations and curves. This keeps future palette, timing, spacing, and shape changes centralized while preserving the supported Library states.
- **The reveal on mount is a property of measuring, and it is now a named treatment (Zé, 2026-09-03).** A measured row does not know a slot's width until `MeasureSize` reports it a frame later, so every slot starts at `left: 0, width: 0` and eases out — left and width share one curve, so the row unfolds as a uniform horizontal expansion from its leading edge. Library got this for free from `reorder`; the feed now asks for it by name. The treatment enum therefore splits **row travel** (`duration`/`curve`) from **chip chrome** (`chipDuration`/`chipCurve`): `reveal` is the measured row's entrance at 600 ms `easeOutCubic` with `standard`'s snappy chip timing, so the feed gains the unfold **without** its filter fill becoming 3× slower. Handing the feed `reorder` wholesale would have done both, and only one was asked for.
- **Reduce motion is honoured by the row AND the chip.** The row snaps its ordering when `MediaQuery.disableAnimations` is set; `SokoTagChip` zeroes both its fill tween and `Material.animationDuration` (the latter is what stops a joined corner squaring off gradually — `Material` interpolates its shape, not just its colour). Split policies here read as a bug: the row lands instantly while the corners ease behind it.
- **A Tag rendered with `provideSemantics: false` hands its ENTIRE subtree to the parent.** It is `ExcludeSemantics`, not a label suppression, so the composite owner must announce everything the chip showed. `/library`'s Date trigger passes the picked date as the node's `value` — a first-class semantics field, so no new localized string — plus `selected`; without it a screen-reader user is told a date filter is applied but never which one.
- **Behavior stays feature-owned.** Feed filters still recompose `/feed/home`, Procura still navigates, Library search still expands inline, and Library's × still peels one filter layer. Sharing a button does not imply sharing provider state or tap behavior.
- **Library goes full bleed by being mounted outside the page padding, NOT by escaping it.** `escapeHorizontalPadding` — the [`OverflowBox`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_tag_bar.dart) trick the two search surfaces use — makes the scroller reach both edges, but painting outside a parent's box does not extend hit testing: every ancestor's `hitTest` starts with `size.contains(position)`, so a tap landing in the escaped strip is rejected by the padded ancestor before it reaches a Tag. Measured on a 400 px viewport: the row painted `0…400` while its own box was `16…384`, and a chip scrolled into the gutter was **visible and inert** — a tap at x=8 did nothing while the same chip answered at x=30. `LibraryScreen` therefore pads its blocks individually and mounts the row unpadded with `margin: 16`. The two search surfaces still escape, and the option now carries the warning.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** Library filter text at the old 18 px size, 12 px horizontal padding, `Soko/Ink10` border, or ellipsized labels is a regression. Joined and accent-filled Library sub-filters are supported states of the shared Tag, not a separate visual component. A Library filter chip that paints in the page gutter but does not respond to a tap there is the escape-vs-hoist regression above.

### D334: Profile memory sub-category bars group under their category header (user-directed, 2026-09-07)

The profile Memory tab dumped every event sub-category bar under one flat
"Mais especificamente" — Jazz, DJ set and Eletrónica now sit under a
"Música" header, Exhibition/Photography under "Arte", mirroring the legacy
memory screen's `CompactFamilySection` grouping. Desporto suppresses the
group header (its section title already carries the context); `food-*`
sub-categories in Gosto gain "Comida e bebida". Grouping is a pure render
split (`groupSubcategoryDimension` in `memory_hierarchy.dart`).

### D335: Memory bars render raw proportional strength; the fifth band is earned (user-directed, 2026-09-07)

The pink fill was quantized to `level/5`, so 19 acts of live music and 5 of
DJ drew identical bars — and the meter conflated certainty with intensity.
The fill now renders the raw certainty (clamped to a visible minimum) while
the 1-5 number stays as the coarse label the − / + rails move. Paired
backend decision (same date): the fifth band is EARNED, not pin-only — the
derived-strength ruler emits 0.95 at 8+ distinct evidences (5+ for stated
preferences), so the system reaches 5 automatically when the evidence piles
up; the user's pin (0.97) remains the override on top.

### D336: An all-weak memory block shows its weak bars instead of a bare "+N mais" (user-directed, 2026-09-07)

The level-1 fold hid every bar of an all-weak dimension, leaving a header
with just the toggle and nothing under it. With the act door each like
starts weak, so a fresh section is often ALL level-1 — when the fold leaves
nothing visible, the weak values themselves fill the window (the
five-visible ceiling still applies). `foldByLevel` in `memory_section.dart`.

### D337: `Procura` is a mode on the feed, not a screen — the filter row morphs into the search chrome (user-directed, 2026-09-03, PROD-4179) — revises D121

- **Decision**: tapping the filter row's `Procura` circle no longer pushes `/feed/search` (D121). It puts the row itself into **Procura mode**: the circle expands slightly and grows into the search input pill, the four chips slide down a row and left onto the page margin, and the facet strips grow in underneath. Every movement reverses on exit. The route, its admin gate and `ProcuraSearchScreen` are deleted; the forked providers survive.
- **The chips carry two meanings.** At rest they write `feedFilterProvider` (which feed to compose). In Procura mode they write `procuraSearchCategoryProvider` (which corpus to search) — and, **only while nothing is typed**, the feed filter too, so the feed still visible underneath agrees with the chip above it. PROD-4081 made "picking Sítios in search cannot move the feed" true *by construction* (two enums, no path between them); this is one deliberate, query-gated hole in that, and `feed_procura_row_test.dart` pins the negative case so it cannot be widened by accident.
- **The feed stays until a query exists** (Zé). Entering changes only the chrome, which is what makes "the page must not jump" achievable. The consequence worth knowing: the pushed screen's browse grid (`DefaultContentSection` — "A acontecer" / "Perto de ti" / "Most followed") **has no role on this surface at all**, because the feed *is* the pre-query state. `Pessoas` is the exception — `ReadersSearchSection` answers the no-query case itself with suggested locals, so it takes over as soon as the category is picked, and that is why the one chip with no feed behind it (D131) needs no empty state.
- **Scroll**: entering does **not** move the page, and neither does leaving. An earlier version scrolled to the top on entry so the morph would play in the inline row; Zé tested it on device and rejected the jump. The morph plays in whichever copy of the row is on screen, and the pinned one draws the same chrome from the same builder. Exiting likewise touches nothing: in the common case the reader never moved, and when a chip moved the feed filter, `_onFilterChanged` has already applied the page's own rule (hold the offset while the row is visible, else land where the bar reveals — the top of the feed content). An exit that jumped to literal 0 fought that rule, with two post-frame callbacks racing for one position.
- **A selected facet counts as a query** (Zé, after QA). The rule started as "the feed stays until a query exists", which left the facet strips the morph brings in — time range, event categories, place types — visibly enabled and doing nothing. A tapped chip is intent, so it takes over exactly as typing does; `SearchResultsSection`'s own `shouldSearchEmptyEvents` / `shouldSearchEmptyPlaces` branches already supported the empty-query search that produces. Which facets belong to which category lives in `feedProcuraTakesOver` so the two call sites cannot disagree.
- ⚠️ **The pinned block carries the chrome only once the BAR is up**, not merely once the block has pinned. That block pins as the location *band* well before the hand-over, and drawing the search chrome into it at that moment put a second one on screen above the inline copy — D32's central invariant, broken. Reported by Zé on device; `feed_procura_mode_test.dart` reproduces it, but **only with a ready Daily Drop stub and a notch inset**: without the top-slot card the inline chrome has already scrolled away by the time the band pins, and the invariant holds for the wrong reason.
- **The pinned header's filters button always means "put the chrome away"** (Zé, after QA). Outside Procura it toggles the filter row — it only ever *opened* it before, so a reader who revealed it by accident had to tap outside or scroll to the top. Inside Procura it leaves the search *and* collapses the row, landing on the plain feed in one tap. An intermediate version hid the button in Procura mode, on the reasoning that the row it toggles is shown unconditionally there; that removed the reader's way back out of the pinned bar, so the button stays and its meaning widens instead.
- **Entering carries the current feed filter into the search category.** Opening search from the Sítios feed and finding `Eventos` selected is the search silently changing what the reader asked for. `searchCategoryForFeedFilter` is the single pairing, shared with the chips, so the selected chip and the corpus cannot disagree. It reads the feed filter and does not write it — the write only happens the other way, through the query-gated bridge above.
- **Tapping `Procura` no longer closes the filter row** — a behaviour older than this ticket. The tile pushed a route, and a row left open behind it was still there on the way back. It navigates nowhere now: it turns *that row* into the search chrome, so closing it closes the thing the reader is about to use, and the ✕ then drops them onto a bare pinned bar instead of the row they opened. `feed_filter_bar_test.dart`'s assertion is inverted rather than deleted, because a test that merely stopped checking would let the old call drift back.
- **Short results hold the reader's place rather than throwing them to the top** (Zé, after QA — he chose this over landing at the top, accepting the cost). Search results are usually far shorter than the feed they replace; the page then collapses under the viewport and `ScrollPosition` clamps the offset away. The page reserves the shortfall, the same trade the feed already makes during a filter change ("the page below should simply be empty"). ⚠️ **The reserve is DECLARED, not measured** (revised after QA, 2026-09-04). Two slivers under the results do it with no callbacks: `SliverFillRemaining(hasScrollBody: false)` pads the page out to a full viewport when the results are shorter than one (and contributes exactly zero when they are not), then a box the height of the held offset. The extent is `max(content, viewport) + held`, which puts `maxScrollExtent` at or above the held offset **by construction** — nothing is ever clamped, so nothing has to be put back.

  The first version measured instead: it let the page collapse, read the shortfall, grew a reserve and jumped back. Every symptom that produced was the cost of being reactive — the page sat at the top for a frame before the correction landed (a visible flicker), the correction had to re-run on every `ScrollMetricsNotification` because a search settles asynchronously, and re-running it meant jumping the reader back whenever metrics changed, **including the changes their own finger makes** (the results "kept being pulled back up"). All three reports came from one design choice, and all three went away with it. Both halves are mutation-checked, and the fill needs its own test: with a ritual card on screen the content above the results already exceeds a viewport, so the other tests pass with or without it. The accepted cost: under a very short result set the reader is parked below it and scrolls **up** to read it.
- ⚠️ **The pinned host now re-syncs on `ScrollMetricsNotification`, not only on its own build** (`soko_pinned_header_host.dart`). Its build-time sync covers a page that rebuilds — a filter change does — but not a *descendant* that shrinks on its own. Procura's search results settle inside their own widget; when they come back short the content collapses under the viewport, `ScrollPosition` clamps the offset during layout **without notifying listeners** (`correctPixels` deliberately does not), and `_visible` stayed latched with both chromes painted. Reported by Zé on device. This is a fix in the shared component, so every adopter gets it.
- ⚠️ **The facet strips carry their own horizontal margin**, passed as `padding` *inside* their scrollable, exactly as `SokoTagBar` does. The chrome sits in `PageContent`, which supplies no margin, so the default `EdgeInsets.zero` put them 15 px to the left of every chip above them. The pushed screen hid this by living inside a `Padding(15)` — which also meant its strips could never scroll past the margin.
- **Exit**: the ✕ leaves on the *first* tap, clearing the query on the way (`SearchOverlay.closeOnFirstClear`); the overlay hosts keep PROD-2280's two-stage ✕. A `PopScope` catches Android and browser back — no route is pushed, so without it back would leave the feed entirely from inside a search.
- **Why one widget and not a cross-fade**: the chips are the same four `SokoTagChip`s at both ends, so a cross-fade would draw each of them twice, slightly offset — ghosting, not motion. They keep element identity and move instead. The one deliberate asymmetry is that the circle sits *inside* the scrolling row at `t == 0` (so it scrolls with the chips on a narrow phone) and is lifted out from the first frame of the morph; the row is scrolled back to 0 as the morph starts so "where the circle was" is answerable at all.
- **Dragging the feed puts the keyboard away; tapping the field brings it back** (Zé, on device, 2026-09-07). Typing and then scrolling used to leave the on-screen keyboard covering the results the reader was scrolling toward. `ShellSliverHost` now takes a `keyboardDismissBehavior` and the feed passes `onDrag`; every other page that mounts the host keeps the framework default (`manual`).

  Two properties worth recording because they are the reason this is three lines rather than a platform branch. The framework's handler fires only when `notification.dragDetails != null`, so **a mouse wheel or trackpad scroll dismisses nothing** — desktop web keeps focus while scrolling with no `kIsWeb` gate. And the listener sits above the whole scrollable, so a nested horizontal scrollable (the chip row inside this very chrome) also dismisses; swiping the chips putting the keyboard away is a fair reading of the gesture, so it is left alone rather than filtered by depth.

  ⚠️ **Two `TextField`s share one `FocusNode` here, and that is NOT the bug it looks like.** Both copies of the chrome stay mounted once laid out, so by the time a drag has happened the inline field and the pinned field are both alive on one node and one controller. Tapping the pinned copy still focuses it and the typed text survives — measured, not assumed, and pinned by `feed_procura_keyboard_test.dart`. The first design for this change was a focus-node split to "fix" that sharing; a probe showed there was nothing to fix.

- **Where it lives**: [`feed_procura_row.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_procura_row.dart) (the morph), [`feed_filter_bar.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_filter_bar.dart) (the two meanings), [`discovery_feed_v2_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/screens/discovery_feed_v2_screen.dart) (mode, scroll, body), [`soko_search_input_pill.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_search_input_pill.dart) (the pill, extracted so the overlay and the morph draw one thing).
- ⚠️ **D121's stated reason is now false in one clause.** It kept the category pills off the feed's row partly because "that row is being deprecated" — the row is instead the surface this whole feature is built on. Its other clause stands and got stronger: `Procura` cannot appear on the chrome it opens, because it *becomes* it.
