<!-- Previous: D336, D335, D334, D333, D332 -->

### D338: A slow feed gets a breathing illustration and a line naming what it is looking for, not a spinner (user-directed, 2026-09-07) — revises D316

- **Decision**: while the feed is composing, the page shows the `feed_end` card's shape minus its button — illustration, `kFeedPageBlockGap`, one centred `Mobile/H3` 26 — in place of the bare `CircularProgressIndicator`. The backend's personalised compose can take **up to ~10 s**, and ten seconds of a spinner is indistinguishable from a hang.
- **The copy names the entity** (Zé, verbatim): Eventos "A descobrir eventos novos para ti...", Sítios "À procura de sítios especiais...", Zines "As melhores zines estão quase a sair...". "We are finding things for you" only reassures when it says *what*. `Pessoas` has a line only so the switch over `FeedFilter` stays exhaustive — it has no feed to load (D131) and cannot reach this widget.
- **The illustration cycles, one drawing per breath, in a per-mount random order** (Zé). Five ink figures take turns: `soko-binoculars`, `personas/local_at_heart`, `soko-with-camera`, `personas/conscious_outdoors`, `soko-with-wine`. All five already ship for other surfaces, so this cost no new binaries. The persona figures are the ones in `assets/images/personas/`, **not** the `personas/cards/` siblings — those are the 880 × 994 tilted paper-card stacks and would letterbox in a 115 px slot.
- **The breath goes all the way to transparent, and the swap happens at the trough.** A shallower pulse would leave the cut between two drawings visible as a hard jump at 45 % opacity. One consequence worth stating: the pulse is therefore the *only* motion — there is no spinner underneath, because two things animating at different rates for one wait is one too many.
- **Shuffled once at mount, not re-picked at each swap.** Drawing at random every time would let the same figure come up twice in a row, which reads as the animation having stalled — the exact impression the element exists to prevent. A permutation cannot repeat before it wraps, and wrapping needs `5 × 2 × 1.4 s` = 14 s, past the worst wait.
- ⚠️ **It is drawn inside D316's blank reserve, NOT in the page's `loading:` branch** — and this is the part that is invisible in the code. D316 reserves a full viewport of blank *before* the feed slivers to hold `maxScrollExtent` open during a filter change. Anything rendered in the `loading:` branch therefore sits one whole viewport below the fold, off-screen for the entire wait, which is precisely the case a loading state exists for. So the reserve carries the element (top-aligned, where the first block would have gone) and the `loading:` branch is gated on `_filterChangeScrollTarget == null` so the two are never both mounted. The reserve's child is also gated on `feed.isLoading`, because the target is cleared by a `ref.listen` that runs *after* build — without it, one frame after the data lands would paint both.
- **This revises D316's "the page below should simply be empty while the new feed is loading."** That held while the wait was short. It no longer does at ten seconds, and the blank is now the loading state's canvas rather than the design.
- ⚠️ **Two of the five drawings are ~1× at the rendered 115 px and will be soft on a 3× screen**: `soko-binoculars.png` is 134 × 124 and `soko-with-wine.png` is 107 × 113. The camera (288²) and the persona figures (400 px) are fine. `figma-cache/screens/discovery/feed-v2-blocks.md` already flags the wine export as needing a crisper version from design; the binoculars has the same problem and now has a second call site.
- **Reduce Motion** holds the first drawing still at full opacity, with no rotation. The line already says the feed is loading, so nothing is lost.
- **Where it lives**: [`feed_loading_state.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_loading_state.dart), mounted twice by [`discovery_feed_v2_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/screens/discovery_feed_v2_screen.dart) (the reserve, and the cold-load branch). The status-listener mechanics behind the pulse are in [`learnings/animation-repeat-reverse-reports-no-turning-point.md`](../learnings/animation-repeat-reverse-reports-no-turning-point.md).

### D339: The feed's friends social-proof line reads "interested", is gated per-bundle by the backend, and shows an initial when a friend has no photo (user-directed, 2026-09-08)

- **Copy**: the per-row social line on bundle event rows now reads *interest*, not *going* — "{name} is interested" / "{a} and {b} are interested" / "{name} + N people interested" (PT "tem interesse" / "têm interesse"). The underlying signal is a like OR a save, never an RSVP, so "vai/going" overstated it. New ARB keys `feedInterestedOne/Two/WithOthers` replace `feedGoing*`; the widget/class/data names (`FeedGoingRow`, the `going` side-map, `FeedGoingEntry`) keep the backend contract's word and are unchanged — only the display copy moved.
- **Gating**: whether the line appears at all is the **backend's** call per bundle, via a new `show_social_proof` boolean on the `bundle` block (`FeedBlockBundle`, default false). The client draws the line only when `show_social_proof` is true **AND** the page's `going` map names a friend for that row's event; otherwise each row independently falls back to its plain venue line. It is set true on the time bundles (Happening today / Ending soon / Free this week), Venues-with-events and Workshops, and false elsewhere. No effect on `venue`/`zine` bundles — the line is an event-row concept, and the `going` map is keyed by event id only.
- **Avatar**: a friend with no profile photo is no longer dropped from the avatar cluster. `MutualAvatarsRow` gains an opt-in `people` list that draws a seeded initial placeholder (the `TexturedAvatar` tint + first letter, drawn circular to match the cluster) for a photoless person; the legacy photo-only `avatars` path is unchanged for its other callers (profile Locals, the zine follow-line).
- ⚠️ **The zine callers stay on `avatars` deliberately — reaffirmed 2026-09-09, user-directed. Do not "fix" them to `people`.** Both zine clusters ([`LibraryZineFollowerProof`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/widgets/library_zine_follower_proof.dart) on the library row, [`ListFollowersInline`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/lists/widgets/list_followers_inline.dart) on the zine page) intentionally **drop** a follower with no photo. It reads as a bug — a cluster showing two faces next to "{name} +5 follow", or no faces at all when none of the top-3 have photos — and was migrated to `people` in a session before being reverted on that decision. Two consequences follow from it:
  - **The `+` overflow badge on a zine cluster is intended.** `MutualAvatarsRow` computes `hasMore = totalCount > shown.length`, comparing the caller's preview count against its own **post-filter** list. A dropped follower surfaces as that badge — i.e. "more people than faces" — which is correct under this decision, not a leak. (`ListFollowersInline`'s "suppress the + badge" comment describes the all-photos case.)
  - **The real fix is server-side ranking, and it is the whole fix.** If the cluster only ever shows people who have photos, ranking the preview by recency alone is exactly what leaves it empty. Both backend paths (`library_projection._follower_previews` and `get_list_followers`) order by `followed_at DESC` with no `avatar_url` preference — tracked in **PROD-4317**.
- ⚠️ **Rewording a shipped string is a key *rename*, not a value edit.** `check-l10n-integrity.py --check values` blocks any change to an existing pt/pt_BR/es value (only the linguist may, via `/translate-update`), so editing `feedGoing*` in place would fail CI. Adding new `feedInterested*` keys and removing the old ones passes both the value guard and the parity check, because new keys are additions and removals are not "modified values". `intl_en.arb` is the one ARB exempt from the value guard.
- **Where it lives**: [`feed_going_row.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/blocks/feed_going_row.dart) (the line + copy), [`feed_bundle_row.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/blocks/feed_bundle_row.dart) (the per-bundle `socialProof` gate), [`mutual_avatars_row.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/profile/widgets/mutual_avatars_row.dart) (the initials fallback). Backend: `show_social_proof` rides on `FeedBlockBundle`, set per `LayoutElement` in `feed_layout.py` and copied through the composer's bundle builders.

### D340: The event relative-timing chip reasons from the whole occurrence set — recurring, exhibition phases, past — and reveals after the image loads (user-directed, 2026-09-08)

- **Problem**: the `RotatedDateTag` chip (event detail hero + title, chat/onboarding cards, zine list card) was derived from a **single** start date (`eventRelativeTagLabel` / `eventRelativeTagIsPast`, or a flat `date`). A multi-occurrence event whose first occurrence had passed — or a backend `startAt` that falls back to the earliest occurrence — read as red **"Past event"** even with upcoming dates.
- **Fix**: one shared resolver, [`resolveEventTimingChip`](../../heyl_app/lib/core/utils/event_timing_chip.dart), classifies from the timing *shape* (spans + authoritative `isPast`) into: **Past event** (red), **Recurring event** (neutral — >1 discrete upcoming run), an **exhibition phase** for a single continuous multi-day run (**First day** / **First day tomorrow** / **Opens next week** — Soko/Yellow; **Final week** (closes ≤7 days) / **Last day** — Soko/Red; mid-run → no chip), or the existing single relative label. Exhibition-vs-recurring pivots on run count: one multi-day span = exhibition, many spans = recurring. Per-model adapters feed each surface's data in; surfaces with only a flat start (onboarding) degrade to single/past cleanly.
- **Follow-up (2026-09-08, user-directed): "Final week" last-chance phase.** A long run that is closing within the next 7 days but is **not** yet on its final day now reads **"Final week"** (PT/ES **"Última semana"**), Soko/Red, sitting between `Last day` and mid-run in the exhibition branch. Because it lives in `resolveEventTimingChip` it renders on every surface via the existing `RotatedDateTag` + per-model adapters — no new widget or per-surface wiring. (A first pass built this as a parallel keyword-matched pill on `CardTagRow`; it was reworked onto the shared resolver — see the learning below.)
- **Follow-up (2026-09-11, user-reported): the opening phase is bounded to real calendar-week windows.** The "opens later" arm was `firstDay.isAfter(today) → "Opens next week"` with **no upper bound**, so an exhibition opening months out (an Oct 15–25 event viewed on Sep 11) still wore "Opens next week" — and one opening in 2 days wore it too, even though that's *this* week. It now shares the single-day ladder's windows: rest of the current week → **"Opens this week"** (new key `eventTimingOpensThisWeek`, all 4 ARBs), the next calendar week → **"Opens next week"**, further out → **no chip** (the absolute date row carries it) — so every relative ladder in the system goes silent at the same horizon. Same ship fixed the Sunday weekend edge in `eventRelativeTagLabel`/`formatEventStickerDate` (`% 7` mapped Sunday's "coming Saturday" to *next* week's, mislabelling it "This weekend") and made the null relative label an explicit `EventTimingChip.none()`. See [`relative-date-windows-need-both-bounds.md`](../learnings/relative-date-windows-need-both-bounds.md).
- **Colour language** (FE choice, mirrors [`RecurrencePhaseChip`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/recurrence_phase_chip.dart)): novelty → Soko/Yellow, last-chance / past → Soko/Red, everything else neutral Soko/Paper; text always Soko/Ink. `RotatedDateTag` gains an optional `background` + `backgroundFor(EventChipStyle)`; the legacy `isPast` red/paper default is untouched.
- **Reveal-on-load**: the chip's stamp-in now waits for the card photo to paint — `CachedImage.onLoaded` (via `imageBuilder` / `frameBuilder`) threads up through `SokoCardImage` → `SokoPhotoCollage.cornerBadgeBuilder(imageLoaded)` and the stateful `SokoEntityCard`, gating `RotatedDateTag.active`. A 2.5 s fallback timer (and a no-image visibility fallback) guarantees a 404/absent photo never hides the chip.
- **Feed image "snap"**: the bundle-row thumbs now use the shared `SokoCardImage` brand-floor (type-colour reveal) instead of a flat grey `ColoredBox` that popped to the photo; the hero poster's placeholder is brand-tinted for the same reason. Every box was already fixed-size — no layout/aspect change.
- **New ARB keys**: `eventTimingRecurring`, `eventTimingFirstDay`, `eventTimingFirstDayTomorrow`, `eventTimingOpensNextWeek`, `eventTimingLastDay`, `eventTimingFinalWeek` (all 4 ARBs; additions-only, es-MX MT untouched). Distinct from the plural `recurrencePhaseNew`/`recurrencePhaseLastDays` ("First days"/"Last days"), which carry different semantics.
- **Reminder button doesn't pop in**: the event-detail action row's reminder bell used to be omitted while the tapped-item seed shell painted (occurrences unknown) and then appear when the network detail landed — a visible pop. `EventDetailSnapshot` gains an `isHydrating` flag (set true by `EventDetailScreen._buildLoading`'s seed snapshot); the row now renders the bell **disabled** (dimmed, inert, no hint/spotlight) during hydration, reserving its slot, and flips it to enabled once occurrences resolve. Still omitted only on a fully-loaded past-only event. The other cells (Save / Share / 👍 / 👎) already act on the event id and stay live in the shell.

### D344: The pinned filter row scrolls with the feed, closes on scroll, and grows out from under the bar (PROD-4316, user-directed, 2026-09-09)

- **Choice:** three rules for the filter row the pinned header opens. (1) **The feed scrolls normally while the row is open** — it used to be frozen. (2) **A scroll closes the row.** (3) **The row grows down from under the bar and collapses back up**, on the header's own 240 ms `easeInOut` rather than appearing in one frame.
- **Why (1) was broken:** D38's tap-outside-to-dismiss affordance is a `Positioned.fill > GestureDetector` over the whole page, and it was `HitTestBehavior.opaque` — which *prevents targets visually behind it from receiving events*, so the feed's scrollable never saw a pointer at all. `translucent` receives events **and** lets them through; the barrier's tap recogniser and the scrollable's drag recogniser then both enter the gesture arena, which resolves it correctly on its own (stationary press → tap, past touch slop → drag). See [`learnings/an-opaque-dismiss-barrier-freezes-the-scrollable-behind-it.md`](../learnings/an-opaque-dismiss-barrier-freezes-the-scrollable-behind-it.md).
- **The barrier STAYS, and (2) does not replace it.** Tap-outside and scroll-to-close are different affordances: a reader who wants the row gone *without* moving the feed still needs the tap. Deleting the barrier would have made the drag work while silently removing D38.
- **(2) is gated on `userScrollDirection != idle`** — the reader's scroll, not the page's. `_onFilterChanged` animates the offset after a filter change, and D38 already closes the row on selection, so an ungated version would be the *programmatic* scroll doing the closing: invisible in the common case and wrong in the uncommon one. It sits **above** `_onScroll`'s paging guards, which return early for reasons unrelated to the row and would otherwise leave it open exactly where it is most in the way.
- **(3) reuses the shape already in this header** — `ClipRect > Align(topCenter, heightFactor)`, which is how [`FeedLocationBand`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_location_band.dart) folds the scallop away as the band becomes the bar. One pattern for "chrome that collapses", not two. `_headerChrome` takes an animated `openT` in place of the `filtersOpen` bool; Procura pins the row to 1 rather than animating it, because there the row is the surface rather than an affordance hanging off the header.
- **The gap above the row animates with it.** `SokoPinnedHeaderBlock` lays out `SizedBox(height: gap)` *then* the widget, so a static gap opens 15 px of empty paper before the row has begun to appear. Both scale with `openT`.
- **The reveal threshold is untouched by all of this** — it is computed at `openT: 0` by construction (`_headerChrome(1, openT: 0, …).heightFor(...)`), so the offset at which the bar takes over cannot move.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** a feed that does not scroll while the filter row is open is a regression; so is a row that appears or vanishes in one frame, or a gap that opens before the row does.

### D343: Business Connect "Find more" builds from the live design system, not the mockup's pink bar — and Google rows merge in unattributed (PROD-4270 S5, user-directed, 2026-09-08)

- **Context**: S5 adds a manual **"Find more"** tier to the Business Connect venue search (`business_venue_search.dart`): after the local (Meilisearch) autocomplete settles, the owner can run a transient Google name search whose deduplicated candidates append to the results. The S5 mockup drew this as a full-width **pink "Find more" bar**, an uppercase **"MATCHING VENUES / n matches"** eyebrow over the local rows, a separate **"Additional matches"** group for the Google rows, a **"Results from Google Maps"** attribution line, and Zalando Sans type.
- **Decision — the mockup is a layout reference, not a component spec.** Build from existing widgets and tokens only:
  - **Find more action** = the area picker's load-more row, extracted to a shared [`SokoLoadMoreRow`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_load_more_row.dart) (`sokoShade5` tint, `sokoInk` 15/600 label, 48 px min height, `Semantics(button)`), used in both the area picker and here so the two deep tiers look identical. **Not** the pink full-width bar.
  - **No eyebrow header** over the local rows (no uppercase "MATCHING VENUES / n matches").
  - App-theme typography throughout; **no Zalando Sans**, no new token or component families.
- **Decision — Google rows are fully merged, unattributed (user-directed).** The Google candidates append into the **same** list as the local rows, one `_CandidateRow` style, with **no "Additional matches" section header** and **no "Results from Google Maps" attribution line**. Rationale: the app already surfaces un-attributed Google-Places fallback rows elsewhere (`searchPlaces` mixes local + Google rows in the list/contribution pickers with no credit line), and these read as regular backend venues; a lone attribution line here would be inconsistent. The S4 response still carries a mandatory `attribution` string — it is parsed for contract fidelity ([`PortalGoogleSearchResponse`](../../heyl_app/lib/data/models/business_portal.dart)) but deliberately not rendered. If Google's Places ToS is later enforced app-wide, revisit here and at the existing `searchPlaces` surfaces together.
- **What the mockup's states became**: the load-more row is the idle affordance; in flight it swaps to a `sokoPink` spinner + a muted "Finding more venues…"; a complete-empty response shows a muted "No other venues found."; partial/failure show a muted message + a ghost `SokoCtaButton` **Retry**; a 429 shows a muted cooldown notice (disabled until `Retry-After` elapses). All status lines are `Semantics(liveRegion)`.
- **Location bias (PROD-4268 S3 integration)**: the Google search reads the same `businessSearchAreaProvider` the local search uses, forwarding `latitude`/`longitude` only when a full pair is known — a bias, never a restriction.

### D347: The Business Connect venue finder is keyboard- and screen-reader-operable — scope-gated panel, arrow-key highlight with an ink bar, Enter activates the highlight (PROD-4295, 2026-09-09)

- **Context**: the PROD-4041 staging validation showed the claim finder's results panel (`business_venue_search.dart`) was gated on the text field's own focus, so Tab could never reach a result row or **Find more**, and a screen-reader activation of a row unmounted the panel and discarded the Google-tier state. Spec AC11 (§4.2, PROD-4270 plan) requires keyboard traversal, a highlighted-row Enter, and screen-reader announcements.
- **Decision — the panel is gated on the finder as a whole.** A scope `Focus` node wraps field, submit, area row, paste link and panel; the panel shows while focus is anywhere inside it. Tab order is field → submit → area → paste → rows → Find more. The existing `TextFieldTapRegion` stays for pointer-down. The current query's local and Google-tier providers are held alive by the finder (not the panel) and the Google tier is reset when the search area changes.
- **Decision — combobox keys, one highlight treatment.** ArrowDown/ArrowUp move a highlight across the rows and the footer action (Find more / Retry), clamped at the ends; Enter activates the highlighted item through the field's own `onSubmitted` (no second Enter handler), otherwise Enter still submits the query; Escape still dismisses. The highlight is a **`sokoShade5` wash + 3 px `sokoInk` leading bar** on candidate rows and the **bar alone on [`SokoLoadMoreRow`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_load_more_row.dart)** (new `highlighted` param) and the Retry ghost button, so the state never relies on colour alone and reads on the already-tinted load-more row. Highlighted items are `selected` + a live region so a screen reader announces where the highlight landed, and scroll into view. No new tokens; the same treatment is the candidate for any future keyboard-navigable list.
- **Decision — the area row is a button.** "Search area: … · Change / Choose area" was focusable but had no role; it now carries `Semantics(button: true)`.
- **Not changed**: pointer behaviour, the Find more state machine and copy (D343), the design-system components used (D343).

### D348: Business Connect "Find more" never searches Google without a country — the area chooser opens instead (PROD-4296, 2026-09-09)

- **Context**: with **Search area: Not set**, the Google tier (D343) sent neither coordinates nor `region_code`, so Google biased by the *backend host's* IP and a Mexican owner got US studios. The spec (§4.3) allowed either "use a chosen city/country as query context" or "unscoped search with a visible area chooser".
- **Decision — bias first, ask second.** The request always carries the best country the app knows, in one derived value: the search area's own country (picked boundary / device fix / Discovery centre), else the IP-detected country, else the app locale's region (`es-MX` → `MX`). Only when all three are unknown (a bare `en` locale, no IP detection, no area) does tapping **Find more** open the **Search area** chooser (the S3 sheet) instead of searching; the attempt is not spent and the row stays. No new copy: the chooser already explains itself.
- **Decision — a region change resets Find more like an area change.** The same provider feeds the request and the reset listener, so a late IP detection that agrees with what was sent does not discard results, and one that disagrees does.
- **Not changed**: local (Meilisearch) search still sends coordinates only when a full pair is known (D343 / PROD-4268); a country never restricts either tier.

### D341: The feed bundle → see-all open moves the whole block as one, and detail mini-maps pop in (user-directed, 2026-09-08)

- **Bundle → see-all is now a container transform of the *whole block*, not a per-thumbnail flight.** Tapping a bundle's title/arrow opens its see-all page by sliding the whole group — centred title + the rows the bundle was already showing — up into place from exactly where the block sat on the feed. Previously each row thumbnail flew on its own (`Hero` per thumbnail), which read as images snapping/deforming across the screen independently of the title and text; the user's directive was "move everything as a whole, not just the images."
- **It is hand-rolled, not a `Hero`.** The bundle rows are already `Hero`s (their thumbnail flies into the item detail), and Flutter forbids a `Hero` inside a `Hero`, so a single group Hero is impossible — it asserts on the feed itself. Instead the feed block records its on-screen rect on tap and the see-all page slides its matching group from that rect (translation only — widths match, so no scale/distortion), over an opaque push. See the learning [`group-container-transform-when-children-are-heroes.md`](../learnings/group-container-transform-when-children-are-heroes.md).
- **Staged reveal.** The highlighted rows are floated to the top so they match what the feed showed; after the slide lands, the **result count** and the **results beyond the highlighted ones** pop in (fade + spring scale, staggered) *under* the rows already on screen. The row → detail poster flight is preserved — the rows' tags are armed a beat after open and dropped on the back pop, so nothing flies between feed and see-all.
- **Detail mini-map pops in.** On venue/event detail the bottom mini-map now enters with a `SokoPopIn` (fade + gentle scale, ~180 ms delay) when it mounts, instead of snapping in — the last piece of the page to arrive gets a small, deliberate entrance. Skipped in the onboarding preview (`sandbox`). Note: the map is a live platform view, so if the scale reads rough on a device, fall back to fade-only (platform views don't reliably honor `Opacity`/`Transform` — see the map container-transform caveat in [`gorouter-notransitionpage-suppresses-hero-flight.md`](../learnings/gorouter-notransitionpage-suppresses-hero-flight.md)).
- **Where it lives**: [`feed_bundle_see_all_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/screens/feed_bundle_see_all_screen.dart) (the slide + staged reveal), [`feed_bundle_hero.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/feed_bundle_hero.dart) (the source-rect provider + rationale), [`feed_bundle_block.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/widgets/blocks/feed_bundle_block.dart) (rect capture on open), [`event_detail_body.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/event_detail/widgets/event_detail_body.dart) / [`venue_detail_body.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/venue_detail/widgets/venue_detail_body.dart) (map pop-in).

### D342: The bundle detail / see-more headers use the large-title page top rhythm (safe-area + 27), not the tighter 8 (user-directed, 2026-09-08)

- **Choice**: the feed **bundle detail** ([`feed_bundle_see_all_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/feed_v2/screens/feed_bundle_see_all_screen.dart)) and its twin ([`shelf_see_more_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/discovery/screens/shelf_see_more_screen.dart)) — both a centred 32 px display title with a back arrow — now sit **safe-area + 27** from the top, matching Library's large-title page. They were at **safe-area + 8**, visibly tighter than the rest of the app.
- **Why 27, not 15**: the app has two header families. The pinned 40 px bar ([`SokoPinnedHeaderBlock`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_pinned_header_block.dart), Discovery) sits at safe-area + `kSokoPageMargin` (15); the **large-title** pages (Library, and now these two) sit at 27. The bundle detail is a large-title page, so it matches Library, not the pinned block.
- **Kept in sync deliberately**: the two screens are pixel-twins by design (their own code comments protect this), so the top padding is changed in both together — a shared comment on each `Padding` says so.

<!-- Most recent: D346 -->
<!-- Previous: D345, D344, D343, D342, D341 -->

### D337: Chip display labels are generated from the backend taxonomy (user-directed, 2026-09-09)

"gosto de futebol" rendered a "Football" chip: the hand-curated display map
carried only 11 sub-category slugs, so ~90 listed slugs and every open
proposal fell to the mechanical English tail. The `event_categories` and
`event_sub_categories` blocks of `_displayLabels` are now generated from the
backend taxonomy (`SUB_CATEGORY_LABELS`/`CATEGORY_LABELS`: 100+18 entries ×
en/pt-PT/pt-BR/es-MX), overlaid with approved curated wording, plus localized
entries for common open proposals (sport-football → Futebol). The DB keeps
canonical slugs only; the label resolves at render from device locale.
Regenerate when the taxonomy moves.

**Venue layer (same decision, 2026-09-09 evening):** the `venue_types` family
got the same treatment with a different mechanism, because the backend has no
label dictionary for its 406 venue slugs (unlike events). Two layers in
`memory_value_label.dart`: ~90 `<x>_restaurant` slugs compose "Restaurante
<adjectivo>" from a nationality/style adjective map (`_restaurantAdjectives`,
pt-PT/pt-BR; EN keeps the mechanical form, which is already correct), and ~85
common venues are curated with the *idiomatic* PT noun rather than a literal
translation (seafood_restaurant → Marisqueira, ice_cream_shop →
Gelataria/Sorveteria, viewpoint → Miradouro/Mirante, gym → Ginásio/Academia,
nightclub → Discoteca/Balada). Slugs outside both layers still fall to the
mechanical EN tail — never worse than before. **User rule: the word
"workshop" is never translated**, anywhere in chip labels — the ambiguous-tail
composer keeps it ("Workshop de arte").

### D346: The library zine row's follower cluster gets its own line under the chips, not the end of the chip row (user-directed, 2026-09-09)

- **Problem**: [`LibraryItemRow`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/widgets/library_item_row.dart) appended the follower cluster ([`LibraryZineFollowerProof`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/widgets/library_zine_follower_proof.dart)) to the **end of the first metadata chip line**, wrapped in a `Flexible`. It competed for width with the curator chip, the visibility chip and the page count — and lost. At 430 px the `{name} +N follow` text did not render at all (avatars only); at 1280/1600 px it truncated to `Ti…`, and the curator chip truncated with it (`Edt. Ma…`). Widening the viewport did not help: the library list is a fixed centred column, so the extra width goes to the margin, not the row.
- **Choice**: the cluster is now its own child of the row's metadata `Column`, 8 px under the chips, at full width. The parameter was renamed `trailingMeta` → `trailingLine` because the old name and its doc comment ("Same meta line, after the chips") described the opposite of the new behaviour; the now-dead `trailing` slot was dropped from the private `_MetaLine`.
- **Row height is unchanged at 80 px** (`FeedBundleRow.rowHeight`). Event rows already render two metadata lines at that height, so the vertical budget was proven before the change rather than negotiated — this is a three-line diff, not a chrome resize.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** on a `/library` zine row, the follower cluster on a **second line** below the chips is correct. The cluster sharing the chip line — or a truncated `Ti…` / a curator chip cut to `Edt. Ma…` — is the regression. <!-- Superseded in part by D349: the first line is now `Edt. … · 📖 Np` only — the visibility chip moved down to join the cluster's line. Audit against D349, not against the `🌐 Public` shown in earlier revisions of this entry. -->
- **Where it lives**: [`library_item_row.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/widgets/library_item_row.dart) (`trailingLine` + the `Column` child), [`library_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/screens/library_screen.dart) (the zine row builder's call site).

### D349: The library zine row's visibility line answers "who can see this, and who follows it" — one line, three states (user-directed, 2026-09-10, PROD-4345) — extends D346

- **Problem**: D346 moved the follower cluster onto its own line but left the **visibility chip** on the first line, competing with the curator chip and the page count. That first line then carried three unrelated things (who made it, who can see it, how big it is), and a public zine could show `🌐 Public` on line one while its followers sat on line two — the same fact stated twice, split across two lines.
- **Choice**: visibility leaves the chip row and owns the second line, which now answers one question with three mutually exclusive answers:

  | Zine state | Second line |
  |---|---|
  | Private (or the retired `followers`) | `🔒 Private` |
  | Public, feed sent `follower_preview` | the faces cluster + `{name} +N follow` |
  | Public, `follower_count > 0` but no preview | `🔖 N` |
  | Public, no followers | `🌐 Public` |
  | Unknown visibility from the feed | **no second line** |

  The first line is `Edt. <curator> · 📖 Np`.
- **Followers replace `Public`, they do not sit beside it.** A zine with followers is self-evidently public, so printing `🌐 Public` next to the faces states the same fact twice.
- **Unknown visibility drops the line rather than guessing.** `libraryZineVisibility` returns null for a value it does not recognise; the old chip was hidden in that case and the new line is too.
- **Row height is unchanged at 80 px.** A zine row could already reach two lines under D346 (chips + `trailingLine`); it still tops out at two. The private and no-follower states use the second `metaLine` slot instead of `trailingLine` — same 8 px gap, same `_MetaLine` styling, no new component.
- **Backend limit worth knowing before auditing:** the middle state is currently **unreachable on the viewer's own zines**. `library_projection.py` nulls both `follower_count` and `follower_preview` for owned rows, so your own public zine renders `🌐 Public` however many followers it has. It works on zines you follow. Tracked as [PROD-4338](https://linear.app/heyl/issue/PROD-4338); the frontend needs no change when that lands.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`:** the first line carrying a visibility chip is now the **regression**, not the baseline — D346's older phrasing showed `Edt. … · 🌐 Public · 📖 Np` and no longer describes intent. A private zine showing a follower count is also a regression (private says `Private`, full stop).
- **Where it lives**: [`library_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/screens/library_screen.dart) — the `_zineRow` builder; the branch picks a `MetaChip` for the second `metaLine` or a widget for `trailingLine`.
### D350: The zine List view's sort chip docks to the first section header, and an event-ordered sort promotes Eventos above Sítios (user-directed, 2026-09-10, no ticket)

- **Problem**: server-side item sorting (backend v1.128.0) needed a control in the List view, and Figma has no frame for one. The first two attempts both gave the chip a **full-width row of its own** above the sections — first left-aligned, then right-aligned. Right-aligning moved one small control across a row it still owned outright: on desktop that rendered as a wide empty band between the action row and the description, the widest empty thing on the page. The user rejected it with a screenshot: *"you're not saving space."*
- **Choice**: delete the row. `ListViewSection` and its sliver counterpart gained a `trailing` slot, and the chip rides the **first rendered** section header — a band that already existed with its whole right side empty. The band grows only by the few px the chip out-heights the 18 px title, instead of costing a title-height + control-height + padding row.
  - **First *rendered*, not first declared**: an empty section returns before claiming the slot, so a zine with no places still gets the chip on Eventos.
  - `Expanded`, not `Spacer`, for the title — a long section name must ellipsize rather than shove the control off the right edge.
- **Also**: under `sort=chronological` the **Eventos** section is promoted above Sítios (`ZineItemSort.ordersByEventDate` → `listSectionOrder()`). The server has no date for a place and sinks places to the end of its ordering, so leaving Sítios on top buries the rows the user just asked to order beneath a block the sort never touched. Expressed as a property on the enum so a future event-centric sort inherits it rather than needing a second edit.
- **Guests never see the chip**: the gated body shows a fixed unblurred prefix (⌈N/3⌉ rows), so resorting would only shuffle which rows they are teased with.
- **No direction arrow**, deliberately. A local reversal of the loaded rows is a *different list* from a server `direction` — under `chronological` the server ranks an event by its LAST occurrence rather than its next, and under `recent` it breaks `added_at` ties on `sort_order` then `id`, which a reversal flips (45,126 of 51,501 production lists carry such ties). Shipping an approximation under a control that will later mean the exact thing is worse than shipping no control. The ask is recorded in [`../openapi-gaps.md`](../openapi-gaps.md); the chip is already positioned with room beside it.
- **Where it lives**: `features/lists/widgets/list_view_mode/{zine_item_sort_menu,list_view_section,list_view_mode_body}.dart`, `data/models/zine_item_sort.dart`.
- **Labels diverge from the wire words on purpose**: `recent` renders as "Recently added", not the Biblioteca sort bar's bare "Recent". On `/users/me/library` recency folds in follows and the latest client `view_item`; inside a zine it means exactly one thing — `user_list_items.added_at`, stamped once at insert with no `onupdate`, so a tip edit never floats an item. `chronological` renders as "By event date" rather than "By date", which never said *which* date.


### D351: The library calendar toggle flips its glyph to a list when open, and its loading spinner centres in the viewport (agent-proposed, user-shipped, 2026-09-11, no ticket)

- **Figma** (`Soko_Library` `7660:30303`) specs only the closed state: a 40 px circle with a 14 px calendar glyph in the header's leading slot. It says nothing about the open state, so the open-state affordance is ours.
- **Choice**: while the whole-library calendar is showing, the same button renders `LucideIcons.list` (semantic label `libraryCalendarCloseSemantic`). A static calendar glyph on the active view reads as a dead button; the list glyph names the destination, matching how the button behaved to get here.
- **Spinner**: the calendar body's first load centres its spinner in the viewport below the header (`MediaQuery` height minus ≈320 px of chrome, floored at 200), not tucked under the title — user-directed ("move the loading spinner to the center of the page"). Reopens never show it: the provider is keep-alive and repaints the parked set while silently revalidating.
- **Where it lives**: `features/library/screens/library_screen.dart` (header + `_LibraryCalendarBody`), `features/library/providers/library_calendar_provider.dart`.


### D352: Library search carries the same category chips as the feed, and deliberately ignores the library filter bar (user-directed, 2026-09-14, no ticket)

- **Problem**: of the three content search surfaces that share one stack ([unified-search.md](../features/unified-search.md)), the library was the only one without the `Events · Places · Zines · People` chip row — `library_screen.dart` passed `showCategoryChips: false`. The reasoning was that the library's own filter bar (Eventos/Sítios/Zines/Pessoas + Futuros/Passados/Data + zine membership) was already the scope control, and `LibraryUnifiedSearchSource` baked that whole `LibraryFilter` into its `GET /users/me/library?q=` query. Side by side, the feed's search and the library's read as two different features.
- **The part that actually bit**: a scope control living *outside* the search panel silently subtracts from the results. Open search from `Eventos → Passados` and a saved place you can plainly see one tab over does not come back, with nothing on screen explaining why — the control doing the filtering is behind the dim barrier.
- **Choice**: same chips everywhere. The library now inherits the default `showCategoryChips`, and `LibraryUnifiedSearchSource` honours the overlay's `categories` (one chip → that `types` value, none → every type) instead of a `LibraryFilter`.
  - **Chips always open with none selected**, rather than seeding from the tab you searched from. Searching the library means searching all of it; the tab is a browsing position, not a question about what you are looking for.
  - **The tab's sub-filters (Futuros/Passados/Data, zine membership) are dropped during search too.** Half-carrying the filter — category from the chips, dates from the bar — is the confusing middle: the panel would show one scope and apply another.
  - **Search never mutates the tab.** Closing the overlay leaves the library exactly as it was; the chips are overlay-local state on `UnifiedContentSearch`.
- **Onboarding needed no change** — it never passed the flag, so it already had the chips. The library was the only fork.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`**: a library search panel with no chip row under the input is the pre-change state.
- **Where it lives**: `features/library/screens/library_screen.dart` (the `librarySearchOpenProvider` listener), `features/library/search/library_search_source.dart`. The chip row itself is shared: `shared/widgets/search/unified_content_search_view.dart`.


### D353: Only the zine page → item detail open carries a shared-element flight; every other page opens from the side (user-directed, 2026-09-15, no ticket)

- **Problem**: over PROD-4160 and its follow-ups the shared-element ("the image flies to where it lands") open spread to nearly every page change — feed hero card → detail, feed venue grid tile → detail, feed/see-all bundle row → detail, Library row → detail, Library/feed thumbnail → zine cover. Each was a good idea on its own. Together they made the flight the *default*, and a default is not a signal: the user's verdict was to *"revert all those"*.
- **Choice**: one page open flies, the **zine item card → in-list detail** — the poster and the card's background colour panel move together into the detail's collage and coloured region. It earns it because the zine page is the one surface where the card you tapped *is* a composed object, and watching it unfold into the detail says something the slide cannot.
  - Everything else uses `_slidePage` (`core/router/app_router.dart`): `CupertinoPage` on iOS (a side slide that also carries the interactive edge swipe-back), a 300 ms slide-from-right on Android and web. The old `_listFlipPage` is gone — with the cover flight removed it was `_detailPage` under another name.
  - `_detailPage` — the 460 ms opaque push whose duration exists purely as a Hero flight window — is now reserved for the three routes that still animate something: in-list venue/event detail, the daily-drop detail, and the feed-bundle see-all.
- **Not reverted, because they are not "an image moving to its destination"**: the Discovery `Mapa` pill expanding into `/map`, the feed bundle block morphing into its see-all page *as one unit*, the daily-drop wordmark flying into the drop header, and the in-place overlay morphs (search pill → search overlay, chat bar, collage → fullscreen gallery). Each of these is a single deliberate gesture on one surface rather than a blanket treatment of every open.
- **The preloading stays — all of it.** `warmHeroImage` (image precache on tap), `cacheDetailSeed` / `standaloneDetailBgProvider` (frame-1 detail seed), `cacheZineCoverSeed` / `ZineCoverLoading` (frame-1 zine cover), `SokoRevealOnSettle` (build the heavy page body off the transition frames), `SokoCardImage.instant`. These were built to serve the flights, but the value they actually deliver is that the destination paints its real content on the first frame instead of a placeholder — which is most of what made those opens feel fast. Removing them with the flights would have made the revert a regression.
- **Consequence for the feed**: `feedHeroTokens` — the one-Hero-owner-per-entity-id election that existed because two Heroes sharing a tag on one route assert — is deleted along with the tags it guarded.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`**: a feed card whose image flies into the detail is now the **regression**. So is a Library thumbnail flying into a zine cover.
- **Where it lives**: `core/router/app_router.dart` (`_slidePage` / `_detailPage`), `features/lists/utils/zine_hero_tags.dart` (the surviving tag pair), `shared/widgets/hero_image_warmup.dart` (`warmHeroImage`, `detailPhotoHeroFlightShuttle`, `linearHeroRect`).

### D354: The detail sheet does not morph into the detail page — it closes and the page replaces it (user-directed, 2026-09-15, PROD-4438)

- **Problem**: the item-detail sheet (a chat card or map pin opens it; `showItemDetailSheet`) had a "See full detail" CTA that grew the sheet to full height while its body cross-faded from the compact preview into the full detail, pushed the routed page underneath, then popped the sheet — one continuous motion, in theory, with no visible page swap. In practice it was four animations on the same content: a 260 ms cross-fade, a 300 ms grow on a different curve, the route's own 350-460 ms push transition playing invisibly behind the opaque sheet, and finally Material's 200 ms sheet exit. That last one contradicted the other three — by then the grown sheet was pixel-identical to the page beneath it, so popping slid the detail *downward off the screen* while a duplicate arrived underneath (on iOS, perpendicular to the Cupertino slide). The user's verdict on seeing it: *"the animation is very weird"*, then *"it should immediately close and be replaced by the page"*.
- **Choice**: no grow, no morph. `_openFullPage` pushes the routed page and drops the sheet in the same frame — `router.push(path)` first so the page is mounted before the sheet goes, then `Navigator.removeRoute(sheetRoute)` rather than `pop()` so nothing animates. The sheet is always the compact preview; the routed page is always the full detail.
  - On iOS the page arrives with `CupertinoPage`'s own right-to-left slide (`_detailPage`), which is the platform's "opened a page" motion and matches [D353](#d353-only-the-zine-page--item-detail-open-carries-a-shared-element-flight-every-other-page-opens-from-the-side-user-directed-2026-09-15-no-ticket). Elsewhere `_detailPage` paints it opaque in place, so it is a clean cut like every other detail push in the app.
  - Deleted with the grow: the `_expanded` state and the in-place preview→full morph, and `maxSize: 1.0` on the sheet (it existed only so the grow could fill the screen; back to the DS default `0.95`).
  - Kept: the sibling-swipe cross-fade between two items inside the sheet, now top-aligned — `AnimatedSwitcher`'s default `Stack(alignment: center)` slid two differently-sized bodies vertically past each other for the whole fade.
- **Same direction as D353**, decided the same day and by the same instinct: the app had accumulated bespoke transitions for page opens, and each was defensible alone. A morph has to earn its place; "the sheet becomes the page" did not, because the page was going to be pushed anyway and the morph only bought a way to disagree with it.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`**: a detail sheet that grows to full height before the page appears is now the **regression**. So is a downward slide of the sheet after the page is already behind it.
- **Where it lives**: `features/item_detail/widgets/item_detail_sheet.dart` (`_openFullPage`), guarded by `test/lint/detail_handoff_motion_test.dart`.

### D355: The card date sticker wraps to two lines rather than overflowing its poster (user-directed, 2026-09-15, no ticket)

- **Problem**: on the onboarding vibe carousel (PT), the [`RotatedDateTag`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/rotated_date_tag.dart) sitting on a [`SokoEntityCard`](../../heyl_app/lib/shared/widgets/soko_entity_card.dart) poster was **wider than the card**. "Este fim de semana" (EN "This weekend") typeset at 14px runs past a 105–170px poster, and the sticker is `Positioned(top:6, right:6)` inside a `Stack(clipBehavior: Clip.none)` — so it spilled left across the neighbouring cards, clipped at the row edge, and read as a floating label belonging to nobody. User: *"we need to make the text adjust"*.
- **Choice**: the tag takes an optional `maxWidth`; when set the label **wraps onto a second line** (`maxLines: 2`, `softWrap`, `textAlign.center`, 1.15 leading) inside that cap. `SokoEntityCard` passes `width - 12` (the card less its 6px inset each side). A short label ("Hoje") is untouched — the pill still hugs it on one line.
  - **Not shrink-to-fit.** The first pass used `FittedBox(fit: scaleDown)`, which held the Figma one-line shape but typeset the long PT/ES labels at ~10px on a 105px card. Wrapping keeps the Figma 14px `Mobile/B2 Reg` size, which is the part of the spec that matters on a sticker this small; the one-line shape is not.
  - **Divergence from Figma**, deliberate: `Tag` (`7660:27990`) is a single-line pill. It is drawn with English copy at detail-hero width, where one line always fits. Nothing in the frame covers a 105px card with a Portuguese label.
- **Uncapped call sites are unchanged** — event detail hero, the zine page, the map sheet pass no `maxWidth` and keep the single-line 14px/1.0 treatment. Only the card surface opts in.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`**: a date sticker overlapping the card to its left, or one whose text is visibly smaller than 14px, is now the **regression**. Two lines on a narrow card is correct.
- **Where it lives**: `shared/widgets/rotated_date_tag.dart` (`maxWidth`), `shared/widgets/soko_entity_card.dart` (passes it), guarded by `test/shared/widgets/rotated_date_tag_max_width_test.dart`.

### D356: The Library calendar is rebuilt from measured Figma values, and its agenda rows are the shared bundle row (2026-09-15, no ticket)

- **Problem**: two years of calendar work had been done *against the previous implementation* rather than against the design. Every number in `ListCalendarView` was inherited — a `sokoShade5` card at 16 px radius, 8 px day cells, `Expanded` + `AspectRatio` cells that touched each other and stretched with the viewport — and each was defensible on its own, so nobody re-derived them. Reviewed side by side with Figma `6453:11699` (Soko -- shared), the card colour, both radii, the cell size, both gaps, the weekday labels and the select-field chrome were all wrong at once.
- **Choice**: re-derive the whole component from the frame, by **measuring it rather than eyeballing it** — `get_metadata` for geometry, and pixel-sampling the rendered PNG for colour:
  - 40 px cells on a 54 px horizontal pitch (14 gap) and 46 px vertical pitch (6 gap), in a 364 px table.
  - Card ground is `sokoInk8`, not an opaque shade — the sampled `#EADFDF` is exactly `sokoInk8` over `sokoPaper`, and the shade ramp has no such step. The month/year fields are the **same token layered over the card** (`#DDD0D0`), which is what makes them read as recesses rather than chips on top. Two different opaque shades had been guessed for these; the design builds both surfaces from one token.
  - Card radius 6 (was 16); day cells 2 (was 8) — near-square, not rounded chips.
  - Day numbers hold one weight in every state. The fill carries the state, and the old w600-on-event-days double-signalled what the pink already says.
  - Weekday labels are two letters, **with a per-locale collision fallback** — pt collapses `seg.`/`sex.` → `se` and `qua.`/`qui.` → `qu`, so a locale whose two-letter set is not 7 distinct strings keeps intl's full abbreviation. Shipping the design's literal two letters everywhere would have made the pt strip ambiguous.
- **The card is capped at the 400 px column it is drawn for.** The Library page sits inside `PageContent`, which caps its column at 480 on desktop; the uncapped card grew to 448, the cells pinned at their 40 px maximum, and every pixel of slack went into the gaps — 22 against the design's 14. Mobile was already correct, so this was invisible on a phone. See [`learnings/pagecontent-is-a-center-and-expands-to-fill.md`](../learnings/pagecontent-is-a-center-and-expands-to-fill.md) § *A component authored at a fixed design width*.
- **Adjacent-month days page the grid on tap** rather than selecting a day the grid cannot draw. The old behaviour put the selection somewhere invisible and swapped the agenda under it — a silent wrong-state, not a missing feature.
- **The agenda rows are `FeedBundleRow.custom` via [`LibraryItemRow`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/library/widgets/library_item_row.dart), not a local layout.** The rows under the grid were the only place in the app drawing a saved event as a 54 px square thumb and a `title • subtitle` pair inside a bordered white card: wrong thumbnail shape (no ticket perforation), wrong type scale, and a card border nothing else has. `ListItemRow` keeps the behaviour (add-to-list sheet, guest gate, owner delete) and hands the drawing to the shared row. `LibraryItemRow` gained `trailing` (Library's own rows carry state in `showPin`, not a button) and `forceListLayout` (the agenda hangs off a picked day and must not become grid tiles because the Library was last left in grid mode).
- **Known divergence, unresolved**: the frame renders day numbers `#44131D` and `get_design_context` reports the style as `Soko/Ink: #44131D`, but `app_colors.dart` documents `#44131D` as `Soko/Ink 10` and `#3B0F18` as Soko/Ink, designer-confirmed 2026-08-28. Shipped on `AppColors.sokoInk` so the calendar does not become the only surface on a different ink; the cost is a 1-2 unit drift when diffing a screenshot against the frame. See [`design-tokens.md`](design-tokens.md) § Soko palette.
- **Still Material glyphs**: the header `‹ ›` chevrons. The design's thin chevrons are not in `assets/`, and the only Soko chevron we ship (`icons/library/icon-arrow-down.svg`) has a stem. Exporting the pair is an open follow-up, not a decision.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`**: day cells that touch, a rounded-chip cell radius, a card lighter than the page's ink-8 tint, gaps wider than 14 px on desktop, or an agenda row inside a bordered white card are now **regressions**.

### D357: The profiling zines "Explore the app" CTA flows inline below the cards, not pinned to the bottom edge (2026-09-16, PROD-2963)

- **Problem**: on the last profiling step ("Só para ti" zines, [`smart_lists_screen.dart`](../../heyl_app/lib/features/user_profiling/screens/smart_lists_screen.dart)), the "Explore the app" CTA was a full-width `SokoCtaButton` rendered as the last child of the outer `Column`, after the `Expanded` scroll region, with a fixed 24 px bottom pad and no safe-area handling. On some Androids (3-button and gesture nav) its lower edge sat on top of the system navigation bar, so a tap on the button also fired the system nav, or landed on the nav only. A user reported it as "the button is difficult to click".
- **Choice**: the CTA moves **inside** the scroll column, 40 px below the zine grid, wrapped in `Center` with `expand: false` so it hugs its label instead of spanning the width. The scroll's bottom pad drops from 96 to 40 (there is no fixed footer left to clear). Net effect: the button sits visibly higher, close to the content it follows, with free space below it, and never overlaps the system bar regardless of nav style.
  - **Not `SafeArea`/`viewPadding`.** A `SafeArea` around the pinned footer would have fixed the overlap but kept the button glued to the bottom, which the ticket explicitly did not want; the inline placement also removes the "last row hidden under the footer on short viewports" problem that the 96 px pad existed for.
  - This is the one profiling step that already broke the "content vertically centered" layout convention of the other steps (D184), so an inline CTA costs nothing there.
- **For `/compare-ui` & `/visual-audit`**: a full-width pink CTA flush with the bottom edge on this screen is now the **regression**. Expected: a centered, label-width pink button a short gap under the last card row.
- **Where it lives**: `smart_lists_screen.dart` (`_ListsFooter` + scroll padding), guarded by `test/features/user_profiling/screens/smart_lists_screen_test.dart`.
